# Learnings archive: 2026-10

Entries dated 2026-10, moved verbatim from `docs/learnings.md` on 2026-11-22, newest first (the original order). Append new entries for the current month at the top of this file (below this note). Promote an entry to the digest in `docs/learnings.md` only if it is durable.

## 2026-10-31: City automation vs the AI's army quota, and the train-1031 UI review fixes

- **"Science focus" picked a Horseman while a Library was buildable because the AI's army-size quota outranks any focus weight.** `automated_choice` reuses Hard's `item_value`, where "the empire has fewer soldiers than cities (+1 from three cities)" returns 2200+ for a military unit, far above a building's value even after the 4x focus weight. A human with five cities and a thin army would have got soldiers from every Science or Gold city until the quota was met. Now a Science or Gold focus skips military units unless an enemy is near the city or no soldier stands in it (`focus_beats_the_army_quota_but_not_an_empty_or_threatened_city`); an empty garrison still wins, and the panel says so (`autoPickReason`: "No Science building available; a Horseman fills the empty garrison."). Only the wasm path calls `city_automation_choice`, so no AI pin moves.
- **An automated idle city must read as handled everywhere:** the banner meter shows "Auto" with the focus icon instead of the red "Build" prompt, the panel title says "Chooses at Ready · Science focus", and the "Production will be wasted" warning is suppressed (the pick lands at Ready). The automation row sits under the title when the city is idle: the idle auto-scroll (`revealIdleProductionPicker`) put the picker heading under the sticky head and scrolled the row out of view at 1000x600.
- **The Auto mark lives inside the banner's meter strip, never above the banner:** a tag at a fixed offset from the name plate overprinted the name and the meters at every zoom. Zoomed out it sits above the seal.
- **`CONSTRUCTION_ANIMATION = false` (designer has not approved the clips) still shows the painted site stills:** `drawConstructionSites` draws from the view and is not gated by the flag, but the render's "active site" and "old site" holds were; with the flag off they hid a site for the length of a moment nothing then drew. Gate them with the flag. `e2e/moments-site.spec.ts` can shoot the wrong place: the selected unit's follow-camera re-centres after the spec sets the camera; press Escape first.

## 2026-10-31: Integration train `claude/train-1031` (17 Codex branches onto the India master; era objectives merged, measured, then left out): the textual conflicts were the easy part

- **What went on, in order.** Infrastructure and safety (`stale-worktrees-apply`, `save-load-limits`, `oss-ci-toolchain`, `test-flakes-1031`, `plan-status-semantics`), `rule-mirrors`, the agreed items (`land-agreed-rules`, `land-agreed-ui`), the faction and AI rules (`germany-mongol-1031`, `sparta-siege`, `aztecs`, `ai-military-scouting` with its gene still 0), the revivals (`revive-artillery-siege`, `revive-aircraft-oil`, `revive-era-objectives`) and the three UI branches (`city-automation`, `art-ship-future-celebrations`, `construction-moments`). The revivals were let in on the designer's bar (human-seat delta's 95% interval includes zero): artillery 0.00 pp [-1.61, +1.61], aircraft Oil -1.22 pp [-2.54, +0.10], era objectives -1.71 pp [-3.61, +0.24] (thin-war check red on master 32/64 and branch 28/64). Mid-train the designer decided era objectives need a full redesign: the merge was reverted with one `git revert -m 1` commit (plus the `BuildingKind::ALL` length), the generated files were regenerated, and the benchmark was rerun. A later landing of that branch is a revert of the revert.
- **Order matters more than the conflict count.** `rule-mirrors` moved faction stats, terrain yields, class bonuses and city spacing out of TypeScript into generated tables read from Rust. Every later branch that had edited the old TS (`germany-mongol-1031`: `unitStatsFor(..., industrialized)`, the Ruhr Foundry numbers) conflicted in `data.ts` against code that no longer existed. Resolution: take the projection side, keep the rule in Rust and the generated table, and keep only what has no Rust home (Germany's move penalty ends with Industrialization, so `unitStatsFor` restores the plain unit's moves when its `industrialized` flag is set, mirroring core `unit_moves_for`).
- **Where git merged cleanly and the build still broke.** `BuildingKind::ALL` is a fixed-size array: India added `ImperialDiwan` (22) and the era branch added `TriumphalArch`, git merged both lines, and the declared length stayed 22 (`cargo test` failed to compile on the box; the earlier `cargo check` had run before that merge). `Faction::Japan` came back in test code from branches cut before India (`event_visibility`, `ui_rules`, wasm `lib.rs`, `ruleProjections.wasm.test.ts`, `actions_tests`). Run `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` on the box after every merge that adds enum variants or touches a faction name, not once at the end.
- **A branch that deletes a test file conflicts with every branch that touches it.** `land-agreed-rules` deleted `scoring_tests.rs` (1,068 lines pinning the legacy five-title scoring behind `legacy_vp`); `save-load-limits` and `aztecs` had both edited it. Git reports modify/delete; the right answer was to delete it again (the tests pin a rule that no longer exists) and to refresh the `actions.wasm.test.ts.snap` sources that still listed `MostTerritory`, `MostAdvanced` and `MostPopulous`.
- **Aztecs vs India: the Swarm rule.** The Aztecs branch was cut when Japan owned the population goal and restored the AI's Swarm planning that master had deleted as dormant (`swarm_weakness_value`, `flower_wars_campaign_value`). On the train the Swarm is the Aztecs' New Fire (`default_rule(Aztecs) == Swarm`), India keeps `Diwan`, and Sakoku stays reserved for the returning Japan. The `Japan` serde alias still loads old saves as India.
- **Codex cannot launch a browser, so every UI branch arrived with stale Playwright specs.** The first `--app` verify failed 22 specs. Stale expectations: the Guide chapter and lede ("The eight peoples" became "The peoples", nine `h3`s, the Foundry sentence), the Mongol sheet's Workshop-capture sentence, the Foundry's piece comparison (Workshop +2 became Factory +5), the lobby loop that picked seat 9 (there are eight seats for nine peoples), the LAN report line (the player's name is appended only when another team shares the faction, and the 9-faction draw no longer repeats one), the coalition fixture (three crowns and a goal are 5 VP; with the default target the "coalition" lead is gone, so it sets `vp_target: 5`). Real defects: Diplomacy's new Inbox/Relations tab row pushed Decline off the card at 1000x600 (the tabs now replace the kicker line), the Cities overview selection column was 15 px (24 px minimum) and overflowed 1000x600 and shifted every `td` index the spec reads, `howYouWin` crashed for an eliminated empire (`row.members` empty), and the turn report said "sent the The New Fire". Never merge UI from Codex on its word, and run `--app` for the train.
- **Three Rust tests failed only on the train and none was a rule bug.** `hard_vs_human`'s two first-map milestone tests (`plain_hard_rivals_are_the_default_benchmark`, `rival_search_is_the_old_benchmark`) joined the workspace run in `test-flakes-1031` and were already stale on master (cities and VP milestones of one fixed game); they pin the train's output now and will move with any AI change. `sim_metrics::difficulty_rotation_reaches_brains_tapes_and_report_denominators` needs a VP leader at the turn limit; no game has one at 20 turns on the train (the crowns alone do not separate them), so its limit is 60. The hall snapshots gained `SupplyShortage` Oil events (aircraft Oil) and lost three retired Mongol Workshop-siege cases.
- **Tooling gotchas.** (1) `vitest run -u FILE FILE` ran only one file in this setup (the first path was consumed as the option's value): put `-u` last. (2) `tools/cloud/verify-remote.sh BRANCH --profile extended` ran only the Rust lanes here (no WASM, tsc, vitest or Playwright); `--app` turns the app lane on. (3) `oss-ci-toolchain` adds `rust-toolchain.toml` (1.98.1): the first cargo call on the Mac downloaded that toolchain, and the box already has it. (4) `run-batch.sh` takes `--label` only after `--` (it is a sim argument). (5) A worktree-isolated agent cannot run `git` inside a compound or loop command; script the edits and keep git calls plain.
- **Generated files, how each was regenerated, once, on the box after the last merge:** `app/src/rules.generated.json` with `UPDATE_UI_RULES=1 cargo test -p legatum-ai --test ui_rules`; scenario hashes with `rehash_scenarios`; `garrison-current-hashes.json` with the ignored `ranged_garrison::repin_current_garrison_hashes`; fixtures and runner pins with `tools/sim/repin.sh`; hall and actions vitest snapshots with `vitest run FILE FILE -u`, reading the diff first. Moved pins against master: `default_ai_tapes` 14081844032445751818 / 1606395668796513029 / 4237561181924065337 became 2961716335793059069 / 10322370699520791456 / 5963887364044227727, and `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape` 3894838396972783344 became 6642887343691284094. During the merges `pins.json`, the three mixed fixtures and every scenario hash were taken from the train side and never hand-merged.
- **Combined measurement (seed 310001, 256 salts x 8 maps = 2,048 games per binary, `LEGATUM_HVH_RESEED_DICE=1`, plain Hard rivals, one all-seats `ctl` arm, paired bootstrap over salts).** Master 456/2,048 (22.27%). The first train, with the era revival, won 434 (21.19%): -1.07 pp [-2.93, +0.73]. The final train, without it, won **517 (25.24%): +2.98 pp [+1.07, +4.88]**, so the revival cost about four points on this benchmark and the other branches together gain. Per map, final train against master: Switzerland 8.3 +5.1 [+0.8, +9.4], Sparta 8.4 +3.9 [-3.5, +10.9], India (old Japan map) 8.4 +0.4, Celts 8.4 +1.6, Mongols 8.5 -1.2, Switzerland 8.5 -1.2, Germany 8.6 +16.0 [+8.2, +23.1] (the Ruhr Foundry), Sparta 8.6 -0.8; no map is clearly negative. Stalls 1 to 0, latest end turn 954 to 873. The first number in the era-objectives, aircraft and artillery reports (450/2,048 for master) differs from this master's 456 only by the seed block.
- **64-game all-faction smoke (Hard, eight seats, one of the nine factions omitted per game in rotation, 56 to 57 seats a faction, seed 8320000), final train:** 0 panics, 0 stalls, games ended on turns 168 to 388, 48 invalid orders (28 invisible targets, 20 blocked spawn tiles; the same kinds as the Aztecs report). Mean cities / VP: Athens 3.81 / 4.68, Aztecs 4.74 / 5.00, Byzantium 3.53 / 4.46, Celts 4.32 / 4.70, Germany 3.65 / 3.84, India 3.75 / 3.60, Mongols 6.53 / 6.26, Sparta 8.58 / 6.75, Switzerland 4.40 / 4.00. Aztecs: 325 Flower Wars population, 20 New Fire completions. The 50-game verify smoke ended with 0 stalls.

## 2026-10-31: Merging the India branch onto a master that moved 500 commits (`claude/india-on`): the conflicts were few, the silent Japan references were the work

- **The India branch retired the Japan slot, it did not keep a second faction.** `Faction::Japan` no longer exists: `Faction::India` takes its place and `Japan`/`japan` (plus the old `Choir` aliases) are serde aliases, so saves, scenario states, AI memory (`japan_links`, `japan_network_guard`, `japan_objective_reach`) and `researchFixture('Japan')`-style strings from before 2026-10-25 load as India. Sakoku, `BuildingKind::BroodHall` (Rice Storehouse) and the Swarm are reserved for the redesigned Japan and selectable by no faction. So "Japan stays playable until Tokugawa" is not what the branch does: there is no Japan seat until Tokugawa lands, and a pre-India Japan seat plays India's rules.
- **Only 25 files conflicted, but master's new code kept saying Japan.** Git merged every master line that mentioned Japan next to unchanged context without complaint: `Faction::Japan` in six Rust test and AI files (`scoring_tests`, `view`, `cities_tests`, `siege`, `civilians`, `army_churn`), `'Japan'` as the example faction in nine Playwright and vitest files, and the faction lists in `scripts/perf-wasm-*.mjs`, `wasm-search-check.mjs` and `crates/wasm/examples/ai_budget.rs`. `cargo check --all-targets` finds the Rust ones (the first run failed on a half-renamed test variable), `tsc` the typed ones. For a rename like this, grep the merged tree for the old name, do not rely on the conflict list.
- **Generated files, how each was regenerated:** `app/src/rules.generated.json` with `UPDATE_UI_RULES=1 cargo test -p legatum-ai --test ui_rules` (no diff: the merged text was already right); scenario state hashes with `rehash_scenarios` over `scenarios/*.json scenarios/lab/*.json scenarios/war/*.json` (17 files moved, every scenario with a Japan player, because the state hash covers the faction variant); fixtures, runner pins with `tools/sim/repin.sh`; `crates/ai/tests/fixtures/garrison-current-hashes.json` with the ignored `ranged_garrison::repin_current_garrison_hashes` test (not part of `repin.sh`, and its five dependent tests fail until it is run). Moved pins: `default_ai_tapes` (all three), `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape`. The vitest snapshots needed no update.
- **Conflict rule that worked:** take master's side of a hunk and read Japan as India, because the India branch's only edit in those hunks was the rename, and master's edit was the real change (rank badges now share one path, build cards gained `summary`, copy-rule patterns, AI search tests). `CityPanel.tsx` was the one hunk that needed a look: master had deleted the `prod-effect` line where the branch had renamed a default faction, so taking master was right.
- **BSD `sed` has no `\b`.** `sed 's/\bjapan\b/india/'` on macOS changed nothing and the next compile failed. Use `perl -pi -e` for word-boundary renames.
- **Old saves:** `crates/wasm/tests/fixtures/saves/p8918-japan-ai-t38.legatum` is the real autosave from before 8.9.18 (Sparta human, a Japan AI in seat 3, `japan_links` in the AI memory). `legacy_saves.rs::playtest_8918_japan_seat_loads_as_india_and_plays_on` loads it, checks the seat is India, plays five turns and checks the re-save writes `India`. The older Japan save `p893-unseen-attacker-2119` already covered the loader.
- **What did not carry over:** the `war-japan-*` scenario ids and their descriptions (the designer's Japan games) stay as history, but their seat now plays India's rules, so their recorded Hard results describe the old Japan; the `japan-1026` AI fixtures load as India and every AI test still passes.
- **Second merge, onto the mounted rebalance and audit round 5 (2026-10-31, master `b8520ae74`):** 7 conflicts, none about India's rules: `unlockCard.wasm.test.ts` and `ForecastCard.aftermath.test.tsx` (master's new numbers, India as the example seat), the progress file, three determinism fixtures and `pins.json`. Master's new `Faction::Japan` / `'Japan'` kept appearing in typed code (`units_tests`, `combat_scenarios/mounted.rs`, `combat.ts`, `aftermath*`, `p881`, `tour/mounted.spec.ts`); renamed. `rules.generated.json`, the scenarios and the garrison hashes regenerated with no change; `repin.sh` moved `default_ai_tapes` (all three) and `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape` again. India rides the generic mounted rules: no faction exemption in `unit_stats_for` or `mounted_city_penalty`, so the 130% riders, +40% class bonus and -33% city charge apply (`units_tests::india_rides_the_generic_mounted_rules`). In 128 Hard games India builds 7.3 mounted units a seat (22.5% of the units it builds, second to the Celts at 25.6%; field 11% to 19%), so its AI uses horses. Extended verify failed only `layout.spec.ts` "two humans: the era chip and the research screen fit" (a browser-console transport error) and `wonder-visibility.spec.ts` "the wonder tile in every lens at 1000x600" (120 s timeout), the two box-load flakes; both plus the `1100x700` layout case passed alone, 12 of 12 at `--repeat-each=3`.
- **Gates (2026-10-31):** extended box verify passed, including the full Playwright run (580 expected, 0 unexpected, 0 flaky, 26 skipped, the screen tour excluded by the profile) and vitest, on a branch whose final Playwright run Codex had not done. Functional smoke, 64 balanced 8-empire Hard games (seed 9100001): 0 panics, 0 stalls, all VP holds. India: 3.1% wins (2 of 64), mean cities 2.5 / 4.1 / 4.9 at T50 / T100 / T150 (field: 2.3 to 3.0 / 3.6 to 4.7 / 4.0 to 5.8), final VP 3.2 against 3.97 to 7.17 for the others, eliminated in 25% of games (field 6% to 14%). It builds Revenue Offices (59 of 64 seats; median first office T79; 30% of non-capital cities have one at T100, 50% at T150; every city has one at some turn in 24 games) and completes the Imperial Diwan in 13 games (median T152, first T71; one more was held through capture), so the goal fires. Functional only: no balance claim.
## 2026-10-31: The mounted rebalance is neutral on the human-seat benchmark, and Hard now builds and uses horses (`claude/mounted-on`)

- **Benchmark (all seats, same salts):** `hard_vs_human` ctl arm, 2,048 games per binary, seed 290001, reseeded dice. Master 431 (21.04%), branch 423 (20.65%); paired **-0.39 pp [-2.44, +1.66]**. No win-rate cost for a rule that changes every empire's army. Per map the swings (Sparta -15, Switzerland -10, Mongols -6, Germany +12 of 256) are map noise.
- **Census (128 all-Hard 8-player games, pre-Modern units):** mounted 15.4 -> 24.2% of units and 17.5 -> 26.9% of Production; melee 40.3 -> 34.7% and ranged 42.2 -> 38.5% of Production. Hard builds horses on its own (the seat: 6.3 -> 9.8 a game) and uses them: attacks 2,984 -> 8,056 on units and 774 -> 1,942 on cities, with the same loss rate per rider (0.66 per unit built). Melee city attacks unchanged (3,200 -> 3,203): the -33% kept city capture the melee line's job, and the rider city attacks replaced ranged ones (3,915 -> 3,463). No class dominates; ranged is still the largest Production share.
- **Combat lab on the merged branch** is byte-identical to the pre-merge `combat_scenarios --mounted` report, so the 130/40/-33 numbers stand.
- **Open item:** two Japan-map games (salts 290003, 290087) ran to the 1,200-turn cap with every empire alive on the branch, none on master. Not yet looked at; check whether it is a rule stalemate before it shows in a playtest.
- **Gotcha, counting use:** `unit_census` now also tallies `Event::Battle` attackers by class (looking the attacker's kind up in the pre-turn state, since it may die in the turn) and `UnitDestroyed`. Production counts alone would have missed that riders were also fought more, not just built more.
- Details and tables: `docs/sim-results/unit-classes/mounted.md` (Merged-branch measurement).

## 2026-10-31: Riders were bad because of the numbers, not the triangle (the mounted fix)

- **Cause:** pre-Modern mounted units stood at or below their era's melee line (Horseman 18 against the Spearman's 20) and cost about 35% more, and the flat +18 class bonus then made every charge into spears a massacre (a Horseman dealt 13 and took 67). At equal Production a spear line beat riders 100% with a 4 to 5.5x Production exchange, and riders lost every fight against a screened gunline (0 to 8%). The AI trained 14.6% mounted before the Modern era.
- **Fix (designer, 2026-10-31, the Civ 6 way):** riders at 130% of the era's melee line (`data::MOUNTED_STRENGTH_PERCENT`; Horseman 26, Cataphract and Keshig 33, Knight 42, Cuirassier 57, Cavalry 79, costs unchanged), the class bonus as 40% of the unit's own base strength (`combat::CLASS_BONUS_PERCENT`, labels "(+40%)"), and riders charging a city lose 33% of base strength (`combat::MOUNTED_CITY_PENALTY_PERCENT`, line "Mounted against a city (-33%)").
- **Chosen from a grid** (`combat_scenarios --mounted`, new, plus a `combat_lab` subset; `docs/sim-results/unit-classes/mounted.md`): 130/40 keeps the spear line winning every era with a 1.4 to 1.8x exchange, the flattest across eras of any variant. A 30% bonus or 135% riders let a 4/1/2 mix beat every archetype late, or riders beat spears; a 50% bonus brings the massacre back late (3x). Riders now crush ranged (3.5 to 10x) and beat a screened gunline (96 to 100%).
- **The city rule was not in the brief; the lab forced it.** Without it, all-mounted armies took 65 to 73% of cities against the melee line's 45 to 59% at equal Production. -25% only levelled walled cities; -33% makes riders level on open cities (their moves) and below the melee line against Walls. The designer should confirm it.
- **The AI had two class-blind strength readings that the new numbers exposed:** `plan_emergency_production` ranked defenders by raw strength per turn (a threatened Byzantine city facing a Spearman built a Horseman over its Legionary), and `modernization_power` counted only a unit's own class bonus, never the foe's against it (the door bought a Horseman against Phalangites). Both now use the triangle both ways (`matchup_strength`, `modernization_power`). The supply budget also treated buying a city's current unit as free, but the city keeps training another that holds its own supply (`supply::within_budget`).
- **Gotchas:** the fuzz generator's Outrider filter checked one unit per tile, so a civilian sharing a tile with a horse let an illegal attack through; `production::sims_target_mix` (gene `force_mix` 6, off by default) hard-codes the old flat-bonus response table and is now stale; the `combat_lab --subset fixed` baseline was already stale on master before this change (13 cells moved on 1e2f2b37e), rewritten here.
- **Census and suite** (32 all-AI Hard games; war suite 64 runs): pre-Modern mounted share 14.6% to 23.1% of units (16.9% to 26.1% of Production), melee 52.9% to 46.2%; games 232 to 248 turns on average; war suite 46% to 49%, parity 12 to 15 of 30 (Japan vs Sparta T142 +39, Mongol bombers T150 +25, Mongols at Chur +23, German stand -5 within noise).
## 2026-10-31: Audit round 5: Hard signs Peace while it is winning, and walks Settlers out of held cities (genes `peace_strong`, `settler_shelter`, off; quarter gates neutral)

- **Ranking on master `cabdbfd5f` (cities @20, r4 -> r5):** whole +0.358 -> +0.317; army +0.166 -> +0.147; garrison +0.147 -> +0.143; diplomacy +0.053 -> +0.051; settling +0.070 -> +0.041; production +0.011 -> +0.017 (VP @50 +0.130 -> +0.047: Sparta's remaining production gap is its opening, not peacetime economy). `docs/sim-results/ai-war/audit-round5.md`.
- **Peace:** he refused 11 Peace offers Hard would sign; in 8 his army was at least 1.5 times the proposer's and the score's "endangered" +30 (any city with pressure above its local defence, or at 100 HP or less, often a freshly captured city in resistance) outweighed the army-advantage and pressing terms. `peace_strong` 50 drops that term when our army is half again the proposer's: 6 of the 8 flip to his answer, and the audit's whole-policy gap shrinks -0.076 cities @20 (Sparta -0.36 ±0.08), robust across the hard, economic and conquest continuations. But VP @50 does not move (+0.016), and the quarter gates read seat-only -0.13 pp [-1.69, +1.43], rivals-only -1.04 [-2.73, +0.72] (1,536 games). Not gated: the extra cities do not become VP within 50 turns, and a rival that refuses its AI neighbour's Peace keeps AI-against-AI wars going.
- **Settlers:** 12 of 28 "exposed: retreat" Settler-turns were in one of his own cities. A civilian's exposure ignored the city shield military units get, though a civilian in a city falls only with the city. `settler_shelter` fixes 11 of 12 in the diff; audit -0.002, seat-only -0.98 pp: too rare to matter. The other 11 were stacked with an escort, a stance (field units avoid every exposed tile) rather than a valuation error.
- **Gotcha, continuations:** `decision_audit --continue hard,...` with `--alt params:GENE` measures only the branch turn: the window's live brain is the continuation's (`branch`), so the alt rows matched the control's 256 of 256. A candidate gene's robustness audit needs the gene inside every continuation (`audit-round5/params/peace-economic.json`, `peace-conquest.json`).
- **Tooling:** `decision_audit --peace-detail` (`peace.jsonl` with `PeaceDiagnosis`, now including `danger`: the cities `endangered` weighs) and `--prod-detail` (`prod.jsonl`, what the routine production scorer valued per city). Of 508 city-turns where he builds economy and Hard a unit, only 74 come from the routine scorer; 434 come from passes that set production first (threat, door, emergency, modernization).
- **Lesson:** an audit gain in cities that the VP horizon does not confirm is not enough for the full gate. Check the round's target category at VP @50 before reading the cities number as strength.

## 2026-10-31: `border_explained` + `fallback_risk` confirmed on the full gate (seat-only +4.35 pp; on by default)

- **Gate:** batch `w31-1031-070814`, 2,048 games per arm, seed 280001, commit `87d3a69d8`, control 364/2,048 (17.8%). Seat-only strength +4.35 pp [+2.47, +6.22] (noninferior); opponents-only +1.42 [-0.55, +3.38] (inconclusive, positive; the three quarter gates pooled +2.21 [+0.13, +4.30]); all-seats +2.54 raw (role-mixed).
- **Shipped on:** a clear seat gain, a positive rivals effect on four independent seed sets, and audit support (settling share +0.070 -> +0.037 cities @20; census +1.6 cities a game at T100). Both default 1; `shot_sweep`, `found_here` and `upgrade_wait` stay 0. `settler_routes::archived_detour_avoids_repeating_a_three_tile_loop` pins the two genes to 0 (the Settler now founds on T150 instead of walking; no assertion changed). Pins moved with `tools/sim/repin.sh`: `default_ai_tapes` [0] and [2], `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape`.

## 2026-10-31: Audit round 4: Hard sent Settlers far because a known city's own border read as a hidden city, and far fallbacks skip the trip-risk check (genes `border_explained` + `fallback_risk`, off; quarter gates seat-only +6.0 pp)

- **Diagnosis tool:** `decision_audit --settle-detail` now covers every Settler of the seat on every turn, and `settler_diagnosis` takes a probe tile (where he later founded with that Settler) and records the site search's verdict on it. `audit-round4/site_diff.py` classes the rows. This is what found the cause round 3 could not: comparing only founding turns hid where the Settler was being sent before.
- **Cause 1, border risk (30 Settler-turns, all four games):** `settlement::border_city_risks` treats every visible foreign tile as evidence of an unseen city within 3, even when a known city of that owner stands 1 or 2 hexes from it. In 29 of 30 his tile was ruled out that way with every implicating border tile explained by a known city. The AI's rule for remembered borders already handled this; the live one did not. `border_explained` applies the same rule live (the shared function and the Settle lens unchanged).
- **Cause 2, asymmetric trip risk (16 Settler-turns, 26 with the border fix):** a preferred site is dropped at legacy trip risk 100 (one enemy unit whose reach plus 2 touches the route's ellipse), but fallback sites are never charged trip risk, so a near site lost to one 6 to 11 hexes away past the same units. `fallback_risk` compares the fallback's own risk with the least risky dropped preferred site.
- **The pair interacts:** border alone raised the trip-risk rejections and read weaker rivals (-3.9 pp in its batch); together they are the gain. Gate them as one change.
- **Measured:** diff, Hard targets his tile 38 -> 48 Settler-turns, home 12 -> 7, far fallbacks 31 -> 6. Audit settling share +0.070 -> +0.037 cities @20 (Mongols -0.09, Swiss -0.12; Sparta +0.06 from one window), whole policy -0.006 (flat). Quarter gate on three salt sets: seat-only +3.12, +8.40, +6.45, pooled +5.99 [+3.91, +8.14] on 1,536 games, seat gain on 6 to 7 of 8 maps; rivals-only pooled +2.21 [+0.13, +4.30]. Census cities per game +1.61 at T100, +2.02 at T150. War suite 38% unchanged. Recommended for the full gate.
- **Lesson:** the audit's settling share undercounts a destination fix. Its 10-turn windows see one or two Settlers; the gate and census see every Settler of a game. When the diff names a narrow mechanism and the census shows it (more cities by T100), the quarter gate on several salt sets is the better judge than the window value.
- **Skipped kills, read across passes:** of the 42, the one shared cause was shooters given a move by a pass before the fire passes (12: routine garrison, strike-range exit, wounded retreat, staging; the fire passes see only unordered units and `fire_from_holds` converts only a Skip). `shot_sweep` turns those moves into lethal shots: 12 -> 7, 5 of 5 shots kills, audit and gate neutral (too rare to matter). The 15 melee holds spread over 8 passes, the top-down layers; no narrow fix. 9 of the 42 were joint kills credited to every attacker, not misses.
- **Quarter gates are cheap now:** a 7-arm, 512-games-per-arm `hard_vs_human --arms` batch on one spot server took 4 minutes and about $0.04 (`run-batch.sh --bin hard_vs_human`, `w04_gate.py`). Run two or three salt sets before reading a quarter-gate effect.

## 2026-10-31: Audit round 3: the gap is army and garrison now; founding in place and upgrade timing are real but small (genes `found_here`, `upgrade_wait`, off)

- **Ranking on master (four of his wins, 61 windows, cities @20, first audit -> now):** whole +0.54 -> +0.36; army +0.21 -> +0.17; garrison +0.09 -> +0.15; settling +0.05 -> +0.07; diplomacy +0.08 -> +0.05; production +0.15 -> +0.01 (VP @50 still +0.13, nearly all Sparta's economy builds); Raze +0.12 -> +0.01. Army and garrison have no dominant pass left: his 42 kills Hard skipped spread over twelve passes. `docs/sim-results/ai-war/audit-round3.md`.
- **Founding resolves after movement and combat (phase 5 after phase 3).** A Settler an enemy can reach this turn is captured before its found order resolves, so "found now and the Settler's danger ends" is false. The first `found_here` founded beside a Mongol Soldier and lost the 8.3 Switzerland gate map 18 -> 0 of 64 in both roles. The gene now requires `settler_capturable_this_turn` to be false.
- **Settler: on 15 of his 39 mid-game founding turns Hard walks off the tile he founds on.** `choose_site` drops the tile for an enemy army within 3, danger, trip risk, or a foreign border that could hide a city (the tile isn't even a candidate). `found_here` 2 takes the diff's misses 15 -> 5, yet the audit's settling share moves only -0.007 (whole -0.013 cities @20; Mongols -0.07 ±0.04). In a live window Hard's Settler rarely stands on his tile: the Swiss gap is where the search sends it (far sites while the near ones are dropped). Census: cities at T100 +0.30 ±0.21. Quarter gate: seat-only -0.98, rivals-only -1.17, noise.
- **Upgrades take all moves, and `plan_modernization` runs in the city pass before any attack.** `upgrade_wait` skips the upgrade for a unit with a shot, strike or counterattack the military passes would take: skipped attacks from upgrades 11 -> 3 in the diff, audit unchanged, quarter gate seat-only +3.71 [+0.98, +6.45] with all of it on two maps (8.3 Switzerland 18 -> 28, 8.4 Sparta 44 -> 50). Not evidence by the 10-31 rule.
- **Gotcha, a Settler's `moves_left` in the view is last turn's:** one that arrived last turn shows 0 and still founds (moves refresh at resolution). Gating found_here or upgrade_wait on `moves_left > 0` silently disabled both on most turns.
- **Gotcha, box census timings:** with 24 audit jobs sharing the box the census read +1.2 to +3.9 ms per seat-turn for `found_here`; 16 games back to back on the Mac read 1.386 against 1.379. Time decisions locally or on a quiet box.
- **Tooling:** `ScriptedBrain::settler_diagnosis` (thread-local Settler trace: plan branch, the verdict on its own tile, the best site of each kind) and `decision_audit --settle-detail` (`settle.jsonl`). `audit-round3/gate_maps.py` prints seat wins per map for every arm.

Opponents-only strength: `capture_hold` alone +2.64 pp [+0.90, +4.37]; both genes +3.32 [+1.51, +5.13]. Seat-only: +0.05 and +1.17 [-0.44, +2.78], inside noise. ## 2026-10-31: Hard pulled its few attackers back into every fresh capture; holding by forecast gains +3.9 pp seat-only (gene `capture_hold`, off)

- **Pattern (decision audit `--army-detail`, four of his wins on today's rules, 1,148 differing unit-turns at war):** `hold_captures` ran before any attack pass and put the nearest unit within 3 into every city in resistance, or held the one inside it, whether or not anything could retake it: 102 unit-turns and 21 of the 60 kills he made that Hard didn't. They are the audit's largest army windows (Athens T160, Swiss T160, Mongols T160, Sparta T40 and T90), often with no enemy unit in sight. Second, smaller: the opportunity strike stages past a wounded enemy he kills (`press_strike`, 14 kills).
- **Fix:** `capture_hold` 1 holds a front city first only when the visible attack would take it empty this turn, a holder would stop that, and the holder has no capture worth more; otherwise the units the attack passes leave free hold it (`hold_captures_late`), and the early anti-Mongol garrison skips it. `strike_kill` 1 leaves a strike unit with a profitable lone kill to the attack passes.
- **Measured (`docs/sim-results/ai-war/army-commit.md`):** audit army share +0.185 -> +0.140 cities @20 and whole policy +0.328 -> +0.297 (@50 +0.305 -> +0.260; VP -0.035); Sparta and Swiss gain, Mongols loses a little. Quarter gate (512, paired): seat-only +3.91 pp [+0.98, +6.84], rivals-only +1.56 [-2.15, +5.27]. War suite 38% in every arm; census captures +0.25 ±0.67, end cities -0.32 ±0.30; decision cost unchanged. `strike_kill` adds nothing in the audit (with both, army -0.021, whole +0.007); its gate arm is +5.47 / +0.59.
- **Gotcha, the audit's categories split one decision:** a unit within one tile of our city at the start of the turn is "garrison", so holding a captured city moves value between army and garrison (garrison share +0.034). Judge such a fix by the whole policy.
- **Gotcha, `--engine COMMIT` instead of `--engine here`:** each launch gets its own temporary worktree and `audit-in/`, so many audit jobs can launch at once (36 here, 5 seconds apart). Launching many `remote.sh` jobs at the same second can fail with exit 255 (`mux_client_request_session: Session open refused`); relaunch.
- **Tooling:** `OrderTrace::passes` names, per unit, the planning passes that gave or changed its order (`Planner::pass`, recorded only when tracing); `decision_audit --army-detail` writes `army.jsonl`; `tools/decision-audit/army.py classes|kills|show` reads it.

Both genes: seat-only +5.03 [+2.98, +7.08], opponents-only +3.47 [+1.48, +5.46], all-seats raw +0.34. Alone, opponents-only: `raze_value` 1 +2.10 [+0.44, +3.76]; `buy_hold` 3 +2.54 [+0.53, +4.55]. The first clear benchmark gain in weeks, so the lead shipped both (the gate's seat-only and opponents-only intervals exclude zero).

## 2026-10-31: Hard burned 40% of the cities it took, and bought on a timetable instead of when Gold paid (genes `raze_value`, `buy_hold`; built off, on by default since the entry above)

- **Razing was near automatic.** `plan_raze` valued a captured city at `city_keep_value` (75 + 20 x population: no yields, growth or VP) and read any nearby army as the loss chance `(pressure - defense) / pressure`, which is high for every fresh capture. All-AI census (512 games): 6.58 ordered razes of 16.44 captures a game. `raze_value` 1 scores the city's output, growth, objectives and Decadence against defending it or the forecast loss: razes 4.19 a game, 72% of captures kept, +0.8 cities a game at the end. In the designer's seat Hard's razes go 4 -> 0 (Mongols) and 8 -> 0 (Swiss); the Swiss raze gap (+0.05 cities @20) goes to zero.
- **Fewer captures is fewer recaptures,** not less conquest: with the cities kept, captures fall 16.4 -> 14.7 a game while every empire ends with more cities. Conquerors gain most (Sparta's census wins 119 -> 156 of 512 with both genes).
- **Where Gold went (`buy_trace` tags each purchase with its planning pass):** routine improvements 3,777 Gold a game (bought 4 turns before the city would finish them), readiness defenders 3,652, routine buildings 3,235, frontline 2,530, door 1,086, rescues 631. Every buy is a 200% copy, so the only question is timing, and no pass priced what the Gold would buy later.
- **Holding Gold works when the holder isn't the spender's gate.** `buy_hold` keeps the Gold for the upgrade bill, a defender per threatened city and (level 3) one era unit per city, capped at 25 turns of income, and only routine, readiness and frontline buys see it. Door Gold 1,086 -> 3,081 a game, rescues 631 -> 1,953, upgrades +16%, treasury median T150 79 -> 113, captures unchanged. The 10-27/10-30 failures held Gold from upgrades and the war; this hold doesn't, and they did not repeat. Cost: about 0.5 fewer cities across all empires at T100 (fewer early improvements), gone by T150.
- **Decision audit on today's master:** `buy_hold` 3 cuts Hard's purchases in his seat 184 -> 68 (Mongols) and 99 -> 71 (Swiss); his production-and-buys lead, mean of the two games, +0.12 -> +0.02 cities @20 and +0.23 -> +0.18 VP. Today's Hard is already much closer to him than the 8.9.17 Hard was (Mongols whole policy +0.14 cities @20 against +0.49). War suite 42% in every arm.
- **Gotcha, `tools/decision-audit/run.sh --engine here`:** every launch copies its inputs to the checkout's `audit-in/`, the box job packages the checkout only at its "box: shipping" line (seconds to minutes after the job id), and each finishing job deletes `audit-in/`. Concurrent launches swap tapes and params or crash ("read memory/params: No such file"). Launch one at a time, each after the previous one's "box: shipping" line, don't let a finishing job delete the folder, and don't edit the checkout until every job has shipped. Check `fidelity.json` (`alt`, `tape_turns`) of every part. The Swiss 8.9.15 tape drifts from about T150 on today's rules; the Mongols 8.9.17 tape replays exactly.

On his 8.9.17 Mongols game from T96 the Celts declare on him in 32 of 32 replays (12 with the gene off) and Hard's win rate in his seat falls 24/32 -> 19/32.

It now skips a city with a `Raze` order too. The gene only changed which game state reached that turn.

## 2026-10-31: The Celts' phony war was two Peace vetoes, not one stubborn AI (gene `dead_war_peace`, on at 12 since 2026-10-31)

- **Chain (8.9.17 Mongols save, replayed with `peace_trace`: 0 order and 0 hash mismatches over 172 turns).** Athens declared on the Celts at T47. The Celts' Contain/Campaign on Athens failed by T87 (8 city attacks, no capture). From T87 to the end *both* plans were "Peace with each other", yet the war ran to T172.
  - **Veto 1, `peace_would_be_broken` (gene `peace_redeclare` 2):** any attack by our units on the enemy in the last 8 turns blocks Peace whatever the plan wants. The war was skirmishes only after T87 (30 unit battles in 85 turns, no city attack), which kept re-arming it: the Celts were vetoed 44 of 86 turns, Athens 42.
  - **Veto 2, the Peace score:** on the other 42 turns the Celts' score was -26: base 10, war age +20, army advantage -25 and "truce forgoes pressing" -25 (their 225 against Athens's 131, counted twice), aggression -6. The zero floor for a plan that chose Peace (`peace_follows_plan`) needs `searched` non-empty, so plain Hard, which ships, never gets it.
  - **Athens's 11 offers:** 3 came while the Celts' plan fought Athens; 2 hit veto 1 (T88, T135); 6 hit veto 2 (T96, T109, T116, T146, T162, T170). The Celts never offered: the same score. Athens would have accepted on its non-vetoed turns (scores 40 to 91).
  - `rival_redeclare_permille` prices nothing: its horizon factor is `(plan_horizon 10 - PEACE_TRUCE_TURNS 10).max(0)` = 0, in both `wants_peace` and the score.
- **Census (512 eight-player Hard games, `tools/sim/dead-war-census.sh`, a war turn is dead after 10 turns with no battle between the two sides):** 27.3 wars a game, 1,197 war turns, 364 dead (30%); 20% of wars never had a battle; 30% had a 20-turn dead stretch and 10% a 40-turn one (median length of those 71 turns). 138 Peace offers a game, 81% refused; in dead stretches 45 offers, 83% refused.
- **Fix, gene `dead_war_peace` 12:** a war at least 12 turns old that the plan does not fight (no campaign, strike or unit within 2 of its cities; it does not endanger us; not a holder, the contained leader, our diplomatic target or a hollow leader) skips veto 1, drops "truce forgoes pressing", and scores +1 a turn past 12 (up to 30) plus +20 for a rival leader in reach. Census: dead war turns 364 -> 254 a game, wars with a 20-turn dead stretch 30% -> 19%, Peace deals 19.9 -> 26.0, wars 27.3 -> 32.6 (freed armies declare elsewhere), captures 16.64 -> 16.64. Decision cost within noise (19.2 -> 18.4 ms of CPU per game-turn).
- **A co-belligerent is not a dead war.** The first version took Chur T137 from 48% to 14%: the Mongols' earlier Peace offer to Japan (a dead war) used Japan's one diplomatic order for the Mongols that turn, so Japan's own Peace offer went to the Swiss, who accepted in 24 of 24 traced runs (5 of 24 without the gene). The gene now leaves alone a war with a team that is itself at war with our plan's target, the VP leader or our diplomatic target. Chur back to 48%.
- **Measured (gene 12, with the co-belligerent rule):** war suite 64 runs, seat 46% -> 47% (Mongol bombers T150 7% -> 25%, nothing else moved), all seats 46%; the W2.4 hollow fixtures stay 0%, as at control; `mongols-rich-and-empty` and `swiss-strike-the-horde` unchanged (0/50, medians 1,240 -> 1,250 and 680). What-ifs from the Mongols save (32 runs, Hard in his seat): T96 24 -> 19 of 32, the Celts make Peace with Athens at T96 in 32 of 32 (control median T168) and then declare on him in 32 of 32 (control 12); T133 26 -> 21; T60 12 -> 17 (noise at 32 runs is about 4).
- **`peace_follows_plan` 2 (plain Hard gets the floor) alone:** census dead war turns 364 -> 253, but captures 16.6 -> 16.1; suite unchanged; T96 24 -> 15, T60 12 -> 18, T133 26 -> 24. It fixes veto 2 only when the plan names this enemy, and leaves veto 1.
- **Alliance fees are a separate cause:** 11 of 19 refusals were "Signing costs 60 Gold; you have 13 to 36". That is the rule (an AI can't sign what it can't pay); fixing it means a proposer not offering to a broke partner, or a fee rule change, not the war valuation. Not in this change.

Neutral, with the right direction: the designer's seat wins slightly less against Swiss rivals that spend their hoard while their plan fights a war. The lead decided to ship it at 2.

## 2026-10-30: Decision audit: Hard in the designer's seat loses half a city per 10 turns, mostly by holding units back

- **Method** (`tools/decision-audit/run.sh`, `crates/sim/examples/decision_audit.rs`; results `docs/sim-results/ai-war/decision-audit.md`): replay each recorded win on its own engine, shadow his seat with the app's Hard every turn, and branch his orders against Hard's from the same state (32 reseeded salts, paired). Five wins, 0 hash mismatches, 4,784/4,784 AI seat-turns reproduced at the branch turns.
- **One-turn swaps barely register.** Hard takes over the next turn and makes most of the same choices again. Whole-turn value was +0.06 ±0.05 cities @50, while win chance swung −11 pp (Sparta) with VP and cities unchanged. A 10-turn policy swap (`--window 10`) gives clean signals.
- **His standing orders do not leak into Hard's play.** Clearing his paths, gotos and Sleep at the handover changed nothing (Sparta identical): Hard re-orders every unit every turn.
- **Swapping one category's orders costs something by itself.** Mixing two planners does, so `fwd` (his orders with Hard's category) and `rev` (Hard's orders with his category) charge it to opposite sides. Report their mean.
- **Results:** his 10 turns beat Hard's 10 turns by +0.51 ±0.03 cities @20 and +0.25 VP @50, in every game.
  - Army share +0.21 cities, positive in all five games: Hard steps units back or skips them where he attacks.
  - Production and buys +0.13 cities and +0.15 VP: Hard builds units and makes 80 to 396 purchases a game, against his 0 to 7.
  - Garrison +0.12, Raze +0.11. Hard razes captured cities in four of the five games.
  - Settling and research are about 0.
- **"Gold banked" is the reverse in his seat:** Hard overspends, and his banked play wins. **Walls first** shows only as the Mongols.
- **Gotcha: `winchance` credits army size.** Japan read −5.9 pp for his play while his cities were +0.63. Rank by cities and VP.
- **Gotcha: a scratchpad shared with other agents.** It held other agents' `saves/` and `mongols/` folders; give tool outputs their own subfolder.

## 2026-10-30: The door can't buy because Hard earns too little, not because it spends in the wrong order (W2 war chest, genes `war_chest`, `war_chest_standing`, off)

- **Baseline** (all-AI census, 512 Hard games, door trace): on 95% of the turns the door wants a defender it can't afford one. It buys on 4.0% of its 83,598 open-gap turns. At an open gap the treasury is a median 74 Gold, against a cheapest buy of 160, and income is a median 13 a turn. Only the Swiss (hoard) buy often: 63% unaffordable. Every war-suite defence case reads 98-100% unaffordable, except the Golden Horde hold (2,072 Gold).
- **A chest sized from the visible armies does nothing.** Armies come out of the fog only three to five turns out, and 13 Gold a turn can't fill it. The chest was held (median 580, above the treasury on 89% of gap turns), yet the treasury at the door rose only 74 to 80, and the suite, census and adaptive Sparta checks were unchanged.
- **A standing chest while at war backfires.**
  - The treasury rises (194 to 263 on threatened turns), but the Gold goes to the rescue and threat purchases that run before the door. Door buys fall from 3,376 to 2,869, while military buys rise from 6.1 to 10.8 a seat-turn.
  - Unit upgrades are what the empire gives up: 5.7 to 1.5 a seat-turn.
  - Attackers stall. War suite (seat) 47% to 40%: Mongols at Solothurn 100% to 10%, Chur 34% to 15%. All-AI captures fall from 16.8 to 15.0 a game, and Sparta's census wins from 114 to 66. Hard Sparta in the designer's seat with the gene wins 12/32 instead of 26.
  - It is the 2026-10-27 standing-army failure again, with Gold in place of units.
- **Stop rule applied** (two rounds of the same idea): both genes stay off, documented in `docs/sim-results/ai-war/war-chest.md`. More door buys need more income, cheaper defence, or a reason to hold Gold that pays off on its own, as the Swiss hoard does.
- **Measuring gotcha:** a `plan_door_defense` stderr trace in a Hard + Search scenario counts every rollout's planner as the seat. German stand logged 41,940 "seat" front-turns for 1,536 real ones. Guard such a trace with a thread-local depth set in `search::rollout_with`.
## 2026-10-30: The Swiss freeze is the hoard reserve, not the frontline check (frontline fix; gene `hoard_at_war`, off)

- **The frontline bug was real but not what froze the Swiss.** `buy_frontline_defender` returned "danger" for any threatened city, and `plan_buy` returned on it: a threatened city that already had this turn's purchase (door defense) or could buy no unit stopped every routine purchase everywhere. Fixed: it saves the reserve plus the city's cheapest quote (`Frontline::Save`) and later purchases continue above that. Neutral: every war suite case keeps its success (43%), census captures 17.55 -> 17.59, pins unchanged.
- **The 5,000 is the rule's Hoard goal used as the reserve, not a banked gene.** `ordinary_gold_reserve` returns `SWISS_HOARD_GOLD` whenever `saving_for_goal` holds, and that holds when income would reach 5,000 within the game: nearly always. Below it the cheapest Great Work quote is the reserve (1,050 in the 8.9.17 T96 fixture). Door defense ignores unheld savings, so the hoard defends; it never funds a Swiss war. Census: Swiss median Gold 530 at T150, every other faction 59-97.
- **Releasing it moves Gold, not cities.** `hoard_at_war` 2 (no hoard or Work reserve while the plan fights a war): Swiss Gold at T150 530 -> 190, Swiss captures per seat 1.04 -> 1.33, wins 20 -> 22 of 256. Level 1 (keeps the Work quote) cost wins (13). Switzerland's banked `siege_window` 2 -> 0: captures 1.41, wins 26. The war suite and `mongols-rich-and-empty` don't move in any arm, and every hollow fixture stays 0%. The Swiss bought Troopers but kept five of eight units in their cities. `hollow_march` 3 is the only lever that moves the suite, and the Swiss levers add nothing on top of it. Details: `docs/sim-results/ai-war/swiss-gold.md`.
- **The attack check now tests the attacker against a fixed defence.** With door defense on, all three attack cases read 0%, so no floor could catch a regression. `--check` plays the defenders with door off (`war_suite --opponent-params tools/sim/attack-check-defenders.json`): 98 / 89 / 81% before and after the fix. The floor is now 60%.
- **Gotcha:** box `war_trace` timings swing 2-3x when other jobs share the box. Compare decision cost only from runs made back to back on a quiet box.

## 2026-10-30: The hollow read never became a march because the army never left its cities (W2.4 `hollow_march`)

- **The chain, from the designer's 8.9.17 save (Mongols, T96: ten cities, four Horsemen, 1,432 Gold; the 8.9.17 brains reproduce all 380 recorded AI order lists, so W2.3 was live):** the Swiss plan was a Contain war on him (value 200-850) for most of T63-T96, but (1) at War with the top threat `threat_exposure` is 1 for every own city and the anti-Mongol guard garrisons every city first, so 5 of 5-6 Swiss units stood in Swiss cities; (2) `could_strike` never counts a unit standing in an own city, so every Mongol city's march force was 0-1 units and `campaign_against` read "army too weak" on every turn; (3) Switzerland's tuned `army_war_extra` is 1 (6-7 units wanted for 6 cities), production went to Colleges, Markets and Settlers; (4) the Swiss hoard reserve is 5,000, and `buy_frontline_defender` returns "danger" with Mongol Horsemen in sight, which ends `plan_buy` without buying, so 768 Gold grew to 1,600 unspent; (5) Switzerland's `siege_window` is 2, so even a gathered force forecast no capture of a 200-HP city. Byzantium: "a march opens no second front" while at War with Athens (to T98), its only known Mongol city (Almaliq, last seen T69) is ten tiles away behind closed Swiss land at Peace, Thin levies cap it at six units, and its Alliance offers to the Swiss were refused (T81, T88, T96).
- **Freeing garrisons is what moves:** `hollow_march` 3 (no standing garrison where no foreign army is in sight, the capital included) takes Chur T137 34% -> 59% and Mongol bombers T150 17% -> 26%; the war suite mean 43 -> 44%, parity 13 -> 14; the census holds (captures per game 17.6 -> 16.9, wars 25.4 -> 24.8). Raising the quota (4) and buying the march force (8) move nothing in the suite; the long-siege forecast (window 6, half the march charged) cost Chur 34 -> 20 and was removed.
- **The new fixtures stay 0%:** the Swiss can't take a walled 200-HP Mongol city with 6-11 Classical units in 20 turns, and from T96 the Hard AI in the designer's seat buys six defenders in five turns (hollowness 85 -> 32). The designer's own play (no buys) is what made him hollow; a fixture with a Hard defender measures something else.
- **The attack check's 0% is door defense, not a march failure:** `war-golden-horde`, `-thin` and `war-walls-rise` read 96/87/81% at 38eef332d and 0/0/0% at a590efad6 (door defense on by default); on today's master with the defenders' `door_defense` 0 they read 98/89/81%. The rich Swiss defender buys the counter at the door, as the W2.1 round-2 table said; the check needs a defender without it or a new floor.
- **Tool:** `war_trace` with `WAR_TRACE_DIAG=<seats>` prints each seat's plan, hollow read, campaign forecast per enemy city (march force, siege, outlook), garrisons and production (`Brain::war_diagnosis`).

## 2026-10-30: The hollow read fires and still produces no attack (8.9.17 Mongols coach review)

- **Evidence:** in the designer's 8.9.17 Mongols win the read changed AI decisions from T63 (Athens declared on him at T66 because of it), and every AI held him hollow (58) by T96. The Swiss ran Contain on him for 80 turns and Byzantium for 73, valued up to +566 and +786, with 0 campaigns and 0 attacks on any of his cities in 172 turns, though 6 to 10 of his 10 to 14 cities were empty.
- **The strongest army was out of play.** The Celts' plan was "Peace with Athens" for 85 turns, yet they refused all 11 Athenian Peace offers. Forced proposals from both sides at T96 were both refused, so the stalemate is in both peace evaluators. Putting the Celts at War with him from T96 cut Hard-in-his-seat from 22/32 to 15/32.
- **The coalition was priced out:** 11 of 19 Alliance refusals were the 60-Gold signing fee.
- **Method gotcha:** an archive's `engine_commit` is the build the recording started on and stays stale after an app upgrade. The T96 save said 8.9.16, but only the 8.9.17 AI reproduces its AI orders (380/380 against 20 departures from T63). Check with `p881_trace` at both tags before blaming or crediting a release.
- **Review:** `docs/playtests/2026-10-30-mongols-review.md`.

## 2026-10-29: A hollow read must survive the first bought defender (W2.3 `hollow_read`, `persist_hollow`)

- **Evidence:** the 8.9.15 Swiss read 66 hollow (army per city against the median empire's) on T137 and 35 on T139, after one purchase. Persistence gated on a fresh read never fired in the Chur fixture (3%); keeping a team hollow down to 25 once read took it to 34%.
- **Hollowness alone is the wrong gate:** a Sparta of four veterans reads 55-74 hollow at Japan T142, and gating persistence on it cost Japan's defence 89% -> 72%. Requiring public riches too (a crown, a live goal, a Power lead, an era lead or a Swiss bounty) put it back at 88%.
- **Conditional persistence is free in full games:** rivals with the hollow read plus `war_reaim` 1 and `peace_own_danger` 2 let the seat win +0.34 pp [-1.38, +2.07], where W2.2's blanket version was +2.10 [+0.38, +3.81] on the same seeds. The hollow read alone: +0.34 [-1.39, +2.07]. The war suite mean doesn't move (47%); the gains are in the case it was cut from. Details: `docs/sim-results/ai-war/baseline.md` W2.3.
- **Turned on (lead, 2026-10-29):** `hollow_read` 1, `persist_hollow` 1, `war_reaim` 1, `peace_own_danger` 2. Japan T142's all-seats drop (89% to 83%) is the attacking Sparta AI persisting too, which is the point. The repin moved only `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape` in `crates/sim/tests/pins.json`. The `reaim_tests` unit tests now set `persist_hollow` 0, because they test the genes against an enemy that isn't hollow.
- **Fixture gotcha:** `war-mongols-solothurn-t158` failed the hash check after the built-in Siege merge; `rehash_scenarios` fixed it. Its success went from 43% (W2.2, before the merge) to 100% in every arm.

## 2026-10-29: Persistent wars beat the fixture and lose the full game (W2.2 `war_reaim`, `peace_own_danger`)

- **Evidence:**
  - With both genes, Hard-in-seat from the Swiss T137 position fell from 44% to 23%, the coach's predicted effect.
  - In the full AWS human-seat gate (2,048 games), rivals with both genes let the seat win **+2.1 pp more** ([+0.38, +3.81]). Re-aim alone: +0.34.
- **Lesson:** the second war-AI change in a row (after door defense) that wins its fixture and doesn't help in full games. A fixture cut where persisting is right says nothing about the many games where it isn't.
- **What to do next time:** persistence must be conditional on evidence that the target is hollow (rec 2's leader read), not a blanket "don't quit". Judge every war gene on the full gate before trusting fixture gains.

## 2026-10-29: Keeping a war alive after one bought defender (genes `war_reaim`, `peace_own_danger`)

- **The march veto, not the target list, made the Mongols quit.** After Chur's campaign ended (T139), the other Swiss towns were never priced: `campaign_forecast` returns None for any march while another war is on ("a march opens no second front"; the Mongols were at war with Japan), and when any unit is in local reach but too weak it never tries the field army. Pricing the same enemy's cities with the war already open, and trying each march window (2 to 6 turns) for the best capture odds, gave Schaffhausen and Lausanne 15–19% where the whole six-turn march read 0–7%: `capture_odds` loses a tenth per turn of march, so the slowest straggler sets the odds. Chur fixture 3% → 17%, T137 Hard seat 44% → 31%.
- **A re-aim alone makes Peace offers more frequent.** Keeping a war plan on the Swiss alive lets the old any-enemy `endangered()` test offer them Peace: in the Scenario Lab cut, offers to Switzerland went from 8 to 48 of 50 runs. The two genes only work together.
- **"Only danger from X" is still too loose.** Restricted to the planned enemy, the usual local test (its units within 5 against ours within 2) fired on one Swiss Field Cannon four hexes from an empty Mongol colony, and the Mongols offered Peace on T160 (T137 Hard seat 50% instead of 44%; lab offers 34 of 50). Weighing that army against all of ours within 5 (level 2) removed it (T137 Hard seat 23%, lab 3 of 50) but cost Japan T142 9 points: there the Japanese army outnumbers the Spartan veterans by the strength estimate and loses every fight, so its Peace offers were its defence. Raw strength is the wrong measure of "losing this war"; the exchange (units lost to that enemy) is the next thing to try.
- **Pricing the bought defender into the forecast kills every target.** `war_reaim` 2 (an empty target forecast as holding the strongest unit its owner fields) put every Swiss town under the 15% floor and changed nothing. Use the purchase in the ranking, not the gate.
- **Fixture trap: scripted Peace acceptances match by proposal id.** In the Solothurn fixture the control's war survives because the Mongol offer comes a turn early and the designer's recorded acceptance names a proposal that no longer exists; moving the offer by one turn (level 1) made it match and ended the war (43% → 0%). Read a scripted-diplomacy fixture's result together with its trace.
- **The coach's recorded-orders seat no longer reproduces on master** (T137 control 2/64 against 49/64 at the 8.9.15 tag): 12 commits change the theatre enough. Size what-ifs with the Hard seat; rebuild the tape baseline on the binary you measure.
- Full games stay neutral (512 per arm on the box: seat −1.2 to −1.4 pp, rivals 0.0 to +0.6, all inside ±4). Details: `docs/sim-results/ai-war/baseline.md` W2.2.
## 2026-10-29: `siegeUnitsFixture` is shared, and its units spend Iron

- **What happened:** the 8.9.16 review fix added an Arquebusier and an Assault Trooper with Siege to `app/src/test/siegeUnitsFixture.ts` for the map shots. `e2e/siege-card.spec.ts` uses the same fixture and counts the Iron left over: Siege uses 1 Iron per unit, so its two deposits no longer left a spare, and Buy Siege stayed disabled. That failed the release check in Chromium.
- **Before adding units to a shared fixture,** grep for every spec that loads it and check what they count: supply, unit totals, positions.

## 2026-10-29: A rule change can be gated against an earlier gate's arm on the same seeds

- **Problem:** `hard_vs_human --arms` compares brains or genes on one binary, but a rule change without a toggle (Siege built in from the Industrial era) changes the binary itself.
- **Solution:** run only a `ctl` arm from the new binary with the same `--games 256 --seed 270001`, the same `LEGATUM_HVH_RESEED_DICE=1` and 4 slices. Then pair it with the earlier gate's arm that matches today's defaults (the W2.1 `door_all` arm). Put both in a fake batch dir: `slice-N/out/{ctl,siege}-games.json.gz` plus a `hard-vs-human-arms.json.gz` holding both reports, and run `w04_gate.py`.
- **Validity:** this holds only if no other AI or rule code changed between the two binaries. Check `git log` on `crates/` first.
- **Cost:** one arm on 4 servers costs about 0.05 server-hours, about $0.03.
- **Result:** Siege built in, 315 → 329 of 2,048 (+0.68 pp [−1.07, +2.43]). Files are in `docs/sim-results/ai-war/siege-industrial/`.

## 2026-10-28: A toggle sharing a line with wrapping text must float first (build cards, `claude/build-density`)

- **Evidence:** with "More" floated right after the yield text, a yield that exactly filled the line pushed More onto a line of its own (WebKit and Chromium, e.g. the Fighter's "Guards 4 tiles… within 5"). That is the waste the designer complained about. Flexbox with `flex-wrap` did the same, and without wrapping it truncated with an ellipsis, which the UI rules forbid.
- **Fix:** put the floated toggle before the text in the markup. It then takes the right end of the first line, and the yield wraps around it onto a second line when long. Inside a `<summary>`, the toggle stays put when the details open, so nothing moves under the pointer.
- **Gotcha:** an older `.prod-effect` rule still set `text-overflow: ellipsis` (computed even on an inline span), so the spec read it as truncated until the new rule set `clip`. The build-card specs now check "More is on the summary's first line, at most two lines, no ellipsis" instead of "nowrap, one line".

## 2026-10-28: Why Hard can't punish an empty, rich leader (8.9.15 Swiss review)

- **Runaway signs need a strict VP lead.** `runaway_read` adds crowns, a live goal and the Power lead only for the strict VP leader, so in a low-VP game (nobody above 3 VP until T158) a human with 1 military unit for 8 cities, 3,600 Gold and two crowns scored 30–44 in the Mongols' threat model, with no race term. The human became their top threat at T158, when the game was already decided.
- **One bought defender ends a war.** Two Mongol campaigns (37% at T137, 51% at T158) ended `insufficient_army` within two turns of a single purchase in the target town, and the plan fell back to Peace (valued −300), with seven empty Swiss cities still in reach. Keeping the T137 war cut Hard in the human's seat from 44% to 25% (64 runs). `leader_commit` 2 + `contain_after_campaign` + `coalition_own_read` changed nothing (48%).
- **`wants_peace` lets `endangered()` override the plan for every enemy.** "Hard's plan decides" returns `endangered() || target != against`, and `endangered()` counts pressure from any enemy at War. So the Mongols, pressed by Japan, proposed Peace to the Swiss mid-campaign (T161) and during the Swiss hold (T175).
- **Door defense had nothing to spend in a real game.** It fired on 24 Spartan turns and bought 0 units: every AI treasury sat at 14–190 Gold, while Sparta bought 8 tile improvements during its war. Door off from T146 changed nothing (80% vs 78%).
- **Method:** a gene that can't act in the game (`air_defense` 0 when nobody has Flight) is a clean neutral control for the tape seat. It held at 49/64, which showed that the 0/32 drops under theatre changes were real effects, not noise.
## 2026-10-28: Built-in Siege from the Industrial era touches saved scenarios, not just rules (`claude/siege-era-gear`)

- **Rule** (designer): Industrial and later land units fight walled cities as if Siege covered every tier (`data::built_in_siege`, `data::siege_cover`); they are never offered Siege, and the bought flag is cleared on upgrade and on load (`save_migration::clear_built_in_siege`) so it stops using Iron.
- **Gotcha:** eight embedded scenario states (`scenarios/lab/japan-finish-the-vow.json`, the German air and Swiss Tepid war scenarios) held Riflemen, Field Cannons and Artillery with `siege: true`. The load migration clears them, so `GameState::hash` no longer matched and `crates/wasm/tests/scenarios.rs` and the app's scenario test failed until `rehash_scenarios` re-encoded them. Any load-time migration needs a scan of `scenarios/` (gzip-base64 states) for the field it rewrites.
- **Kept `Unit::siege` as the bought flag** rather than setting it for built-in units: `search/determinize.rs` copies `UnitView::siege` into a sim state, and `resources` counts a set flag as an Iron user, so a "true for built-in" view would have charged phantom Iron inside search.
- **War suite** (Hard, 64 runs, 28 scenarios, before/after): suite 43% to 46% goal success, parity 11 to 12. Swiss Tepid T220 23% to 100% and T220 fresh 53% to 100% (its guns now ignore the Star Forts); Japan vs Sparta T142 82% to 89%; German air early 28% to 0% (a defence: Germany's Walls no longer slow Japan's Modern army; cities lost 2.71 to 3.00); Chur ring enemy losses 3.3 to 7.6, Mongol bombers T150 7.3 to 11.2 with goals unchanged. Every pre-Industrial scenario is identical.

## 2026-10-28: A panel that fits by 1 px gets a scroll hint that never leaves (WebKit unit panel, 8.9.15.1)

- **Evidence:** the 8.9.15 release check failed one WebKit tour test: an Archer's unit panel at 1000x600 scrolled (body 354 px of content in 327 px). Chromium passed. Without the scroll hint, the body has 355 px, so the content fit by 1 px.
- **Why it sticks:** `useScrollHint` shows "Scroll for more…" once the body overflows. The hint takes 28 px from the body, so the overflow becomes real and the hint stays. Any transient overflow (WebKit's slightly taller text, a late font) latches.
- **Fix:** tighter compact spacing (`max-height: 700px` in `ui/unitPanel.css`): Improve heading, needs lines, action gaps, Disband row. That gains about 20 px of slack. The spec's fit message now prints scroll and client heights, so the next failure shows its margin.
- **Rule:** budget a scroll-hinted panel with real slack (aim for 15 px or more in WebKit), not "fits in Chromium".
- **Same release:** the Fighter card capped its head at `min(60dvh, 450px)`, so at 600 px tall the head scrolled internally and sliced its hint while the dock had 80 px free. In the dock the cap is lifted (the dock scrolls the card), and the aircraft tour case now checks the head is whole.

## 2026-10-27: Defence sized to the visible army works where a standing quota backfired (gene `door_defense`)

- **Evidence** (`docs/sim-results/ai-war/door-defense.md`): with the gene on the seat, Golden Horde hold 1% → 100%, Japan T43 71% → 98%, Germany Berlin T65 95% → 100%, German stand 85% → 89%; attack checks unchanged (Golden Horde 96% → 98%, First Blood 100%). Hard Sparta from T40 against defenders with the gene wins 12/32 instead of 19/32, the opposite of the army-quota arm.
- **Idle treasuries were the gap, not missing code:** the Swiss in Golden Horde hold kept 2,235 Gold while Aarau fell, because `buy_emergency_defender` skips while saving for the 5,000 hoard unless the city is forecast to fall this turn, and the readiness buy returns while the goal is pending. A goal 40 turns away shouldn't outweigh a city.
- **Walking defenders in over several turns hurts** (Japan T142 84% → 67%): the walkers get picked off and the cities they leave go next. Only units that arrive this turn reinforce.
- **`war_trace` didn't match `war_suite`** for scenarios whose opponents played Hard + Search (German stand): it seated plain ScriptedBrains. Fixed; it also takes `WAR_TRACE_PARAMS` and prints the seat's planning time.
- **With the gene in every seat** AI attackers fail in two fixtures (Golden Horde 0%, Walls Rise 0%), but not in full games: 512 all-AI Hard games keep 15.0 captures and 0.69 eliminations per game (off: 14.6 and 0.64). The fixtures' defender had an unusually deep treasury.
- **Attribution (2026-10-28):** buys (+3.6 points of the war-suite mean) and garrisons for empty threatened cities (+3.1) carry the gene. Upgrades (−0.6) and the retake (−0.3; it fires, but nothing gains) are off by default (`door_parts` 29). The local human-seat gate is neutral in both roles (seat −1.2 [−4.5, +2.2], rivals +1.0 [−2.8, +4.7] pp, 512 games per arm).

## 2026-10-27: More defenders is not better defence (8.9.13 Sparta what-ifs)

- Against his recorded orders, a bigger army quota for every AI (`army_war_extra` 10, `army_item_bonus` 300) or a scripted Walls+shooters floor cut his captures by T125 from 10.6 to 3 and to 2 (from T45). Against a Hard Sparta in his seat the same arms made Sparta stronger: from T40 it won 7 → 16 of 32 with the quota, 31 of 32 with the floor, because the defenders gave up their economy. `modernization_upgrade` was the only arm that helped against both (Hard seat from T1 7 → 2 of 32; his captures −4 to −5). Measure defence changes against an adaptive attacker as well as a recorded one.
- A game stopped early (here T126) leaves the tape seat passive after its last turn, so the branch's winner is meaningless; read captures and VP at the stop turn.

## 2026-10-27: One Bomber deletes a walled city's HP per turn, and his tape seat is fragile mid-conquest

- **Air ignores Walls entirely** (`combat::wall_damage_percent` returns 100 when `air`): in the 8.9.13 Athens game one Bomber did 204–261 damage to 200-HP Mongol cities at city strength 39–67, so every strike took a city to 0 and an Infantry walked in. The only counter is a Fighter (same Flight tech) or recapturing the base; an empire an era behind has none. Rule question for the user in the review.
- **No AI got Flight on its own**: Byzantium spent T156–T169 in the Modern era without it and received it from his Share the Learning on T169, then queued Bombers, not Fighters.
- **Method:** from T133–T150 his recorded-orders seat loses 32 → 0/32 under any perturbation of the Mongol theatre (a Celtic plan reset as much as the upgrade gene), because one missed capture (Sarai, T165) stops his hold and his frozen orders never recover; neutral perturbations stay 31–32/32. Size effects with the Hard seat; the runner (`coach8913_whatif.rs`, with `reset_plan`) is in `~/LegatumRuns/coach-tools/coach-8913-athens/`.
- **`LEGATUM_PLAN_TRACE` is gone at 8.9.13**; `p881_trace` still prints each AI's plan terms per turn and matched 696/696 order lists.

## 2026-10-27: Hard+Search is 5x over the 1 s turn budget late in the game; plain Hard uses 3% of it

- **Evidence (W0.3, `docs/sim-results/ai-war/baseline.md`, 6 seeds, 8 empires, every seat AI):** with every seat on Hard+Search (`SearchConfig::in_app(8)`), the whole-lobby turn p95 is 0.58 / 1.7 / 3.8 s native and 0.76 / 2.0 / 4.9 s WASM at T50 / T150 / T250. A seat searches on about 10% of its turns and one search costs 0.2 / 0.7 / 1.5 s native at T50 / T150 / T250; up to 4 seats search on one turn. The `in_app` comment ("keeps a search turn near a second", max 690 ms for 8 players) came from `app_search_time`'s first 60 turns; search cost grows with game size. Plain Hard's lobby is 31 ms p95 in WASM at T250 (27 native), so about 950 ms a turn are free for new search (two humans reserved).
- **WASM costs 1.1x to 1.3x native here, not the 1.4x the `in_app` comment says,** and native and WASM play identical games (hash equal on all 2,858 turns).
- **Per-seat time on WASM needs `performance.now()`**: `Instant` panics on wasm32. The `seat-timing` feature of `legatum-wasm` does this; a default build is unchanged. Wall time in Node on a loaded Mac is fine for these numbers (plain Hard repeats to within a few ms), but the Tauri app runs JavaScriptCore, which was not measured.

## 2026-10-24: `climb_program.py start` already launches the daemon; a following `ensure` starts a second one

- **Evidence:** `start --round r3 ...` then `ensure` in one command printed "daemon started" twice (pids 51194 and 51205, both stepping the same `state.json` files). `ensure` only checks `daemon.pid`, which the first daemon had not yet written. Two daemons would submit every batch twice. I stopped the one not in `daemon.pid` before it submitted anything.
- **Changed:** nothing in code yet. After `start`, check `pgrep -lf "climb_program.py daemon"` instead of calling `ensure`; the fix is for `ensure` to write the pid file itself (or take a lock) before returning.
- **Also seen:** `cmd | tail -1 && next` runs `next` even when `cmd` fails (the pipe's status is `tail`'s): a failed `git merge --ff-only` was followed by the worktree removal. Check `$?` of the merge itself (repeat of the 10-24 "piped merges hide failure" entry).

## 2026-10-24: Combat animations: what the event stream gives you, and how to film and measure them (`claude/combat-fx`)

- **The viewer's events are already in resolution order**, and that is enough to stage a turn: every ranged `Battle` (city shots included, `attacker: { city }`) comes before the first `UnitMoved`; melee `Battle`s sit between the moves of their round. Rounds are not marked, but every unit takes at most one step per round, so a unit stepping again opens the next round (`map/combatFx.ts`). A kill's `UnitDestroyed` follows its `Battle`, so the loss lands on that blow. Destroyed units are missing from the new view: draw them from the view before the turn until their blow lands.
- **Shots resolve simultaneously:** an Arquebusier killed by a cannon in the ranged phase still fires back that phase (8.9.6 T190). Play both, then the death.
- **The spine tape's `orders[t]` belong to the state `T<t>`**, and a `MoveUnitPath`'s `path` lists the steps without the start. The designer's Lugano captures happen while resolving turns 191 and 200, not 190 and 199.
- **Under Playwright's fake clock `performance.now()` is frozen**, so the map's own perf counters read 0 ms in frame-stepped captures. Measure frame cost in a separate real-clock run (`perf` / `perf-off` in `e2e/media/combat-fx.media.ts`).
- **Effects sized to the hex vanish at trailer distance:** unit miniatures are capped at 36 px (`tokenHalf`) while the hex keeps growing with zoom. The first pass (cannonballs 4 px, faint smoke) was invisible in a 1080p clip. Effects need about 1.5x and a vellum halo on dark missiles.
- **Soft radial-gradient puffs read as airbrushed smudges on the atlas map.** Engraved clouds read as the map's own art: round lobes with one ink outline (stroke every lobe thick, then fill them all on top) and a few hatches on the shaded side. Keep the count low, because every overlapping sprite draws its own outline.
- **Rebuilt T200 plus the designer's Hold/Heal orders crashes the app**: `splitAmounts` (`ui/amountParts.ts`) gets `undefined` text and throws on `matchAll`. This is unrelated to the animations and exists on master. The capture clip replays only his moves and shots.
## 2026-10-24: Banking a pre-rename climb needs the gene aliases, not just the faction names

- **Finding:** round 1's `best.json` files (commit `23a6588cb`) carry the old gene name `oathbound_force_campaign` in every faction's overrides. Master's loader accepts it through `#[serde(alias = ...)]`, so the gates passed, but `BUILTIN_FACTION_DECISIONS` is matched by the canonical name (`sparta_force_campaign`), and the round-1 runner's `bank` prints the old name (it also reads the old `params.rs`).
- **Rule:** for round 1, build the bank lines against master's `params.rs`: master's entries for the faction, plus `best.json`'s overrides with each serde alias mapped to its field name, minus values equal to the shared default. Rounds started on master write canonical names, so `bank` from master's runner is fine from round 2.

## 2026-10-24: Gate climb candidates on the code they land on; old binaries can make the war check read 0%

- **Finding:** the round-1 climb candidates were first gated on master's binaries and then again on the round-1 binaries (commit `23a6588cb`). The human-seat benchmark gave different verdicts (Japan opponents-only +1.9 pp on master, −2.1 pp on `23a6588cb`). The war attack check read 0% on `23a6588cb` for both Japan-only and Mongol params: the Mongols never declared in 61 to 64 of 64 runs, so the check can't tell anything on that code (compare the 10-22 entry: old scenario states break the attack check).
- **Rule:** run the banking gate's benchmark and war parts on binaries built from the commit the candidate will land on (master), never on the commit the climb ran on.

## 2026-10-23: The development layer is public, and a CC0 track replaced the CC-BY one (`claude/oss-review`)

- **Decision (designer):** publish the skills, agents, hooks, `CLAUDE.md`, cloud and ops tooling, plans, status and learnings; scrub only credentials, the AWS account ID, instance IDs, IPs, home paths, key material and personal details. The export is now "everything tracked except run output and scratch" (`tools/release/export-public.sh`). Its personal-data scan first wrote the values to hide into the script with character classes so it would not match itself; that published them, and it was replaced on 2026-11-21 by generic patterns plus an untracked private file (see the 2026-11-21 entry).
- **Make the account ID a run-time lookup, not a constant.** `tools/evolve/{climb_program,milestone_climb}.py` called the bucket `legatum-sims-<id>`; they now call `tools/cloud/lib.sh bucket_name` (env `LEGATUM_CLOUD_BUCKET`, else STS). The Playwright bridge found "the main checkout" from a hard-coded home path; it asks git (`rev-parse --git-common-dir`).
- **Music licence:** Scott Buckley's CC BY 4.0 terms (in-game use only) do not fit a public repo that lets anyone download the file alone. "Memories Of Stone" was replaced by RandomMind's CC0 "Exploration" (OpenGameArt), built with `tools/audio/build_audio.py --music-only`; the old file, terms and credits are deleted. The new source peaks near full scale, so the -18 LUFS target was capped at -20.0 LUFS by the pre-encode peak limit; the manifest now records the reached level. The in-game credits list music by file path, not by "not CC0", which would have misfiled a CC0 track under sound effects.
- **Licence audit** (`THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`): everything permissive; `r-efi` offers an LGPL option we do not use, and five Tauri-side crates are MPL-2.0 (unmodified, so notices only).

## 2026-10-23: The public tree is an export, and it had hidden dependencies on private paths (`claude/oss-review`)

`tools/release/export-public.sh` builds the open-source tree from a commit (allowlist, runtime-asset pruning, text transforms, personal-data and gitleaks scans) and pushes it to the `staging` branch of the public repository. Building and testing that tree on the box (a git repo made from the export, `tools/cloud/remote.sh <dir> -- ...`) found what the private tree hid:

- **Five `crates/sim` examples `include_str!`ed map and suite data from `docs/sim-results/`**, so `cargo test --workspace` (which builds examples) could not compile without the 800 MB results tree. The data now lives in `crates/sim/data/`. Keep inputs out of results directories.
- **`scripts/build-wasm.sh` and `tools/sim/repin.sh` called `tools/ops/timed.py` unconditionally.** The timing wrapper is optional now. A public script must work when the private tooling next to it is absent.
- **A vitest read `.claude/skills/.../haptics.md`**; the contract is `docs/design/haptics.md` now. A test must never depend on an agent's skill files.
- **Only 42 MB of the 256 MB in `app/src/assets` is loaded**; `tools/release/runtime-assets.py` expands the `import.meta.glob` patterns to find it, so the export drops the other 184 raster masters without a hand-kept list.
- Verified from the export on the box: `scripts/build-wasm.sh`, `cargo test --workspace --release --no-run`, `npm ci`, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx vitest run` (235 files, 2845 tests) all pass with no `.claude`, `tools/ops` or `tools/cloud`.
- `tools/release/check-links.py` counts dangling links in an export.

## 2026-10-23: Alliances cost Gold again, and an ally's own war is a call to arms (`claude/alliance-calls`)
