**User decision:** "Let's go back to alliances costing upkeep in gold. That was fine and limits just accepting them all. Also let's make it so that allies auto join defensive wars but they get prompted for whether they want to join offensive wars... you say you don't want to join, and you still stay allied." Rules, timing and code: `legatum-rules` §5 ("Defenders join at once; an ally's own war is a call to arms").

- **Rule version, not a revert.** `DiplomacyState::calls` is `Some` in new games and tapes of version 6 (`TAPE_VERSION`), `None` in older saves and tapes, which keep free Alliances and `share_wars`. The fixed-order tapes of versions 4 and 5 replayed to the same hashes after the change, which shows the legacy path equals master's behaviour; only the mixed tapes, the alliance tape and the pins moved. `legacy-war-rules` (a setup experiment) starts a game under the old rules, so a rule benchmark needs no second binary; `start-met` lets a tape test Alliances without exploring.
- **Timing chosen:** a declaration makes defenders join in the same turn (a surprise war starts the war at the start of resolution); the call appears in the next planning turn's inbox, stays open two turns, and Join resolves at the boundary like a declaration (at War from the turn after the order). A lapse is a decline. Joining raises no calls of its own, and only direct allies defend or are called (a chain of Alliances is one side for Peace and conflict checks only).
- **Peace needs only the teams at war.** Under whole-side Peace, an ally that declined the war would have had to agree to a Peace it was never part of (`peace_parties`).
- **Census, 256 eight-player Hard games, seed 7400001 (`tools/sim/alliance-census.sh`; new rules / new rules without the bloc cost / old free rules):** largest bloc at T100 1.71 / 2.02 / 2.39 of 8, at T200 2.08 / 3.26 / 2.44 (largest ever 5 / 7 / 4); Alliances formed per game 6.1 / 9.3 / 11.8; wars opened per game 34.3 / 32.8 / 29.9; captures per game 22.1 / 22.3 / 23.6. The bloc-size cost still pays with Gold back (T200 bloc 3.26 -> 2.08, Alliances 9.3 -> 6.1), so it stays, now as the gene `bloc_size_penalty` (60).
- **Gates.** `cargo test --workspace` 1,886 passed on the box; mock/WASM parity (`diplomacy.wasm.test.ts`, new tests for defence, calls, lapse, Peace without the decliner, signing mid-war, old saves) all pass. Human-seat benchmark (Hard, 8 maps x 128 reseeded salts, all seats): new rules 39.6% (95% 36.9 to 42.0), master 37.7% (35.1 to 40.3), the same binary under `legacy-war-rules` 34.8% (31.8 to 37.6): no drop. War attack check (`war-golden-horde-thin`, 128 runs) 54%, the floor is 30%: it equals master by construction, because the war scenarios are saved states and play the old rules (re-cut them to test the new rules).
- **A scenario state is a save, so it keeps the old rules.** `scenarios/**/*.json`, the war suite and archived replays have no `calls`, hence free Alliances and shared wars; only `new_game` setups (the benchmark maps, leagues, the app) play the new rules. A rule benchmark must start from setups.
- **The rules reference had lost its Diplomacy section.** The Celts rework's docs commit (`0c0234b0c`) deleted about 200 lines of `game-rules.md` (Fortify/heal, Promotions, Zone of control, all of §5) next to the paragraph it meant to remove; restored here from its parent and rewritten for the new rules.
- **Tests that read archived states keep master's expectations** (`ranged_garrison`, `settler_escorts`, the Mongols search fixture): a mechanical revert of the 2026-10-21 commit would have broken them, since the AI behaves as before on those legacy states.
- **A lint caught a lowercase "at war"** in a core refusal now shown on a screen: copy uses "at War".

## 2026-10-22: Each era is defined by its resource: one key building per era, not every building; old scenario states break the attack check

**Built** (`claude/era-resources`, design `docs/design/research/era-resources-2026-10-22.md`, rules `legatum-rules` §3): Copper (Ancient, Market), Iron (Classical and Medieval), Niter, Coal, Oil, Lithium; land units from the Classical era on and each era's key building use 1; an upgrade switches resource.

- **Why only one key building per era.** With every new building of an era using its resource, a mature city needs three or four supply per era (Ancient alone: Workshop, Market, Library), and a five-city empire 15 Copper: no deposit count that leaves resources scarce can feed that, and the opening's Production and Science would stall. One key building per era (Market, Forge, University, Bank, Factory, Research Lab), with a higher tier keeping its lower tiers' needs, puts about one user per city per era and keeps old resources worth holding all game.
- **Census (32 Hard games, Standard 48x32, 8 empires; `docs/sim-results/era-resources/census-32.json`):** 15 deposits of each resource a map, about 1.9 nearest each start. A typical empire (4-5 cities, land army 6-9) supplies a median 2-3 of its era's resource against 4-9 wanted (its users plus older land units that could upgrade into it); 88-92% of empires want more than they supply at every snapshot from T50 to T250. About 36 deposits a game change hands, every one with a captured city; 18.9 of 20.8 captured cities a game hold a deposit. Idle users stay rare (2-14% of empires) because every brain starts users only with spare supply; the scarcity shows as armies of older units (era-resource unit users 1-2 of 6-9 land units).
- **Opening untouched** (`opening_pacing`, 64 Hard games, seed 9100): first Settler T18, second city T25.
- **AI-impact gate** (human-seat benchmark, Hard, reseeded dice, 8 maps x 128 salts): all seats 39.3% (36.6 to 41.9) against master's 38.6% the same day; the seat ignoring supply value (`supply0.json`, seat-only) 31.4% (-7.9 points: the generalised valuation is worth a lot to the seat); opponents ignoring it 36.8% (-2.5, inside noise).
- **The war suite's attack check (`war-golden-horde-thin`, 0%) is not this branch:** it was already 0% at this branch's master base (`b4cd176d7`, bisected by the lead: free Alliances; see the 2026-10-23 entry). Separately, scenario states saved before the rule would load with armies that need supply they don't have (the Mongols' 16 Iron users on 0 Iron); they are now re-encoded grandfathered (`resources::grandfather` via `json::parse_state`), and with that the check still reads 0%, as on master. Player saves from before the rule are grandfathered the same way (only units with a supply need and supply tiers are marked).
- **Gate on the merged tree (2026-10-23):** master 37.7%, branch 33.0% (all seats). The gap is mostly the Celts map (31% to 2%: the Celts sack instead of keeping cities, so they hold no deposits) and 8.5 Switzerland (62% to 34%), partly offset elsewhere. **Aircraft using Oil cost another 10 points** (bisected twice: 25.2% to 34.2% and 23.2% to 33.5% with only that reverted); a rule keeping the AI's Bombers off contested Oil did not help, so the mechanism is unknown. Aircraft need no supply until an AI pass (lead).
- **At landing (Siege, Celts rework, Alliance and stall fixes merged):** master 39.7%, era resources 37.6% (gap 2.1 points, inside noise); the losses sit on the Celts (14% to 5%), 8.5 Swiss (69% to 50%) and 8.6 Sparta (65% to 46%) maps, partly offset by Germany (37% to 52%). Each master merge moved the gap (4.7, 4.2, 2.1): judge it only on the merged tree.
- **Gotcha:** `Resource`'s derive order (Wheat, Cattle, Stone, Copper, Iron, Coal, Niter, Oil, Lithium) is neither `Resource::ALL` nor era order (`Resource::STRATEGIC`, `ERA_RESOURCES`); BTreeMaps and ties follow the derive order, and the app mirrors it as `RESOURCE_ORDER`.
## 2026-10-23: Climb program stage 1: 92 hard-coded gates and constants became genes with no behaviour change; four gates were not equivalent to their "off" value
## 2026-10-23: the Celts rework: two rules deleted, the sack grows, the share does not move (`claude/celts-rework`)
- **User decision:** Celtic homeland damage and hidden hunters are deleted ("does not appear to be meaningful"); the sack also steals a technology and raises a warband camp. The engine, AI, mock engine and UI were all touched (about 70 files); removed state fields (`Unit::revealed_to`, `hunter_sightings`) are simply ignored when an old save loads, no migration was needed.
- **Result:** balanced league Celts 7.6% -> 7.0% over 4,096 games (paired -0.6 pp +-1.0); the Celtic-seat map of the human benchmark 26.6% -> 15.6% (+-9). The seat gets weaker without its land and hidden archers: eliminated 4% -> 9%, cities 4.4 -> 3.9. The new take is small: a technology only on 17% of sacks (the victim must know a technology of the Celts' own current era that they lack, so only a victim ahead in the era race pays) and one camp per sack that rarely lands inside Celtic land, so it seldom counts for Raise the Warbands.
- **Where a rule hid in the AI:** the Celts' rules were also hard-coded in other factions' AI (skip Celtic cities as targets, +120 cost on Celtic land, a "viable target" test, `prey_skips_celts` added by the gate-to-gene work the same day). Deleting a rule means grepping every `Faction::Celts` in `crates/ai` and every `celts`/`hunter` in `app/src/mock` (the mock engine is a parity mirror).
- **A latent AI bug surfaced:** `mixed_opening_orders_validate_in_real_fogged_games` failed on the changed trajectory with a move that ended in an ally's city (refused by the engine; the opening brains' orders were only filtered for stays). `drop_stays_in_allied_cities` now also drops such moves for every brain.
- **Per-test flake:** `celts_raze_removes_conquered_territory` failed only under `cargo test --workspace`, where a wounded unit healed 10 HP in the resolved turn (feature unification changes the rules set); assert the HP did not drop instead of equality.
- **Merge hotspots again:** `crates/sim/tests/pins.json` and the three determinism fixtures conflicted with master on every merge; resolve with either side then `tools/sim/repin.sh` and `rehash_scenarios` on the box, and commit what comes back.
## 2026-10-23: The AI movement preflight cancelled turns the resolver would have taken in turn; the war check did not move (`claude/ai-movement-wait`)
- **Cause.** `movement.rs::movement_conflict` simulates a turn's movement round by round and, when two of our movers would end a round on one tile, returned the lower-priority one, whose whole order became a Skip. The resolver (`rules::movement`, step 5) already settles that case: claimants of one tile that share an owner take turns by unit id, the lower id advances and the other waits a round with its moves intact, and both still arrive. The preflight was cancelling a turn the engine handles by itself, most often in a crowded staging area (u168 at T136 in the Golden Horde trace).
- **Fix (gene `movement_wait`, default 1).** The simulated round does what the resolver does: the higher id of two movers that enter one tile in the same round waits (position and moves kept), a mover entering a tile whose occupant waits also waits, and a waiter adds a simulated round (up to double the rounds). A mover blocked by a unit that is not moving (parked, or arrived and standing) is still a conflict, and the old end checks still fail an assault member that did not arrive or an escape that is no safer, so a group is not sent in short. At 0 it is the old rule exactly (6,144 games identical).
- **Re-measure of `war-golden-horde-thin`: no recovery of the 95%.** On the move-through tree (`8d7bec73c`, 256 runs): thin 69% with the old preflight, 71% with the new (the 95% to 67% drop stays the engine rule, as the move-through entry says); Golden Horde 84% to 65%, the Swiss hold 14% to 33%. On tree `c5082605b` (master as of 2026-10-23 early, plus this one commit; 128 runs) the old-preflight arm read 0% for all three attack scenarios, which disagrees with the 68% the coordinator measured on `98e01c48c` and is unexplained (the first reading was taken before the "reads 0% on master" entry of this file, which blames older scenario cuts; not verified), and with the gene on it read thin 0% to 11%, Golden Horde 0% to 24%, the hold 100% to 75%, suite mean 42.5% to 43.6%. The preflight was not what held the attack down; it moves these three knife-edge scenes by 10 to 25 points either way.
- **Benchmark and balanced games.** Human-seat benchmark (8 maps x 128 reseeded salts, Hard, 1,024 per arm, genes on only where noted): control 37.0%; seat only +2.1 points [-1.5, +5.5]; opponents only -2.2 [-5.6, +1.4]; all seats +0.6 [-2.9, +4.1]. 6,144 eight-player games (seeds 30001+): mean 304.9 to 305.0 turns, captures a game 18.8 to 19.3, games over 600 turns 12 to 8, no faction moves more than 2 points. Rows: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ledger.jsonl`, `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/movement-wait-1023/`.

## 2026-10-23: Rare stalls: a game past its expected length stopped planning faction goals, so a 9-VP Mongols never built its Hall (`claude/ai-stalls`)
- **What stalled.** Era objectives step 1 (2 of 256 games at the 600-turn cap) and the opening-ramp removal (1 of 400 at 1,200). On current master the ramp cohort does not stall (4,000 games, seeds 5200001+: 0), and seed 5200142 at the ramp commit (`ecc2c952e`) ends at turn 330, so those two seeds can't be replayed; the same tail shows in every large cohort instead. 6,144 Hard 8-player games (seeds 20001+, master `b4cd176d7`): 85 games over 500 turns, 22 over 600, 5 over 800 (max 959); 1,536 games (seeds 9100+): one stall at the cap (9983) and 9322 ending at turn 1,160. Method: `crates/sim/examples/stall_trace.rs` replays a `--tape-out` tape and prints every VP source gained or lost, every hold start and break, a standing (VP by source, wars, each empire's open objectives and progress) every N turns and, for one turn and player, every city's buildings, production and buildable list.
- **Who holds the target, and who breaks the hold.** Nobody holds it for hundreds of turns: three or four empires sit at 8 and 9 VP, and when one reaches 10 a rival takes a crown from it within one to four turns (Largest Economy and Strongest Army flip at the margin; 9322, 5201637, 5202437, 5203228, 5200484). The empire that sits at 9 for the longest is a Mongols in four of the games we traced (9322, 9983, 22229, 5203228): all six era points, the Great Work and a crown, and only the Courier Hall (2 VP, 600 Production) missing. In 9983 it had 18 cities, 25 units and six wars, and at turn 800 five cities were idle and nine repeated a Drone or Bomber with 90 to 470 banked Production.
- **Three AI causes, in order.** (1) `goal_actions::last_turn` put the end of a game with no limit a tenth of the expected length (22 turns) ahead, so past turn 200 a 50-turn Hall never "paid" (`hall_pays` false in every sample from turn 750 to 900 of 9983) and the Celts, Sparta and Japan rites went the same way (the calibration of 2026-10-07 had only fixed builds under 22 turns). (2) Where it did pay, `hall_ready` also wanted the Hall city garrisoned and `hall_outmatched` false; outmatched is "any city of ours under more fire than it answers", true most of the game for an 18-city empire at war with six (954 of 1,232 samples of Mongols empires within 2 VP of the target; garrisoned and not outmatched in 30). (3) Where it was ready, the goal pass only set production in an idle city, and a city repeats the unit it builds, so no city was ever idle; the Hall's 3,000 value, discounted over 45 turns, also lost to a 2,200 garrison unit that takes five.
- **The fix.** Gene `goal_open_end` (default 1): a game with no limit has no last turn; lateness (`is_late`) is still measured against the expected length. Gene `hall_decisive` (default 1): when the Hall's 2 VP take the team to its target the build needs neither a garrison nor a calm border, is worth 4,000 per VP (it was 1,500) and replaces the unit the Hall city repeats. Both default 0 reproduce master exactly (6,144 games, every result and winner identical). Effect on 6,144 games (same seeds): games over 600 turns 22 to 8, over 800 turns 5 to 0, over 500 turns 85 to 55, mean 305.2 to 299.4, p99 520 to 493, longest 959 to 762, 0 stalls in both. Mongols completes its goal in 37% of its games (28% before) and wins 17.0% of all-AI games (11.9% before); the other factions move by 0 to 2 points. Pillar 8 note for the lead: Mongols was the faction under its fair share (12.5%) and is now above it.
- **Not fixed, and why.** The long games that remain (about 1% over 500 turns) are crown contests at the margin and eras that cannot finish. What the traces say about the objectives themselves: "capture from the VP leader" cannot be met while the top is tied (`objectives::vp_leader` wants a unique leader, so a standoff of four empires at 9 VP has none), "from an empire ahead" cannot be met by the leader, a gain of N cities with the building (Library 4 more cities) cannot be met by an empire that already has it in every city, and a Star Fort or Autoworks objective needs a two- or three-step chain (Castle, Star Fort; Workshop, Forge, Factory) the AI's objective effort (`crowns.rs::objective_effort`) prices as one building, while it rates a Territory gain at six turns a tile per city whatever the map still allows and pursues the cheapest estimate. The era-objectives redesign (random pool, revealed on entry) is where those belong; none is a rule exception here.
- **Gate (2026-10-23, 8 maps x 128 reseeded salts, Hard, 1,024 games per arm, one binary, genes off = control).** Control 39.3%; seat only -0.5 points [-1.1, +0.1]; opponents only -0.6 [-1.4, +0.2]; all seats -0.8 [-1.7, +0.1] (rivals finish games they used to stall). War suite (64 runs, 14 scenarios): 42.7% with and without the genes, every scenario identical (they are 12 to 27 turns long); the attack check reads 0% on both arms, but that run was on `c5082605b`, before the Alliance pricing fix (98e01c48c); current master (fceb0eed3) passes it at 65% of 64 runs (floor 30%). Rows: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ledger.jsonl`, `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/stalls-1023/`.

## 2026-10-23: Climb program stage 0 and 3: AI-only 4-player Small games run 220 to 700 turns, where the user wins at T60 to T230

**What changed.** `crates/sim/examples/climb_arena.rs` is the climb program's full-game objective (one faction's Hard AI in a generated 4-player Small lobby against a Hard field, every arm on the same seeds) and `tools/evolve/climb_program.py` climbs each faction on it, on the box filler with a capped spot fallback (`docs/sim-results/ai-quality/climb-program/README.md`). `BUILTIN_FACTION_DECISIONS` in `params.rs` is where a faction-scoped winner is banked.

**Non-obvious.**
- **The AIs don't close games.** In the smoke batches (16 games) AI-only 4-player Small games ended between T218 and T500, and one hit the 700-turn safety cap with no winner; the 8.9.x smoke game Sparta won ended at T571. His wins on these maps came at T61 (Sparta) to T233 (Athens), median T116. The arena plays to the end, so the climb rewards winning at all; a win-turn term is the obvious next objective change if games stay this long.
- **Seat by seed, not by index.** `run-batch.sh` splits `--games` across servers and each server's game index restarts at 0, so a seat chosen from the index would differ between a split and an unsplit run of the same seeds. `climb_arena` seats the faction at `seed % players`.
- **A built-in per-faction default must not beat the file.** `AiParams::decisions` applies `BUILTIN_FACTION_DECISIONS` only to genes the file left at the shared default, then the file's own `faction_overrides`, so a climb candidate (always written as `faction_overrides`) means exactly what it says.
- **A paused filler batch could hang forever after it finished** (`tools/cloud/box/box-filler`). Pausing sends SIGSTOP to the batch's systemd scope; its `sudo systemd-run` parent, outside the scope, stops itself too (sudo mirrors its child's job-control stop), and the resume only continued the scope. The batch finished, the stopped sudo never reaped it, and the filler waited two hours on a zombie with the whole queue behind it. The resume now also continues the scope's parent.
- **Cost.** A 4-player Small game is about 7.6 core-seconds (32 games in 29 s on the box); a generation of 16 arms x 160 games is about 5 minutes of the whole box.

## 2026-10-23: the Celtic gates are gone and the sack is priced, but the Celts' share did not move (`claude/celts-sack`)
- **The premise had expired.** The coach measured the AI Celts at 2.4% on the 8.9.7 tag and 5.2% with the city-attack gates lifted. By the time the gates were deleted, master's Celts were already at **8.3%** (7.3% on a fresh seed) after the Celts camp-count fix (`e840acb4b`, "the crown-upkeep stall was a Celts camp-count bug"). Re-measure the control on the current master before quoting a coach number; 169 commits had landed since the tag.
- **Result: sacking happens, winning does not change.** Sacks per Celtic seat 0.01 -> 2.0 (tape replay, `crates/sim/examples/celts_wars.rs`), but the balanced 8-empire league reads 8.3% control, 7.7% priced, 6.5% with kept-city pricing, and +0.7 pp on a fresh seed (each ±1.5). The Celtic seat of the human-seat benchmark rises 10.9% -> 20.3% (±8.5); no other map can move because the changes only run for a Celts AI.
- **Where the AI Celts lose:** a seat that never sacked won 2.1% against the control's 6.2% in the same games, seats with three or more sacks won 14 to 18%. Wars that cannot be paid for in sacks cost more than they bring; `sack_commit` (a funded siege route before a Contain war, like the Sparta) did not move the share on its own (7.6%). The remaining lever is war selection, not the gates.
- **Raise the Warbands holds less with wars:** 78% held at the end of the game against 67%, even in seats with no sack, and the Celts' own units clear 12 to 16 of their own camps per seat either way (they re-raise them). Look at what the army does around the camps before calling this a cost of the wars.
- **Result: a bloc-size cost in the Alliance value restores both** (`BLOC_SIZE_PENALTY` 60 per team an Alliance adds beyond a pair, `crates/ai/src/diplomacy.rs`; the fix also keeps `inherited_front_cost` and `helper_reach_percent`, the ally's help counted only within 16 hexes of the enemy). 512 eight-player Hard games, seed 7400001 (old tree / free Alliances / no bloc cost / 35 / 60 / 100): largest bloc at T100 1.32 / 5.31 / 4.96 / 2.85 / 2.39 / 2.14 of 8, at T200 1.79 / 5.46 / 4.57 / 2.77 / 2.38 / 2.07; alliances formed per game 6.3 / 19.8 / 20.4 / 13.5 / 11.9 / 10.7; wars opened per game 36.1 / 17.9 / 22.5 / 29.0 / 31.3 / 32.7; captures per game 23.2 / 20.9 / 19.0 / 20.5 / 21.1 / 21.2. Plan trace of the mechanism (`72fc33073`, the scenario at T136 to T149): only teams 1 and 3 are allied at the start and four teams already fight team 7; at T136 the Mongols propose an Alliance to team 4 (free now, 24 Gold needed none before), by T138 teams 1 to 6 are one bloc, the Mongols inherit the war on team 7, the Aarau campaign ends `NegativeValue` at T139 to T140 and in run 1 the plan stays "Contain team 7" (value 56 to 152 against a campaign worth 2,400 to 5,300) to the end. War suite (64 runs): `war-golden-horde-thin` 0% -> 68% (floor 30%), `war-golden-horde` 0% -> 84% (78% on the tree merged with master's stalls work), `war-golden-horde-hold` 100% -> 15% (21% merged; the two scenarios read as the user's own game again), `war-german-stand` 96% -> 100%, every other scenario unchanged. Human-seat benchmark (all seats, 8 maps x 128 reseeded salts): 38.5% (95% 35.8 to 40.9) on the master control, 37.8% (35.3 to 40.1) with the pricing, -0.7 pp. The constant barely matters between 35 and 100 for the war checks, so 60 is the middle of the range that holds the bloc under 2.5 of 8. Tooling: `crates/sim/examples/alliance_metrics.rs` (blocs, alliances, wars, captures from tapes; see `tools/sim/flip-metrics.sh` for how tapes are made). If bloc size matters to the user as a rule, one Alliance at a time is still the cleaner lever.
- **The campaign pricing already covered the Celts' target choice** (`assessed_target_value` had the sack Gold since 8.9.x): the gates, not the valuation, kept them home. The new terms are in `price_campaign` (win-chance currency: Gold to army, victim's VP and output, no kept city) and the strike value (Production currency).
- **Method:** two binaries cannot be split by seat in the human-seat benchmark; with no Celtic rival on the benchmark maps, seat-only and opponents-only are exactly zero off the Celtic seat's map, so compare binary against binary per map (`tools/sim` arms through `run-batch.sh --bin hard_vs_human --ai-params ARMS.json -- --arms`, slices merge by concatenating per map).
## 2026-10-23: Climb program stage 1: 92 hard-coded gates and constants became genes with no behaviour change; four gates were not equivalent to their "off" value

**What changed.** `crates/ai` had faction-keyed early returns, exclusions, per-faction number tables and literal thresholds that no gene reached ("you have a bunch of hard-coded rules and you're not iterating or exploring them", user 2026-10-23). 92 became new genes in `params.rs` and four existing Sparta-only genes (`planned_stage`, `sparta_force_campaign`, `early_campaign_muster`, `campaign_march_patience`) lost their `faction == Sparta` check. Each faction's old behaviour lives in `BUILTIN_FACTION_DECISIONS` (70 entries), so every pin and fixture hash is unchanged. Inventory, with what was skipped and why: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/climb-program/gates.md`.

**Non-obvious.**
- **"Off" is not always the early return.** `plan.rs::grind_forecast` skipped Sparta with a faction test, but `campaign_persist` also acts in `campaign.rs` and `siege.rs`, so setting Sparta's `campaign_persist` to 0 would have switched those off too. A separate gene (`plan_persist_skip`) keeps the old behaviour. Likewise `contain_commit` is also used by the `leader_commit` 2 path for every faction, so the Sparta-only march check got its own gene (`contain_march_outlook`).
- **Flipping a gene's shared default is safe only when every use sits behind the old faction test.** The four flipped genes did. A parameter file that sets one of them to its old shared value now switches it on for every faction in that run.
- **Some faction checks are about the rival, not us.** The scout threat radius keys on the owner of the tile (`v.faction_of(owner)`), so it cannot be a gene of our own faction; it stays as rule knowledge about rivals' public rules.
- **Helpers with only a view cannot read genes.** `adaptation::early(v)` (turn < 120) is called from the opponent model; converting it needs the decision parameters plumbed through `opponents.rs`. Left structural.
- **A frozen proxy is not live policy.** `proxy*.rs` (the human-proxy benchmark) keeps its literals on purpose.
- `crates/sim/examples/climb_arena.rs` (climb-program commit ffd1cce7c) had an unused `GameState` import that failed `clippy -D warnings`; removed here.

## 2026-10-22: Passing through your own units is a hop, not a stop; the benchmark did not move

**Rule (user, 2026-10-21, Civ 6):** a unit may pass through a same-class friend of its team, or its own garrisoned city, but never ends a move on one. He saw a unit route round Kanazawa instead of through it. **Why the old engine could not:** every movement round ends with one unit per class per tile, so a step into a friend that stays was a failed step, and the planner treated every such tile as a wall (the Last Bridge, playtest 6.2). **What we built:** a route that starts across friends that stay (parked, out of moves, attacking in place) is one atomic *hop*: the unit pays every tile it crosses and lands on the first tile beyond (`path::hop`, `movement.rs::movement_round`). No round ever ends on a friend, so nothing in the claim, bounce or chain logic changed; `UnitMoved` now goes from the unit's tile to a landing up to two tiles away (the app and mock test that read it as a single step had to allow that).

**Where a hop cannot be made, the old rules stay.** (1) The landing is an enemy or a hostile goal: an attack cannot start from two tiles away, so the Last Bridge route still goes round the friend to the free tile beside the city. (2) The goto is the friend's tile: the unit comes up beside it and reports "blocked by P0's Soldier, which did not move". (3) The crossing costs more than a unit's full moves (a friend on hills plus the tile beyond for a 2-move Soldier, two friends in a row): `path::crossing_faults` walls that run for the search, except behind a friend that moves on later, which is waited for rather than circled when the way round is no faster (the playtest 8.6 rule). (4) A hop that needs more moves than are left this turn waits on the last free tile it passed and hops next turn, silently: it is not a block, so a confirmed `stop_on_block` route does not ask for new orders (one AI test and one mock parity test assumed it would).

**Gotchas.** The `blocked` predicate must answer for the goal too (the search exempts the goal itself), or a route crosses a friend to attack a goal two tiles away. `path_turns` must count a hop as one move that waits a turn when it does not fit, or the planner compares routes by the wrong turn count. The mock already trims a confirmed route on each `UnitMoved` event (`mock/resolve.ts`), so trimming it again in the movement loop dropped the route; a hop lands several tiles along it, so the trim is now by position, as core does. Two Playwright specs (unique-pieces, aftermath-forecast) failed once under load and passed on rerun; `vp-v2` "race panel" fails the same way on master (its spec still expects the generic crown rule where `data.ts` now gives each crown its own), so it is not this change.

**Effect on Hard (2026-10-22, master `99918a75` as control, the same box build for both):** the human-seat benchmark, reseeded dice, 8 maps x 256 salts (2,048 games), Hard in every seat: the seat wins 35.8% -> 35.5% (-0.2 points, 95% interval -2.7 to +2.2). Per map (wins of 256) 8.3 Swiss 29 -> 47, 8.4 Sparta 124 -> 124, 8.4 Japan 122 -> 115, 8.4 Celts 18 -> 24, 8.5 Mongols 11 -> 9, 8.5 Swiss 204 -> 182, 8.6 Germany 93 -> 90, 8.6 Sparta 132 -> 137: map-sized swings in both directions, nothing systematic. A rule that applies to every unit cannot be applied to one seat, so seat-only and opponents-only arms do not exist for it; the same-binary pair above is the measure. Eight-player games (2,048, seed 9210001): average length 308.0 -> 306.2 turns, 0 panics, captures per game 22.41 -> 22.52, wins Byzantium 608 -> 626, Athens 344 -> 352, Mongols 301 -> 305, Switzerland 268 -> 251, Sparta 219 -> 232, Germany 177 -> 172, Celts 71 -> 63, Japan 60 -> 47: the same ranking. War suite (64 runs, master `caf88dfd` against the branch merged onto it): `--check` (Golden Horde, thin treasury) 95% -> 67% (128 runs: 92% -> 67%), above its floor of 30%, but two sibling scenarios swing the other way: Golden Horde 34% -> 81% and the Swiss defending it 56% -> 15%; every other scenario is within 1 run in 16 of master (First Blood 100% -> 93%). The suite mean is 47% -> 45%. The Mongol assault is a knife-edge scene (the informed Swiss defender buys the army off when it sees it coming), so how the army files past its own units decides it either way: with the old engine and the old AI preflight the thin scenario reproduces 92%, the new engine with the old preflight 68%, the new engine with the new preflight 67%. The AI's preflight (`movement_conflict`) cancels a mover's whole turn when two movers would share a tile in a simulated round, which the trace shows happening in the crowded staging area (u168 cancelled at T136); letting the lower-priority mover wait a round instead of dropping its turn is a candidate fix, not tried.

## 2026-10-21: Era objectives count gains from the last era score (R1, R2, `objective_seek`): the old counts cannot be kept, and the calibration is coupled

- **R1 and R2 on the level counts broke pacing:** median 479 turns, 46 of 256 games at the 600-turn cap (master then 303, 0), though era scores per empire barely moved (2.52 against 2.61). Master's winners were made by stacking: 310 of 2,048 empires scored all six eras and the best empire of a game did in 188 of 256 games (with R1: 100 and 87). Gains of 9 or 11 cities are nearly never met (Expand 1% of scores against 17%). The pool had to be rewritten as gains (70th percentile of the best gain per era), and `DEFAULT_OBJECTIVE_SCALE` is now 100 (the pool is written at final counts).
- **The calibration is coupled across eras.** Scoring any era restarts the count of every older open era, so easing late eras (pace) lowered era-2 scoring from 51% to 46% with era 2's own pool unchanged. Four passes of `tools/sim/era-objectives.sh` (3 minutes each) landed at median 318 turns, 49% of entrants scoring, 0 same-turn double scores. Details and the pool: `docs/sim-results/era-objectives/step1.md`.
- **The AI's objective pursuit barely changes what is met** (drawn and pursued minus drawn and not pursued: about 0 for Expand and Fight, +11 at most for one Build kind, Castle), so about 27% of the pool is met by accident, 34% for Fight. It does cost game length: `objective_seek` 0 is 18 turns slower (336 against 318). Human-seat benchmark (after merging master's crown-upkeep removal): 40.2% default against 43.6% when no seat pursues objectives (master 36.5%). Final cohort against the current master: median 318 against 298, two capped games, 0 same-turn double scores (master 795).
- **`verify-branch.sh`'s repin step repins inside the slot and reports PASS; it does not fail on a pin diff.** The `test` step fails on `recorded_hashes_*`, `*_tape_is_pinned` and `replay_export`. Run `LEGATUM_OFFLOAD=1 tools/sim/repin.sh` in the worktree (about 10 minutes through the box queue) and commit `crates/sim/tests`.
- **`agent-worktree.sh acquire` fails when no pool directory exists** (it tries `git worktree add <pool> master`, and master is checked out in the main checkout). Create the pool once with `git worktree add --detach .claude/worktrees/pool-1 master`, then acquire.
- **A merge in progress while `verify-remote.sh` starts verifies the committed branch, not the merge.** It merges master itself onto the commit; a conflict in a skill file failed the verify in 3 minutes. Commit the resolved merge first.
- **Benchmarks on the build box queue behind everything** (a 128-salt, 8-map run on a box at load 200 timed out at 5,400 s after 6 cores); `tools/sim/hard-vs-human.sh --cloud` on a spot server took 8 minutes for the build and the run.

## 2026-10-21: A closed Japan's vow is defended by Peace, not by the VP arithmetic; the 8.9.6 Swiss review's "VP that reaches the target" was never true

**The review's premise did not hold.** The T192 state the vow was lost from shows Japan at 4 to 5 VP (standing, closed, countdown 27 of 30), not 9: the 9 at T198 was 7 plus the vow's 2, and 7 + 2 is still a point short of 10. A gene gated on "the vow takes the team to the target" (`vow_closes_the_race`) therefore never fired from T190 and left every branch identical to its control (a debug print of the standing showed it). The gate that works is the vow itself: closed, and holding or within 15 turns of scoring (`vow_at_stake`). The garrison keeps the "within one point" gate.

**Effect (gene `vow_hold`, 60 runs per branch, his orders in seat 0, Japan alone):** Japan signs 10 to 11 peaces instead of 4 to 5, loses 1.3 to 1.6 cities instead of 2.0, peaks at 10 VP instead of 8 (T160 to T190); his seat's wins 46/50/53 of 60 -> 47/50/51; nothing at T196 (the vow is already contested and the Peace comes too late). In 768 all-AI games the gene is neutral (Japan wins 24 -> 21, cities lost while closed -5.5%). Landed off.

**The denial price is a cliff, not a slope.** `vow_denial_percent` 20 cut the AI closes from 0.55 to 0.15 per Japan seat and Japan's wins by half; 100 at T160 made Japan stay open, take 9.6 cities instead of 1.4, and his seat won 2/60 (the seat-2 AI 49/60). A Japan that does not close frees its army, which is a stronger Japan for the others to lose to. Pricing the denial properly needs the chance a rival marches in, not a per-rival share.

**Flickering vow:** in the all-AI games the vow scores and is lost about 6 times per close (2,583 completions and 2,421 losses over 407 closes in 768 games) and Japan loses 3 cities per close while closed. Closing is a bet the AI keeps losing; fixing that needs the defence of the countdown, not a better close decision.

## 2026-10-21: Counting a count-completing capture as a VP leaves the AI seat alone and makes the other AIs convert; Japan itself could not (8.9.6 Swiss)

**Gene** `capture_completes_count` (default 1): `objective_capture_value` counts a capture that completes a live Cities (one to go) or Territory (the city's ring covers the tiles left) objective, like the Fight objectives. **Why Japan's own case did not move:** from the 8.9.6 Swiss T190/T196 states a Japan with the gene captured 0.1 cities and played identically to the control (identical outcomes in all 60 runs): it is closed and declares no war, and the 0.1 captures say no campaign with the VP prize was on offer. **Where it does move:** with every AI on the gene the user's seat wins 46/60 -> 0 from T160 and 50/60 -> 0 from T175 (seat 2, the AI that wins these branches, 14/60 -> 60/60 and 10/60 -> 40/60), and the human-seat benchmark reads opponents-only -1.0 pp [-1.9, -0.1] with the seat-only arm unchanged (-0.4 [-1.0, +0.1]). So the gene helps the AIs that can fight; the closed-country conversion needs the vow side (landing 3), and `objective_effort` still hides the capture route for a Cities objective with no site.

**Scenario Lab cannot see it:** `japan-big-empire`, `byzantium-last-point` and `athens-next-points` are 30-turn windows from positions where no capture completes a count; the scores did not move to the point (713.4 mean both ways). `legatum-sim scenario --ai-params FILE` (new) plays a scenario with a gene on every AI seat.

## 2026-10-21: Peace-then-war is 28% of all AI wars, not only Sparta's capture cycle; pricing the redeclaration cut it by 40%

**The 8.9.5 cycle.** Sparta's plan log: `captured -> peace [desertion -124]`, next turn `peace -> campaign [broken peace -190, peace credibility -280]` at +1,000. Cause: while the army holds a captured city (`held_capture`, until the city's resistance ends or it passes half HP) the held units are not a strike force, `campaign_candidates` has no forecast, and the quiet plan is the only candidate, so Hard signs Peace with every enemy at war (a Peace plan names one team but `wants_peace` accepts all but the plan's target). The charges for the next declaration were paid by the campaign after the peace, never weighed against it.

**The cycle is general.** `tools/sim/flip-metrics.sh` (192 to 256 eight-player Hard games on the box, `war_metrics` on the tapes): 47 wars and 56 Peaces per game, 28% of wars declared within 10 turns of a Peace with the same target (Sparta 30%, every faction 13 to 32%), 36% of captures of a city captured in the previous 20 turns. `flip_trace` (declarer's and target's plan at the Peace and at the declaration) on 24 games: 19% of flips had the declarer holding a captured city at the Peace; the rest are Peace plans after `campaign_ended`/`infeasible` that a `better` campaign reverses 3 to 10 turns later, and war-plan seats ending the wars they do not fight, then fighting them next.

**What did and did not move it.** The hold alone (`peace_redeclare` 1): 13.3 -> 13.0 flips per game, nothing (the hold is a fifth of flips and the price lets some Peaces through). Extended to any war the AI fought in the last 8 turns (2): wars per game 47.2 -> 36.3, flips within 10 turns 13.4 -> 8.1 per game, captures per war 0.57 -> 0.68. Pricing the best campaign on the enemy's nearest cities instead (the first version) read `None` for 93% of cities: `funded_forecast` only has a forecast for the nominated target (`conquest_city`, the strike, the plan's target), so a price that needs arbitrary cities cannot be computed from the forecast and the retaliation-versus-charge comparison is used instead. The Nagoya churn itself (captures retaken by the old owner within 10 turns, 25%) did not move.

**Method trap:** a `remote.sh` job started while the working tree was still being edited ran the old code; two runs of the same gene file disagreed (46.7 and 36.3 wars per game) until the commit landed. Rerun any result that reads "no effect" before believing it.

## 2026-10-18: Strength is a percentage per era (30%), so late eras jump hard; the 8.9.4 Tank losses were a wounded Tank left next to a Walled city and a Field Cannon

**What killed his two Tanks (8.9.4, T232, `p894-tanks-lost-t232`, replayed on the 8.9.4 engine, every hash matched).** Not archers. Both Tanks had been fighting for two turns and ended each turn wounded next to the Swiss-side city (pop 14, Walls, strength 48) and a veteran Field Cannon. Tank 281: Field Cannon shots of 17, 25 and 31 on T230 to T232 (the Cannon was level 3, 61 against the Tank's 53 + 18 "Mounted against ranged"), plus a Crossbowman's 9, and it never left its tile to heal. Tank 282: a city strike of 25 (T231) and 33 (T232), a Phalangite melee hit of 26 on T231 (a Classical spear, level 1, 46 with its +18 against mounted, against a Tank at 50), and an Arquebusier's 11. Each shot was only 9 to 33 damage; the loss was the third turn in range, not one fight. What was absurd was the shape of the numbers: a Classical Phalangite traded 26 for 35 with a Modern Tank, a Tank that attacked a Rifleman (one era behind) died (`era_duels` before: "dies, 43 HP dealt back"), and a city strike did 30 a hit to it.

**Rule (user, 2026-10-18, 8.9.4 and a follow-up: "each era should be thought of as a percentage increase"):** the melee line is 20 strong in the Ancient era and each era adds `data::ERA_GROWTH_PERCENT` = 30%: 20, 26, 34, 44, 57, 74, 97 (was 20, 26, 32, 38, 44, 50, 56). Every other strength is a design number (the old straight-line value: Knight 32, Field Cannon 47/36, Walls 10) scaled by `MELEE_LINE[era] / DESIGN_MELEE_LINE[era]` (`data::era_scaled`), so every class keeps its place against the melee line of its own era and only the gap between eras grows. That includes mounted (Tank 53 to 78), ranged (Artillery 55/43 to 81/64), the Fighter and Bomber, the siege bonuses vs city HP, the faction edges (Byzantium +4, Celts -2 and +3, the Keshig's bow, added before scaling) and, with the owner's era, the city lines and Walls tiers (x1.00 Ancient and Classical, 1.06 Medieval, 1.16 Renaissance, 1.30 Industrial, 1.48 Modern, 1.73 Future). Flat things stay flat: the +18 class bonus, terrain, fortify, wounds, the phalanx. One constant tunes it. I picked the percentage over a wider gap term in the damage formula because it reaches everything at once (city, Walls, siege, faction edges), keeps one table, and leaves the formula that every forecast, tooltip and AI projection shares alone. 30% over 25% because it leaves Ancient and Classical identical to before (rush balance untouched) and gives the 40-point Modern-over-Medieval gap the user asked for; at 25% the same sims read 301 turns against 312 and captures per game 12.6 against 12.9, so the choice is about feel, not side effects.

**Evidence (`crates/sim/examples/era_duels.rs`, mean rolls, open ground, full health, no promotions):** same era, first striker wins narrowly, counters as before; one era ahead, melee kills in 3 attacks with 53 to 61 HP left up to the Renaissance and in 2 (82 to 88 left) from the Industrial era; two eras ahead 83 to 100 HP left in 2 attacks; three eras ahead 1 or 2 attacks. A Tank now kills a Rifleman (one era behind) in 3 attacks with 47 HP left, a Pikeman (three eras behind) in 2 with 89 left, and the shots in his T230 to T232 fights change like this on a healthy Tank: the Phalangite's hit 26 to 9 (and the Tank now kills it in one blow), the Arquebusier's 13 to 7, the Walled city's strike 25 to 33 down to about 19, and the level 3 Field Cannon 17 to 15 (it is one era behind, so it still hurts; that is the intended near-even fight across one era, with the Tank's +18 against ranged already counted). The class counter is still worth about 18 points, which is one to two eras early and about one late: a Knight still dies attacking a Spearman, and a Cuirassier needs 4 attacks to kill one. Side effects, 480 four-player Hard games each (seed 6000001): length 300 to 312 turns, techs per empire 33.7 to 33.9 (no tech-race change), city captures per game 14.7 to 12.9 (cities scale with their owner's era, while flat bonuses weigh less), eliminations 0.24 to 0.21; 240 eight-player games: 313.6 to 318.9 turns, captures 22.6 to 22.0, eliminations 0.87 to 0.95. War suite (Hard, 64 runs): 9 of 11 scenarios unchanged; "The Golden Horde comes" (2,235-Gold) went from 1% to 76% attacker success and "Hold Against the Horde" from 98% to 23%, both nearer the user's own 8.9.2 result; the attack check `war-golden-horde-thin` stays 100% (floor 30%). Human-seat benchmark (Hard, all seats, dice reseeded per salt, 8 maps): 64 salts on the cloud 31.8% (95% 28.3 to 35.5) to 31.1% (27.5 to 34.8), -0.8 pp; 48 other salts run on the Mac while the box queue was 20 deep, 31.5% to 30.7%, also -0.8 pp (standard error 3.3). Not worse (the first 16 salts alone read -7 pp, which shows how noisy 16 are). Combat lab (`check`, 21 fixed cells, 12 dice seeds, five policies): ok once the lab's defender stands in the attacker's era (before that fix it knew Masonry only, so a Renaissance army met an Ancient-era city and one Storm cell moved 0% to 50%, which the check rightly flagged); the committed fixed baseline was rewritten on the new lab and the curve. Full grid (41,580 cells, 12 dice seeds, five policies, 2.5 million fights on the box, new lab on both sides): Ancient and Classical identical to the last digit (the curve is unchanged there), Renaissance capture rates up 3 to 6 points (the AI's own policy 26.4% to 30.0%, Storm 46.8% to 53.0%, Focus 40.0% to 46.3%) because the flat defence bonuses (fortify, terrain, the +18 class counter) weigh less against the bigger Renaissance numbers.

**Gotchas.** (1) City defence now needs the owner's era in every caller (`combat::city_defense_for_rules(..., era)`; `PlayerView::era_of`, `GameState::era_of`); a fogged city uses its owner's current public era. (2) The TS mock mirrors it (`eraScaled`, `cityDefenseStrength(..., era)`) and reads `designStrength`, `designRanged` and `siegeBonus` from `rules.generated.json`. (3) A different trajectory exposed a latent AI bug: the Byzantium hire (`plan_hire`) could add a Call to Arms to a team it had already sent a treaty proposal, which validation refuses ("one diplomatic order per other team"); it now skips such a team. (4) Four AI/core unit tests used a Trooper's exact strength and were retuned (a 63% kill instead of 60%, Phalangites for the siege-readiness test).
## 2026-10-20: Walls as a price: the Engineer genes on, and the three ways turning them on first went wrong
`walls_as_cost` and `engineer_for_walls` now default to 1 (`sally_engineer` stays 0). Measured on the coach's T187/T200 branches of the user's Athens game (his own orders, 30 runs): his wins 30/30 -> 0/30, with the AIs building 24 Engineers a game and taking 5 to 8 of his cities (control 2). What it took:
(1) **Virtual cover for a faction that cannot build Engineers is a phantom.** `walls_as_cost` alone priced Engineers the Mongols (no Engineers before a Workshop city) will never have; the siege forecast then committed melee early and `war-golden-horde-thin` fell from 95% to 51%. A planned Engineer needs a faction that builds it, a war that builds it (`engineer_for_walls`) and a city that finishes it.
(2) **An Engineer is 80 Production, so cover costs turns, and a small economy cannot pay.** Priced at 40 and no delay, `walls_as_cost` made Sparta (8.4 map, human-seat benchmark) march on a walled city whose Engineers were 16 turns away and miss the open city next door: seat wins 88 -> 44 of 128, first Settler T72 instead of T112, 1.0 cities at T50 instead of 1.8. `engineer_for_walls` alone: 80, noise. Fix: `COVER_PATIENCE` = 10 turns for the whole force (`plannable_engineers`), build turns added to the capture time; an economy that cannot meet it keeps Walls as a veto, as before.
(3) **Partial cover is not a plan.** Covering two units of six (counted as plannable) brought the 8.4 Sparta seat back down to 29 wins: the half-covered siege still beat the open city in the forecast. All or nothing for the force gives 82 of 128 (control 88).
- **Gate:** seat-only -1.9 pp [-5.2, +1.4] and opponents-only -4.3 [-7.7, -0.9] before the era landing; on merged master -3.0 [-5.9, -0.1] and -0.6 [-3.9, +2.6] (1,024 games per arm). The Hard seat builds 8 to 12 Engineers a game and captures no more cities; against another Hard AI the Engineers are a cost, against the walled thin-army line the user played they decide the game. So the benchmark under-values the genes, and the tape branches are the measure that sees them (`docs/sim-results/siege-engineer/walls-as-price/results.md`).
- **`sally_engineer` stays off:** on top of the other two his wins against the Athens T187 line went from 0/30 to 15/30 (the AIs got weaker), and the benchmark showed no gain.
- **War suite:** `war-sally-engineer` (the defender) 100% -> 0%: the Mongol attacker with 11 Engineers takes a German city on turn 4 (the scenario predicted a 30% hold); `war-walled-engineer` stays 0%: the assault planner counts only units that can reach a ring tile this turn, and Keystone sits behind an impassable ridge, so three of six arrive and the forecast fails every turn. Staging by path rather than distance is the open fix. The attack check `war-golden-horde-thin` reads 67% on merged master with or without the genes (95% before the era landing): that drop is the era landing's.
- **Method:** a `--arms` run of `hard_vs_human` on one map with the genes one at a time found which gene hurt in 8 minutes (`tools/cloud/run-batch.sh --bin hard_vs_human --ai-params ARMS.json -- --arms --map ID`); `LEGATUM_HVH_TRACE=MAP:SALT` plus the plan events showed the lost open city. A hard-vs-human batch that stops with exit 254 right after launch is an AWS call failing (spot count, rate limit): relaunch. After the era landing the 8.9.6 tape no longer replays on master, so the branch numbers above are from the 8.9.6 engine with the same AI crate.

## 2026-10-20: Coach, two 8.9.6 wins: the shipped AI reads Walls as a ban, and decadence decided the Swiss game
(1) **With the Engineer genes off, Walls are a ban, not a price** (fixed the same day, see the entry above). 8.9.6 Walls halve every uncovered attack on a city's HP. `walls_as_cost`, `engineer_for_walls` and `sally_engineer` ship at 0, and no AI built a Military Engineer in either 8.9.6 playtest or in any control branch. In the Athens game every one of his 8 cities was walled and most were empty at T190, and AI campaigns ran at 3–10% odds and ended `walls_before_arrival` or infeasible. From his T200 position (his own orders, 30 runs), `walls_as_cost` alone took his wins from 30/30 to 0/30 (4.9 cities lost against 2.0, with 0.1 Engineers built: the AI just attacks), while `engineer_for_walls` alone changed nothing (Engineers built, walls still read as a ban). Hard in his seat won 29–30/30 either way, which is why the Hard-seat benchmark (+2.9 pp opponents-only) missed it: measure siege genes against a walled, thin-army seat.
(2) **Decadence decided the Swiss game, not Japan's strategy.** Japan, the biggest empire all game (7 cities at T50, 90 population at T200), paid 55–63% decadence around T50–T75 and 35–47% from T100 to T200: 38.9% of its output (13,657 Production) against his 10%. Its Power lead holds partly because Power counts Food, the yield its faction rule adds. Decadence off from any turn T100–T196 makes Japan win 24–30/30 (crown upkeep off alone: nothing); five forced Japanese strategies under the bands moved it to 0–4/30. With the bands off it wins on three crowns (Largest Economy, Most Advanced, Strongest Army), which net yields decide.
(3) **Tape-seat fragility has a control:** compare each branch's failed-order count with a neutral perturbation that still wins. A forced war on a closed Japan is silently blocked ("can't declare war"), so read the branch log before reading a no-change result.
Reviews: `docs/playtests/reviews/2026-10-20-8.9.6-athens.md`, `docs/playtests/reviews/2026-10-20-8.9.6-switzerland.md`; tools in `~/LegatumRuns/coach-tools/coach-896/`.

## 2026-10-18: Coach, 8.9.5 Athens loss: one capture can score three eras, unreseeded what-ifs fake certainty, and the scenario validator rejects Japan's cities
(1) **Era objectives stack.** Fight objectives count from the turn each era was entered, and an era stays open until scored. So one event can meet the same or similar objective in several eras at once. AI Sparta's recapture of Sellasia from the Mongols (then the VP leader) at T146 scored eras 3, 4 and 5 (+3 VP in one turn), and its 12th city at T211 scored "Own 12 cities" for eras 6 and 7. Recapturing your own city counts as a capture from the leader. Raised with the user as a rule question; it is how Sparta won.
(2) **Hard AI what-ifs without reseeded dice are one sample, even for the Hard seat.** From T150 of his game, Hard in his seat won 25/30 with AI salts only (near-identical games) and 16/30 with `reseed: true`; from T170 it went from 0/30 to 8/30. Always reseed the Hard and proxy seats; only the tape seat must stay unreseeded.
(3) **A static defence floor is not deterrence.** Forcing Walls, 2 shooters and a melee unit near each of his cities from T150 left Sparta's harvest of Mongol cities almost unchanged (7.0 captures against 9.7) and displaced his Great Work. What cut it to 4.1 was a seat that fought Japan for the same border cities (Hard Athens).
(4) **`core::scenario::validate_state` uses the 4-hex `MIN_CITY_GAP` for every faction.** Japan legally founds cities 3 hexes apart (`data::min_city_gap`), so every position containing Japanese cities fails ("illegal position: Okayama is within 3 hexes of Sapporo"), in `cut.sh` and in the wasm scenario loader. The fix is the smaller of the two founders' gaps (patch in `~/LegatumRuns/coach-tools/coach-895-athens/`); `scenarios/lab/athens-next-points.json` is held back with `requires` until it lands.
Review: `docs/playtests/reviews/2026-10-18-8.9.5-athens.md`.

## 2026-10-18: Coach, 8.9.4 Sparta sweep at T40: Walls don't hold the nearest one-city neighbour against phalanx Hoplites, and early-rush what-ifs need a scripted attacker
(1) **Walled Athens (pop 2, Walls from T9, 2 units) fell three turns into the siege to 4–5 Hoplites.** Phalanx Hoplites fight at 27 against the walled city's 24. The split counterattack costs each of three attackers only 7–9, wound immunity keeps them at full strength, kill heals refill them, and an Archer's shot does 16–20 to them. 200 HP at about 30 a hit is two turns of four attackers, Walls or not. In the what-ifs (scripted storm seat, 30 runs, dice reseeded), Athens fell by T40 in 30/30 as built and 27/30 with forced Walls + 3 Archers + a Spearman from T5; with the phalanx off as well, 14/30.
(2) **Against Hoplites, Spearmen beat Archers.** Three Spearmen halved Karakorum's fall rate (23 → 11/30), while two Archers left it unchanged (24/30). The AI's threat defence answers every threat with a shooter (`threat.rs::plan_threat_defense`, `RANGED_LADDER`) and stops at Walls or one shooter within 2 hexes, whatever the army's size.
(3) **Seat models for an early rush.**
- The `tape` seat reproduces the game exactly, but misfires once a defender stands on a tile its orders used, so defence branches against it are upper bounds; reseeding dice breaks it.
- `proxy_v8_sparta` sat at home and lost its capital.
- Hard in his seat took 0 cities by T40 even from his T25 position at Therapne's walls.

A crude scripted storm (all units on the nearest at-war city, attack at 3 within 2 hexes) gave usable variance. The runner is in `~/LegatumRuns/coach-tools/coach-894/`.
(4) **Runner gotcha:** in the what-if runner's event `match`, an earlier `Event::CityCaptured { .. }` arm shadows the later seat-only arm, so `seat0_captures` and `wars_on_seat0` are always empty. Read `caps` and `wars` instead.
(5) **There is no matplotlib locally or on the box.** Write charts as SVG and convert them with `sips -s format png in.svg --out out.png`, which keeps the size; `qlmanage -t` crops to a square.

## 2026-10-18: Mongol siege unlock: a gated rule is mostly state plumbing, and a faction-only rule can still move the whole benchmark by chance (branch astra/mongol-siege-capture)
(1) **The gate has to be state-aware in every reader.** Availability (`data::available_to_player`), the unavailable reason, city-HP damage (`combat::damage_to_city_hp`), the AI's production filter, its tech-benefit test, the locked build row and the Guide all read `Player::engineers_absorbed`; the plain `available_to_faction` stays for rules that never change. The flag is omitted from saves while false, so old archive hashes do not move. (2) **Rivals can see which cities have a Workshop** (`CityView::has_workshop`, the same level of detail as walls) so the Mongol AI can value capturing one; the treasury-style hidden numbers are untouched. (3) **Benchmark (Hard, 8 maps x 64 salts, all seats): 27.1% on the same master, 24.8% with the rule.** The Mongol seat stays at 0% on both and the Mongol rival map stays at 0%; the other six maps moved by up to 12 points although no Mongol is in them, so a one-field change to the view reshuffles the AI's random draws and that spread is chance, not the rule. Judge a faction-only rule on the maps that hold that faction. (4) **The war suite is blind to this rule right now:** all ten scenarios are identical with and without it, and on current master the Golden Horde (Mongol attack) scores 0% and Mongol Walls 1%, against 43% and 100% before the Keshig landed. The Mongol attack scenarios need re-baselining or an AI fix before they can say anything about Mongol siege. (5) A 1 px overflow of the Mongol faction sheet at 1000x600 came from one extra sentence: the sheet carries the siege rule in a short line and keeps the damage detail in the Guide and the Walls hint.
## 2026-10-18: The Golden Horde attack check fell from 100% to 0% because the Swiss defender can now see (territory sight and the attack reveal), not because the attacker broke
(1) **Bisect on the box (64 Hard runs a probe, one job):** `ac975214c` 100%, `097af665d` 100%, `2a76649b` 100%, `5c27218d` 0%, `430210c48` 0%, `897570d2` 6%, `dbf1395e7` ("Make owned territory and border ring always visible") 6%. The commit adds two sight rules for every team, AI included: the team's own tiles plus one adjacent ring are always visible, and an ordinary unit that attacked a team is visible to it through its next planning turn. Keshig, decadence, boost removal, stockpile cap, unique pieces, Fighter strike, the Celtic sack and the siege flags are not involved. (2) **Either sight rule alone is enough, and both must go to restore 100%:** with both off master reads 100% (median 7 turns); territory sight off 1%, attack reveal off 0%. The Hard Swiss defender (P0, 2,235 Gold the user left unspent) now sees the Mongol army on turn 137 and buys a Soldier, then a Marksman a turn in Aarau and a second city; the Mongol Campaign (priced "capture at 30%") ends on turn 139 (`InsufficientArmy`: the forecast needs the garrison it just met) and the Mongols propose Peace. The Mongol AI's march and siege are unchanged: it is a defender that spends its treasury, as the scenario's own prediction said it might. (3) **Swiss Gold sweep on today's master, attack/hold, 64 runs:** 0 Gold 100/0%, 300 100/0%, 700 100/0%, 1,200 84/15%, 2,235 0/100%. The attack check needs a defender that cannot buy the garrison out, not a different attacker. (4) **Fix:** a new scenario `war-golden-horde-thin` (same start, Swiss treasury 500 Gold; `war-build` recipe with a treasury edit) is now the `--check` scenario (floor 30%, reads 100%). The two original scenarios stay as the user's game; read them as "attack against an informed defender with a full treasury" (0%) and the matching hold (100%). No AI change. (5) **Lesson:** a rule that gives the AI information changes every AI-vs-AI scenario, attackers included; when a war-suite number moves with no AI change, bisect with `git bisect run` on the box (one job, build plus 64 runs per probe, 4 to 5 minutes a step) and toggle the suspect rules with a temporary environment switch before touching the AI.

## 2026-10-18: Siege lab: a Catapult is a worse archer, walls are a number not a kind of defence, and a Military Engineer closes the gap (branch claude/siege-research)
(1) **Per Production a Catapult does 7% more wall damage than Composite Archers (34 against 32 per 100 Production against a pop-3 walled city) and dies in essentially every fight** (1.0 lost per fight in the lab). Per Production, plain melee is the game's best wall-breaker (55 to 60 per 100), the opposite of Civ. The AI builds under 0.1 siege units a game because `composition_bonus` gives the class no value, and that is the correct reading of the rules. (2) **The city strike is not what kills siege engines.** The variant in which the strike never targets siege was identical to the decimal: the strike shoots the lowest-HP unit in range, a wounded melee unit; engines die to the garrison's shooters and sallies once the melee are gone, one move from safety. Protection rules are not the fix. (3) **Halving all non-siege damage to a walled city's HP makes walls what the user described**: four melee against a typical walled city with one shooter go from 35% to 0%, Production lost per capture 473 to 1,084; archers alone still breach, slowly (Classical 27%, Renaissance 75%). A Siege Engineer proxy (a Settler marching with the army; covered units ignore the halving and the Walls' +10) brings four melee to 44%, slightly above today, with the one-turn setup included; lifting the halving alone recovers only to 23%, so the +10 is half the deterrent. Doubling engine damage leaves a one-engine escort at 25%. (4) **Simultaneous turns give a free setup turn:** adjacency is read when a fight is planned, so an Engineer that moves up this turn covers next turn's attack. (5) **Walls' +10 does not scale by era:** Renaissance armies take a pop-3 walled city 71 to 78%; Castle and Star Fort are the scaling. (6) **Method:** the levers are opt-in `RuleExperiments` (`walls-blunt-*`, `siege-city-pct`, `siege-defense`, `city-strike-spares-siege`, `siege-engineer-proxy`) read in `plan_fight_on` and the city strike; the lab's `engineer_orders` policy keeps the proxy beside the units about to attack. Civilians stack under own military (`movement.rs`), so "next to" was simulated as distance 0 or 1. The lab's defender AI never sallied for the Engineer (under 1% captured), so attacker figures are upper bounds. 517,120 fights in 30 s on the box for about $0.09. Design: `docs/design/research/siege-rework-2026-10-18.md`.

## 2026-10-18: Why the siege broke Sparta and the Celts: it takes their units before their own war passes (branch claude/eu4-wars-2, not landed)
(1) **With the faction exclusions removed, Sparta's seat on the 8.4 Sparta map falls from 66% to 23%** (128 salts, map-only arms), the Celts' on 8.4 Celts from 11% to 6%. Germany no longer collapses on today's master (28% against 29%). (2) **An ablation mask (`siege_parts`) found the cause:** every part that moves Spartan melee or runs before its own passes hurts: the melee screen, the early pass on the campaign city, the field-army raid, and keeping a failed campaign's army at the front. With shooters only and the late pass only (`siege_melee` 0, `siege_parts` 2) Sparta is back to 56%, Germany 31%, but the Golden Horde falls back to 1%, because it needs the early pass; with the early pass too (`siege_parts` 3) the Golden Horde is 43% but Sparta drops to 8%. The early pass preempts Sparta's own storm and expedition machinery. (3) **Celtic sack campaigns (`celtic_campaign`, idea #25) help the Celts only without the siege** (14% against 11% off). (4) **An all-zero `siege_parts` arm reproduced the off arm game for game**, so the counters were clean. (5) **Coalition target by own threat read (`coalition_own_read`)** could not be read on the 8.9.4 Swiss what-ifs: on today's rules his position wins only 0 to 4% from T166 or T186 (exact tag states), with or without it.

## 2026-10-18: Idle wars came from a containment army parked out of reach, and from campaigns that counted only the units they began with (branch claude/eu4-wars)
(1) **The cause.** Per-war sampling in `hard_vs_human` (`LEGATUM_HVH_WAR_DIAG=1`) showed the declaring side spent most idle war turns under a Contain plan with its army 4 to 8 hexes from the enemy city: `campaign_staging_spot` keeps to tiles no enemy can reach, and the assault waits for a capture forecast that a walled, garrisoned city never gives. In the 8.9.2 Swiss game Germany sat 73 turns and Japan 41 at war with no attack. (2) **A siege objective works, but only with a value test and the right place in the turn.** `war_siege` (shooters in range where they survive the city's strongest strike, when our force outweighs the local defence; a city hit this turn doesn't heal) raised captures per war from 0.80 to 1.06 at no seat cost (256 salts: -0.0 pp), but run before the retreat passes for every war it made rivals waste units (opponents-only +7 pp); only the running campaign's city is sieged that early now. (3) **Campaigns counted only the units that began them** (`Campaign.committed`): the Golden Horde campaign began with two units, had six at Aarau the next turn and ended "insufficient army". `campaign_persist` refreshes the force and prices a grind instead of ending at the first forecast miss: 0% to 71%. The Mongol wall gate in `assessed_campaign_allowed` also removed the city from `targets` after a miss, so the siege code reads the defence directly (`city_defence_weight`). (4) **Faction exceptions are real:** sieging cut Germany's seat on the 8.6 map from 30% to 2% and Sparta's on the 8.4 map from 53% to 16% (1% with the grind); both are excluded. Read every benchmark change per map before the total. (5) **Stalled-war decay (`war_pressure`) backfired like the declaration gate:** peace with the leader doubled in the what-ifs and helped him, so it stays off and never applies to wars on the leader. (6) **Zero-battle wars barely moved (16.3% to 15.3%).** The rest are short Snipe wars, unwanted wars the other side won't end, enemies behind a third empire's closed land, and melee-only containment armies. (7) **What-if trap:** replaying a release tape on today's engine drifted from turn 1 (hash mismatch every turn; 4 cities at T150 instead of 10), which the 10-18 coalition what-ifs also did. Branch from states extracted at the release tag instead (`~/LegatumRuns/coach-tools/eu4-wars-2026-10-18/`), and raise the runner's 300-turn cap now that games are longer.

## 2026-10-18: Combat lab stage 2: a storm beats the AI's own timing in most sieges, and the forecast is wrong in both directions (branch astra/combat-lab-stage2)
(1) **With a live defender (300 Gold, real city orders, real tactics), 41,580 situations x 32 dice seeds x five policies took 438 s on the box for about $0.09.** In the report's 48 situation classes, storming now is the best policy in 39, focus fire in 8 and waiting for shooters in 1; surrounding first never wins. (2) **The forecast calls 23% of clearly winnable sighted sieges hopeless** (some policy captures in at least half the runs) **and says "captures" in 34% of cases where none of the five policies ever does.** The AI commits to a capture it forecasts within three turns, and a storm still wins most of the "turn 4" and "turn 5+" cases. With the city unseen, the forecast assumes one fortified Soldier whatever is really there, so it gives the same answer for an empty city and one with three archers. (3) **The check subset is a rules tripwire, and it fired** on the first merge with master: the Spartan Hoplite phalanx moved two Sparta cells from 0% to 58% and 100% for the storm and focus policies, so the baseline was re-recorded. The published results describe the earlier AI and rules; rerun `sweep` before quoting Sparta numbers. (4) Sim and docs only: no AI decision code changed.

## 2026-10-18: Japan's 3-tile spacing helps Japan and its rivals alike; read the benchmark by which maps hold Japan (branch astra/japan-tokyo)
(1) **Benchmark, Hard, 8 maps x 64 salts, all seats:** 31.2% on master against 27.1% with the rule, but the movement is all on the maps that contain Japan. As the human seat (map 8.4 Japan) Japan wins 53% of runs against 36% before (+17 points, a skill gain, not a regression). As a rival, Japan made Switzerland 8.5 drop from 50% to 19% and Sparta 8.6 from 45% to 20% (a stronger Japanese neighbour), while Sparta 8.4 rose 6 points. The four maps without Japan moved by at most 2 points. A faction rule that changes one AI shows up as a drop in the total, so split the maps by whether the faction sits in the seat or among the rivals before reading it. (2) **Spacing has to be applied in every place that asks "is this site legal"**: founding validation, same-turn founding conflicts, the AI's site search, its settler-danger tables, the Settle lens and the mock all read `data::min_city_gap` now, and a Japanese Settler is held to the stricter faction when two found at once. (3) The hive art (`hive.ts`, veins, the capital spire) is deleted, not hidden.

## 2026-10-18: The Celtic Sack: a rule that needs a hidden number must not publish it (branch astra/celtic-sack)
(1) **Luna made every treasury public to show "Sack: +N Gold"**, deleting three tests that asserted rivals' Gold is hidden, and gave every AI a made-up reward for capturing rich cities. Only the Celts take Gold, and the true treasury stays private: the forecast shows the sure part (25 per population) and says a quarter of their treasury comes on top; the event total goes only to the Celts and the victim (`event_visibility.rs`, mock `events.ts`); the Celtic AI prices the treasury part from the public city count, 40 Gold per city, never the real balance. (2) **A rule that removes a restriction opens AI code paths nobody tested**: Celts were excluded from conquest targeting because they raze; now they are not. The human-seat benchmark (Hard, 8 maps x 64 salts, all seats) moved 31.4% to 31.2%, and only the Celtic map changed (7.8% to 6.2%, one game), because Celts are rarely in play on those maps. (3) **An e2e that pins a Gold total to the unit breaks on the first turn's income**: the victim's 10,000 became 9,930 before the capture, so the spec now checks the range.

## 2026-10-18: Territory sight touched three oracles, and the benchmark swings per map while the total barely moves (branch astra/territory-sight)
(1) **A sight rule has more than one entry point.** Luna added owned land plus one ring to `native_visible_tiles`, but `update_explored` (exploration memory, threat observation, first contact) reads `native_sight_by_team`, which bypassed it, so views showed the tiles while memory did not. The independent oracle in `visibility_equivalence.rs` caught it only after the oracle itself was taught the rule. Both paths now call `add_territory_sight`. Luna's own run skipped the full workspace tests after repinning, which is how two failing suites reached the gate. (2) **The human-seat benchmark cannot split a rule change by seat**, so it is a master-versus-branch comparison. 8 maps x 64 salts, Hard, all seats: master 32.0% against 31.6% with the rule (inside the +-3 point interval), yet single maps moved by up to 26 points (Sparta 8.4 from 97% to 70%, Switzerland 8.3 from 0% to 20%). A rule that changes what everyone sees reshuffles who wins on each map; judge it by the total. (3) **Ordinary attackers are now revealed to the defender for the next planning turn**, so the "unseen unit" wording cannot appear for an attacker.

## 2026-10-17: Tech boosts removed: the discount was random per technology, and costs scale back era by era (branch claude/no-boosts)
(1) **Boosts were earned very unevenly, which is why they felt random.** Over 256 four-player Hard + Search games, the share of researched technologies whose boost was earned first runs from 0.6% (Recursive AI) and 1.2% (Steel) to 78% (Gunpowder) and 89% (Combustion), so the 40% discount landed on whichever technology a typical empire happened to meet the condition for. Weighted by cost it was a 4% cut in Ancient, 10 to 13% in Classical to Renaissance, 18% in Industrial and Modern, 9% in the Future (`docs/sim-results/era-overhaul/no-boosts.md`). (2) **Pacing came back with a first-order rule:** scale each era's costs by one minus its measured effective cut. The first pass landed every era entry within 3 turns; the second only shuffled cost between neighbours (a technology cost lands in the next era's entry turn, so what one era gained the next lost). Median winner entry T27/63/103/140/174/204, before T27/63/103/140/173/200; median game 242 against 243.5. (3) **"Current pacing" in the brief (T32/64/100/132/162/189) was the 2026-10-10 calibration; master measures differently today.** Measure the baseline first, on the same seeds, and target that. (4) **The card's discount percent was derived from the Science it removed.** At small costs the rounding shows: a 16-Science tech at the rule's 20% loses 4, so the card said 25%. `costParts` now takes the percent from the rule (10% per empire, cap 50%) and the cost lowering made the error visible. (5) **Old saves need no shim:** `Player` has no `deny_unknown_fields`, so a save's `boosts` and `tallies` simply drop out on load (a test pins it); no saved or recorded data held `BoostEarned` events. (6) **The gate:** human-seat benchmark, 64 salts on 8 maps, all seats (a rule change reaches every seat, so the seat-only and opponent-only arms cannot isolate it): Hard 33.0% to 38.1% (paired delta +5.1 points, 95% [+0.4, +9.8]), Hard + Search 29.1% to 29.1% (0.0 [-4.5, +4.3]); war scenarios 41% to 40% mean goal success (Behind the Walls 90% to 87%). (7) **Cloud traps:** four batches launched at once hit `MaxSpotInstanceCountExceeded` (the quota is shared with the box and other agents' batches), and run-batch just stops; launch gate batches one at a time with a retry on "no capacity". `legatum-sim` no longer takes `--speed` (Standard only). The hard-vs-human chart script crashes on an old ledger row without `overall_score` (row 188), so chart redraws fail until that row is fixed.
## 2026-10-17: Removing the strategic stockpile cap changed nothing for the AI, and showed that Iron and Coal are almost never spent (branch claude/no-stockpile-cap)
(1) **Why it was removed.** A top-bar tooltip read "Coal: 60 stored, 0 per turn from 2 sources" because the cap of 60 (copied from Civ 6) zeroed the displayed income; the user: "Why is the cap 60? Why is there a cap at all frankly?" The cap went from core (`resources::collect_income`, `StockpileView.cap`), the mock, the generated rules and the UI. The tooltip now reads "Coal: 60 stored, +4 a turn from 2 sources (revealed by Iron Working)" and a stockpile with no deposit says so. Coal really is revealed by Iron Working in `resources::stats`, so that part of the old text was right.
(2) **Measured** (`docs/sim-results/no-stockpile-cap/`, Hard on the 8 human-seat maps, 256 salts, 2,048 games per arm; one binary, an arm's `legacy_cap` puts the old cap back on chosen seats after each turn). Human-seat win rate: control (cap everywhere) 31.7%, seat-only uncapped 31.7% (-0.0 pp, 95% -0.9 to +0.8), opponents-only uncapped 31.5% (-0.2, -1.4 to +0.9), everyone uncapped 32.3% (+0.6, -0.7 to +2.0). Faction win shares, game length (median T276) and Trooper counts did not move.
(3) **Hoarding is large and harmless.** Uncapped, the median empire holds 86 Iron and 76 Coal at T100, 148 and 186 at T150, 220 and 290 at T200 (p90 about 570 and 730, max 1,352 Iron), against a cap of 60 that 67% of Iron holders and 84% of Coal holders were sitting at on T100 (60% and 94% at T200), which is exactly the "0 per turn" state the user saw. The AI spends almost none of it: 1.8 Troopers built per empire per game (1.6 capped) and under 0.1 siege units, because the AI's production values never ask for them. Troopers are 13% of the army at T200 either way. By faction at T200: Celts (Celts) hold the most (median 520 Iron, 866 Coal), Mongols builds the most Troopers (3.7 a game), Byzantium builds almost none (0.1). Nothing snowballs.
(4) **What it means.** The cap never bound anything that mattered, so removing it is free, but it also shows Iron and Coal are a one-time gate, not a resource decision, once an empire has a deposit. If the user wants the stockpile to matter, Civ 6's answer is upkeep (each unit of an Iron or Coal class costs a little every turn); that is a design change, left with the lead.
(5) **Method and its limits.** The main batch ran on the branch just before it merged master's swiss-interest and later commits. A post-merge check (32 salts x 8 maps = 256 games) of the emulated control against a true master binary matched 234 of 256 games exactly (91 vs 92 wins; the rest differ where a refunded Trooper lands above the old cap, which the per-turn clamp cannot reproduce), while the uncapped run differed from master in 152 of 256 games (88 wins), so the rule does steer game trajectories chaotically yet not their win rate. Emulating a removed rule inside the benchmark (the arm clamps after each turn) gives a seat-only and opponents-only split for a rule that applies to everyone, without keeping the old code in core. Per-empire stockpile and Trooper/siege counts at T60/100/150/200/250 are now in every `hard_vs_human` game result (`strategic`, `strategic_built`).
## 2026-10-18: Reading the runaway moves armies; requiring a staged force before declaring takes the pressure off the leader (branch claude/ai-coalitions)
(1) **The race term is the lever.** With `runaway_read` (crowns held 30+ turns, a live goal, the public Power lead, the race from 30% of the target, no distance discount for the strict leader) the Mongols take Aarau in the Golden Horde scenario in 64 of 64 runs instead of 43%, and as opponents the Swiss hold all five cities in 1% instead of 56%; Sparta presses the Mongols' walled cities in Behind the Walls (they held 7% instead of 100%). In the Swiss what-ifs (proxy v4 in his seat, 96 runs) his win chance from T126 fell from 63% to 40% and from T150 from 55% to 45%. On the human-seat benchmark it is neutral (256 salts: seat-only +0.9 pp, opponents-only -0.2), because those eight maps rarely have an economic runaway before the end.
(2) **"Declare only with a force staged for a named city" backfired.** Extending `contain_commit` to every faction (`leader_commit` 2) cut wars on the leader from about 9.6 to 3.9 per game in the Swiss what-ifs and raised his win chance (T126 Hard seat 23% to 38%). The token wars the user disliked still cost him: forced spending knocked his reserve goal off for 27 turns. Commitment should come from pricing (the peace forfeit, the joint-strike term), not from a gate on declaring.
(3) **Zero-battle wars are a minority in AI-only games.** The new counters in `hard_vs_human` (8 maps x 256 salts): 15.4 wars a game, 16.8% with no battle, 0.59 captures per war, and in a third of the pairs of empires at war with the same target both attacked it within 2 turns of each other.
