The coalition genes moved these by under a point. The user's "fake coalitions" are a human-leader situation (a leader the AIs fight late and from far away), which the Swiss what-ifs and the war scenarios measure better than the benchmark.

## 2026-10-17: AI Sparta's rush failed before the assault: closed borders, a walling first target and a forecast that called a 4-turn storm 9 turns away (branch claude/ai-convert-armies)
(1) **Closed borders make the march slow.** At Peace an army cannot enter the target's land, so a Sparta that waits to declare until a siege is forecast (`early_campaign_muster`) stands at the border, 3 to 5 hexes out, while the target walls up. The user declared with his four Hoplites one to three hexes from Nicaea and stormed it four turns later.
(2) **The first target is the first city the scout finds.** From turn 1 AI Sparta knew only Japan's Kyoto on T13; Japan had seen a rush faction next door and walled up by T14. Scoring targets by value (`value_first_target`) cannot pick a city it has not seen. Earlier scouting is the next lever, for attackers and defenders alike.
(3) **The siege forecast, not the target, blocked the storm.** With the user's T18 army in place, plain Hard took Nicaea in 0 of 64 runs; pricing the split counterattack and Sparta's kill heal on unwalled cities (`storm_forecast`) made it 64 of 64 (median 5 turns). In the full game the same genes moved captures by T87 only from 1.2 to about 1.4.
(4) **The combat lab measures the tactical layer, not the plan.** Widening the forecast window (`siege_window` 6) and the storm forecast raise the forecast's capture calls (51% to 71% of cells) but the AI's actual captures in the lab by only 1 point: the assault check (`city_assault_succeeds`) assumes every visible defender strikes our weakest unit every round, while the lab's defenders mostly hold. Tune that against defenders that do sortie (a human does), not only the lab's.
(5) **Replay tools:** `tools/scenarios/extract-war-states.sh` now lists the 8.9.2 Sparta save (T18); the combat lab takes `--ai-params` for the attacker; the what-if runner from `~/LegatumRuns/coach-892-sparta/tool/` replays the Swiss tape on this engine only with the old +5 interest cap restored (uncommitted, in the throwaway worktree), otherwise every turn from T62 mismatches.

## 2026-10-17: The AI's siege forecast is pessimistic, and the AI only commits when it sees a capture within three turns (combat lab stage 1)
The lab (`crates/sim/examples/combat_lab.rs`, results in `docs/sim-results/combat-lab/`) played 30,240 city-capture situations 32 times each (different dice every time) under two attacker policies, Hard's own planner and a forced "storm now" (every melee and mounted unit walks into the city every turn): 1.9 million fights in 118 s on the 64-core box, 4 ms of CPU per fight. (1) **The forecast misses a third of the winnable cities.** With the city in sight, the forecast (`forecast_siege`) calls 33% of the situations that one of the two policies wins hopeless; it is worst for melee-only armies (43%), cities with three defenders (48%), melee garrisons (58%) and the Mongols (42%, whose city damage is halved). Cause: it only looks about four rounds past the army's arrival, and a storm often takes 4 to 6 turns, with few losses. (2) **The AI commits when the forecast says three turns or fewer** (98% at turn 2, 74% at turn 3, 25% at turn 4, 2% at turn 5+), so it skips 25% of the situations a storm takes in at least 80% of the runs. Known Sparta case: 2 to 3 Hoplites against an unwalled city with one melee defender, 2 hexes away: the forecast says no, the AI never attacks, a storm takes it in 100% of runs in 3 to 5 turns with 0 Hoplites lost. (3) **Storm beats the AI by 25 points or more in 3,059 of 15,120 cities in sight; the AI beats storm that much in 324** (mostly shooter-heavy armies, where a storm wastes the shooters). A storm loses 2 to 3 times as many Production as the AI because the AI mostly doesn't fight. Walls roughly double the price of a storm and do not stop an army of 4 to 6. (4) **Fog dominates:** at three hexes the city is unseen, the forecast assumes one fortified Soldier whatever is there, and the AI attacks in a third of the runs. (5) **Ford and river:** the forecast ignores a chokepoint (24% false alarms) and the AI mostly stalls at it (8% capture against a storm's 47%). (6) **Caveats that matter when reading the numbers:** the defence is Hard's tactical planner with no production, so it can't buy Walls or reinforce mid-siege (storm results are an upper bound); extra melee defenders hold their tiles and do not counterattack; cities are pop 3 (strength 16); the strike-level forecast (`opportunity_strike`) never fired in the arena because the stub city's value is small, so the column is not usable. (7) **Method traps:** runs that vary only AI seeds are near-identical, so the dice seed varies per run; an attacker with no home city never gets a strike forecast, so each side owns a far capital;

## 2026-10-17: The alliance-offer loop was the client's one-order-per-empire queue, not the engine (branch claude/alliance-loop)
Playtest 8.9.2 T20 (`~/LegatumRuns/bug-saves/p892-alliance-loop-t20.legatum`): Switzerland offered an Alliance, the user declined, declared War on it and attacked; the offer came back as "Respond to offer", answering it cancelled the war, the attacks became invalid at Peace ("units confused"), re-ordering them asked to declare War again, and so on. (1) **Cause.** Core takes one diplomatic order per other team per turn, so `queueDiplomacy` replaces the order toward that team: the war replaced the decline, and `pendingDecisions` counted an offer as answered only by a `RespondToProposal` for it, so the offer was pending again; answering it replaced the war. No unit order was deleted: they failed validation once the war was gone. (2) **The engine was already right:** the surprise war bumps the relation version, so the offer is invalidated at the boundary; it is now named ("War was declared", `diplomacy::WAR_ENDS_OFFERS`, mirrored in the mock). (3) **Fix (client):** a queued `DeclareWar`, `BreakTreaty` or `PayTribute` toward an offer's sender settles the offer (`offerSettledBy`), declining it then is a no-op that keeps the war, and its inbox card says "Ended by your War on X" with no reply buttons. Accepting is still an explicit choice that replaces the war. (4) **General rule for the app:** any decision a client derives from "has the player queued X" must also count the orders that make X moot, or a replace-on-conflict queue turns into a loop. Tests: `crates/wasm/tests/p892_alliance_loop.rs` (his save), `turnFlow.test.ts`, `e2e/alliance-offer-war.spec.ts` (his save, the full click path).
## 2026-10-17: The Keshig moved to the Classical era; one constant names its slot (data::KESHIG_KIND)
The user moved the Keshig out of the Ancient era for flavour ("the Mongol horde was in the 1200s"): it now replaces the Cataphract (Iron Working), Mongol Horsemen are ordinary and upgrade into it, and it upgrades to the Knight without its bow. Because the first version hard-coded `UnitKind::Horseman` in about forty places (core, AI tests, sim report, TS data, art, pieceCompare, tests, e2e specs), the slot is now one constant, `data::KESHIG_KIND`, exported as `RULES.keshig.kind` (`KESHIG_KIND` in `data.ts`); a faction piece's slot belongs in data, never in every call site. Numbers scale by the era steps rather than a fresh guess (mounted cost ×1.36: 60 → 82; the ranged line's +8: 10 → 18), and the Classical sweep (`docs/sim-results/unit-classes/README.md`) confirmed that 82/18 keeps the Ancient profile (mirror 42 vs 34 for the bowless rider, mixed army 30 vs 0, walled towns 47 vs 20, still 0% against Phalangites). The AI needed no logic change for the move itself (it reads a dual unit by what it can do, `composition::dual`, not by its kind), but the gate found one: **a bow changes what the AI does with a unit even when it rarely helps.** In the Golden Horde war scenario (the Mongol AI's siege of walled Aarau at T136, one Cataphract in its army, now a Keshig) the AI took Aarau in 33% of 256 runs against master's 44% (paired by run: 28 wins lost, 1 gained). Ablations pinned it: with the bow removed (ranged 0) it was 45%, so the AI-semantics changes were neutral and the bow alone did it; skipping city shots or rivals' purchase risk changed nothing (identical numbers mean the path never ran); skipping the Keshig in the early volley (`ranged_fire`) restored the declarations and losses to master's. The early volley ran before the campaign's march and staging passes, so the Keshig spent its turns on opportunistic shots and charges instead of marching with the siege. Fix: `military.rs::dual_fire` gives a dual unit its shot-or-charge choice only after the plan's moves (just before `rest`); Golden Horde 39% (paired: 25 lost, 12 gained against master, McNemar p about 0.03), the rest of the war suite unchanged (49% suite either way), human-seat benchmark flat (all-seats 32.2% against master's 32.0%; seat-only with the choice on against off +0.0 pp; opponents-only -0.2 pp). Deferring only Scout targets did not help (33%): the lesson is the ordering, not the target. Open: the remaining 5 points; a follow-up could let a campaign claim its Keshig explicitly. Mongol win share (Hard + Search, paired seeds, current master): 35.5% to 38.1% in 512 four-player games (fair 25%) and 7.4% to 11.7% in 256 eight-player games (fair 12.5%), inside the noise (about ±6 and ±4 pp); captures per Mongol seat 3.08 to 3.56 and 2.04 to 2.38. Iron Working and Chivalry's upgrade lines are rewritten for the Mongols in `data.ts::factionEffectLabel` ("Horsemen can upgrade to Keshigs", "Keshigs can upgrade to Knights and lose the bow").

## 2026-10-17: The Keshig: a dual unit needs a fight class, and "ranged == 0" meant three different things (branch claude/mongol-keshig)
(1) **No new order was needed.** Every military unit could already move onto an enemy (a melee attack), and `RangedAttack` only checks `unit_stats_for(..).ranged > 0`, so a Mongol Horseman with a bow is valid for both orders with one order a turn. Ranged units already could not move and shoot in one turn (the shot is the unit's whole order, resolved before movement), so "move to a tile and shoot" was never possible for anyone; the brief's assumption was wrong. (2) **The class bonus was keyed on the unit kind, not the fight.** Left alone, a Keshig shooting a Spearman would hand the Spearman "+18 melee line against mounted" against arrows, and a Keshig shooting an Archer would get "+18 mounted against ranged" for its own arrows. `combat::fight_class(kind, shooting)` makes a mounted unit's shot a ranged-class attack; every other unit fights as its class, so no other fight changed (core, forecast, TS combat, mock and the AI's exposure maths all needed the same switch). (3) **`ranged == 0` was doing three jobs:** "can capture or reinforce a city", "is a melee threat" and "keep it back as a shooter". A dual unit is all three. Core movement now asks `data::attacks_in_melee(kind)` (base stats, so the same for every faction); the old code used *base* `unit_stats`, which happened to give the Keshig 0, while the mock used *faction* stats and would have refused the Keshig's captures; the mock's shot validation and forecast had the opposite bug (base stats: no bow). Grep both engines for every use of a stat that a faction rule can change. (4) **Numbers by sweep** (`docs/sim-results/unit-classes/README.md`, "The Keshig"): at equal Production the price dominates (55 made it beat a mixed spear-and-bow army 70% of the time, 65 lost the Horseman's own mirror), range 1 was worse than range 2 at every price, and no setting lets riders shoot their way past a spear line in the open. Chosen 60 Production, 10 ranged at range 2 (that Ancient version was superseded the same day, see the entry above). (5) **Full games, Ancient version** (paired seeds, Hard + Search, AWS spot, about $0.85 for four cohorts): Mongol win share 34.7% → 33.6% in 512 four-player games (fair 25%) and 12.1% → 9.0% in 256 eight-player games (fair 12.5%), both inside the noise (±5.7 and ±4.0 pp at 95%); Mongol captures per seat 3.03 → 3.37 and 2.02 → 2.29. Not dominant. (6) **Upgrade price can go negative** for a faction piece priced near its upgrade (`upgrade_cost_for` is the cost difference): it is floored at 0 in core and the mock.

## 2026-10-11: Unlock art wiring: three traps (branch claude/unlock-art)
(1) **A Vite glob in a data module breaks Node-side specs.** `techCardUnlocks.ts` and `celebrate/eraUp.ts` are imported (through the mock and fixtures) by Playwright specs running in Node, where `import.meta.glob` is not a function. Importing `unlockArt.ts` (which globs `assets/unlocks`) there failed every spec at load. Paintings are now looked up in the components (`unlockEntryArt`, `itemArtUrl`); data modules carry only the item, tech and faction. (2) **Class-name collision.** The era-up takeover's key-art wrapper is `.et-art` with `img { width: 100%; height: 100% }`; naming the new list miniature `et-art` stacked every piece at the panel's top-left. Grep a stylesheet for a class before naming a new one. (3) **Moving a focusable block changes "the first focusable".** With the unlock rows (focusable hovercard rows) moved above the cost, `accessibility.test.ts`'s `.era-card [tabindex="0"]` found a row instead of the cost tooltip; target the element by its own container. Also: 46 paintings came from three parallel Luna image tasks in about 15 minutes each, all with real alpha; the five approved samples' prompts copied verbatim kept the set consistent.

## 2026-10-11: Painted map art drawn late hides the marks of the tiles it overhangs, and a map bonus nobody can see is not a bonus (branch claude/wonder-visibility)
(1) **Cause.** The natural-wonder painting (2.65 hex radii wide, so it spilled over all six neighbours) was drawn in the screen-space pass after the lens tint, the yield icons and the resource medallions, so it covered the neighbours' yield icons in the Yields lens and their medallions everywhere (user, playtest 8.8.1, Grand Canyon). The bonus itself (+25% of one city yield) was only in the hover card, as "Owning city: +25% Science", with no owner and no number. (2) **Fix.** The painting is now drawn right after the terrain, before improvements and every overlay, 2.5 radii wide, at 0.55 of its alpha under any lens; a "+25% [yield icon]" parchment pill on the tile sits above the lens tint and below units and banners, narrow enough to stay inside its hex (a first, wider pill would have landed on the upper neighbours' yield icons: they sit 28 px up and 28 px aside at the default zoom). (3) **Test method.** `e2e/wonder-visibility.spec.ts` draws the same view twice, with and without the wonder, and compares pixels at the six neighbours' yield-icon boxes and resource medallions in every lens at 1000x600 and 1440x900. A layering rule is easier to pin by measuring what is *hidden* than by asserting draw order; `wonders.test.ts` additionally pins the call order in `render.ts`. (4) **Discovery state needed a view field.** The client could not tell whether a wonder's one-time reward was still open (`wonders_discovered` is state, and the discovery event goes to the finder's team only), so `TileView.wonder_found` is public like the wonder. (5) **Where the number lands.** The core already put "Grand Canyon (+25%)" in the city breakdown, but the Science hover lumped it into "Faction bonus"; every place that sums a breakdown by label needs its own row for a new source. (6) **Design check (no sims; from the rules, ceil(25%) of the pre-wonder base).** A typical Standard city has about 5-7 Science, 9-11 Production, 10-12 Food and 3-6 Gold at T50 (pop 4-5, Library, Workshop), so the bonus is +2 Science, +3 Production, +3 Food, +2 Gold; at T150 (pop 8-10, College or University, Machine Shop, Market and Clearinghouse) about 15-20 Science, 18-22 Production, 20-24 Food and 8-11 Gold, so +4 to +6. That is about a Library (+3 Science, 80 Production) at T50 and a College at T150, for a tile you also have to grow into. Civ 6 wonders feel covetable because they show a big flat tile yield (+3 to +5) on the map and in adjacency; +2 at T50 reads small next to the tile's own art. Recommended for the user (not changed): a floor of +3 (so no wonder is worth less than a Library at T50), or +33%, or a flat tile yield on top (the wonder tile itself yields +2 of its bonus yield when worked); the first two change one number in `economy.rs` and `wonders.ts` (`wonderBonus`), the third adds a tile yield.
## 2026-10-11: Stray ",." on the Celts' piece card: stripping stat phrases out of prose is a bug factory (branch claude/text-artifacts)
(1) **Cause.** `pieceTextWithoutStats` built the card's rule text by regex-deleting the stat phrases the card already shows as icon pairs from `itemDescription`, then patching punctuation with more regexes. The prose said "152 Production" (scaled to Standard) while the pair said 76 (unscaled), so the phrase never matched and "152 Production,." survived; the Spartan Hoplite's "20 base melee" never matched "20 melee" either, and the Athenian sheet said the Lyceum replaces the Laboratory while its own prose said the Library, so the heading strip missed and the whole "replaces" clause leaked into the card. The deletion approach cannot be made safe: every new wording is a new edge case. (2) **Fix.** The unique piece is structured data (`UNIQUE_PIECE` in `data.ts`: cost, effect numbers, required tech, rule sentences). The card prints the numbers as icon pairs (the price now scaled to the game speed like every other price) and its text is only `uniquePieceRules` (empty for a pure-stats piece, so no paragraph at all); the prose in `FACTION_INFO.unique`, the sheet heading (`uniquePieceHead`) and the unlock sentence (`uniquePieceGain`) are all composed from the same record. The `.replace(/: \d+ Production, /...)` in `TechDetail` was a no-op of the same family and is gone. (3) **Guard.** `ui/textArtifacts.test.tsx` and `.screens.test.tsx` check every unit, building, improvement and project in every faction, the tech blurbs, quotes and card effects, the faction sheets (data and rendered, all eight), goals, the Guide section by section, titles and milestones, treaty, tribute and hire texts and the diplomacy panel in its richest states for the patterns that only ever mean a join went wrong (`test/textArtifacts.ts`). The screen tour runs the same detector over each shot's visible text (`visibleTextBlocks`). Everything but the piece card was already clean when the guard first ran: this was one defect in one pattern, not many. Left alone: `unlockSentence` still parses core blurbs for plain buildings and units (`stripReplacement`, "Upgrade from X;" prefixes); the guard covers every output of it, but a core blurb in a new shape should get a structured field rather than another regex.
## 2026-10-11: Hard on 8.8: a train-and-disband loop, search-only war genes and deserting regiments, not the new units (branch claude/hard-88-regression)
(1) **Crown upkeep can exceed an empire's whole income, and the AI didn't count it when training troops.** The 8.4 Sparta seat paid 175% of its gross Gold in crown upkeep from T10 to T90: each Spearman took or kept the Strongest Army crown, the treasury emptied, the insolvency logic disbanded a unit and the Sparta opening trained another (first Settler T81). Fix: `troop_runway` charges a new troop's upkeep and the crown it would win (public title values) against a 10-turn runway. (2) **`sparta_search_only=1` silently kept plain Hard Sparta out of its own war genes**, so an earlier "all genes off" ablation measured zero for Hard. In Sparta's First Blood, Hard went home in every run and Search won every time. Check which seats a gene really reaches before reading a null result. (3) **Hard's goal-driven plan bypasses the faction-specific diplomacy paths:** a routed plan declares only the war it names and makes peace in every other, so `sparta_restless_target` never ran and 8.6 Sparta lost 26 regiments a game to desertion. (4) **Forcing unit mixes is a cheap, sharp experiment.** Every mix with a real mounted share lost 5–10 pp (more units and cities lost: a horseman garrison dies to Spearmen), the combat simulator's best response lost 5.5, and the AI's own mix was as good as anything tested. Equal-Production arena results don't transfer to full games, where cities, Walls and garrisons dominate. (5) **A tactics gene that never fires measures as exactly zero.** Identical per-map wins across 512 games mean the code path didn't run: Hard orders only about two unit-on-unit melee strikes in the field per game. (6) **Rule ablations as diagnostics:** turning off crown upkeep, decadence or both moved single maps a lot (8.4 Japan 31 → 3) but not the total (−0.8 to +2.7 pp), so the rubber bands weren't the regression. Result: Hard seat-only 27.7% → 31.8% (+4.1 pp [+0.8, +7.4]), opponents-only −4.9 pp. Details: `docs/sim-results/hard-vs-human/hard-88-regression.md`.

## 2026-10-11: The forecast now shows the aftermath on the advanced-to tile, says it assumes fortification, and player routes avoid known enemy reach (p881 UX fix, no rule change)
Built from the user's own save: `crates/core/examples/tape_views.rs` replays the tape on the core alone (every hash matches, about 90 s cold) and writes the PlayerView he saw before turns 29 and 30; `app/src/test/p881/situation.json` is that trimmed (40 KB) and drives `p881.test.ts` and the Playwright scene. **What turn 29 really showed him:** the core said 0% to kill (Archer fortified, 14 against 37) and nothing about Nara's walls two tiles from the Archer's tile, and Nara's two Spearmen were in fog (city known, `visible: false`), so a card limited to what he knew can only say "Nara's walls (about −42) can reach it. Exposed." plus "units you cannot see in Nara are not counted"; the "2 Spearmen (about −63 each, +18 vs mounted), Likely lost" line appears once the garrison is in view (the turn-30 view reproduces the brief's numbers exactly: 42, 63, 63 against 100 HP). A city out of sight within three tiles of the tile never reads Safe. **Fortification:** the 0% is correct for a stationary fortified Archer; it fires first (its fortify clears when a unit acts, `rules/battle.rs`), and without the +6 the same charge kills 39% of the time, so the card now says "Assumes the Archer stays fortified (+6 Defense). An Archer that fires first loses the +6", with the chance in the aftermath line ("If it destroys the Archer (39% if it fires first), ...") The aftermath line is also shown when only the unfortified case can win. **Routes:** the model that separates his first step (18,13) from the safe ones is one step per round: my unit on its j-th route tile at the start of round j+1 is struck when an enemy with at least j+1 moves can stand next to it after j steps (`enemyReach.ts`); the two Spearmen (2 moves) reach (18,13) in one step but not (19,12), matching the counterfactual replays. The player's route search re-plans around those tiles only when the arrival turn stays the same, and otherwise warns "Enemies can catch this unit leaving."; the AI's `previewPath` calls do not pass the option, so its routing is unchanged. Two things to remember: `previewRoute` is TypeScript (the core follows the submitted `MoveUnitPath`), so this changes only what the player's order contains; and the demo (mock) engine and the WASM core give the same card because the lines are computed from the PlayerView in `app/src/aftermath.ts`, not in either engine.
## 2026-10-11: Organic terrain: the hex "grid" over the art was the veil, not the grid (branch claude/terrain-organic)
(1) **The pale line along every hex edge is the unexplored veil.** `renderChunk` cuts each explored hex out of the veil at `HEX_SIZE - 0.6`, so a 1.2-unit sliver of veil stays between explored hexes. Blurred, it draws a soft pale line along every edge, and since the veil is drawn after the terrain art, that line ruled a continuous wood back into tiles. The 0.14 `grid` stroke is drawn under the art and is barely visible. Overlapping reversed hexes in one path cannot fix it: under nonzero (and even-odd) the overlap of two holes fills again. Organic mode instead removes the explored hexes with a `destination-out` union and draws the faint grid over the art. (2) **Generated ranges are mostly one hex wide** (seed scan of 400 Standard maps: largest ranges 10 to 19 tiles, almost no interior tiles), so a "massif" cannot come from interior depth alone: peaks grow along the spine, and a true massif needs multi-hex sprites. (3) **Per-region state must reach chunk invalidation:** a tile's organic look depends on far-away tiles (region size, edge distance, spine), so a per-tile signature (`organicSig`) goes into `TerrainCache.sig`, and exploring more of a wood repaints the tiles whose role changed. (4) **Shared edges need position-keyed noise:** nudging a hex outline's corners and edge points by a hash of their rounded world position gives neighbours the same wobbly edge with no gaps; offsets relative to the edge direction flip sign between the two hexes. Numbers and shots: `docs/design/terrain-art/README.md` (Round 3).
## 2026-10-11: Unlock art mocks: Playwright wipes `test-results/` at the start of every run
A design sheet spec run after a mock spec found no shots: Playwright clears its output directory (`test-results/`) when each `playwright test` starts, so shots written there by an earlier run vanish. Design shots that a later run reads go outside it (`app/unlock-art-shots/`). Also: injecting art and CSS into the real screen after it renders (`page.evaluate`, CSS `order` to move the unlock block) mocks a card layout without touching app code; the mock shots measure each card's footer against the scroll panel's bottom, which is how the 1000x600 budget (cards end at 545 of 550) was checked. The Luna image task painted 15 samples with real alpha in 13 minutes.

## 2026-10-11: The p881 horseman death was correct play by the rules: a kill advances into wall range, and a retreat's first step can be caught (no bug)
Replayed the user's 8.8.1 autosave (`~/LegatumRuns/bug-saves/p881-horseman-t31-0922.legatum`, full tape, every hash matched at engine 0f917ec4) with a probe example. **T29:** Japan's Archer shot the Sparta Horseman for 11 in the ranged phase (37 defence: the +18 mounted-vs-ranged bonus applies under fire too); firing cleared the Archer's +6 fortification, so the Horseman's charge (36 vs 8) hit for 104 and killed it, where the forecast (Archer fortified, 14) had said about 75 and 0% win. The kill advanced the Horseman into the Archer's tile, two tiles from walled Nara; the Sparta kill heal took it from 79 back to 100. **T30:** Nara's walls fired first (28 vs 19, 37 damage, 63 HP left). The player's retreat route (18,13 then 17,13...) put its first step beside the first step of a Spearman that the AI had ordered to attack it; a melee attack order follows its target, every unit takes one step per round, so in round 2 the Spearman (20 + 18 vs mounted = 38, against 16 wounded) struck it as it left: 84, dead. Every number matches the rules (class bonus direction, G21 parting hit without fortify, wound penalty, wall targeting, phase order). **Exact odds:** a full-HP Horseman dies to one wall shot plus one Spearman 93% of the time; to the Spearman alone, never. Counterfactual replays of T30 (`horse_whatif`): the same-length route north first (19,12, 18,12, ...), a straight retreat north and one east all survive at 63 HP, because their first step leaves both Spearmen's one-step reach; holding in place dies to both Spearmen. The death came from the wall shot plus a route whose first step went sideways along the enemy screen. **What it means:** a mounted unit that kills a screened ranged unit near a walled city ends its turn inside wall range next to the screen, and the attack forecast describes only the fight and this turn's city shot from where it starts, not next turn's exposure on the tile it advances into; the move preview and pathing do not weigh enemy reach either (ties break on hex order). Design options went to the lead (forecast "after the attack" line and danger-aware retreat routing first). Rules reference: added a pursuit/advance paragraph to section 4 and fixed section 9's stale "+10" class bonus (it is +18).

## 2026-10-11: War scenarios from the user's saves: exact replays work, and the AI's gap is committing to attacks (branch claude/war-scenarios)
(1) **Old saves replay exactly on their own engine.** Each save's archive (tape plus per-turn state hashes) replayed on a worktree at the save's `engine_commit`, with a small example dropped in (`tools/scenarios/war_extract.rs`), matched every recorded hash in all six games, so a scenario can start from the real position at any turn. Two traps: builds of several old commits in **one** `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` silently reused the first commit's example binary (same package and example name; 79 hash mismatches looked like a wrong engine), so use one target dir per commit; and zsh mangles `${c}:path` (`:c`), use bash. (2) **Loading an old state into today's engine** needs only the save migrations plus two fixes: Pacts in pre-2026-10-03 diplomacy become Peace (`war_build::migrate_legacy_json`), and decadence needs a 30-turn Power window the old engines never kept, so it is filled with today's yields (a reconstruction; a dominant empire starts at up to 37% decadence). (3) **Result (`docs/sim-results/hard-vs-human/war-scenarios/`).** On seven campaigns the user won, Hard meets the goal on 27% average and Hard + Search 42%, and both only on the two defences (95-98%). On the five attacks Hard is 0-1%: it never starts the first Spartan war (marches the army home), proposes Peace to Japan while winning, and never takes walled Keystone; Search takes Germany in 100% of runs but turns down the Keystone war (2 of 64 declarations against Hard's 64). The full-game benchmark's 27.7% had hidden that the gap is offence and commitment; the user's 8.8.1 Sparta game says the same (the AIs didn't treat him as a threat once he started winning battles). (4) **Bug hunting by `validate_state` overreports**: it flags units standing in foreign land at Peace, which the rules allow (land closes under a unit; a stranded unit may walk out). The suite now flags only a step from open land into land closed at both ends of the turn; no impossible states or panics remained in 896 runs. (5) The suite is cheap: 448 runs take about 7 s on a 64-vCPU spot server, so the cost is the 5-minute box build, not the sims.
## 2026-10-11: The faction picker's hover preview and Play button were the wrong model; the click is the selection (branch claude/picker-click-selects)
User: "I want the items on the left to change when I click on them... the click is the selection, and then you close the window when you're done. No hover-over preview." The 2026-10-06 design (hover previews after a 150 ms intent delay, click picks and closes, a "Play as" button for the keyboard) had two parallel states, a previewed faction and a chosen one, and every fix piled on a timer (`HOVER_INTENT_MS`, touch first-tap-previews, `previewTap` read in `pointerdown`). Removing the preview removed all of it: `FactionPicker` has no `active` state, it is controlled and the sheet shows `value`. Click and arrows call `onChange` straight away, and Return, Esc and Close only close. Gotchas: Safari/WebKit does not focus a button on click, so the tile focuses itself on click (arrows need a starting point); tests that used `hover()` to view a sheet now `click()`, and the screen tour must click the default faction back before closing, since the tour's later shots assume it; unit tests need a stateful harness because the picker is controlled.

## 2026-10-11: Master's full Chromium `game` run had 11 failing specs: mostly stale text, one real feedback bug (branch claude/fix-master-e2e)
The release check always ran every spec; branch verification ran four until the box took over, so a day of landings left drift nobody saw. (1) **Stale after the historical rename and eras:** diplomacy and war-interstitial expected the old mottos (the motto now is the quote on offer cards and the declaration screen), diplomacy's coalition text names the fixture's player name (`Celts`, not `Celts`), lobby-seats still picked "Mongols", "Sparta" and "Byzantium" by old names, game.spec expected `/8` where the solo VP target is now `/10`. A blanket rename missed strings built from fixtures and from regexes. (2) **Real bug:** `TechPanel` called `feel.select()` and then `editQueue` (which commits with its own `feel.commit()`); the 80 ms haptic/sound debounce swallowed the commit, so choosing "Research now" or "Queue" gave a select tick instead of the commit feedback. Fixed by dropping the extra `select`. (3) **Environment:** `staging.spec` spawned `target/debug/legatum-server`, which the box does not build; it now uses the release server every other spec uses. The territory label at 1000x600 fills the 282 px panel and Linux text rendering wraps it (30 px against 15 px on macOS, same Libre Franklin), so the bound is looser off macOS. (4) **Load flake:** clarity's "tooltips wait" check waited 150 ms against a 450 ms show delay and failed once with six workers plus a box run; the wait is now 40 ms.

## 2026-10-10: Byzantium, bought peace (tribute) replaces the salvager (branch claude/bought-peace)
(1) **Slot, not boundary.** Tribute resolves at the start of resolution next to surprise wars (`diplomacy/tribute.rs::pay_tributes`, called before `declare_surprise_wars`), not at the diplomacy boundary: peace has to exist before ranged fire and movement or "I paid and still lost the city" happens. The boundary (`resolve_boundary`) must skip `PayTribute` (it still validates it against the untouched planning state), and everything that keyed on the pair at the boundary sees the relation `version` bumped, so pending proposals lapse as "Relationship changed" with no new code. (2) **Reuse the peace guarantee for the enforced 10 turns.** `Relation::guarantee_until = turn + 1 + 10` already blocks `DeclareWar` for both sides (`check_guarantee`) and already drives the UI sentence "Peace guaranteed through turn N"; a new field on `Relation` would have touched ~25 struct literals in tests and frozen proxy AIs. The tribute-specific bookkeeping (escalation count, the peace's end, so the UI can say who bought it) lives in `DiplomacyState::tributes`, skipped when empty so old pins keep their hashes. (3) **Gold has to be budgeted twice.** `diplomacy::validate_orders` sums the tributes of one turn (several wars) and `rules::validate_orders` subtracts the tribute from the Gold that purchases and upgrades validate against, because tribute is paid before city orders resolve. (4) **Deleting a faction identity is mostly deleting reads.** `economy::denied_near` still serves barbarian-held tiles, so the `squatted` parameter name survives across the economy, yields cache and projects (it now means "barbarian-held"); renaming would only have made the era-overhaul merge harder. Removing `data::city_science_accrues` made `tech_card_effects_for` per-faction pointless: `techCardEffects` in `rules.generated.json` is now per tech. Byzantium ruin doubling, Peace passage and the Relay Uplink Archive exception went with the salvager. (5) The price reads the payee's full army (a Production total), not the viewer's fog: Strongest Army is already public and a fogged price could not be quoted in the order's tooltip.
## 2026-10-10: Overreach v3 (decadence): a blanket cut compresses Power where a Gold tax could not (branch claude/overreach-v3)
(1) **Result.** One rate `45 L / (L + 45)` percent (L = Power lead over the median, trailing 30 turns, no cap) cuts every city's Production, Gold and Science and slows growth by the same share; pooled 4p + 8p, 512 Hard + Search games per setting: top Power over median 56% (off) and 54% (v2 Gold surcharge) fall to 33% at T100 and 62%/55% to 32% at T150; T100 leader wins 54.3% (v2 60.5%, off 68.2%); lead changes 11.2 (8.9, 8.2); units lost to unpaid upkeep 1.6 a game (v2 4.9, off 1.8); game +28 turns (v2 +13). Medium R 30 matches v2 on the race, Gentle R 20 is inside the noise of off. A Gold surcharge added on top does nothing measurable. (2) **Rounding decides where a cut can live.** Yields are integers, and a city's spare Food is 1-6 points, so an 11% cut of the Food surplus rounds to zero most of the time and to 20% when it does not; applying the share to the growth cost instead (`cost x 100 / (100 - N)`) is exact, cannot starve a city and keeps the same "N% slower" promise. Production, Gold and Science are large enough to round to nearest. (3) **Penalties from a 30-turn lead need a full window.** With a partial history the first turns of a two-empire test (or a real opening) already read as a large lead and cut the opening; decadence starts only once an empire has a full window (`scoring::decadence_percent_now`), which also kept every opening-pacing pin. (4) **Cache and tag traps.** `yields_cache::CityKey` must include the owner's decadence or cached yields go stale across the turn boundary; any bankruptcy of an empire with decadence is tagged as an overreach desertion, so the sweep's honest "units lost" metric is `upkeep_disbands` (it includes the overreach ones). (5) **Cloud quota is shared.** Three 64-vCPU batches plus other agents' batches hit `MaxSpotInstanceCountExceeded`; run-batch just failed those cells (a sweep driver continues to the next), so check for `FAILED` lines and rerun them one at a time. Data: `docs/sim-results/vp-v2/README.md` ("Overreach v3: decadence").

## 2026-10-10: Sakoku (Japan's closed-country vow): "no foreign unit at Peace" was already the rule, and four traps (branch claude/sakoku)
(1) **Peace already closes every border.** `passage_allowed` lets a foreign unit into land at Peace only through exceptions (Alliance, Mongol Scouts/civilians, every Byzantine unit, a Courier Hall's open road). The vow therefore removes those exceptions for a closed land owner and nothing else; its own team (teams are not Alliances) still crosses. (2) **`visibility::unit_visible_to` is concealment only** ("concealment never grants sight"), not "is in sight": the first `SakokuView.intruders` listed fogged units. Use `visible_tiles(team)` plus it; the rule itself (`sakoku_intruders`) counts every unit, hidden hunters too, like any public fact. (3) **An Alliance is between teams, so the vow binds the team**: a teammate could sign an Alliance on the turn Japan closes (both orders validated against the open country), so `resolve_boundary` re-checks every signature against the post-action state, and a close may not ride with a declaration or an acceptance in one order list (`diplomacy::vow_conflict`). (4) **A goal is a rule, not a faction** (`goals::GoalRule`, `Player::goal_rule` override): the Swarm stays whole for the Aztecs, but every fixture that used Japan as "a permanent goal that is done" (VP hold fixtures, alliance/diplomacy parity tests, Swarm UI tests) now sets the Swarm rule by hand, because a Live goal is recomputed every turn end and drops a hand-set flag. The barbarian `Player` record borrows `Faction::Japan`, so `goals::rule` special-cases barbarians. Process: a `node_modules` symlink is not matched by the `node_modules/` ignore rule; worktrees `cp -Rc` it. Sims and balance: none yet (user: functionality only); the AI's close/reopen numbers (`goal_sakoku.rs`) are assumptions to calibrate once the AI is competent.
## 2026-10-10: Era overhaul A1 (eras, strict gate, boosts): how the pacing came out, and what surprised us (branch claude/era-overhaul-core)
- **Pacing is set by Science income, not by the early costs.** Cutting Ancient and Classical costs by 15% moved the winners' Classical entry by only 3 to 4 turns, and the late eras moved even less, because Science grows with cities and Libraries and the cheap early techs are a small share of the total. Each pass moved era entry by about a quarter of the cost change; it took five passes (and the AI's boost and threat ordering) to land. Per-era gaps are tuned independently (an era's gap responds to its own cost), then rechecked, since earlier changes shift later eras.
- **Result (cloud, Standard, Hard+Search, 256 games per lobby; `docs/sim-results/era-overhaul/`).** Median winner enters Classical/Medieval/Renaissance/Industrial/Modern/Future at T32/63/97/129/161/191 in 4-player games (median length 178) and T29/63/100/134/169/203 in 8-player games (median 204). Era lengths for winners are 31 to 34 turns, a little under the 35-turn floor: the floor and the proposal's fractions (15/30/47/63/80%) cannot both hold in a game this short, so we held the fractions. 72% (4p) and 79% (8p) of winners reach Modern; the winner's era minus the median non-winner's is 0 at the median (mean +0.4). Zero stalls.
- **The "idle research" number lies unless the era-entry turn is excluded.** An empire has nothing legal to queue on the turn it enters an era (the gate forbids queueing next-era techs), so the first telemetry showed about 1,000 idle turns per 64 games. The real stalls were Easy seats lapsing their research order; the plan is now exempt when empty.
- **A tech called "Ballistics" existed in both trees.** The old Trooper tech and the new Industrial tech share a display name, so old saves' "Ballistics" would have loaded as the wrong tech. The new one's identifier is `Cannonry` (shown as "Ballistics"); the old name maps to Feudalism. Ambiguous legacy names need distinct identifiers, not a clever mapping.
- **Old saves.** Legacy tech names deserialize through `TechId::from_name` (deleted techs map to the nearest new one; building names through serde aliases), an ungated research plan is repaired by `normalize_research` the first turn it resolves, and the archive hash check applies only to archives of current rules (a migrated game can't match an archive made under old rules). The wasm `legacy_saves` regression stays green.
- **The Arcology arm in a frozen proxy AI swallowed every later match arm** (Autoworks, Embassy, Aid Station were unreachable); deleting Arcology fixed it. Deleting content can change frozen baselines: pins moved and were re-pinned.
- **One technology completes per turn.** `ResearchView.schedule` must add a turn per queued tech even when stored Science covers several; the first forecast showed two techs arriving on one turn.
- **VP v2 on seven eras (merged 2026-10-10).** The solo target `min(eras + 4, 10)` is 10 (team 20) with seven eras and stalled nothing (512 Hard + Search games, longest 420 turns, median 220: 40 turns longer than without v2). Both trees had an `EraEntered` event and an era index (VP: 1-based, entered on the first tech of an era; eras: 0-based, entered when the previous era's last tech lands); they are now one event and one rule (1-based public numbers, 0-based inside `eras`). **Era objectives stay open forever, so a threshold is really "reach X at any time"** and every non-Fight measure has the same all-game peak distribution in every era: the first pools (set by hand) were met by 96% of Classical entrants. Set thresholds by percentile of entrants' recorded peaks and check any-of-three offline before spending a cloud run; the offline model predicted the measured scoring rates within 4 points. A `Techs { n }` objective is useless once eras are gated (everyone has the era's techs eventually), so the new pools have none.
- **Units are unchanged in A1**, so eight techs (Metallurgy, Rifling, Military Science, Ballistics, Replaceable Parts, Combustion, Steel, Flight) and three Future ones only announce what arrives in A2 (`CardEffect::Soon`). Playtests between A1 and A2 will feel thin after Renaissance.
## 2026-10-10: Overreach v2 (lead surcharge): a Gold tax moves the VP race but cannot flatten Power (branch claude/overreach-v2)
(1) **Result.** A share of Gold income `100 L / (L + k)` (L = Power over the median empire, trailing 30-turn average of total yields; no cap) at k 45 ("Strong", landed): T100 leader wins 64.8% to 57.6%, T50 56.6% to 53.6%, lead changes 8.2 to 9.8, last change at 91% of the game (83%), median game +21 turns, 5.0 units deserting per game (top-Power empire 3.5, from 0.5). Gentle (k 180) and Medium (k 90, 60% lead pays 40%) are inside the noise. 512 games per cell, Hard + Search, pooled 4p + 8p; `docs/sim-results/vp-v2/README.md`. (2) **Power does not compress** (top over median at T100 53.0% off, 52.9% Strong): the tax is on Gold, Power is mostly Food and Production, and the AI pays in units, so the race tightens through crowns and armies rather than yields. If the pack must be closer in yields, the next levers are the user's fallbacks (rising building costs, weaker units for overreachers). (3) **The faction mix moves:** Switzerland 23.7% to 9.3% and Sparta 9.4% to 3.9% of wins, Byzantium 4.7% to 12.7%; the Gold factions pay the most. (4) **Power history must be recorded after every empire has paid** (`process_cities`), or the second empire's bill sees the first's new history and turn order leaks in. (5) Record Power under VP v2 even with the surcharge off, or the before/after cells have no Power to compare (the first off cells had to be rerun). (6) The cloud spot quota (`MaxSpotInstanceCountExceeded`) is shared with other agents: launch cells in waves and retry; a 256-game cell is 0.1 to 0.3 server-hours, so the whole sweep (16 cells) cost about $3.

## 2026-10-10: VP v2 skateboard (crowns, overreach, era objectives): what the sims said
(1) **A 10 VP target is unreachable on four eras.** Permanent ceiling = 3 era points + 2 goal + 2 Great Work = 7, so target 10 needs all three crowns at once: 75% of 4-empire Hard games hit the 450-turn cap, 22% at target 9, 0.4% at 8 (game length 217 vs 194 before). `solo_vp_target` is ceiling + 1 capped at 10, derived from `data::era_count()`, so seven eras give 10 without a change. (2) **Era objectives need lobby scaling and a peaks probe.** Natural reach is far higher at 4 empires than 8 (empires are twice as big), and with eras open forever every threshold is met eventually, so "met by game end" is the right calibration. `vp_race.rs` records per seat and era the peak of every objective measure; pick thresholds at the percentile you want (about 25-30% per objective gives about 60% for any-of-three), scale count thresholds 12% per missing empire. Result with Hard + Search: 58-67% / 50-54% / 34-39% of entrants score eras 2 / 3 / 4. (3) **Overreach shapes crowns but not early predictability.** Gold rate and the x1/x2/x3 escalation move the leader's overreach from 13% to 40% of its city Gold and halve the all-three-crowns share, but T50/T100 leader-wins (53-56% / 61-66%) stay within the 3-point noise; early leads come from the opening. Strong costs the AI 5.8 units a game in unpaid upkeep. (4) **Crown upkeep bankrupts empty test economies.** A crown at T7 with 0 Gold disbands a unit through the existing bankruptcy rule, so tests with hand-built states and old saves/archives set `legacy_vp` (`legatum-ai` fixtures default to it; `ViewBuilder`). Replays of pre-v2 archives pin new hashes after setting it. (5) Era objectives are drawn from `Rng::derive(seed, [era, OBJECTIVES, slot])`, so they cost no saved state; fights since the era was entered come from a per-empire `FightStats` snapshot taken at era entry. Data: `docs/sim-results/vp-v2/README.md`.

## 2026-10-10: VP v2 UI: the demo mock stays on legacy rules, and the race panel has one line to spare (branch claude/vp-v2-ui)
(1) VP v2 is the engine default, but the TS demo mock (`app/src/mock/`) still plays the five-title rules, so every native game built from a mock state must carry `rule_experiments.legacy_vp` or the `*.wasm.test.ts` parity tests diverge (138 failures on the first run: overreach in income, three crowns, 20-VP pair targets). `mock/setup.ts` sets the flag in `createState` and `makeStandardSetup`; tests that replace `setup.rule_experiments` must spread the old object. The TS types keep the full `Title`/`Milestone` unions for the mock; the UI lists only `CROWNS` and the Great Work (`vpModel.ts`). (2) The race panel at 1000x600 shares its column with This Turn: a two-line crowns strip pushed the rival's row under "1 more". The strip is one line (three crown chips and "One more crown: +4 Gold/turn"), what a holder pays is a "−N Gold" chip in the holder's own row, and `reserveRace` now keeps the leader, the runner-up and your row whole. (3) A `data-source` token can now exist twice (a crown chip and its holder's row token): arrow-key flipping prefers the same-owner match, else the first in DOM order. (4) `withV2(view, options)` (`app/src/test/vpV2Fixture.ts`) stages v2 numbers on any view for unit tests and the Playwright specs (`vp-v2.spec.ts`, `vpStage.ts`).

## 2026-10-10: Any Playwright run wipes `app/test-results/`, including the screen tour's shots
The screen-tour spec writes to `app/test-results/screen-tour/`, which is Playwright's output directory, so starting any other Playwright run (even a different spec) deletes the tour. Evidence: an art-map run right after a tour left no tour shots. Copy the tour to the scratchpad straight after it finishes, and run extra shot specs with their own output (`ART_MAP_SHOTS=<dir>` for `art-map.spec.ts`) only after copying. A full two-size tour is 118 tests and takes about 5 minutes with `--workers=4` (all 118 pass on a fresh build; serial takes 17 minutes). The 2026-10-10 art audit reported 6 failures in the city-under-attack and Byzantium-Heir groups: not a regression. The main checkout's `app/dist` (Oct 7) and `target/release/legatum-server` (Oct 7) predated those features, and the e2e fixture serves `app/dist` through the release server, so the tests were running an old UI. Before trusting a tour, rebuild all three: `scripts/build-wasm.sh`, `(cd app && npm run build)`, `cargo build --release -p legatum-server`; a failing group whose feature landed after the build date means a stale build, not a bug.

## 2026-10-07: Roadworks (player-built roads) share the Hall's network, which needed two separate flags (branch claude/player-roads)
Roadworks (any faction, Field Logistics, 70 Production, +1 Gold flat as the building's own yield, links its city to every city within 8 hexes) reuse `roads::connect` rooted at the Roadworks city, so a Roadworks cluster and the Hall's network merge by walking over each other's road tiles for free. Three things had to change from the Hall-only version. (1) `Map::road_hub` was doubling as "the map has roads" and as "the Hall's roads were announced"; a Roadworks road laid first would have suppressed the Hall's `RoadsLaid` event. It is now only the Hall's root, and a separate `Map::roaded` flag drives the scaled A* (`has_roads`). (2) "Is this city already linked" was "its tile is a road tile", which a lone Roadworks road would satisfy without being on the Hall's network; it is now "it is in the hub's network" (BFS over links from the hub). (3) A road tile of another network used to be an uncrossable terminal in the layout search; it is now walked over at zero cost and linked in, otherwise a Roadworks road across a peninsula would have blocked the Hall's layout. AI: 23 of 24 four-empire Standard games build at least one Roadworks, but the warlike bonus (+80, +20 per enemy city in range for Sparta and Mongols) did not show in the share of cities with one (Sparta 12%, Mongols 16%, Germany 24%, Byzantium 8%): it competes with every other build in the same city, and the Sparta pay +50% on non-war buildings. Left as is; tune with the AI agent if wanted.
## 2026-10-07: A policy trained for turn-60 survival loses full games even when gated to turn 60 (branch claude/es-port)
The ES rush-defence policy (A1) lifted turn-60 capital held from 58.6% to 90.2% (512 held-out seeds, Hard vs Proxy v7 Mongols). Ported ungated it collapsed full-game results (duel matrix vs Proxy v7, 64 games: 5 Hard wins against 20 without it). Gating it to its training horizon (60 turns at Standard, 30 at Quick) looked fine at 64 and 128 games (18/64 and 35/128 against 20/64 and 38/128), but 2048 games on one cloud server each (seeds 9100001+, about $0.03 in all) showed Hard's score 28.2% with the gene on against 34.0% off, paired -5.8 points [-7.9, -3.7]: 128 games could not see a 6 point loss (noise there is about 4 wins, i.e. 3 points per 100 games). A second gate (enemy within 4 of a city in the last 5 turns) kept no more wins and cut turn-60 capital held to 82.2%. So the gene `learned_defence` ships OFF; the code, the 90-turn and 60-turn equivalence tests (`crates/sim/tests/learned_defence_equivalence.rs`, identical orders and state hash on 32 seeds) and the gate stay. Lessons: size a full-game check at 2048 games (the cloud runs 2048 duel-matrix games in about 3 minutes), and score a learned overlay on the whole game, not only the horizon it was trained for. The sightings window is saved in `OpponentMemory`: without it the save/load exactness tests fail.

## 2026-10-07: Courier Hall roads: a per-tile cost rule, a scaled A* only on maps with roads, and a layout that grows a shortest-path tree (branch claude/hall-roads)
(1) **Rule choice.** "Entering a road tile costs 1 whatever the terrain; a unit that starts its turn on a road has +1 move" needs no per-unit or per-turn state (a "first road step is free" rule needs a flag in `Unit`, the view, the courier-crossing sums and `path_turns`), and the cost stays a function of the entered tile, so the ~70 `move_cost(h)` call sites (engine, `movement_path`, the AI's `Ctx` table, forecasts, the client preview, reach and movement lenses) only had to learn `Map::move_cost`/`TileView::move_cost`. Civ 6's "road to road only" would have needed an edge cost everywhere. The +1 is also given to a unit that leaves the road at once (a unit starting in any connected city); that was accepted as simpler than tracking road use. (2) **Equal-cost ties.** Road cost 1 equals plain flat cost 1, so A* would walk beside a road as often as on it and lose the next turn's +1. `find_path_by_step` scales costs by 256 and takes 1 off each road tile, but only when `Map::has_roads()`; every map without roads searches exactly as before, which kept all old pins byte-identical (16 duel seeds: the 12 games with no Hall have identical final hashes; the 4 with roads differ). The scale must exceed any path length, and the heuristic scales with it (`distance * 255`). (3) **View.** `TileView.road` is public and always current on every explored tile, not remembered: a remembered tile would show a stale cost for a road laid since, so the preview and the engine could disagree. A view's terrain `Map` flags `has_roads` from its tiles. (4) **Layout.** Cities join nearest-first, each by Dijkstra to the road tile minimising new-road cost plus the trip along the existing network to the Hall (new tile 64 x terrain cost, network step 48, a small penalty per 0.4 tile off the straight line city-to-Hall). The network step being cheaper than a new tile is what makes branches join trunks instead of running beside them; the straight-line penalty is what turns the many equal hex geodesics into one straight road instead of an L or a zig-zag. Mountains and water are impassable, so "no detours through mountains when a pass exists" holds without code. Checked on 2, 4 and 8 empires, Small/Standard/Large, far hubs and cities founded later (`crates/sim/examples/roads_debug.rs` renders SVG; the only unlinked adjacent road pairs are the triangle at each Y-junction). (5) **Mock parity** needed the exact same integer arithmetic (no floats; `isqrt` by correction), heap order `(cost, index)` and the same pending sort; the three parity tests passed first time because every tie-break was written down in the Rust doc comment first. (6) **Perf:** `sim_bench` (one thread, `/usr/bin/time -l`): Quick duel 16 games 30.90 -> 31.07 G instructions (+0.55%, same 1488 turns), Small duel 4 games 14.14 -> 14.24 G (+0.74%); the eight-player pair changed length (474 -> 441 turns, 62.3 -> 52.4 G) because a Hall changes those games.
## 2026-10-07: A selected city's panel painted one frame too short (stale `--lens-height`), which failed the WebKit screen tour at 1000x600
"Turn 1: the first city panel shows its build choice at scroll 0" failed every time in WebKit (Linux container) at 1000x600 and passed at 1440x900 and in Chromium. Not a click or focus bug and not WebKit layout: the panel was open, but it was measured before any frame had been painted (its `slideUp` animation was still at `currentTime` 0, so the failure screenshot showed no panel). The dock's height is `bottom: calc(var(--lens-height) + ...)`, and `LensSwitcher` publishes `--lens-height` from a `ResizeObserver`, which only fires after the next frame. Selecting a city collapses the lens to its one-line toggle (`collapsed`), so for that first frame the dock still reserved the expanded chooser's height: the panel was 393 px tall instead of 456 at 1000x600 and its first build card ended 42 px below the panel's bottom. Headless Linux WebKit under load delivers that next frame late (more than a second at load 80), so the test read the stale layout; Chromium delivered it fast enough to pass. In the Mac app the same stale frame was a one-frame jump of the panel on every city selection. Fix: `LensSwitcher` re-measures `--lens-height` in a layout effect keyed on `compact`, in the commit that collapses the lens, so the first frame is already right (the observer stays for later size changes). The test is unchanged. Lesson: a ResizeObserver-published CSS variable lags one frame; anything that must be right on first paint needs a synchronous measure on the state change that causes it, and e2e layout asserts in WebKit run before that frame arrives.

## 2026-10-07: The Celts's wild camps are exempt from Celts land attrition
Wild camps raised by Call the Wild stand in the raiser's own territory, and the Celts land attrition (12 HP a turn, 10 HP floor) applied to barbarians like any intruder, so a rite camp's guard, scout and raiders sat at the 10 HP floor: trivial to clear, and their raids toothless. Lead decision: the wild belongs to the land. A unit that is the guard, scout or a raider of any camp with `Camp::rite = Some(_)` is skipped by the intruders block in `resolve_turn` (`BarbarianState::is_wild_unit`; mock: `isWildUnit` in `mock/barbarians.ts`, used in `resolve.ts::run`). Natural-camp barbarians and rival units still take it. The forecast (`forecast_attack_mode`), the mock forecast and the AI's attrition estimates read only the viewer's own units, never barbarians, so none needed changing. Tests: `actions_tests.rs::a_rite_camps_units_are_exempt_from_celts_attrition_but_other_intruders_are_not`, `app/src/mock/wildAttrition.test.ts`, and a core/mock parity case in `actions.wasm.test.ts`. Celts games change (fixture pins were repinned with this change).

## 2026-10-07: A public fact that feeds passage must be in BOTH `PlayerView` constructors (goals increment 5, the Courier Hall)
The Hall opens its holder's land to every empire, so `PlayerView::passage_allowed` needs the holders: they ride the public `PlayerView.courier_halls`. `view.rs` builds a `PlayerView` in two places, the full `player_view_cached` and the light `order_check_view` that move validation uses; the first draft filled only the first, the unit tests (engine versus full view) passed, and only an end-to-end "a Soldier ordered into the holder's land at Peace" test failed with "destination cannot be reached over land with the current borders". `tests/mongols_passage.rs` now has that test; any future rule that opens or closes borders needs the same engine-versus-order-validation check. Also: a Held goal's identity must survive the item changing hands, so the Hall is tracked by `Player::hall_city` (the city it was built in, valid while the city still stands with the Hall in it) rather than found by building kind (two Mongols empires exist in tests); a Celts capture razes the city and so destroys the Hall, which is exactly when it may be rebuilt. The mock has two building-completion paths (`resolve.ts` production and `cityOrders.ts` purchase), and the bought-Hall parity test found the second one missing `hallCity`: completion-time state must be set in both. Removing the Markets goal also removed the settler-site "spaced network" ×3 / ÷3 score in `settlement.rs`, which existed only to serve it.

## 2026-10-06: "Winner at or above the target" no longer means a VP win; the sim classifies by a completed hold (branch claude/hold-sim)
With the hold-to-win rule (`GameSpeed::vp_hold_turns`: 10 Standard, 5 Quick), a team can stand at its VP target at the turn limit and win on highest VP without having completed its hold. The old classifiers (`stats::classify`, `wasm/src/history.rs` outcome "vp") used `team_vp >= team_target` and would have called that a VP win. Both now read `state.vp_holds[winner] >= vp_hold_turns` (`stats::winner_hold_complete`). Per-game hold telemetry (`telemetry.victory_holds`: every hold with start, end and result, plus `hold_win` for VP wins) and the report summary (`summary.victory_holds`, `length_by_outcome`, `by_faction[*].share_of_wins`) are in `crates/sim/src/hold_telemetry.rs`; `tools/cloud/merge.py` recomputes them over a merged batch with `tools/cloud/hold_summary.py`. Two measures to keep apart: first reach to win (includes broken holds) varies, but final hold start to win is always `turns_needed - 1`, so it only checks consistency. Quick 2-player Hard smoke (12 games): 17 holds started, 5 broken, all 12 games ended by a completed hold with no stalls.

## 2026-10-06: The victory-hold alarm rides the existing alarm screen, and public lines must have no players (branch claude/hold-ui)
The hold-to-win rule (core: `VictoryHoldStarted`/`VictoryHoldBroken`) needed an alarm for every player. It is an `Alarm` kind (`victoryHold`, `celebrate/alarms.ts`) on the war-declared screen's framework, ranked right after a war on you, with `HoldDetail.tsx` for the body (countdown pips, rule, no suggested route) and `victoryHold.ts` as the one place the rule is worded. Three things to remember. (1) A report line with `players: ['someone else']` is hidden by the default Mine/Team filter, so a public announcement about a rival (hold started or broken) must leave `players` empty ("global announcements always show"); the first e2e run found the broken-hold line missing for exactly this reason. (2) Reopening a card from a past report line has no pending alarm to lean on: the line carries `reopenHold` (the event's numbers), the store has `holdReopen`, and `AlarmScreen` rebuilds the alarm against the live view (count 0 means the hold has ended). Memoise that rebuilt alarm, or the screen's "armed" effect restarts on every render. (3) Staged fixtures must patch `standings[].victory_hold` as well as the report, or the card and the race panel disagree (`e2e/holdFixture.ts`). GameOver words a hold win from the `GameWon` reason ("X held 10 of 10 VP for 10 turns"): a team over its target at the turn limit has not finished holding, so the old "reached N VP" inference was wrong.
## 2026-10-06: Amounts in the display italic read as "1 1", and the sans that fixes them is wider (branch claude/p831-fixes)
8.3.1 review blocker B1: `.amt-n` inherited Cormorant italic old-style figures, so a 10px "+2" and "11 stored" were illegible. `Amounts.css` now sets the number upright sans, weight 600, lining proportional figures (tabular only where columns align: `.vp-num`, the top bar). The sans digits are wider: the city panel's "62 ◎ (+2 ◎/turn) to spend · grey prices need more Gold" wrapped at 1000x600 and pushed the first build card below the fold (the "Turn 1" tour test caught it), so the number-to-icon gap is 0.15em and the icon 1em. The icon gap now sits on `.amt-n`, not a flex `gap`, so the screen-reader word never takes one. Other fixes: an `.relation-badge.alliance` `background:` shorthand reset `background-clip` and bled 5px (set `background-color` only); the sticky desertion warning is an opaque band (padding, panel colour) so race rows cannot show through margins; a coalition Alliance offer's headline carries the fee note and the Accept chip says "Free to sign"; the hover-intent lobby test runs on Playwright's paused clock because the real-time sweep flaked under load.
## 2026-10-07: Gilt means "yours" only, a shared `target` symlink wiped a worktree, and a node_modules symlink can empty the shared one (branch claude/vp-holdings)
(1) The 8.3.1b review found a rival's card read as "Yours": the amber seat colour sits 7 degrees from the gilt (`#8f6f1c`) and a rival's double frame copied yours. Gilt and the double frame are now yours alone; a rival gets one 1.5 px rule in its ink, and `PLAYER_INKS` keeps every hue 20 degrees or more from gilt (amber moved to orange; `theme.test.ts` guards it). (2) Milestone circles must name an owner: each empty socket shows its empire's crest, faded, holders first; a shared card names both holders. (3) Names beat tokens: the name column is `fit-content(150px)` and the tokens wrap; the race is 352 px on a 1360 px window. (4) Process: a `target` symlink made to share a build dir was committed by `git add -A` (the `target/` ignore rule matches directories, not links) and its fast-forward replaced the real `target/` dir; use `.git/info/exclude` and `git add -u`. On 2026-10-06 the shared `app/node_modules` was emptied while worktrees symlinked to it: give each worktree its own copy (`cp -Rc`). (5) Under load (load average 58) vitest's 5 s timeout failed 60 tests that passed at `--testTimeout=60000`; a red gate on a loaded machine needs a rerun with longer timeouts before it means anything.

## 2026-10-06: The VP race as holdings: a text badge costs the name, steel needs an ink shade, and the old row tests were really layout tests (branch claude/vp-holdings)
Round-2 C replaced the race rows' bar and tooltip with per-empire tokens. Three non-obvious things. (1) The relation badge ("Peace ≤80") plus seven tokens left the empire name 30 px ("C…"), and a second line for it made rows 43 px, so only 4 of 8 rows fit at 1440x900. The relation is now a pin on the roundel (red cross, green check, gilt star; the text is in the tooltip, the button's name and `data-relation`) and rows stay one line (about 29 px). (2) The Celts steel `#d8dde3` is 1.1:1 on parchment (lime 1.4, jade 1.5, amber 1.7): as a ring or glyph it vanished. `playerInk()` in `theme.ts` gives every seat a shade at 3:1 or better that keeps its hue; `CrestMedallion`, `Crest` and `PortraitRoundel` use it, fills keep the seat colour. (3) Tests that count `.vp-row .relation-badge` or open `.tip-info` were measuring layout and one-line facts, so they moved with the design: `.vhc-rel[data-relation]`, tokens, and the card. At 1440x900 with the minimap, the title-change chips and the Nobody row, the race now shows whole rows plus "N more" instead of all eight; a Nobody row with nine open tokens must stay one line (its VP-open count sits under the name), which is what keeps the early game close to the old height.

## 2026-10-06: Sticky stacks, pinned badges and the tablet "explanation covered" failure (branch claude/p83-ui-fixes)
Three 8.3b review findings had one cause: something pinned or sticky that the surrounding layout did not account for. (1) The expanded race's auto-scroll (`VpTrack.tsx`) subtracted only the sticky `.ph` head, but the Sparta `.vp-restless` warning is sticky too (top 48 px), so the first title landed underneath it. It now subtracts every sticky child of the panel above the target (computed `top` + height + margin), and `screen-tour` asserts the first `.title-head` starts below both. (2) The queue order badge is absolute at the card's bottom-left; at 20 px (26 px for "x2") it reached past the 18 px icon column into the text column ("x2No upkeep"). It is now 18 px (10 px font) and `queue-click.spec.ts` asserts a 3 px gap to every `.prod-effect/.prod-cost/.prod-turns/.prod-upkeep` box. (3) `.vp-label .relation-badge` relies on a transparent 5 px border as a hit area; `.relation-badge.alliance` in `DiplomacyPanel.css` repainted that border, so the Alliance badge needs `border-color: transparent` in the race rule. Also: the treaty emblem (`.te`) was `color: var(--accent)` (red), so an Alliance read as War; red is for `.te.bad` only. The tablet failure `touch-layout.spec.ts:76 "explanation covered"` was NOT the queue badge (nothing is queued in that test): the city panel's sticky `.cp-head` (tall on a tablet, with the capture-bounty line) covered the top of the row that `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded` had "scrolled into view". The test now scrolls the row clear of the head; Playwright's scrolling knows nothing about sticky heads, so any test that taps rows in a panel with a sticky head must do this itself. `mockClient.test.ts "keeps view() fast"` (5 ms budget) failed at 5.9 ms under a loaded full run and passed alone: a load flake.
## 2026-10-06: A hover-preview list needs an intent delay, and a touch tap focuses the tile before its click (branch claude/picker-click-chooses)
Playtest: after the faction picker became hover-previews, click-chooses, the user still found it hard to reach "Play as" because the pointer crosses other gallery entries on the way and each one swapped the sheet. `FactionPicker` now switches the preview only after the pointer rests on a tile for 150 ms (`HOVER_INTENT_MS`; every mouse move restarts the wait, leaving cancels it), and it must not focus the tile on `mouseenter`, because focus calls `setActive` at once and defeats the delay. Touch has no hover, and a tap focuses the tile before `click` fires, so "was this tile already previewed?" must be read in `pointerdown`; happy-dom's `el.click()` has `detail` 0, so tests cannot tell a tap from a keyboard click by `detail`. The e2e measures each swept tile's dwell in the page and repeats a slow sweep instead of asserting wall-clock time (a total-time assertion failed at 118 ms under 4 workers). `tablet-touch` "atlas: production explanations..." fails on master too ("explanation covered"); not caused by this change.

## 2026-10-06: Scouts are free, and every empire starts with 30 Gold (branch claude/scout-upkeep-start-gold)
User playtest (Switzerland, 1v1): on turn 6 with 0 Gold at -5/turn the top bar warned "Ranger will be disbanded". They had trained a Ranger first, then got a free Scout from a ruin; the Switzerland pay 3 Gold for each military unit beyond the first two, and the Scout counted as military. ("In Civ 6 scouts don't cost upkeep, and players start with some gold.") Decision (lead, pillar 2): `data::pays_upkeep(kind)` is military and not Scout; every upkeep count (`economy::upkeep_breakdown`, the bankruptcy victim, the forecast, the build-card preview, the mock, the AI's upkeep, runway and disband maths) uses it, so Scouts neither pay nor occupy a free slot, in every faction. `game::new_game` (not `blank_game`, which tests build on) gives every empire `START_GOLD` (30 Quick, 60 Standard via `speed.scale_cost`). It stays under the Swiss interest's first step (50 Quick, 100 Standard) and far under their 200-Gold goal, so neither is trivialised (test in `crates/core/tests/faction_balance.rs`). Gotchas: the mock used to start players with 10 Gold while core started them at 0 (they now agree); many old tests used Scouts as stand-in "upkeep units", so they now use Soldiers; the AI had a "rush opening disbands a Scout it cannot fund" branch (`civilians.rs::plan_scouts`) that saved nothing once Scouts were free, so it was removed. A side finding: with the starting Gold, the `store.test.ts` 5-turn seed-42 AI game ended in an exact tie, and the TS client had no handling of `Event::GameDrawn` (turn-report crash on `l.icon`); it now formats, icons and tone-tests a draw. The user's own save is the regression fixture (`crates/wasm/tests/fixtures/playtest-switzerland-bankrupt-turn7.legatum`, `switzerland_scout_upkeep.rs`).
## 2026-10-06: A queue that grows above the build list shifts every card under the pointer, so it needs scroll compensation, not just a quieter confirmation
Playtest 8.2: "+ Queue" inserted a full-width line under the card, so a second click landed on the wrong card. Removing that line was not enough: the new queue section sits above the list, so each added row also pushes every card down. Three things keep the pointer's target still: (1) the confirmation lives on the Queue button ("Queued #2" for 1.5 s) and in the card's existing `prod-go` slot; (2) the order badge is `position: absolute` on the card corner (a badge inside the name wrapped long names and changed the card height); (3) `keepInPlace` in `CityPanel.tsx` records the clicked card's top (or, if the card leaves its list, the next card's) and a layout effect scrolls the panel by the difference. Chrome's own scroll anchoring does nothing at scrollTop 0. Side effect: the queue section can scroll out of view under the sticky head after queueing; it is visible again at the top. The empty queue line costs about 20 px at 1000x600, so the idle first-card-at-scroll-0 test needed 6 px back from block paddings (`.production-queue`, `.production-choose-block`, `.cp-sub` top margin). A test must compare bounding boxes across the click with sub-pixel tolerance (scrolling leaves 0.1 px noise). `git stash` is shared by every worktree: a parallel agent's pop applied this branch's stash to another tree. Use WIP commits.

## 2026-10-04: Clearing a barbarian camp had no celebration popup; the Byzantium's camp Science was already paid, just not shown together with the Gold
A Byzantium playtest report ("cleared a barbarian camp... +50 Gold... didn't feel like enough of a win") turned out to be two separate gaps, not one. (1) `momentsForTurn` (`app/src/celebrate/moments.ts`) had a case for `RuinExplored` but none for `BarbarianCampCleared`, so clearing a camp never got the "Rewards get a celebration" popup at all — only the plain turn-report line. (2) The Byzantium's +12 salvage Science for a cleared camp *is* paid correctly and with full mock/WASM parity: `rules/mod.rs::resolve_turn` emits a separate `Event::SalvageRecovered { source: "barbarian camp", science: BYZANTIUM_CAMP_SCIENCE }` right after `BarbarianCampCleared` for a Byzantium clearer (mirrored exactly in `app/src/mock/resolve.ts`), and `app/src/format.ts` already had a case for it — but as its own disconnected report line ("recovered 12 Science from barbarian camp"), never merged with the Gold line or shown in any popup. Fixed by having both `momentsForTurn` and `format.ts`'s `BarbarianCampCleared` case look up the paired `SalvageRecovered` event (same owner, same `at`) and fold it into one gain ("+50 Gold, +12 salvage Science"); the standalone `SalvageRecovered` line is now suppressed for `source: 'barbarian camp'` to avoid reporting the same reward twice. Lesson: a core event that's correctly computed and even has report-line code can still be invisible as a *reward* if nothing merges it with the event the player actually acted on — check the popup/report surface, not just whether the payment happens. The identical gap exists for ruins (a Byzantium's ruin-exploration `SalvageRecovered` is also never merged into the ruin's own card or line) and was left alone here as out of scope; flagged as a follow-up.
- Borders growing over an abandoned camp (`unit: null`, `gold: 0`) still earns no celebration — there's nothing to celebrate — consistent with the existing minor-tier report line for that case.

## 2026-10-04: Upkeep shown on production cards (branch claude/upkeep-in-picker)

- **The forecast's "projected" state already answers "how many military units will there be this turn", reuse it rather than recomputing.** `forecast::forecast_cities` builds a `Cow<GameState>` that simulates this turn's completing units (for the bankruptcy forecast's "Upkeep of completing units" line) and actually spawns them into the projected state. Counting military units in that same `projected` state (after the loop, before it's dropped) gives the right baseline for "would building one more be free" without writing a second simulation. Threaded through as `CityForecasts.military_units` (and the mock's `forecastCities` equivalent) into `city_detail`/`cityDetail`, then into each `ProductionOption.upkeep` (free/paid, free slots left, rate, Gold/turn after). `economy::upkeep_breakdown` was split into a thin wrapper plus `upkeep_breakdown_with_military(state, p, military, logistics)` so the preview can ask "what if military were N+1" using the exact same Strategic Logistics rounding as the real breakdown, instead of approximating it.
- **`ProductionOption` is a plain struct literal in a few places outside `view.rs`** (`crates/ai/src/fixture.rs`'s test fixture builder) — adding a field there is a compile error only under `--all-targets` (tests), not a plain `cargo check`/`cargo build`. `cargo check --workspace` passed after adding the field to `ProductionOption`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` caught the missed fixture literal. Run clippy with `--all-targets` (or `cargo test --workspace`) before trusting a core struct change is complete, not just `cargo check`.
- **A `Tip` wrapping a button (`controlTip = true`) only opens on hover (or keyboard focus), never on click** — its `onClick` explicitly returns early when `controlTip` is true, so the click reaches the inner button instead (e.g. it fires Build). Driving these tooltips from Playwright needs a real `hover()` on the trigger (or keyboard Tab + Enter), not a click; a click on a production card's outer Tip actually builds the item.

## 2026-10-01 — AI route search: unexplored tiles must count as passable
`AiContext::move_cost` returns None for unexplored tiles, while `PlayerView::movement_path` treats unknown terrain as passable Grassland. The new settler-escort shared route used the former, so pairs at the fog frontier held forever. The fix (6e434d9) treats fog as Grassland and falls back to the civilian's route. Any new AI path search must decide fog semantics explicitly; ViewBuilder tests default to a fully explored map and won't catch it. The remaining 4 stalls in 1,120 duel games are endgame stalemates (Economic/Tall pairs stuck at 7-9 VP to the 1,200-turn cap): passive openings lack a closing plan.

## 2026-10-01 — V9 re-landed (9d75a9d); balance still outside pillar 8 on Hard
Merged on top of the real-Rush and identity work. The one conflict (military.rs doc comment) kept master's Rush functions. A two-dot tree diff of a branch cut before a merge makes the merged code look "deleted": always trial-merge (`git merge-tree --write-tree master <branch>`) before concluding a branch drops work. Astra's Hard validation (10,000 8-player games): best/worst ratio 3.02→2.31x at Quick tuning, 4.48→2.73x at Quick held-out, 2.60→2.44x at Standard tuning, 3.36→1.81x at Standard held-out. Better everywhere, still above 1.5x. Germany is last throughout; Byzantium and Celts lead. Report: docs/sim-results/2026-10-01-reland-balance-v9.md.

## 2026-10-01 — Teaching the AI to fear city fire made it passive
Astra's ai-city-strike-awareness (branch, not merged) added city fire to the scripted AI's danger, exposure, retreat, heal and assault forecasts. Over 400 Hard 8-player Standard games, city-fire losses fell from 12.9 to 5.9 per game, but captures fell from 0.215 to 0.083 per game. The Proxy benchmark didn't improve (127 → 125 of 128 lobbies).
