Risk-aware tactics need a matching "commit when the forecast is good" rule, or the AI just stops attacking, which makes it easier for humans. Judge AI changes by captures and wins, not only survival. Revision queued as ai-city-strike-awareness-v2.

## 2026-10-01 — `start-settlers=N` means N EXTRA Settlers
The rules experiment adds N Settlers to the normal starting Settler (crates/core/src/game.rs ~113), so start-settlers=3 starts with four. Every "settlers3" result from 2026-09-30 is a four-Settler start. "Start with 3 Settlers" (the user's pillar-10 proposal) is `start-settlers=2`. In the tuned-genome sweep that gave GreedyExpand 0.60, Rush 0.62, Tall 0.27 and DefendedExpand 0.42. Spotted by Astra's Tall analysis.

## 2026-10-01 — Identity gates in evolution: noisy held-out rates need a fixed floor, not relative slack
With strict no-regression against the incumbent's held-out gate rate (60-120 seat-games), DefendedExpand's large, significant win gains (+0.25, p<1e-6) were vetoed when identity dipped 0.60 → 0.59. Relative slack (incumbent − 0.05) fixes that but compounds: 0.55 → 0.50 → 0.45 over successive promotions. The rule now in tools/duel/duel_evolve.py: an incumbent at or near its gate keeps a fixed floor of required − 0.05, a failing incumbent gets no slack, and comparisons use an epsilon.

## 2026-10-01 — City-fire awareness v2: commit to good assaults (merged)
The v2 revision keeps city fire in exposure, retreat and heal for uncommitted units, and makes assaults mass melee plus ranged support and accept the forecast shot when capture is viable. Over 400 Hard 8-player Standard games: captures 0.19 → 0.27 per game, city-fire losses 11.2 → 5.7 per game, turns +0.9%, no stalls. Default's duel mean rose slightly. Astra's note: staging at range 3 leaves infantry blind, so an assembled army needs a survivable range-2 reconnaissance step. The TS mock mirrors exposure and retreat but not massed-assault coordination.

## 2026-10-01 — Tall VP plan (merged 30922b6): modest gain, structural ceiling remains
Tall's AI now targets its reachable VP: growth to two population-8 cities and 20 population, the faction goal, a Great Work, Walls before science when threatened, and science as the closing title. In built-in duels its mean went 0.21 → 0.26, and Tall vs Rush 30% → 50%, at 72% identity. It is still far from valid. Every title measures empire totals, so a 3-city empire still needs three titles, a rules question for the user (the Tall analysis proposes a National Academy Great Work or scholarly specialisation; .reviews/astra-tall-design-analysis.md). Integration bugs found on the way: both war-dispatch layers could drop prepared faction declarations, and both production selectors could rebuild a building bought that turn.

## 2026-10-01 — Valid duel openings still don't make a harder 8-player Hard
With the fixed, evolved openings (six of seven duel-valid), an 8-player Hard whose seats play them for the whole game (uniform pool, no Tall) was not harder for a human-style challenger than the scripted Hard. Challenger wins: GreedyExpand 11.0% → 15.3%, Snowball 11.0% → 11.3%, Rush 4.0% → 6.8%, Proxy 1.6% both. It also had 12 stalls against 0, and leader pressure fell. Duel strength and multiplayer strength are different skills: the scripted Hard wins 8-player games through mid/late leader containment, which the openings lack. Next experiment: openings early, then hand off to the scripted Hard (Astra hard-openings-handoff). Report: branch astra/hard-openings-confirmation, docs/sim-results/2026-10-01-hard-openings-confirmation.md.

## 2026-10-01 — Openings-then-hand-off matches old Hard's difficulty and keeps opening variety
Hard v2 with a hand-off (each AI seat plays an evolved opening early, then switches permanently to the scripted Hard) played 400 8-player Standard games per challenger. Challenger wins: GreedyExpand 12.0% old → 11.0%, Snowball 11.0% → 10.0%, Rush 5.0% → 5.5%, Proxy 1.6% unchanged (paired p ≥ 0.67). Wars per game returned to old levels (15.3 vs 16.5); full-game openings had cut them to 5.4. There were no stalls. So the hand-off adds variety without losing difficulty, but doesn't make Hard harder. Rush handed off on turn 2 because its starting Soldier met the army target; fix queued (handoff-rush-fix). None of these sims reproduces how badly a human beat Hard. The challenger stand-ins win only ~11%, so "harder for a human" still needs a human-like benchmark.

## 2026-10-01 — Faction goal planning merged; Mongols wins dipped
Rebased goal planning (d4b090b): Sparta plans Walls, guards and a legal expedition around city fire, and Mongols keeps feasible Market trios and only founds safe replacements. On 600 matched Hard 8-player Standard games: best/worst ratio 2.76x → 2.55x, Sparta wins 10.2% → 12.8%, Mongols goals 36.3% → 36.7%, but Mongols wins 10.7% → 7.8% (64 → 47; about 1.6 sd, cause unisolated). In a zero-sum 8-seat game, helping one faction takes wins from others; judge faction AI changes by the pillar-8 ratio and by per-faction paired seeds, not by one faction's gate. Mongols is the next balance target.

## 2026-10-01 — The disk watcher only cleans below LOW_GB (150 GB) free
tools/cleanup/disk-watch.sh logs every 30 minutes but runs the cleanup only when free space is below LOW_GB (default 150) and AUTO_APPLY=1. Overnight, Astra worktrees grew to 370 GB (free 482 → 351 GB) with no cleanup, as designed. A manual `tools/cleanup/disk-cleanup.sh --apply` freed 237 GB of merged, idle worktrees. During heavy Astra nights, run the cleanup by hand at heartbeats rather than waiting for the low-disk trigger.

## 2026-10-01 — A human-playbook benchmark (Proxy v5) gets 17%; humans still win far more
Proxy v5 (`--proxy-version 5`) plays the user's stated plan: about 3 cities, army, early conquest, gold into armies, then turtle and tech. It wins 17.3% (CI 13.9-21.3%) against 7 scripted Hard AIs in 8-player Standard games, against v4's 1.75% and a GreedyExpand challenger's 12.75%. It has 2.8 cities at turn 100 and 3.5 at turn 136; the user had 8 by turn 136. So "Hard is too easy for humans" is still not reproduced in simulation. The gaps are execution (siege timing, capture coordination, purchase bursts, consolidation). City fire resolves before movement, so retreats need planning a turn ahead. v6 is queued.

## 2026-10-01 — Human-playbook benchmark v6: 31% against 7 Hard
Proxy v6 (`--proxy-version 6`, opt-in) adds coordinated captures, earlier army assembly, purchase bursts, settler raids and consolidation to v5's plan. It wins 31.3% (CI 26.9-36.0%) against 7 scripted Hard AIs in 8-player Standard games, against v5's 15.0% on the same opponents. It has 3.7 cities at turn 136 and makes 580 captures in 400 games. Still short of the user's real dominance. Findings: staging at radius 3 can deadlock outside two-tile sight, and successful captures need immediate relief troops. This is the first benchmark that measures "hard for a human-like player"; use it, not GreedyExpand challengers, to judge Hard changes.

## 2026-10-01 — Against human-style play, the hand-off Hard is no harder
v6 wins 31.2% against 7 scripted Hard AIs and 34.8% against 7 hand-off Hard v2 AIs (400 games each, same seeds; docs/sim-results/2026-10-01-v6-vs-hard-variants.md). Opening variety doesn't buy difficulty. Difficulty for humans must come from better scripted mid-game decisions (defending settlers and thin cities, reacting to an early conquering neighbour), measured with v6.

## 2026-10-01 — Balance remeasure on Hard: 2.5x; goal completion isn't a proxy for winning
On master with everything from tonight, scripted Hard, 2,000 games per speed: ratio 2.59x Quick and 2.49x Standard (held-out 2.30x / 3.11x). Germany (~7%) and Mongols (~8%) are weak; Celts (16.8% Standard) and Byzantium (18.6% Quick) are strong. The best V10 bundle (A) reached 1.66x at Quick tuning but 3.17x on Standard held-out, so single-cohort wins don't transfer. Byzantium completed its goal in 98% of games without winning more: judge faction changes by wins on held-out seeds, not goal rates. The levers are merged opt-in; the iteration is queued.

## 2026-10-01 — Balance bundle D: rewarding weak factions' goals converts survival into wins (1.16-1.57x)
Germany and Mongols usually survive but lose the VP race. (A fresh 2,000-game Quick cohort later confirmed D at 1.44x vs 2.59x for defaults.) Raising their existing identity goals to 3 VP (requirements unchanged), plus small drawbacks on the leaders (Byzantium conventional buildings 120% → 130%, Celts 100% → 110%) and +5 production on Sparta Settlers, gave best/worst ratios of 1.49x/1.16x (Quick/Standard tuning, 2,000 games) and 1.57x/1.27x (held-out, 1,000 games). Every faction lands at 9.8-15.8%. An extra 500-seed Quick cohort read 1.68x, so Quick sits right at the edge. It's an opt-in `--rules-experiment` bundle on master, awaiting the user's decision (docs/sim-results/2026-10-01-balance-v10-iteration.md). 500-game held-out cohorts were too noisy to judge bundles; use 1,000+.

## 2026-10-01 — How the human-playbook benchmark beats Hard, and what fixing it costs
Astra's 400-game v6 audit: 545 of 580 city captures came after at least 3 consecutive turns of visible warning, 554 began with no garrison, and 513 of 561 captured cities were never retaken within 10 turns. The scripted Hard sees the threat, but doesn't garrison or recapture. 47 candidate fixes were tried. Broad defence quotas lengthened AI-vs-AI games beyond 10% or raised stalls and faction spread. Focused guards, escorts and early containment cut v6 from 31.25% to 26.0% while passing those guards. The run died on model capacity before committing (WIP 812b7e2 on astra/hard-vs-human-playbook); the continuation is queued. Lesson for long Astra tasks: ask for intermediate commits so a crash doesn't strand hours of work.

## 2026-10-01 — National Academy (4-VP Great Work for tall empires) lifts Tall to ~0.32, not enough alone
Opt-in lever on branch astra/tall-national-academy-lever (e80bd68, not merged; awaiting the user's decision): an unbuyable Great Work needing Research Networks and two population-8 College cities, 800 production at Standard, worth 4 VP. In duels, Tall's mean score went 0.28 → 0.32 (built-in genomes) and 0.22 → 0.32 (evolved), with no significant winning edge. The 8-player faction ratio went 2.17 → 2.47 and turns +1%. Tall's gap is bigger than one VP route; candidates are combining it with the 3-Settler start or option B (scholarly specialisation).

## 2026-10-01 — Hard defends warned cities now (merged 8814567): human-playbook v6 32% → 27%
The scripted Hard (Hard only; Normal unchanged) now garrisons cities under visible threat, escorts settlers, buys emergency defenders and contains early conquerors (crates/ai/src/hard_defense.rs). On fresh seeds (3610001, 400 games), v6 wins fell from 32.2% to 27.0%; on its tuning seeds, 31.25% → 26.0%, so there is no winner's curse. Hard-only 8-player games run 7% longer, with no stalls and the faction ratio within its guard. The 22% target was missed; the biggest remaining gap is recapture (most captured cities are never retaken), then thin defence after warning. Long Astra tasks should commit intermediate work: the first attempt died after 7 hours on model capacity.

## 2026-10-01 — The 3-Settler start and balance bundle D interact; don't stack them blindly
8-player Hard, 1,000 games per cell (docs/sim-results/2026-10-01-decision-evidence.md). 3 Settlers (`start-settlers=2`) made games 12.5-13% shorter, raised Standard wars (17.3 → 22.9) and captures (0.21 → 0.34 per game), and cut human-playbook v6 wins from 31.25% to 19.25%. v6's early lead grows, but it loses ground from turn 75. But the faction ratio became 2.08x Quick / 2.59x Standard. Bundle D alone is 1.71x / 1.28x; D combined with 3 Settlers is 3.41x / 2.72x (Germany's 3-VP goal becomes too easy with more cities). Balance levers are tuned for a specific start, so retune for any start-rule change. Also, `FourCities` can be met by two tall cities; measure actual city counts. A D3 bundle for 3 Settlers is queued.

## 2026-10-01 — Decision: Tall is not a viable strategy (removal queued)
The user decided expansion, by settling or by conquest, is the core loop, and "tall" (staying small) is deliberately not viable. Pillar 10 was updated and the National Academy branch abandoned. Removing the Tall opening and the scripted Strategy::Tall archetype is queued (remove-tall, after the in-flight AI tasks, since they touch the same files). With Tall excluded, all six remaining openings are duel-valid under base rules and under the 3-Settler start (docs/sim-results/2026-10-01-opening-validity.md).

## 2026-10-01 — CORRECTION: dropping Tall exposes missing counters; and no 3-Settler start
The "6 of 7 openings valid" result leaned on Tall as everyone's victim. Without Tall (evolved genomes, base rules, ~/LegatumRuns/confirm-final/base-evolved), the significant edges are: GreedyExpand > DefendedExpand, Snowball, Economic; Default > DefendedExpand, Economic; Rush > Snowball. DefendedExpand, Snowball and Economic beat nobody significantly, so they fail "beats at least one". All six still win 43-58% against the field and none dominates. The intended counters (DefendedExpand > Rush, Rush > GreedyExpand) haven't formed. Lesson: validity criteria must be computed on the opening set that will actually ship; a weak member props up everyone else's "beats at least one". Separately, the user decided against the 3-Settler start (2026-10-01): balance work continues with one Settler (bundle D), and the D3 tuning task was stopped.

## 2026-10-01 — The opening results were measured on a slowed-down early game (G8 pacing still off)
Turn-by-turn production from recorded duels (Standard): GreedyExpand's first Settler took 35 turns (T2-T37); Snowball and Economic build a Monument first (T2-T26, the fallback while Workshop and Market are locked behind Fabrication and Exchange), then the building, so their second city comes around T77-81; Rush builds a Market between Soldiers and declares at about T61. G8's diagnosis (2026-09-28) still holds: Standard costs are 2x Quick, and capitals sit at the 3-production floor. Its fix (capital-production + opening-ramp) has been waiting on the user since then. Consequences: Rush can't strike early, GreedyExpand isn't punished (0.11 settlers and 0.04 cities lost per game), and "infrastructure first" openings look odd. Opening build lists must only name items available at turn 1, or say explicitly what happens while an item is locked. Retune openings and balance after pacing is decided (Astra pacing-validation queued). The user also dropped DefendedExpand as an opening.

## 2026-10-01 — Astra runner: pin the model per task; the Codex default was unusable
tools/astra/runner.sh passed no `-m`, so tasks used whatever ~/.codex/config.toml set. When that became `gpt-6.1-sol` (rejected for ChatGPT-account Codex: "model is not supported"), tasks died within seconds (pacing-validation, a master review). The runner now takes `model: astra|sol|luna` and `effort:` headers (gpt-6-astra / gpt-6-sol / gpt-6-luna; default sol medium; the reviewer uses sol medium) and logs the model on each start. Runner loops were restarted by PID after the script was replaced with `mv` (a running bash reads its script incrementally, so never edit it in place). Separately, "Selected model is at capacity" errors from gpt-6-astra killed two long tasks; ask long tasks for intermediate commits.

## 2026-10-01 — Hard recapture logic didn't move the human-playbook benchmark (not merged)
Astra's recapture planner (branch astra/hard-recapture-r2, 558b816) re-gathers troops, ranged support and reinforcements to retake recently lost own or allied cities. v6 won 109/400 against it vs 110/400 against master (seeds 3620001), with Hard-only guards neutral. No measurable gain, so it wasn't merged. Lost cities are rarely recoverable by the time the AI reacts; prevention (the merged warned-city defence) is what moved the number. Forecasts must also budget the garrison's faction healing during travel: targeting a city's current low HP overestimates viability.

## 2026-10-02 — macOS /bin/bash is 3.2: no associative arrays
A `#!/bin/bash` study script used `declare -A C=([d]=... [pd]=...)`. On macOS's bash 3.2 that fails silently: every key evaluates arithmetically to index 0, so all five "conditions" ran with the same flags, and 2 hours of sims produced identical rows. Use a `case` lookup function (or /opt/homebrew/bin/bash), and smoke-test the condition-to-flags mapping itself, not just the simulator flags. `source <(...)` is also broken in bash 3.2. Identical rows across conditions should be read as a harness bug.

## 2026-10-02 — G8 pacing re-measured on current master: halves the opening, makes Hard harder, needs a balance retune
Standard, 8-player Hard, 1,000 games (docs/sim-results/2026-10-02-pacing-study.md): capital-production + opening-ramp moved the first Settler from turn 49 to 18 and the second city from 55 to 26; cities at turn 100 went 2.09 → 3.05; games ran 6% shorter; human-playbook v6 fell from 26.0% to 22.5%. Bundle D's ratio rose 1.69x → 1.96x, so balance needs retuning under pacing. The built-in GreedyExpand's 30-turn Settler interval (15 at Quick) was what kept it at ~2 cities at turn 50; with interval 0 it averages 2.66 with 3+ cities in 68% of duels, matching the Civ 6 benchmark. GreedyExpand still beats Rush 73-77% under pacing: counters, not economy, are the remaining problem.

## 2026-10-02 — Decision: playtest-5 ships G8 pacing and balance bundle D as defaults
The user chose pacing (capital +2 production, opening ramp) plus bundle D for playtest-5, after the pacing study (first Settler T49 → T18; human-playbook v6 26% → 22.5%; ratio 1.69x → 1.96x, still to retune), and asked for the three Hard-defence review bugs to be fixed first.

## 2026-10-02 — Playtest-5 shipped (9faf28e)
Defaults: G8 pacing (capital +2, opening ramp) and balance bundle D. The scripted Hard now has warned-city defence, settler escorts, emergency buys and early containment (human-playbook v6 32% → 22.5% combined with pacing). Release check: fmt, clippy, 1,233 Rust tests, WASM, tsc, 1,381 vitest, app build, 163 Playwright, all green. No manual full-game click-through this time (to save tokens); Playwright's game flows stood in. The phone server must be restarted on the new binary after each build (it serves app/dist and runs its own engine). Open issue found in validation: scripted AIs disband about 7 units per game for unpaid upkeep (AI money management).

## 2026-10-03 — Faction redesign chain merged (2fa55ee)
Four TI4-style redesigns ran overnight as a chain: each Astra task merged the previous branch first, and a script released the next only after an exit-0 finish. Switzerland/Japan, Byzantium/Celts, Germany, Mongols, then the Tall/DefendedExpand removal. Chaining cut Claude review to one pass, but each step measured only "branch vs master", so combined effects showed up late: Standard ratio 2.49x after Mongols (Japan 16.7%, Switzerland 6.7%), then 1.58x after the removal (Hard-only, 1,000 games). Switzerland' drop was hoarding, not conquest: no captures and about 10 Gold of bounty per game; goal VP rose 0.18 → 0.78 while every title fell, and Great Works bought didn't rise, so the AI never used its exclusive purchase. Also: AI-vs-AI games average about 0.02 city captures per game (AIs don't conquer; the human-playbook benchmark captures about 1.45), and AIs average about 7 forced upkeep disbands per game. Follow-ups queued: tune-switzerland-japan, ai-conquest, then ai-budget and byzantium-salvage-goal.

## 2026-10-03 — Planning-time validation must apply the same phase order as resolution
Playtest-5 bug: declaring war at Peace and ordering a ranged attack on that empire's city in the same turn is legal at resolution (surprise wars apply in phase 0, before ranged fire). But the planning UI validated the ranged order against the current Peace state, flagged "no enemy unit or city to attack there", hid the city from ranged targeting, and trapped the unit in the "needs orders" loop. G20 had fixed only the melee path. Any rule that resolution applies early (surprise wars) must also apply in planning validation, targeting overlays, forecasts and AI planning; check every order type, not just the one in the report. Fix queued: fix-ranged-same-turn-war.

## 2026-10-03 — Turn report history: layout and test gotchas
- The This Turn panel has no spare height at 1440x900: any new row breaks `minimap.spec` "all eight rows fit" (1 px tolerance). The Last turn control therefore replaces the panel subtitle once a report exists.
- The Court look hides `.ph-sub`, so anything placed in a panel subtitle disappears there unless explicitly overridden.
- Report history is in memory only (last 20), not in the localStorage autosave, to stay clear of its size limit.
- `Event::Battle.attacker_at` is serde-default and omitted when `None`, so state hashes and pinned tapes didn't change; fogged attackers are redacted in `event_visibility.rs`.
- `mockClient.test.ts` "runs 30 turns with lively AIs" takes about 5 s alone and timed out at the 5 s default under load average ~170; it now has a 20 s timeout. With many parallel branches building and simming, expect wall-clock-bound tests to flake: rerun before blaming the change.

## 2026-10-03 — Walls-only city strike: Rush becomes viable, conquest rises
Cities now fire only with Walls (any tier; one core function `combat::city_can_fire`). Measured on the branch before merge:
- Duel Rush vs GreedyExpand (60 seeds, 1,800 games): Rush 25.0% → 45.8%; Rush is now a valid opening (mean score 0.385 → 0.521). Economic is the only invalid opening left. Rush still reaches an enemy city by turn 80 in only 44% of games: delivery, not the city's guns, is now its limit.
- 8-player Hard Quick (500 games, seed 5200001): captures per game 0.07 → 0.35, Walls built 10.7 → 12.4, faction ratio 1.87 → 1.33, turns 123.5 → 125.0, no stalls. Standard and v6 were unfinished at merge because of machine load.
- The finding confirms the earlier diagnosis: unwalled cities shooting back was the main thing blocking rushes, more than unit costs or AI.
- Part of Rush's shortfall against GreedyExpand is the new AI wall response (walls valued more when a rival masses nearby), which is the intended counter.

## 2026-10-03 — Unverified UI shipped in playtest-6 (tech panel)
The tech-panel polish (Astra) reported "visual acceptance remains unverified" because Chromium can't launch in Codex's sandbox, and it was merged anyway under the playtest cutoff. The user immediately hit the same bottom-row overlap at ~1000x600, an illegible near-black current-research card, and cards that still felt boring. Rule: UI branches merge only after Claude has taken and looked at Playwright screenshots at the user's window size; route taste-heavy UI to Claude agents.

## 2026-10-03 — AI upkeep bankruptcy: a deliberate-disband safety net, not stricter spending
Scripted AIs never checked upkeep before buying or building, so `rules::cities::pay_gold` force-disbanded their costliest unit about 11 times per game (200 Hard Standard games). Tightening production and purchase solvency cut disbands to 2.4 per game but suppressed armies enough to reopen a title-race stall (`coalition_stall`, seed 910030). What shipped (claude/ai-budget, merged 3335d915) is a safety net only: when `PlayerView.economy.bankruptcy` says this turn ends bankrupt, or this turn's own purchases tip it negative, the AI disbands its weakest idle garrison unit first. Result on the walls-only tree: upkeep disbands 11.3 → 0.72 per game; captures 1.07 → 1.19; faction ratio 2.38 → 1.32; v6 34.0% → 31.5%. These are single 200-game samples, directional only. New telemetry: `upkeep_disbands`.

## 2026-10-03 — "Captures per game" is not an AI quality metric
The user pointed out that aggregate captures mix factions that should conquer (Sparta, Mongols) with ones that shouldn't (Celts razes; Switzerland turtle). Report conquest per faction against its identity, and judge AI combat skill by efficiency (captures per war declared, losses per capture, failed walled assaults) rather than volume. Earlier reports in this log that cite total captures (walls-only, AI conquest, AI budget) are directional at best. The method lives in the legatum-factions skill.

## 2026-10-03 — New state fields must skip serializing their default
The Byzantium salvage goal added `Player.byzantium_salvage_science` with plain `#[serde(default)]`. Every archived AI test fixture then re-serialized with an extra `"byzantium_salvage_science":0`, and six AI test binaries failed their archive-hash pins. `crates/core/tests/factions.rs` also still used the old Byzantium goal. Neither showed up until the full release check, because the branch was merged before its agent finished its own full test run. Rules: new state fields use `skip_serializing_if` for their default, as state.rs already does; and run the whole workspace test suite on the merged tree before shipping, even under a cutoff.

## 2026-10-03 — Byzantium goal retuned: salvage Science, 1,200
Byzantium's goal was "Deep Survey + explore 100 tiles" (completed in about 98% of games, a freebie). It is now 1,200 Science earned from salvage camps on rivals' land, the faction's broken rule. On 1,000 eight-player Hard games per speed (seed 5000001), completion is 44.2% Quick and 58.4% Standard, inside the 40–60% band, and Byzantium wins 10.4% and 10.9%: slightly weak, unchanged by the goal. Faction ratio on that run: 1.53x Quick, 1.67x Standard. Switzerland are worst at Standard, and that's the pending tuning task. Gotcha: `cargo build -p X --example Y` doesn't relink X's default binary, so one validation run silently used a stale threshold; cross-check a small run against expectations before trusting a big one.

## 2026-10-03 — Hard AI ignores a runaway conqueror (playtest 6)
The user rushed as Mongols on Hard and eliminated the Athens and Byzantium by turn 52. Every other AI stayed at Peace: no walls, no alliances, no second front. Leader containment (coalition warnings, Calls) keys on VP pressure, and the user had only 4/10, so a conqueror with low VP is invisible to it. Walls-only city strike plus the conquest AI made a human rush much stronger, and the AI had no counter. Threat must be scored from public aggression and momentum (captures, eliminations, wars, armies near borders), not VP alone. The work is on claude/ai-threat-response, built on Astra's stopped opponent-assessment branch.

## 2026-10-03 — Orders that look accepted but fail
In playtest 6 a ranged order on a city at Peace queued, drew its icon on the map, and was then rejected by core, with a misleading "no enemy there" message. Moves already asked "Declare War?" first; ranged orders never checked the relationship. The MapCanvas ranged test passed because it never asserted that queued orders had no errors. Fixed on claude/attack-at-peace. Also: at 961–1024px wide the desktop turn dock wasn't a flex column, so a taller call to action overflowed at 1000x600; the dock's budget there comes from the race panel's minimum height.

## 2026-10-03 — War events: two timings, and how ally calls appear
`WarDeclared` has two timings. For a surprise war at Peace, `relation.effective_turn` equals the resolved turn; when a Pact or Alliance is broken, it's the resolved turn + 1. An ally answering a call to arms emits its own `WarDeclared` (declarers = [ally]) and then `CallAccepted`; breaking a treaty to declare war emits `TreatyBreached` before `WarDeclared`. The war interstitial (`app/src/celebrate/alarms.ts`) takes all its wording from `format.ts`, so it can't drift from the report. To add one sound without rebuilding all audio, import `build_audio`, call `build_sfx` on the new entry, then run `node app/scripts/audio-credits.mjs`.

## 2026-10-03 — Playtest-6 replay: the AI doesn't defend at all
A full replay of the user's Mongols rush (70 turns, every state hash matched) shows:
- **No defence.** No AI built Walls; only 4 ever researched Fortification (t54–65). The Walls valuation fires only when a rival city is within 8 hexes, and the user's capital was 9 away.
- **No wars.** AIs never declared war on each other. The Sparta declared war on the user at t57 and then withdrew. A Celts–Japan alliance against the user lasted two turns.
- **Passive garrisons.** The user's first two sieges were each held by one Ranger for 8–10 turns while ranged garrisons skipped about 350 HP of shots.
- **The user's recipe:** 2 Rangers plus 1 melee, positioned in range before a surprise war, killing garrisons from range and capturing with melee. The AI's Rush opening goes to war at about t12 with 2 melee units and no ranged support, and rarely captures (27% of games, median t75).
- **Mongols:** the v6 proxy wins as Mongols far more than as any other faction (10/13 and 22/25 in two runs), which is worth a balance look.
- **Walls timing:** earliest Walls is about t45–55 at Standard, against a human capture at t35.

## 2026-10-03 — Walls-only full validation: Hard got easier for human-style conquest
Final runs (Hard, 8-player, seed 5200001; v6 400 games, seed 3610001), master vs walls-only:
- **Quick:** faction ratio 1.87 → 1.33.
- **Standard (300 games):** ratio 1.93 → 2.17. Mongols is best at 16.7% and Switzerland worst at 7.7%.
- **v6 proxy:** wins 25.0% → 34.0%, and its captures per game rise 1.03 → 2.05.
- **AI walls:** per seat they didn't increase against v6 (1.46 → 1.34). The aggressive-neighbour trigger (2+ units within 6 hexes) doesn't fire before a human-style surprise war.

Walls-only is intended (rush works against the undefended); the missing piece is the AI's defence. Counting the later AI conquest and budget changes, v6 was about 31.5%. Threat response (claude/ai-threat-response) and the garrison rules must bring it down.

## 2026-10-03 — Passive ranged garrison: a faction-conditional pass ate the unit's one order
In playtest 6 the Athens' Ranger in Resonator held for three turns and died without firing. `plan_military` runs an early `garrison_cities` pass, only when a Mongols empire is at war with us, and it gave the garrison `hold_or_upgrade`. A unit gets one order per turn, so that pre-empted `ranged_fire`. The existing test passed only because its enemy wasn't Mongols: synthetic fixtures with a single enemy faction hide faction-conditional passes. Fix (claude/ranged-garrison-fires, merged fcefc16d):
- **Holds become shots:** `fire_from_holds` converts any ranged `HoldUnit` with a target in range into a shot, at every difficulty.
- **Target choice is value-based:** threat removed × kill chance, with a capture threat counting the city's HP. Shooting would-be captors first comes from the scoring.

**Results** (200 Hard games):
- Idle garrison-turns with a target in range: 14.7 → 1.6 per game.
- Mongols win rate: 33% → 26%; its rivals' garrisons were the ones affected.
- Faction ratio: 4.7x → 3.7x.

Open judgement calls: firing forgoes fortify and the resting heal, and a concealed Celts hunter now reveals itself by firing.

## 2026-10-03 — Scenario baselines: what the Hard AI does at five decision points
50 Hard runs per scenario (merged ef89274a; results in docs/sim-results/scenarios/):
- **Glass at the Gate (rush defence):** the AI proposes peace, only fortifies, never sallies against Rangers in the open, and loses the city in 50 of 50 runs.
- **The Last Bridge (rush offence, Sparta):** it captures in 49 of 50 runs, but declares war about 6 turns late.
- **The Courier King (the user's game at turn 40, as Switzerland):** it never declares war on the runaway conqueror, even with its new cities at 15–25 HP (0 of 50).
- **Three Fires by Fifty:** 3 cities by turn 50 in 40 of 50 runs, never 4.
- **Squatter's Rights:** Byzantium earns only 25 salvage Science in 12 turns; its Ranger fortifies at home.

Also:
- The scripted AI is nearly deterministic, so baseline variance is tiny and comes from combat dice.
- A rusher with 4 Soldiers 3 hexes away never attacks; 2 Soldiers and 2 Rangers 2 hexes away always do.
- Vite and Vitest need `server.fs.allow` to import the repo-root `scenarios/` folder.
- Archive hashes from before 6a5f3e5f don't replay exactly; compare recorded telemetry instead.

## 2026-10-03 — UI gotchas from the 6.1 review fixes
- The `.tip-trigger:has(> .tip-info)` flex rule silently overrides grid layouts on any element that's also a Tip trigger. It broke the race rows: ranks sat under crests and progress bars were 0px wide.
- `alarmsForTurn` plus `formatEvents` run from Node, so alarm screens can be toured in Playwright without driving the AI (app/src/test/alarmFixture.ts).
- Adding a title-menu row (Scenarios) overflowed the menu at 1100x700, and in the Court look at 1440x900. layout.spec catches it, so keep the title's vertical budget in mind when adding menu entries.

## 2026-10-03 — LLM opponent seat: first 10 games (Luna escalating to Astra vs 7 Hard)
Merged b8fc1cbc; see docs/llm-seat.md.
- **Results:** the LLM seat won 1 of 10, with mean VP equal to scripted Hard (3.9 vs 4.0). It walled before its first threat in 8 of 10 games, where scripted Hard is late or never walls. It declared 52 wars for 1 capture, and never rushed.
- **Luna vs Astra:** agreement is 80% on war targets, but Luna says "no war" 96% of the time and agreed with Astra's wars 6 of 35 times. Peace agreement is 48%, accepting offers 62%. Luna is overconfident: 75% agreement even at 0.9+ confidence.
- **Cost:** each `codex exec` call carries about 25k tokens of Codex's own agent instructions, mostly cached. A direct API call would cut input about tenfold. Games take about 16 minutes each.

**Hard AI blind spots it found:**
- **Containment.** It only fires against an aggressor with 5+ cities (`diplomacy.rs::containment_target`, `cities < 5 * members`).
- **Late action against VP leaders.** Wars on the eventual winner cluster in the final 5–20 turns.
- **Wars without pressure:** 0.19 captures per war, and unwalled enemy cities left untouched for turns.
- **Late Walls.** The median gap from a nearby enemy to building Walls is 29 turns.
- **Pact sprees.** Accepting every Pact sends Influence from +16 to −29, and Pacts get signed with empires never met.

**Findings channel (502 reports).** The top design flags are "accepting every Pact is a no-brainer" and "expansion is too slow" (Settlers take 14–52 turns; 3–5 cities at t200). Some "bugs" were gaps in the briefing (missing faction-goal progress, no Influence breakdown).

**Note:** the skills describe the Sparta redesign, but it's still on an unmerged branch; the factions skill now says so.

## 2026-10-03 — City shields its garrison: measured effect (branch claude/city-shields-garrison)
The Civ 6 rule (the city absorbs damage while it has HP, and the garrison is exposed at 0 HP) is implemented, being integrated with master's ranged-garrison scoring on claude/city-shields-integrate. On the pre-fix base:
- Garrison deaths roughly halve: 2,543 → 1,235 per 400 Hard games, and 3,380 → 1,595 in v6.
- Conquerors' captures per war fall 10–30%. Mongols wins 35.5% → 30.5%.
- The v6 proxy's win rate dips 21.5% → 20.5%.
- Rush in duels is slightly better (Rush vs GreedyExpand 43.8% → 48.8%, not significant): garrisons no longer soak a first strike in the field. Rush still beats the undefended, as the user wanted.
- The faction ratio is high either way on seed 5200001 (5.46 → 4.88), so balance needs separate work.
- New sim-only telemetry: siege length, garrison deaths, declarations per team.

## 2026-10-03 — The user's first scenario runs: the AI misses obvious plays
- **Glass at the Gate:** the user scored 210, above all 50 AI runs (80–100). The Mongols rusher captured Hymnal on T24, then abandoned it, and the user retook it on T30.
- **The Last Bridge:** the user declared war on turn 1, marched every unit, shot the garrison and captured on T26 with 8 turns to spare (520). The Sparta Hard AI next to an unwalled one-city capital fortifies for about 6 turns before declaring (350). The AI lacks opportunism: it doesn't seize an overwhelming local advantage.
- **The Courier King** (corrected the same evening): it is NOT broken. The user won on a replay with 300 by capturing a conqueror city on T54. Their first loss, 150 like every AI run, happened because the scenario never showed where the conqueror was, so they ran out of time searching. The lesson is that scenario targets must be marked on the map from turn 1, with a "Show on map" action (pillar 9). The Switzerland AI never declares war on the conqueror (0/50); the human did, and won.
- **Compare tool:** tools/scenarios/compare.py crashed on non-seat unit owners (barbarians).
- **Fixes:** claude/ai-attack-opportunism and claude/pathfinding-bounces. Pathfinding sent a Soldier through a friendly unit, so it bounced instead of taking the equally short free route.

## 2026-10-03 — Worktree hygiene and agent spawns
86 worktrees accumulated: the disk watcher only cleaned below 150 GB free. It now removes merged, clean, idle (3h), unlocked worktrees every 30 minutes; the first run freed 270 GB. At load ~150, isolated agent spawns repeatedly failed with "git could not be run to resolve it". The fallback: create the worktree yourself (`git worktree add -b <branch> .claude/worktrees/<name> master`, then `git worktree lock`, and symlink app/node_modules), and spawn a non-isolated agent told to work only there.

## 2026-10-03 — Squatter's Rights: the Byzantium AI barely squats
In 12 turns the user earned 178 salvage Science playing normally and 381 "salvagemaxxing" (about 50 per turn by the end). The Hard AI earns 25 (50/50 runs), with its Ranger fortified at home. The normal run is the AI benchmark. The maxxing run is a balance ceiling: if the AI squats like that, the 1,200 goal could fall in about 24 turns, so a squatting AI plus Salvage Works must be checked against the 40–60% completion band. The user's own framing: maxxing is how you'd play to win the scenario, not how you'd typically play the faction. So treat scenario-maximising play as an upper bound, not as typical play.

## 2026-10-03 — Pact fixes: contact rule and value scoring (claude/pact-fixes, folded into remove-influence)
- **Effect of value scoring:** AIs signed about 80% fewer treaties (Standard: 15.1 → 2.8 per empire per game). Influence-debt turns fell from 123 to 4 per game, and wars between treaty partners fell about 90%. The restraint comes purely from the scoring.
- **The cost:** the Switzerland collapsed at Standard (9.0% → 3.7%) because they relied on blanket Pacts for safety. Faction identities can lean on AI habits; check every faction when changing a shared system.
- **Lesson for humans:** a treaty with only a one-off fee is a no-brainer. It needs ongoing upkeep or an exclusive downside.
- **Tape versioning:** new GameState fields change every tape's initial hash. A tape-version gate in `Tape::initial_state` keeps old tapes and archives replayable.
- **Mock timing:** the mock's contact recording must happen at core's update points, not on every `refreshVision`, which also runs every movement round.
- **Shared scratch files:** agents shared the scratch script `scratchpad/nice.sh` and one overwrote it to `cd` into its own worktree. Agents must use per-agent scratch subfolders.
## 2026-10-03 — Where sim time goes, and why sccache can't share our own crates
- **Profile** (xctrace Time Profiler, master, 8 players, Hard, Standard, 1 thread): building `player_view` is 52% of a game, AI planning 37%, `resolve_turn` only 16%. Inside the view, `research::tech_unlocks` (26%) previews every building and unit in every city, calling `city_yields` 2+ times per item per city. Each `city_yields` scanned all units (`squatter` via `units_at`) and all cities (`city_at`) for each of the 18 work tiles, and `Hex::within` grew and sorted a fresh Vec (`grow_one` alone 9%).
- **Fix (branch claude/perf-player-view):** squatted tiles and nearby cities are collected once per `city_yields` call; `within` pre-sizes and skips the sort (it is built in `Ord` order already); `tech_unlocks` reuses per-city results (Production depends on the item only through its auto focus, and a unit leaves the after-yields unchanged). Same outputs: tapes byte-identical to master on 10 games (Quick and Standard with barbarians), and the runner tape pins and determinism tests pass. About 30% less CPU per game (user CPU 15.1 s → 10.5 s for 4 Quick games, 44.2 s → 30.7 s for 6 Standard games), about 1.44x the games per core.
- **Next hot spots:** the Inheritance usefulness experiment (`sim::inheritance`, ~12%: shadow games resolved every turn plus extra planning) runs in every sim but only feeds research telemetry, so it could be opt-in; AI clones and drops `PlayerView`s (~8%).
- **sccache only shares third-party crates across worktrees.** Cargo's `-C metadata` for a path package includes its path, so legatum-core/ai/sim in two worktrees never share a cache entry (tested with two copies of a crate: the registry dependency hit, the local crate missed, with or without `SCCACHE_BASEDIRS`). Expect ~30–40% Rust hit rates; it saves dependency builds, not ours.
- **Overload is mostly sims:** at load ~220 on 16 cores, ~53 runnable threads were sims (11 cohorts, 3–6 threads each, at least four of them master baselines on seed 5200001 or the proxy v6 cohort) and ~28 test binaries. A release build of legatum-sim took 28 minutes wall for 73 s of CPU.

## 2026-10-04 — Too many concurrent Opus agents hit the session limit
At about 23:50 on 2026-10-03, roughly 12 concurrent Claude agents exhausted the Claude Max session limit, and every agent died mid-task. Agents spawned with `isolation: worktree` couldn't be resumed afterwards ("worktree could not be verified"). Agents pointed at worktrees the lead had created by hand resumed fine, and their background sims kept running as orphans. Lessons:
- Keep about 5 Claude agents running at once.
- Hand long simulation runs and mechanical work to Astra (Codex).
- Prefer lead-created worktrees, which survive a resume.
- If an isolated agent dies, start a fresh agent in its existing worktree with a full brief.

## 2026-10-04 — Pathfinding bounces (claude/pathfinding-bounces, merged)
A melee unit attacking an adjacent target is a "mover" that never leaves its tile. Treating every friend with a pending order as passable sent units through attackers, so they bounced. Now one shared costing (`core::path::route_around_friends`) is used by preview, resolution, AI preflight and the mock. Friends that stay put are never passed, and friends that are moving out are risky, so a free route wins ties. Friendly bounces fell 8% and friend-caused failed attacks 31%. About half of the remaining AI friend blocks are destination collisions (the AI picks a tile a friend occupies), which is an AI destination-choice problem, measurable with `crates/sim/examples/bounce_probe.rs`. Still open: move and attack orders in the unit panel show raw coordinates ("MOVE TO 11,4").

## 2026-10-04 — City shields its garrison merged: measured on the integrated tree
Hard Standard (400 games, seed 5200001):
- garrison deaths 2,306 → 1,220;
- captures per war declared 0.198 → 0.177;
- Mongols 28.5% → 24.5%;
- stalls 2 → 0;
- the faction ratio is unchanged at about 4.1. Balance still needs its own pass.

Human-playbook v6 proxy: wins 23.0% → 18.0%, so Hard got harder for human-style conquest.

Scenario rush-defense ("Glass at the Gate"): the AI defender now holds in 50 of 50 runs (median 210, was 0/50 at 90), because its shielded garrison survives a 2 Soldier + 2 Ranger rush. The scenario must be re-tuned with a stronger rusher once the attack-opportunism work lands. Also check that rushes still beat players who leave cities lightly defended (pillar 10).

Gotchas:
- `legatum-sim scenario` writes into docs/sim-results/scenarios by default, so pass `--out` for experiments.
- The cached master baseline lacks city-combat telemetry (garrison deaths, siege length) unless master includes that telemetry commit.

## 2026-10-04 — Greying out the pop-1 Settler costs the AI about 3 opening turns
Requiring pop 2 to *start* a Settler (Civ 6) pushed the Hard AI's first Settler from T18 to T21 at Standard, even with first growth cut to 10 Food (pop 2 by T4). The cause: Production before pop 2 now goes into a filler item instead of the Settler. Three Fires by Fifty fell from 80% to 2% for the AI, and v6 rose from 23% to 28%. Being restored with a cheaper first Settler (claude/production-banking).

Other lessons from this branch:
- Any AI filler unit costs Switzerland 3 Gold a turn and cuts their goal rate; prefer upkeep-free fillers (Monument).
- Hiding faction-useless items roughly doubled the Byzantium's win rate. They had been wasting Production on Laboratories.
- Barbarians had been targeting concealed Celts hunters; fixed.
- Playwright won't click `aria-disabled` buttons, so tests of greyed-out cards need `force: true`.

## 2026-10-04 — Faction-accurate research; competent squatting breaks the Byzantium goal
(Branch claude/faction-tech-tree, being re-synced with master before merge.)
- **The helpers:** core `data::available_to_faction` / `tech_has_benefit` / `effective_building_yields` (TS mirrors in app/src/data.ts) drive faction-accurate tech cards, names and stepping-stone styling.
- **Salvage Works:** +1 salvage Science per camp within 4 hexes, per Laboratory-family tier.
- **The goal breaks:** with a competent Byzantium squatting AI (crates/ai/src/salvage.rs), goal completion jumps from about 37% to about 90% at both speeds, and the Byzantium wins 19.8% at Quick. The goal was calibrated against an AI that barely squatted. The driver is the "+5 per tech lead" term counted per camp; a limiter is awaiting the user's decision.
- **Self-harming research:** the same term makes research self-harming for the Byzantium, which is why value-based AIs avoid Signal Theory.
- **Barbarians:** their ranged targeting must use `unit_visible_to`.
- **Merging:** branches that last synced with master hours ago conflict in the shared hotspots (runner pins, telemetry, the screen tour, the fixtures README). Ask the agent to re-merge master just before handing back.

## 2026-10-04 — Celts concealment results (claude/celts-concealment)
Hard Standard, 400 games:
- Celts hunters fire about 27% less (31.7 → 23.2 shots per game) because they now hold their cover.
- Attrition dealt on Celts land rose (425 → 499 per game).
- Bumps happen about 1.9 times per game, and the Celts win rate is within noise.
- Mongols still dominates (27–33%), so the faction-ratio problem lies elsewhere.

Integration notes:
- Hidden hunters must also be invisible to the route-around-friends costing; otherwise path bends reveal them.
- A hidden garrison is only fought, and revealed, once the city is at 0 HP (city shields).
- Barbarians now plan only against units they can see, which changed one fixture from turn 69.

Gotchas:
- `rehash_fixtures` overwrites its input, so copy a fixture before experimenting with it.
- A stale wasm-pkg after a merge produced 120 false parity failures, so rebuild WASM before running vitest.

## 2026-10-04 — Thread-cap pool starved small requests
The first slot.sh had no queue order: 6-thread sims kept grabbing freed tokens, so a 2-thread probe waited more than 40 minutes. A mutex directory left empty by a killed process (it died before recording its pid) also deadlocked every caller. Fixed with a first-come-first-served ticket queue, and by breaking owner-less mutexes older than a minute. When replacing a script that's in use, `mv` the new file over it: running bash instances keep the old inode.

## 2026-10-04 — Budgets and restarts: what the supervisor must watch
- **Correction (same day):** the Fable weekly line read as unused while the all-models weekly line was well along. I took that to mean Fable had a separate, spare budget. The user says it doesn't: Fable burns the weekly budget faster. Never use Fable; offload to Codex Sol or Luna instead.
- **The restart fixed BTLEServer** (stuck at 100% CPU for 13 days, even with Bluetooth off).
- **Locks survive restarts.** Worktree locks naming a long-dead agent PID survive a reboot and silently stop cleanup from reclaiming about 53 GB. Unlocking them was blocked by auto-mode for agents, so it needs the user's OK.
- **The Astra runner resumes on re-queue.** It reuses an existing `astra/<name>` worktree (its `worktree add` fails, then it merges master into the existing one), so re-queued tasks continue from their saved commits.
- **The supervisor role.** The perf watcher became the ops supervisor (.claude/agents/legatum-ops-supervisor.md), with a self-maintained playbook (.claude/skills/legatum-ops) covering budget projection, postmortems, throughput and the machine.
- **Stale locks are now cleared automatically** (user-approved). `tools/cleanup/unlock-stale-locks.sh` unlocks worktree locks naming a dead PID; the disk watcher runs it each cycle before cleanup. PID-less locks (release builds, shared baselines) are untouched. The earlier dead-PID locks had already been released by the stopped agents.
- **A reboot orphans Codex tasks.** Their files stay in `.progress/astra-queue/running/` with no `codex exec` behind them, so the queue never restarts them. Four sat there after this morning's restart (sparta, salvage limiter, terrain resync, Switzerland/Japan); moved back to pending. The supervisor now checks for this.
- **Budget policy:** pace Claude usage to the weekly reset, keep extra usage off, and near a limit switch to token-free CPU/GPU work (sims, builds, Laya training) and Codex.
- **Codex couldn't write ~/LegatumRuns.** The runner's sandbox writable roots were ~/.cargo, /tmp, .git and .progress only, so `cached-baseline.sh` and every ~/LegatumRuns run folder failed inside Codex tasks ("mkdir: ... Operation not permitted"). Tasks silently fell back to private dirs and re-measured master. Added ~/LegatumRuns and ~/.cache/huggingface (for Laya) and restarted the runners. Gotcha: in zsh, `kill $pids` with a newline-separated list doesn't split; pass PIDs explicitly.
- **The Vite font 403s were caused by the worktree setup.** Adding the Scenarios fs.allow (`['.', '../scenarios']`) replaced Vite's default allow list. In worktrees, app/node_modules is a symlink to the main checkout, so fonts resolved outside the list, 403'd, and broke the console-clean Playwright checks (lenses ×3). It may also shift layout metrics. Fix: also allow `realpathSync('node_modules')`. The staging spec also needs `cargo build -p legatum-server` (debug), which the release script didn't build.
- **Race panel at 1440x900.** Since the whole-rows race panel (UI-review fix), on the turn after a title change one row scrolls behind "+1 more" (minimap.spec.ts:127 fails by 15px). Shipped in 6.3 as known; the fix goes on master.
- **Sims are cheap now.** An 8-player Hard Standard game takes about 5 CPU-seconds (10.4 s user for 2 games on master's baseline binary, 19 MB RSS, measured under load ~130). A 400-game cohort is about 35 core-minutes. The machine's overload comes mostly from parallel Rust builds and test suites across many worktrees (each builds the whole workspace; sccache can't share our crates), plus Playwright.
- **Lanes replace one worktree per task** (user's idea). Every Codex task used to get a fresh worktree, so every task paid a cold build of the whole workspace in 3 profiles (clippy, release tests and sim, WASM), with ~26 GB of target each; there were 62 worktrees. Each queue worker now owns a persistent locked lane (`runner.sh queue <k>`) and switches branches in it. Several agents in one worktree at once was rejected: cargo's build lock serializes them, and mixed edits break builds and confound sim results.
- **The supervisor runs every 30 minutes on Claude Opus** (lead cron; user, 2026-10-04). A brief Codex-shift variant was dropped: Codex capacity is limited too. The lead writes usage to `.progress/supervisor/usage.json` first; the supervisor keeps runs lean via `tools/ops/snapshot.sh`. Each run names the top constraint on progress and acts on it. The constraints named today, biggest first:
  1. the Claude budget;
  2. lead-only UI verification: Codex can't run a browser, so the Playwright bridge is queued;
  3. hotspot merge conflicts on tape pins and fixtures: the one-command repin tool is queued;
  4. CPU oversubscription from builds: lanes are done and the build-slot cap is queued;
  5. long-lived branches.
- **Correction: Codex/ChatGPT capacity is not abundant** (user, 2026-10-04). Both the Claude and Codex budgets are limited and fixed. CLAUDE.md and the ops playbook said "abundant ChatGPT credits"; both are corrected. Pace both budgets, and keep Codex to about 4 lanes.
- **Heartbeat** (user request): `tools/ops/heartbeat.py` prints phone-width progress bars for every outstanding Codex task. The stages are queued 0%, running (elapsed against the typical run time for its effort, capped at 80%), awaiting review 85%, and merged 100%. It runs on request only (user, 2026-10-04). A cron prompt can only run on this session's model, so the script does all the work and the turn is one tool call. A separate Haiku session running the same script would be cheaper still. Hide superseded tasks by listing them in `.progress/heartbeat-retired.txt`.
- **An Xcode auto-update breaks git and linking machine-wide.** At 10:27 Xcode updated from 26.6 to 27.0, and until `sudo xcodebuild -license accept` is run, every `/usr/bin/git`, `cc` and `ld` fails with "You have not agreed to the Xcode license". A lane runner then failed all 8 queued tasks in 6 seconds. Workaround for one-off commands: `DEVELOPER_DIR=/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools`. The supervisor added runner guards: pause while git fails; tasks that fail within 2 minutes go to hold/. Also, inside the Codex sandbox, `kill -0` on outside processes returns "Operation not permitted", so slot.sh run by Codex took live holders for dead ones and broke the thread cap. The fix is folded into the build-slot task.
- **Workaround while the Xcode license is pending:** `DEVELOPER_DIR=/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools` gives a working git and linker (tested: rustc link, git, clang 21). runner.sh now falls back to it automatically, so the queue no longer needs the user at the Mac. Codex tasks already running keep the broken environment until they're re-queued.
- **Resolved:** the user accepted the Xcode 27 license and turned off App Store auto-updates (`com.apple.commerce AutoUpdate = 0`). The runner's Command Line Tools fallback only triggers on the license error, so it now stays dormant.
- **RAM is not a constraint.** The workload is CPU-bound: a sim game uses about 19 MB, builds a few GB, and almost all of the machine's memory is free. The headroom matters for the GPU, which shares unified memory: Laya training, or a local open-weights LLM as a token-free cheap tier (proposed experiment: measure agreement with Luna before use).
- **Glass at the Gate: the AI attacker's "give up" was bankruptcy.** In the user's run, Mongols started at T20 with 9 Gold, one city and five military units (army strength 90). It spent T21–23 shooting from range, then on T24 disbanded three units to avoid bankruptcy (`production.rs::disband_weakest_unit`), and the rest drifted off. No part of the AI weighed "my money runs out in 3 turns" against "taking the city is worth VP", so the bankruptcy handler quietly overrode the war plan. This is the user's broader point: the AI is a stack of local rules with no common currency (VP or win odds) and no persistent campaign commitment.
- **Per-change sims cut back** (user): full balance cohorts per branch measured rulesets that would never ship and slowed landings (39+ min per gate under load). Now per change it's correctness plus a 50-game smoke check plus a targeted probe; full balance runs once per integration (7a, 7b), then the balance pass.
- **Terrain LOS landed (dfd3f2e6).** Playwright runs fine on the host even though Codex's sandbox can't launch Chromium, so UI branches from Codex must get their browser checks at landing. Under load around 165, three vitest tests timed out but passed in isolation; purchases.wasm.test.ts (a 300-turn synchronous mock) now has a 90 s timeout because terrain LOS adds per-turn mock cost. For information only: the Standard faction ratio is 3.08x after terrain LOS, against master's 4.06x.
- **The batch landing (Sparta, Switzerland/Japan, perf, build label) hit content conflicts beyond the pins:** the factions skill, game-rules.md, app data.ts, GuideContent and the mock state. Under the combined rules, promotion.json's archived orders no longer produced a promotion; it was regenerated with the promotion_tape example. Expect fixed-order archive tapes to break whenever combat or XP rules combine, and regenerate them; don't patch the expectations.

## 2026-10-04 — WebKit's null relatedTarget broke the game menu's outside-focus close (playtest 6.3)
"Exit to title" (and in principle every other item) in the in-game menu did nothing in the Mac app, but a quick Chromium check passed, which hid the bug for a while. Root cause: `GameMenu.tsx`'s `onBlur` handler closed the menu whenever `!e.currentTarget.contains(e.relatedTarget)`. Chromium populates `relatedTarget` with the element about to receive focus; WebKit (the Mac app's WKWebView) fires `blur` with `relatedTarget: null` when a mousedown moves focus from the already-focused first menu item ("Save to file…", auto-focused on open) to another button in the same menu, *before* the new element takes focus. The handler read `null` as "focus left the menu" and closed it mid-click, so `mouseup` landed on whatever the menu had covered (confirmed with instrumented pointerdown/mousedown/mouseup/click listeners: mousedown target was "Exit to title", mouseup target was a "Peace" button underneath) and no `click` ever fired. Fix: never trust `relatedTarget` for this; defer to `requestAnimationFrame` and check `document.activeElement` once focus has settled. Any other `onBlur`-based "did focus leave this container" check in this codebase should use the same pattern, not `relatedTarget`. Diagnosis needed the real engine: `LEGATUM_E2E_BROWSER=webkit npx playwright test` reproduced it in under a minute; Chromium (Playwright's default) never does, since it gives a real `relatedTarget`. Worth a standing check: run the game-menu/exit spec under webkit when its surrounding code changes, not just Chromium.
- **Headless Playwright browsers play audio through the speakers.** The user heard Legatum music while landing tests ran. The app now skips audio when `navigator.webdriver` is set, which works in every browser engine. Vitest's environment also sets webdriver, so the check is limited with `import.meta.env.MODE !== 'test'`; without that, 12 sound tests fail (branch claude/exit-and-growth-label, 76075229). Landing this branch, I'd initially merged the branch tip before it was amended to add that guard; the stale commit broke the same 12 tests, so always re-check a branch's current tip just before merging, not just when the lead names it.
- **Agents leave hidden built-in browser tabs open.** The exit-fix agent left a tab on localhost:5799 (its own legatum-server) in the Claude app's browser pane; with the pane hidden, the game's music kept playing. Agents that use the browser pane must close their tabs and stop their servers when done.
- **A stale dev app bundle can shadow the installed playtest.** The main checkout's `app/src-tauri/target/release/bundle/macos/Legatum.app` (built Oct 2, no Scenarios menu) was registered with LaunchServices, and the user's launcher opened it instead of /Applications/Legatum.app (6.3). Fix: trash the stale bundle and `lsregister -f /Applications/Legatum.app`. Release builds now trash the source bundle after installing it.
- **WebKit surfaces the browser's own ResizeObserver backpressure notice as an uncaught `weberror`; Chromium doesn't.** Running `screen-tour.spec.ts` under WebKit for the first time, the scenario-completion test failed "No browser console errors" with a message that printed as empty (`e2e/fixtures.ts` built it with `stack ?? message`, and WebKit gives an empty-string stack, which `??` doesn't fall through on). Logging the raw error showed the real text: "ResizeObserver loop completed with undelivered notifications." — a benign browser diagnostic, not an app fault. Fixed both: the fixture now allowlists that exact message and uses `stack || message` so a future empty-stack error isn't hidden again. Expect more such WebKit-only diagnostics once `tools/release/check.sh`'s new `playwright-webkit` step runs regularly; log the raw error object before assuming it's a real regression.
- **The threat-response branch didn't survive integration with the faction batch:** 5 AI behaviour tests failed (garrison retreat vs walls, the Celts unique unit, the Swiss war at T51, a Pact threshold, elimination tracking). It was stopped mid re-merge loop, so it never re-verified. Lesson: branches that rework shared AI decision logic should land before, or together with, faction AI changes, not race them. Moved into ai-campaigns for 7b.
- **7a baseline (faecae92; 8-player Hard, 400 games per speed):** Standard ratio 15.4x (Mongols 30.8% vs Sparta 2.0%); Quick 9.6x (Byzantium and Mongols 19.2% vs Sparta 2.0%); v6 21.0%. The redesigned Sparta is the outlier at 2% at both speeds. The diagnosis task asks whether it's the rules or the AI not playing the identity. Informational, not a gate.
- **Playtest 7a shipped (tag playtest-7a; master ad7fe254 after merging the release).** The UI review found two 1000x600 blockers. (1) The growth label was clipped mid-word: ellipsis can't cross the nested tooltip span inside the inline-flex .pbar-label; fixed with flex-wrap. (2) The forecast card's city-fire math sat below its overflow-hidden box; it now scrolls on short windows. That second fix needed `.forecast.forecast`, because the base `.forecast { overflow: hidden }` rule comes later in styles.css and would otherwise win over the media query.
- **The user's 7a game, Sparta (Hard) at T53:** one city (first Settler queued at T52); declared war on the human at T43 but sent 2 of 4 units; the wounded attackers then held position (no field healing), never pressing to a capture. Restlessness probably drove the war, and a 'phony war' dodges desertion cheaply (design flag for the user). Fed into the sparta-diagnosis task.
- **The re-pin tool landed (eca157ed).** Tape pins now live in crates/sim/tests/pins.json, and tools/sim/repin.sh regenerates the fixtures and pins in one go. On master, repin.sh reproduced the prior hard-coded hashes exactly (zero drift), confirming it's a faithful replacement. A merge conflict in these files is now settled by taking either side and running repin.sh.
- **The Playwright bridge landed (2b44c95e) and its daemon is running.** Codex tasks can now run Playwright through tools/ui/pw-request.sh, with results and screenshots in .progress/pw-bridge/results/<id>/. Codex UI work no longer has to wait for the lead to run browser checks. The daemon must be started each session; it's in the lead's memory notes.
- **ResizeObserver self-observation loops are WebKit-visible only.** VpTrack's row-fit effect observed the element its own callback resizes. Chromium stayed quiet; WebKit raised page errors (4 celebrate.spec failures). Never observe the element a ResizeObserver callback resizes. Found at landing because the Codex sandbox has no browser (fixed in 6724d8c2). Also from the user's Sparta playthrough: the early military edge, conquest as economy and the overall identity all landed. Healing at 2 HP/turn was the main pain ('one attack and they're out of commission'). An edgier option, no healing at all with healing by capture or kills, is proposed and awaiting the user's decision.
- **Kill-healing must come after deaths.** Under snapshot resolution, any per-kill reward (Sparta's +100 HP) must be granted after the stage's damage is applied and dead units are removed. Otherwise a unit killed in a mutual exchange could be 'healed back' by the kill it scored (the user caught this). The same rule applies to any future on-kill effect.
- **Codex 'Selected model is at capacity' kills tasks mid-edit** (sparta-blood-healing, 2026-10-04 17:55; seen before in several logs). The runner now re-queues a task that dies with a capacity error (up to 3 tries, 10-minute back-off), instead of filing it as a failure. The branch and worktree keep the partial work.

## 2026-10-04 — `WorldBackdrop`'s decorative `PlayerView` has no `players` array; `TerrainCache` code must guard for it
Building the Japan root-veins/spire art (`app/src/map/hive.ts`, baked into `terrain.ts`'s `drawTowns`), `TerrainCache.update()` reading `view.players` unconditionally (`new Set(view.players.filter(...))`, to find which owners play Japan) broke every screen-tour test that starts at the title or lobby screen: `WorldBackdrop.tsx` builds its fixed decorative map with `world = { width, height, tiles, cities, units: [], me: 0 } as unknown as PlayerView` — no `players` field at all, cast through `unknown` to dodge the type check — and feeds it straight into `new TerrainCache(view, ...)`. The crash ("Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'filter')") only ever surfaced as every title/lobby/new-game test failing `watchErrors`' "no uncaught exceptions" check, not as a Japan-specific failure, because the backdrop mounts behind the title and setup screens generally, not just when Japan is picked. Fixed with `(view.players ?? [])`. The existing Switzerland-bastion lookup at the same call site (`view.players.find(...)`) has the identical latent hole, just never hit because the backdrop's cities are never walled. Lesson: any new `terrain.ts` code touching `view.players` must tolerate it being absent, since the title-screen backdrop is a real (if minimal) `PlayerView` consumer, not a toy; run the full `screen-tour.spec.ts`, not just the scenario you're adding, before trusting a terrain-layer change.
- **Campaign AI step 1: mixed.** Over 100 matched Standard games, abandoned sieges went 121→107 and favourable peace offers 78→39, but engine bankruptcy disbands went 13→209. Campaigns kept armies the treasury couldn't pay for. Commitment without funding just moves the failure. A campaign-funding fix (reserve ledger: Gold runway, then fund, assault or make peace before the engine disbands) is queued ahead of landing.
- **layout.spec.ts never tested the 1000x600 hard constraint.** Its sizes start at 1100x700, and the screen tour only screenshots 1000x600 without checking for overflow. The faction-sheet agent added a real 1000x600 overflow test, which caught Sparta's sheet overflowing by 73 px. Other 'fits the window' claims probably have the same gap; add 1000x600 to layout assertions when touching them.
- **The attack-path detour was a client preview bug** (app/src/path.ts), from the user's turn 38. previewRoute blocked every tile any pending friend would step on, but core only lets a lower-id friend's step this round take a tile first (movement.rs round declare). And re-issuing an order reused the unit's own stale path whenever the destination matched (commands.ts moveSelected). Lessons: the client preview must reserve tiles exactly as core declares them, and order creation must leave out the unit's own old order. To reproduce a live-game bug, replay the tape and test order *sequences*, not just the final orders. Fixed in 18a7bc85.
- **Production banking landed (fdcb3b27).** Two branches had written their own productionStallText; banking's 'drops to population X' was wrong, because available population is counted after growth. The landing kept the accurate wording plus banking's 'stays banked' clause. The opening-race baseline on the merged tree matched the branch (median 340, 25/50 wins).
- **Split counterattack and overflow are implemented (dce745d6).** The counterattack is computed once, against the strongest melee attacker (the lowest retaliation value; a judgement call), and split with the remainder going to the strongest. "Already fought back this turn" is read from the turn's shared event log, so any new caller that passes a fresh events vector mid-turn would allow a second counterattack. Overflow uses the snapshot's garrison defense, so city damage is applied first. A garrison stepping out of the city that round is exempt, matching the existing rule. Rush probe: rush-offense captured in 50/50 runs vs 44/50, about a turn faster; rush-defense held 50/50 both ways. No scenario yet tests a rush against walls. Also: cached-baseline.sh passes --label and --out-dir, which `legatum-sim scenario` rejects, so call the cached master binary directly for scenario A/B runs.
- **Sparta blood healing done (2980585b).** Probe on 200 games: win rate 1.5% → 3.5%, median first capture T107 → T93.5, captures 0.74 → 0.92 per game, worst-faction ratio 20.3 → 8.7 (Mongols 30.5% unchanged). The hidden cause of the passivity: AI-wide 60 HP "recover first" thresholds permanently benched wounded units for a faction that can't rest-heal. Every Settler paying +50% moves the Sparta first Settler from about T15 to about T23. Two general fixes came with it. Chromium sends no hover events to disabled buttons, so their tooltips never opened; a styles.css rule now passes the pointer through. And a shared sccache server started inside a Codex sandbox couldn't write worktree target/ dirs ("error writing dependencies … Operation not permitted"); restart sccache outside the sandbox, or use RUSTC_WRAPPER= as a fallback.
- **Campaign funding fixed the bankruptcy regression (2e4a96f1).** Root cause: settle_insolvency let the engine choose whenever covering a deficit took several units, and a structural deficit repeats, so the engine disbanded the costliest unit every turn (978 engine disbands at any distance, against 68 on master). The fix is a reserve ledger: Gold runway with a floor of 4 turns at Standard, checked before diplomacy; production switches toward Gold; unfunded purchases are vetoed; cheap units far from the front are trimmed deliberately; a campaign continues only if capture lands inside the runway. 100 matched games: bankruptcy near cities 13→6, front disbands at war 428→178, favourable peace 78→37, abandoned sieges 121→118 (noise; most now follow unit deaths, not money). Replay of the user's Glass at the Gate: the attacker captures Hymnal at T24 and stays solvent. Lesson: compare AI cuts against what the engine does *repeatedly*, not once.
- **Influence removal landed (4578d4ad), with 11 conflicted files.** Lesson: when a merge deletes a whole UI subsystem, trace the call sites of anything kept from the other side (BorderDelay lived only inside the deleted Influence/Pact UI). And never take 'ours' mechanically for fixtures or scenario files: two still held pre-removal orders (Pact proposals) with stale hashes, which rehash_fixtures caught. One-line treaty definitions matter at 1000x600: a 3-clause Alliance definition pushed Accept and Decline below the fold. A WebKit menu-close-mid-click bug remains on 'Reset hints' (same class as Exit to title), filed as a separate task.
- **`e2e/layout.spec.ts`'s `SIZES` (1100x700, 1280x800, 1440x900) never actually exercise the user's laptop (1000x600).** Building the TI4-style faction picker (claude/faction-sheets), every size in that array passed while the real 1000x600 overflowed the Sparta sheet by 73px internally (`scrollHeight - clientHeight`) — 1100x700 alone gives a fixed-height dialog 100px more room than 600 does. `screen-tour.spec.ts` does take 1000x600 screenshots, but `shot()` only records layout-audit findings for the reviewer; it never asserts on them. Added a dedicated `1000x600` test to layout.spec.ts that checks every faction's sheet for zero internal scroll, since that's the one size this project's "fits the window" tests were silently skipping. Any future "fits the window" assertion should include the literal 1000x600 case, not just sizes larger than it.
- **Expansion landed (52f797e2) under the functional-only gate.** It had failed only the old balance limits (Switzerland 0/50, Mongols 25/50). A blocked git reset in the landing worktree turned out not to matter: under the new policy the 'abandoned' merge was exactly the commit to land, so nothing had to be discarded. Before destroying work to retry, check whether a policy decision makes the existing work landable.
- **The user's Byzantium game, 132 turns, 7a-era build.** Human Byzantium techs by turn: 4 at T50, 9 at T100, 13 at T131 (Science 46/turn, top). The AI Byzantium led tech from ONE city (10 techs at T75, 12 at T100) until it was eliminated by T125. AI expansion is still the glaring gap: Mongols 1 city all game, most AIs 1–3 cities at T130, the human 9–10. Coalitions formed against the human leader as designed. War micromanagement fatigue started around T130.
- **Settler risk is a judgement, not a rule** (user, 2026-10-04): the human sent unescorted Settlers only toward areas with no other players' units, and captured exposed AI Settlers opportunistically. The playtest-8 AI spec now prices trip risk from visible threats and adds opportunistic Settler captures.
- **Landing split-counterattack-and-overflow (dce745d6, into c113b388/23d1a8a7): a test's independent tape replay must mirror production's event-attribution logic, not just its event *counts*.** `sim_metrics.rs`'s `instrumented_metrics_match_replayed_tapes` replays each tape itself and compares the result to the live report, to catch instrumentation drift. It tracked `PlayerEliminated` as bare `{player, turn}`, while `runner.rs::observe_events` also back-scans the turn's earlier events for a `CityCaptured`/`CityRazed` naming who did it, and stamps that onto `Elimination.by` (an existing field, from before this branch, `skip_serializing_if` empty so it rarely showed up). This branch's new split/overflow damage was the first thing in this fixture's history to put a capture event immediately before an elimination in the same turn, so `by` populated for the first time and the test's hand-rolled version silently fell behind. Fixed by giving the test's replay loop the identical backward scan. Lesson: when a test reimplements production logic to get an independent check, a passing test for years doesn't mean the two stayed in sync — it can mean the divergent code path never fired yet.
- **Campaign funding landed (73b509fd); the hardest merge of the day.** The branch predated banking, the Influence removal and expansion. New files can't show merge conflicts, so `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` was essential: it caught new modules built against removed Pact, Influence and trust APIs. Archived Pact *relations* were mapped to Peace, not Alliance, so empires weren't falsely pre-allied. The Glass at the Gate replay on the merged tree still captures Hymnal at T24, solvent throughout. Lesson: after merging a long-lived branch across an API removal, always run a full all-targets check, not just the tests.
- **Goal-driven AI step 2 (claude/p8-goalai): fewer, better wars.** Hard now picks one plan (Economy/Peace, Campaign, Contain, Snipe) by its change in a fitted chance-to-win estimate. On 100 held-out Standard games against master: abandoned sieges 134→67, favourable peace 53→20, wartime disbands near cities 174→57, wars declared 2,514→753, captures 806→400 (captures per war doubled), cities per seat at T100 2.70→2.79, zero plan switches without a reason. Details: docs/sim-results/2026-10-04-goal-driven-ai-step2.md.
- **The siege forecast overstates new wars; calibrate it on observed captures.** New wars priced at 61%+ took the city only 38% of the time, 41–60% only 11%, and marches judged by army strength alone almost never (13 of 592). Fix: marches use the same siege rollout from their assault tiles, and odds are corrected to (raw − 30) × 1.2; on held-out seeds 41–60% then took 52% and ≤40% took 21%. A first correction overshot (captures halved) — check the correction on a cohort it wasn't fitted on.
- **In the fitted win estimate, Gold barely matters.** The economy title share dominates (8 logits per full share) and the runway shortfall weighs −0.08. So the estimate can't stop a plan from draining the treasury; the plan needs explicit gates (no campaign on a runway under the floor unless its capture comes first). A campaign plan that committed a wider force than the local ring also blocked the siege clock's pre-emptive cut and led to an engine bankruptcy disband in the Glass at the Gate replay: reverted.
- **"Avoid every tile a rival could reach" strands Settlers next to rivals.** Hard marks rival reach as Settler danger; a Settler caught inside it found no safe route and held, turn after turn, beside the army that could take it (183 of 188 surprise snipes in one cohort). Holding is the worst choice there: head for the nearest city.
- **Rubber-banding wars need a reachable enemy.** Pricing a containment war by "VP denied to the leader" made every seat declare on whoever led (5,396 wars, 2,627 reversed) until denial required armies within 15 hexes of the target and the current containment target stayed on offer while the war lasted. The 2%-of-target denial per horizon is still an assumption, not a measurement.
- **`Tip.tsx`'s `placeBubble` only keeps a bubble clear of the top bar for the `bottom` placement.** `Bubble`'s `viewport.top` (the minimum `top` a bubble may use) is set to the top bar's bottom edge only when the trigger sits *outside* the top bar; for a trigger that lives inside it (e.g. the Science stat), `viewport.top` is just the 8px gap, so a `left`/`right` fallback can float the bubble up over the top bar itself. Found adding the Science hover's multi-line source list (G-science-hover): marking the unit panel `data-tip-avoid` (so a tall hover wouldn't cover the selected unit's name/HP at 1000x600) pushed `placeBubble` to pick `right` instead of `bottom`, which then clipped into the top bar's own Turn counter — a worse overlap than the one being fixed. Reverted rather than fix the general case; a top-bar trigger's tall hover is left to overlap whatever panel sits below it at narrow widths. A real fix would clamp `top` to the top bar's bottom edge for every placement when the trigger is inside it, not just `bottom`.
- **Restarting Claude with agents mid-flight** (21:45): background agents die but their worktrees persist on disk, uncommitted files included, so a mid-merge state survives. Before restarting, have each agent commit WIP and report its exact next steps, save those in resume files (.progress/*RESUME.md and the handoff doc), then respawn fresh agents in the same worktrees. Services started with nohup (pw-bridge, reaper, disk-watch, sccache) survive; session crons don't.
- **Sparta kill-heal missed overflow kills.** Participants were collected only from Battle events with a unit defender. Garrison damage from combined-assault overflow is recorded as a hit on the city, so a garrison killed through the walls healed nobody (unless a capture's full heal covered it). Decision: overflow garrison kills count, shared by everyone who hit the city tile that stage. Lesson: when one rule changes how damage is attributed (overflow), audit every rule that reads those events (kill-heal, kill Gold, XP).
- **Laya teacher data without Codex:** gen-data.sh hard-wired the Codex deciders. LAYA_LLM_CMD and LAYA_ESCALATE_CMD now override them, so Claude (claude-decider.sh with Sonnet 5.5, escalating to Opus 5.5) can teach while Codex is out of budget. The first Sonnet-taught batch (4 games) and an MPS fine-tune started overnight on 2026-10-04 (~/LegatumRuns/laya/overnight.log).
