## 2026-10-05 — Reuse a sibling branch's conflict resolution when landing
Landing blood-healing on top of split-counterattack conflicted in 17 files. The reinforce branch had already merged the same two sides (2b01d4e9), and our side matched its pre-merge side in every file, so the landing took reinforce's resolution file by file (`git checkout 2b01d4e9 -- <file>`) instead of re-resolving. Before resolving a big conflict by hand, check whether another in-flight branch has merged the same pair. Diff each side against that branch's parents first; reuse is only safe where they're identical. Separately, the screen-tour spec matched report text case-sensitively and broke when a captured-city report began "Its counterattack…". Match report prose case-insensitively.

## 2026-10-04 — Reinforcing your own city goes first (claude/reinforce-priority)

- **Built as a pre-fight step inside `movement_round`, not a new phase.** After declare and classify (and the Mongols claim and swap passes), `reinforcements()` picks, per city tile, the lowest-id non-ranged military unit of the city's owner that makes a plain move into it, when no military unit holds the tile and an enemy melee attack (`Attack(City)`) targets it. That unit is moved at once (pos, step cost, `UnitMoved` then the new `CityReinforced` event), `occ` is updated, and enemy `Attack(City)` intents at 0 HP are rewritten to `Attack(Unit(garrison))`. Everything after (contested ruins, overflow, the capture rules) then sees an ordinary garrison, so no other rule needed a special case. Limiting it to threatened cities means a peaceful move into your own city is untouched, which kept the determinism tapes and every pinned hash unchanged.
- **Three older tests encoded the rule being replaced** (`movement_contention_tests.rs`: f12 incoming defender, mutual kill plus two own survivors, teammates' HP after a contested ruin). They asserted a meeting engagement between an incoming defender and the attacker; now it is a melee against the garrison. If a future change makes a test like these fail, check whether it was written for the old "both claim the ruin" behaviour before treating it as a regression.
- **A unit an enemy attacks where it stands this round gets no priority** (it takes the parting hit and completes its step as before). Moving it first would have made the enemy's planned fight at its old tile hit nothing, and handling that cleanly needs a second fight-planning pass. Revisit only if players hit it.
- **The turn-107 save cannot be replayed on current code.** It comes from engine faecae92 (Pacts, Influence), so neither its tape (`unknown variant Pact`) nor its state (`missing field protected_until` after mapping Pact to Peace) loads. The test `the_turn_107_shape_...` rebuilds its geometry (wounded Trooper into a 0 HP city, two Soldiers from the other side) instead. Archive replay of a save only works for saves from the same schema; for older ones, rebuild the shape.
- **Garrison killed by overflow is an Sparta kill** (lead decision): `heal_sparta_killers` now also counts damaging Battle events against the victim's own city tile (`Defender::City` with owner and `at` matching), from any stage this turn. Before, only `Defender::Unit` events counted, so a garrison that died to overflow healed nobody, even though the iron-price gold already counted city-target fights.
- **A pre-7b save fails with a raw serde error, so `loadErrorMessage` now covers it.** Loading the user's 7a autosave on current code throws `invalid saved game: unknown variant Pact` (the title screen showed "Could not load the save: Error: invalid saved game…"). `app/src/saveErrors.ts` now treats "invalid saved game" and "unsupported save version" like "incompatible engine": plain words, and the save file is kept. `app/e2e/old-save.spec.ts` injects a state with a removed treaty kind and checks the title screen. Making 7a saves actually load needs a JSON migration, not just a serde default: Pact to Peace in relations and cooldowns, drop Pact proposals, reshape the alliance `security` record (`minimum_end` becomes `protected_until`), and drop or re-hash the archive. With those patches the 7a autosave loads in the engine (checked offline).

## 2026-10-05 — Removing a rule variant breaks every older save
Removing Pact and Influence made 7a saves fail to load: unknown variant `Pact`, missing `protected_until`, and a reshaped alliance `security` record. The archive can't be replayed either, because its engine commit and rules fingerprint differ. Until the reinforce branch, the title screen showed the raw serde error. `app/src/saveErrors.ts` now shows a plain "made with an older version" message. Loading a 7a save would need a JSON migration in `crates/wasm/src/json.rs::parse_state`: map Pact to Peace, drop Pact proposals, and map `minimum_end` to `protected_until`. That's about half a day, and the game would continue under the new rules. Before removing an enum variant or a serialized field, decide whether user saves matter for that release, and say so in the release notes. The LAN server's own save-load path still shows the raw error.

## 2026-10-05 — Rule text changes can break the faction-sheet fit
Blood-healing lengthened Sparta's sheet card to three lines, and the sheet overflowed 1000x600 by 9 px. `layout.spec.ts` catches this, but landing 1 ran only the screen tour, so it reached master; landing 2 shortened the text (c591d386). Landing gates now include `layout.spec.ts`.

## 2026-10-05 — macOS bash 3.2 treats an empty array as unbound under `set -u`
The overnight Laya run made 0 teacher decisions. `gen-data.sh` passed `"${options[@]}"` with an empty array, and macOS's /bin/bash 3.2 aborts on that with "unbound variable". The script died in its first second, and the fine-tune then failed with no logs. Expand optional arrays as `${arr[@]+"${arr[@]}"}`. Overnight scripts should check each stage's output (the decision count is 0) and stop with a clear message, not carry on.

## 2026-10-05 — WebKit Playwright aborts under heavy load
During the 7b release check, 41 WebKit tests failed with `browserType.launch … exitCode=134` and click timeouts while the load average was 74 (other landings running). The same build passed 58/58 once re-run at lower load, and Chromium was unaffected. A WebKit failure at a load average above about 40 is load until proven otherwise: re-run that step when the machine is quieter before debugging. Also, a contrast audit must wait for entrance fades: the faction picker measured 1.2:1 mid-fade.

## 2026-10-05 — Goal-driven AI step 2 against the human-playbook benchmark (Proxy v6): not easier, not harder
Landing p8-goalai on top of reinforce-priority and the 7b UI work (200 Standard games, seeds 3600001+, Proxy v6 against 7 Hard AIs, same seeds both sides). Master: the proxy wins 51/200 (25.5%, CI 20.0-32.0%, median winning turn 180), makes 343 captures and loses 457 cities. Merged: 45/200 (22.5%, CI 17.3-28.8%, median 161), 347 captures, 411 cities lost. Both win rates are inside each other's CI, so the plan layer didn't make Hard easier for a human-like player, even though wars fall from 20.9 to 6.7 per game and total city captures from 1,978 to 1,307. The proxy's captures are unchanged (343 vs 347); why isn't established. The AI now takes about 11% fewer cities from the proxy. The sim doesn't record "AI recaptures" separately; cities lost by the proxy is the nearest measure.

## 2026-10-05 — The overnight supervisor never ran: session crons need an idle, living lead
Overnight 2026-10-04→05 the ops supervisor ran 0 times: its last run was 13:08 EDT, and only 1 of about 30 scheduled ticks reached the lead after 13:30. A CronCreate job fires only when the lead session is idle, and the lead was busy with agent hand-backs almost continuously. The Claude app then died at about 23:18 and stayed down until 07:50, so no tick could fire and no agent could resume. Meanwhile the Laya run I launched at 21:49 died in its first second, and I never checked its first output. Fixes: a token-free shell watchdog (`tools/ops/watchdog.sh`) tracks jobs registered via `tools/ops/bgjob.sh` and sends macOS notifications. It doesn't alert when the app is down, because nothing else runs then (user). And the lead checks every background job's first unit of progress within minutes of launching it.

## 2026-10-05 — A title-screen message must live in the menu's flow, and Playwright's scrollIntoViewIfNeeded scrolls the app root
The older-save message was absolutely placed under the menu and overprinted the footer at 1000x600; the menu already had only about 35 px of slack, so it now sits above the buttons, hides the tagline and trims button padding at short heights, and makes Single Player primary while it shows. Separately, `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded` on a turn-report line also scrolled the whole app, shifting the screenshot: the tour now scrolls `.report-body` itself (`scrollWithinReport`). A forecast card has `pointer-events: none` and a height cap, so it cannot scroll; the tour now asserts the last line's text box ends inside the card (`expectWhole`).

## 2026-10-05 — Laya with a Claude teacher: hardcoded Codex tier names, and a 73% escalation rate
Once the bash fix was in, the first Sonnet-teacher game recorded 33 decisions. But `gen-data.sh` still accepted only answers labelled `luna`/`sol`, so it rejected the Claude answers and stopped. Training then refused to run, because it needs teacher answers from at least two games for a by-game holdout. The check now accepts any teacher tier, and games already recorded are skipped instead of paid for again. Cost: Sonnet escalated 24 of 33 decisions to Opus, and a Quick game took about 1,100 s, nearly all of it LLM time. Before scaling Laya data, check whether Sonnet-only answers are good enough targets, or tighten the escalation threshold.

## 2026-10-05 — Laya fine-tune "below chance" was the untrained head plus too little training, not a label bug
The first Laya fine-tune scored 12% on the first 100 held-out questions (chance 28%). Round-trip tests (`tools/llm-seat/test_laya_train.py`) show the label mapping is right for choice, score and noul, and the training logits match `system_one`'s. The cause was that the untrained head is anti-correlated with this teacher (23% on all 440 held-out questions, 8% on threat scores), and 300 steps at batch 1 and lr 2e-5 barely moved it: the old checkpoint scores identically to the base on all 440. Reporting only the first 100 questions (all early turns) also hid that: the baselines there are 67% and on the whole game 50% and 54%. With 6+ epochs at batch 8 and lr 1e-4 it reaches about 50%, level with the "most common answer per question family" baseline and below the scripted default (54%); longer training overfits (79% train, 46% held out). Gotchas:
- Evaluate on every held-out question and print chance, per-family majority and scripted baselines every time; a number without them says nothing.
- Calling `agent.model.train()` also turns on dropout in the frozen encoder (the first version did): keep a frozen encoder in `eval()`.
- A frozen encoder's output never changes, so cache it once (the default with a frozen encoder; `--no-cache` turns it off) instead of re-running 400M parameters every epoch. On a busy GPU the one-off cache took 10 minutes; three concurrent MPS jobs make every step several times slower.
- `scripted` on a research or focus question is a tech or city name, while the menu also has a literal `scripted` option meaning "let the scripted AI choose"; both count as agreeing with the scripted AI.
- In zsh, `$VAR` holding several file paths (or a glob) is not split or expanded when passed to a command; use a wrapper script or an array. It cost two failed 10-minute waits here.
- `grep` in a pipeline buffers when its output is a file, so a background run looked stuck for 20 minutes. Use `python -u` and log directly.

## 2026-10-05 — Playwright WebKit crashed macOS WindowServer twice and logged the user out
The two "computer crashes" (2026-10-04 23:17 and 2026-10-05 08:15) were WindowServer aborts. Each one logged the user out and killed every app, the Claude app and all agents included, and about 20 hours of agent time were lost. The 08:15 report shows the request came from a process named "Playwright" (`voucherInfos`), in `_XWindowListCreateImage → CreateCaptureSurface → iosurface_create_common → WSIOSurfaceDebugTallyAndAbort`. WindowServer was holding 46,099 IOSurfaces (55.1 GB) and had been running only since the previous crash. Playwright's WebKit on macOS takes screenshots through WindowServer window capture, even headless. With several agents each running screen tours (about 90 shots per size) in WebKit, the capture surfaces piled up until WindowServer hit its limit. Chromium headless renders off-screen and is unaffected. Fix: `app/playwright.config.ts` refuses WebKit on the macOS host unless `LEGATUM_ALLOW_HOST_WEBKIT=1`. Only `tools/release/check.sh` sets it, with 1 worker. Agent and landing gates use Chromium only.

## 2026-10-05 — Icon pass: a test stops family-icon reuse coming back
The art audit found tiers reusing a family icon (Reactor was the Workshop, Embassy the Monument) and `■` falling back for War Shrine, because `icons.ts` aliased tiers to their family. The alias map is gone and `unitIconName`/`buildingIconName` (in `data.ts`) resolve a faction piece by its own name (`building-customsbastion`), so a new unit, building or faction replacement with no SVG fails `src/icons.test.ts` instead of silently showing a neighbour. The set is solid silhouettes with knocked-out detail (the README said monoline, which only one file was). Drawing gotchas: a shape plus its holes must be one `evenodd` path (overlapping solids in one path cancel), touching parts need a 0.2 overlap or a seam shows when scaled. Two first drafts read as the wrong thing (the Legionary shield with a bolt looked like a battery, the Oathbearer great helm like a tombstone), so check every new icon at 16 px before keeping it. Replacing the text glyphs changed `textContent` that tests asserted (`'▼1 bad'`, `'✓ Ready'`), so those now assert the icon element instead.

## 2026-10-05 — IOSurface leak follow-up: one clean WebKit run doesn't leak
Measured with `ioclasscount IOSurface` (baseline about 400–650, with ±200 noise from other apps). 40 WebKit screenshots then a clean close: no growth. 40 screenshots then SIGKILL of the browser: no clear growth at this scale either. So a single run, clean or killed, isn't the leak. The 46k build-up came from something about the night's volume: many parallel agents' tours, and WebKit processes aborting under load (`exitCode=134` at load 74). It wasn't reproduced. The watchdog now logs the IOSurface count every 5 minutes (`.progress/jobs/iosurface.log`) and alerts at 10k. That gives evidence for when it climbs, and a warning long before WindowServer's limit.

## 2026-10-05 — A forecast-feasible campaign can still never begin, so a seat-declared war can sit on paper
The plan layer's `campaign_forecast` (a siege rollout over the units in reach) and the campaign's own start gate (`campaign_can_take`, over units within 4 hexes, plus "no new campaign while the army holds a captured city") disagree. Forcing a seat-chosen war into the plan on a bare forecast gave 3 paper wars in 6, all at 12% capture odds or less; the plan dropped them within 2 to 23 turns and the war ended in peace. The seat's war options now require capture odds of at least 15%, no held captured city, and (for a force already in reach) the campaign's own gate (`Planner::campaign_against`). Forced wars usually price negative in the plan's own terms (value -30 to -180): the seat may choose a bad war, but never a war that cannot start.

## 2026-10-05 — The PlayerView has no events: infer own losses, finished items and peace from turn-to-turn diffs
The LLM seat sees only the PlayerView, which carries no event log, so the verification's advice to "show losses from the events" cannot be followed literally. `SeatMemory::observe_changes` diffs consecutive views instead (own units gone, `producing` Some to None, research current to None, War to Peace) and names the cause heuristically. A city with `producing: None` at turn start has just finished an item or was just founded; the scripted AI queues the next one in the same turn's orders, so the briefing must say that rather than "builds nothing".

## 2026-10-05 — `laya_plan_trace` needs a single-game decisions file
The multi-game warlike run writes all games' decisions into one JSONL, and the tool keys directives by turn, so games overwrite each other (78 order differences). Filter the file to one `game_id` first; then the replay matches the tape exactly (0 differences in 148 turns).

## 2026-10-05 — An LLM teacher's "bug reports" mostly describe its briefing, not the game
Of the Laya teacher's top bug claims, only one was a real behaviour bug: paper wars. Under the Hard plan layer a campaign starts only from the plan (`campaign.rs:285`), so wars the LLM seat declared were never fought. The rest came from the seat's text briefing:
- turn-start "builds nothing" and "Research: none" on the turn after a completion;
- no barbarians in "seen near us";
- the Sparta desertion drawback missing from the faction text;
- `SeatMemory` recording declarations but never peace;
- the army line dropping Settlers and Scouts.

The teacher only sees a snapshot every 5 turns, so one-turn states read as long idle spells. Lesson: before filing an LLM's finding as a game bug, replay the game from its recorded answers (`tools/llm-seat/replay-decider.py`) and check the events. Fix briefing gaps before generating training data, or the student learns from mislabelled states. Report: `.reviews/laya-findings-verify-2026-10-05.md`.
## 2026-10-05 — The tech unlock card: four gotchas
Building the unlock card (branch `claude/p8-tech-unlock`) turned up four things worth keeping. (1) `celebrate/moments.ts` is imported by Playwright specs, which run in plain Node and can't load SVG or other asset imports, so everything it imports must stay asset-free: `unlockCard.ts` reaches `ui/techCardUnlocks.ts`, which took `YIELD_KEYS` from `bits.tsx` (SVG imports) and broke every spec until those moved to `ui/yieldKeys.ts`. (2) The e2e specs serve `app/dist`, so a screenshot taken without `npx vite build` shows the previous CSS: a z-index fix looked like it had failed until the rebuild. (3) A `position: fixed` card inside the moment lane is still painted in the lane's stacking context (z-index 4), under the right and bottom docks; the lane itself needs the higher z-index (`:has(.moment-card.unlock)`). (4) `itemDescription` drops a Byzantium Archive's "+2 border growth" (the Byzantium gets no Archive Science, so the gains list is empty and the text starts with ". No Science"); the card's sentence rebuilds it from `BUILDING_STATS`, but the tooltip text still has the bug. Also: a research finishes at most once per empire per turn, so there is at most one unlock card per turn and no card queue is needed; and the tech sting is now `sting.major` because the card is tier major.

## 2026-10-05 — Unlock card review: held cards, hidden panels still "in view"
Following the UI review, the unlock card is now held until its own controls, Esc or Return close it (`interrupt` is a no-op while it is up, because the map, the unit panel and the report all call it on gestures), and it waits out any open `<dialog>` instead of hiding behind it: a modal dialog makes everything outside it inert, so a top-layer card could not be clicked, hence deferral through a MutationObserver on `dialog[open]`. Two traps: an IntersectionObserver still reports a city card as "in view" when a modal research panel covers it, so unseen marks keep clearing behind the panel (a tour test that re-set the set while the city panel was mounted raced with that; deselect first), and a sticky city head means an auto-scroll must align the list to the head's bottom edge, not use `scrollIntoView`, which put "Choose what X builds" half under the head. Pinned cards in the Just unlocked group already carry the same text as normal cards (same `renderOption`, tested): building descriptions are one short line, only units add upkeep.

## 2026-10-05 — Reward emblems: the engraving mask eats later shapes, and the lost-city card never shows
Art pass 2 gave each reward kind its own emblem (`app/src/assets/rewards/`, generator `rewards/src/build-rewards.mjs`, kit shared with the seals in `heraldry/src/kit.mjs`). Two gotchas. (1) In the `Seal` canvas an engraved `cut`/`cutFill` removes everything drawn before AND after it unless the later shape has a `halo`; a cornerstone star and a half-tone face both vanished until given `halo: 1`. (2) A lost city never shows its lane card: the alarm screen announces it first and the story filters it, so only the report line exists; fixtures for it time out waiting for `.moment-card`. Also: the standard fixture's two players are on one team, so a "captured" or "lost" fixture needs a third empire (player 2) as the other side. The camp card's gain list is now data on the moment (`Moment.gains`, icon names only, so `moments.ts` stays asset-free for the Node-side Playwright specs); at 1000x600 the explanatory detail lines are hidden to fit, gains stay.


## 2026-10-05 — Population is territory (claude/p8-territory): the rules, the retune and what broke
- **Rules as built.** A city works every owned tile within 3 hexes; each population above 1 claims one tile, nearest ring first and the best by focus within a ring (user: "nearest first, prioritizing quality"; also beyond 3, where tiles are claimed but unworked and hatched on the map). Losing population releases the worst unworked tile, else the latest. Buying is limited to tiles within 3 hexes, any order, priced `base × 100/125/150%` by ring (`tile_price`; the base is 20 + 10 per tile gained, speed-scaled). The former border-growth sources became Food (accepted).
- **The opening got far faster, and the spread wider.** Working all of ring one at population 1 took a 48-game Hard Standard probe (seed 9100, 60 turns) to a median first Settler at T11 and second city at T17 (target T18/T25). Levers, in probes: capital +0 Production and 65% first Settler T12/T19; capital +0 and 100% T16/T23 (30 Food first growth) or T18/T24 (45); capital +2, 100%, T17/T23 at 60 Food, T19/T25 at 75. Shipped: capital +2 kept, first Settler full price, first growth 65 Food: Standard T18/T24, Quick T16/T23. Dropping the capital's +2 looked tidy but left grassland-only capitals at 3 Production (a Settler in 25 turns at Quick). Start Production now spans 5 to 14 by terrain (it was 5 to 6), because the whole ring is worked: expect bad-start complaints, and consider a Production floor that scales or a start-fairness rule in mapgen.
- **Forecasts hold territory fixed.** The growth and tall-empire forecasts don't add the tile a growth will claim, so they undershoot growth slightly. Tests that compare a forecast to real resolution must box the city in (own every other tile), or the real city grows faster than forecast.
- **Fixtures whose premise was the old rules.** (1) Archived states: `border_progress` is gone and `grown_tiles` is new, so their re-serialized hashes changed; the pins in campaign_memory, defense_retreat, scout_threats, settler_threats and ranged_garrison moved, with a note in each. (2) Archived PlayerViews carry old city-detail fields: `escort-t168-t170.json.gz` was migrated (border fields dropped, new ones empty). (3) Scenario files embed a state with a checked hash: `cargo run --release -p legatum-sim --example rehash_scenarios -- scenarios/*.json` re-encodes them (baselines untouched). (4) A hazard fixture's geometry moved on its own: a rival city that grows now claims ring 2 toward the settler and puts a foreign border in its sight, so `settler_threats` keeps its rival city in resistance (resisting cities neither grow nor claim). (5) Counterfactual replays diverge sooner now (a unit built earlier, a treasury shortfall): the replays drop what no longer applies and say why.
- **AI site scoring.** `site_potential` and `site_strategic` scan the 37-tile work radius, ring 3 at half weight (each yield halved, rounded down), foreign tiles still -8 each, so scores run about 1.5x higher than before and `settler_min_site_score` (60, an evolved gene) is now a looser bar. Not re-tuned: no balance sims while the AI is weak. Pinned in the proxy v2 test (36 plains x 7 - 8).
- **Mock parity is mostly free if the mock ports whole functions.** `settle_territory`, `next_claim`, `next_release`, `tile_price` and the buy gate were ported one-to-one and the WASM parity tests (diplomacy, purchases, resources, ruins) passed after assertion updates; the cost was in fixtures that hard-coded `borderProgress`.

## 2026-10-06 — The Monument and Assembly are cut; what the old border sources became (claude/p8-territory)
- **The user rejected "everything becomes Food"** (an Archive giving Food didn't fit); the lead decided the rest: the Japan keeps Food (Brood Hall +2, resonance +1); the **Archive** is +3 Science; **Civic Networks** is "bought tiles cost 25% less" (a percent in `tile_price`, shown in the price breakdown); **Public Administration** makes a new city claim its best ring-2 tile (by the growth order, not counted in `grown_tiles`, so never released, and no `tiles_claimed`); **Soil Reclamation** is +1 Food in every city and the **Granary** needs no tech (buildable turn 1). Monument and Assembly are gone everywhere. Public Administration is a prerequisite (Closed Habitats, Federated Markets), so it was never a dead end.
- **Brood Hall as its own variant.** `BuildingKind::BroodHall` replaces `Monument` (`#[serde(alias = "Monument")]` so old states, orders and fixtures still load); `available_to_faction` makes it Japan-only. Anything that used the Monument as "the cheap neutral building" (tests, the AI's pre-Settler filler, the Switzerland' recommended opening) now uses the Granary. The AI filler order is now Scout (only if none), Granary / Brood Hall, and a Soldier only as the last resort (the Soldier at 40 would otherwise beat a 50-Production Granary and cost Switzerland 3 Gold a turn).
- **Dropping the Monument shrank the Germany's goal to five ordinary buildings** (`ORIGINAL_BUILDING_FAMILIES`: Granary, Workshop, Market, Laboratory, Walls). Faction text says "all five".
- **An effect that is only Science is "does nothing" for the Byzantium**: the Archive (+3 Science only) briefly became faction-unavailable for them (it had been available through its +1 Food), which would have locked them out of the Relay Uplink (it needs an Archive). The lead's call: no building that does nothing, so the Archive stays unavailable to the Byzantium and `data::great_work_requirement_for(faction, building)` drops the Archive requirement from the Byzantium's Relay Uplink (core `building_blocker`, the AI's `investment.rs` plan and the mock all use it; `great_work_requirement` stays the faction-blind base). Note `building_blocker`'s Great Work checks (one per empire, already done) must not hang off the requirement: a `None` requirement used to skip them.
- **Opening pace did not move** (48 Hard games, seed 9100, 60 turns): first Settler T18 and second city T24 at Standard (T16/T23 Quick), the same as before the cut, so the first-growth cost stayed at 65 Food. The Granary is buildable at once but the AI rarely benefits early.
- **Fixture fallout.** The serialized name changed (`Monument` to `BroodHall`), so every archived-state hash pin moved again (campaign_memory, defense_retreat, scout_threats, settler_threats, ranged_garrison, attack_opportunism; the stored hashes in settler-cleared-goto, settler-detour-loop and rush-defense-t21-t29-orders were rewritten). `Assembly` can't load at all, so it was stripped from the two AI view fixtures (listing/unlock entries and building lists); a serde alias can't map a removed building to a different one. Scenario states re-encoded with `rehash_scenarios`. Bare sims need `tools/sim/repin.sh` for the tapes.
- **The AI fixture builder lists items by tech only**, so it offered the Brood Hall to every faction (the Switzerland picked it); `ViewBuilder::detail` now filters it with `available_to_faction`. Similar silent assumptions will bite the next faction-only item.

- Conflicts were mostly mechanical; the semantic ones: master's `faction_excluded_building` was the right home for "Japan-only Brood Hall" (core and TS mirror); master's unlock sentence code read `borderGrowth`; `BRANCH_ICON` named `building-monument`; the Sparta Settler premium (every Settler, first included) now stacks with the full-price first Settler (113 Production at Quick, from 112.5 rounded).
- The `llm_seat` war-gating test depends on a game that reaches a feasible campaign: the new economy changed which seeds do (3610001 no longer; 3610005 does).
- A city-panel e2e that tests "arriving with something new" must unmount the panel first (`select(null)` and wait), because the scroll-to-the-group effect only runs when the city id changes.
- Byzantium-only exclusion: the Archive is unavailable and the Relay Uplink drops its Archive requirement for them (`great_work_requirement_for`); the 48-game Standard probe stayed T18 / T24 after the merge.

## 2026-10-06 — IOSurfaces jumped from 250 to 32k during the Playtest 8 release check, then plateaued
The watchdog's IOSurface log showed 248 (23:52), 1,474 (23:57), 25,484 (00:02) and 32,262 (00:06), then flat. Over that window the release check ran its single-worker WebKit step (23:52–23:56) and the Tauri bundle (23:56–00:03), and the ChatGPT app started its computer-use node servers at 00:00 (screen capture also goes through `_XWindowListCreateImage`). The surfaces weren't released afterwards, and only a WindowServer restart (log out or reboot) frees them. Attribution is still open: `tools/release/check.sh` now logs the IOSurface count before and after every step. A logout or reboot before the next heavy session resets the count safely, instead of a crash at about 46k.

## 2026-10-06 — City-built improvements (branch claude/p8-improvements): what was not obvious
- **A new `ProductionItem` variant compiles but is silently wrong where code matches `Unit | Building` by hand.** `City::holds_progress`, `switch_production` and the BuyItem "carry the bank" branch listed the two old kinds explicitly, so an Improvement's progress counted as unassigned Production and switching away lost its bank. Rust's exhaustiveness check can't see `matches!` lists; the banking and purchase tests in `crates/core/src/improvements/tests.rs` caught it. Search for `ProductionItem::Building {` in `matches!`/`if let` before trusting a clean build.
- **`#[serde(tag = "kind")]` forbids a variant field called `kind`**: the item is `Improvement { improvement, tile }`, mirrored as `{ kind: 'Improvement'; improvement; tile }` in TypeScript. The tile rides in the item (so queue, bank, order and event all name it) and the kind is redundant but validated against the tile's resource.
- **Capture keeps improvements** (lead, 2026-10-06; the first build cleared them): Civ-familiar and conquest is the core loop. Razing, release and founding on the tile still clear them.
- **Strategic stockpile income deliberately ignores improvement.** Requiring a Mine would orphan deposits beyond the 3-hex work radius (they can never be improved), so owning a known deposit is still enough and the Mine only adds the tile's yield. Revisit if Mines should gate Trooper and Siege Engine supply.
- **Claim and release scoring keep valuing a resource at its improved worth** (`owner_tile_potential`); only the worked yields, the claim preview and the yields lens show what the tile gives now. Otherwise claims stop leaning toward resources the moment improvement is required.
- **Mock/WASM parity fixtures need three teams.** A two-empire fixture whose players share a team ends the game on turn 2 by elimination victory ("The game is over") even though both engines agree; use a third empire on its own team (`improvements.wasm.test.ts`). A release test that needs population to stay put sets `resistance` (no growth), because a full ring-3 city grows every turn.
- **Opening pace** (48 Hard Standard games, seed 9100, 60 turns, `OPENING_TURNS=60 opening_pacing`): first Settler T19, second city T26, against T18/T24 before. 160 of 384 empires had an improvement among their first six builds (median turn 23: Farm 86, Pasture 41, Quarry 33, no Mines), so the AI builds them after its first Settler, as intended. 50-game Standard Hard smoke: 0 panics, 0 stalls, all finished, 24 of 400 empires never founded a second city (6%). Not balanced; the pace shift is the cost of paying 30 Production before a Wheat tile's Food counts.
- **Art lessons.** The first sprite pass read as a technical diagram (thin single strokes, rectangles); what made it read as engraving was hatching on the shadow side of every cut (furrows, quarry walls, hillside), irregular outlines instead of ruled ones, and a tonal wash under the line, all at the hill pen weight. Check the art at high zoom first (a throwaway Playwright spec at camera zoom 3), then at the real tiers. The mine's base must sit well inside the hex (the clip cut the adit off at first). A tooltip on the picker row covered the very tile the hover was lighting, so improvement rows carry none.

## 2026-10-06 — Art pass 3 (map): what the shots taught
- **A stale `app/src/wasm-pkg` makes 183 vitest tests fail** (`BUILDING_STATS[...]` undefined in every `*.wasm.test`) and looks like a regression. Run `LEGATUM_WASM_NO_OPT=1 scripts/build-wasm.sh` (35 s) in a worktree before trusting vitest; `cargo build --release -p legatum-server` and `npm run build` before the e2e gate.
- **Translucent strokes drawn per piece double up where pieces overlap**, which is exactly what made the Japan roots look like beads and barbs. Fill every piece of one object as a single path (nonzero rule) and set the alpha once.
- **Label de-collision must not depend on which labels are on screen**, or names pop in and out while panning. It depends on zoom only; offscreen labels still take part in the greedy pass.
- **Seat colour as a tint**: the UI's seat colours mixed 30% with umber keep their hue and all eight stay at least 38 RGB units apart (`seatColors.test.ts`); a parchment plate with a seat-colour rim reads at a glance, the old solid fills did not sit in the atlas palette.
- **`e2e/art-map.spec.ts` + `src/test/mapArtFixture.ts`** give a busy, deterministic map (eight empires, camps, ruins, resources, a Settler) for before/after shots at normal and far zoom; the fixture's `free()` must read the live state, not the pre-found-city one, or units stack on one hex.

## 2026-10-06 — Laya and the LLM seat removed; what stays true
- **Why:** imitation learning is capped by its teacher (the LLM strategist only tied scripted Hard, and Laya reproduced habit-level answers at 46 to 51% held-out). The AI is now built as search over the deterministic engine (legatum-design skill). Journal kept: `docs/sim-results/laya/journal.md`.
- **Removed:** `crates/ai/src/llm/`, the sim's `--llm-*` flags and `llm_seat.rs`, `PlanReason::Directive` and the directive hooks in the planner (research, production, garrisons, diplomacy, plan candidates), `tools/llm-seat/`, `docs/llm-seat.md`, and the examples `laya_plan_trace`, `llm_prompts`, `llm_timeline`, `snipe_trace`, `long_war_probe`, `plan_timing`, `rush_campaign_replay`. Scripted play is byte-identical (`tools/sim/repin.sh` shows no diff).
- **Kept for the search AI:** `plan.rs::campaign_outlook` and `campaign_against` (read-only campaign forecasts, `#[allow(dead_code)]` until used).
- **Still true from the LLM-seat work:** a recorded LLM "bug report" usually describes its briefing, not the game; replay the recorded game before filing a bug. Entries above that mention the seat or `llm_seat` tests are history.

## 2026-10-06 — Playtest 8 final UI review fixes
- **The turn-1 city panel is the worst case for height at 1000x600.** The italic claim-rule paragraph (3 lines) and a separate red disclosure line pushed the first build card under the fold. The rule is now the tooltip on "claims <tile>", the tile totals are one labelled neutral line under the territory bar ("Tiles: 13 Food, 10 Production (2 Food eaten)": gross tile yields, not income), and an idle city with nothing queued scrolls "Choose what X builds" under the sticky head when its first card is below the fold. Tests: `production-picker.spec.ts` (turn-1 fixture, card in view at scroll 0) and a tour shot `11a-turn1-city-panel`.
- **Flex items each add the `gap`, so a label of inline text pieces ("Grows to 2 · claims X · unlocks Settler") is wider than its text.** Wrap such a label in one inline block (`.cp-grow-label`) and drop decorative icons; the extended one-line test (pop 1, "unlocks Settler") caught the wrap at 1000x600 that the pop-4 test missed. Keep "ring N" in the tooltip only.
- **One glyph, one meaning:** the red diamond is NEW only. "Needs a decision" is a round "!" badge (`.research-chip.needs::after`); "pending until Ready" is a hollow ring with a gap plus the word "pending" in the Now-producing line.
- **A transparent border used as click padding gets painted over by an unrelated `border-color`.** `.relation-badge.war.new` set `border-color: var(--bad)`, which turned the race-row badge's 5px transparent top and bottom border red and made "War · new" look cut off. The race label also clipped (`overflow: hidden` on the label) its badge frame; the name now takes the ellipsis, not the badge.
- **The release label was already right:** `tools/release/check.sh` exports `LEGATUM_BUILD_LABEL`, and `npm run build` and the Tauri `beforeBuildCommand` both inherit it (`LEGATUM_BUILD_LABEL="Playtest 8" npm run build` bakes "Playtest 8"). "Dev · after 7b" in the tour came from a build with no label, which is what Playwright serves from `app/dist`.
- **The Playwright specs serve `app/dist`, not source:** run `npm run build` after every UI edit before re-running a spec, or the old UI is tested.
- **Old-save title:** Continue is retitled "Saved game (older version)" and disabled for that autosave after the failure (kept in a module set keyed by save time for the session), so it can't repeat the failure.
- 21g's HUD shift came from the report opening scrolling `.game` (`overflow: hidden` still scrolls programmatically); the tour now resets `.game` scrollTop after `openReport`, and the 21g screen shows the HUD in place.

## 2026-10-06 — Search AI step 1 (claude/search-ai): rollouts beat the analytic plan layer in 1v1
- **Determinization from one seat's view is close enough to trust.** On a real Standard game the determinized state reproduces the seat's own units, cities, treasury and research exactly (own city yields within 1-2 per city, from guessed tile assignments), every known city, and each rival's public city count, army and population titles within a few percent. Without buildings the rivals' Largest Economy came out 20-40% low and the seat's win chance read 826 instead of 652 (turn 80) and 6,868 instead of 5,607 (turn 120). Giving rival cities economy buildings up to their public economy title (`search::calibrate_economy`) brings it to 680 and 5,572.
- **Rollouts must seed every unknown, including a rival with no city yet.** A first version gave such rivals no units at all, so turn-1 rollouts played against an empty seat. Now they get their starting Settlers at the sampled map's start for their seat.
- **A far Japan city made the scripted work plan divide by zero** (`investment::work_plan`: a Japan building's network gain is negative far from the capital, which drove the rate to 0). Only odd determinized states hit it, but it's a real latent bug; rates are now floored at 1, and plain-Hard tapes are unchanged.
- **Rayon nesting inflates measured decision time in the simulator.** With games and rollouts on the same pool, a search turn sometimes waited 52 s: while waiting on its rollouts the thread stole and finished another whole game. Per-turn decision time from a parallel sim run is an upper bound; measure it with games played one at a time (`examples/search_cost.rs`).
- **1v1 results (512 games, all 64 ordered faction pairs from both seats, Small map, seeds 7100001+):** plain Hard against plain Hard scores 47.1% for side A (seat 0 wins 51.8%, no seat bias). Hard+Search k3 h20 d2 scores 51.6%; paired on the same games it gains +4.5 points [+1.7, +7.3] (38 games better, 15 worse). Horizon 10 gains only +2.3 [-0.3, +4.9]. Most of the gain is against the Byzantium (+27 points): see the next entry.
- **The Byzantium wins 1v1 against Hard by squatting at Peace, and plain Hard never fights it.** Plain Hard beats the Byzantium in 23% of games and declares war in 21%; with search it wins 52% and declares war (a containment war, around turn 51) in 86%. Written up with a recommended AI fix in `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/exploits.md`.
- **1v1 faction spread under plain Hard is wide:** as side A, Japan scores 70% and Sparta 20% against Hard. That's the 1v1 balance baseline, not a search effect.
## 2026-10-06 — The release check doesn't leak IOSurfaces; last night's jump came from elsewhere
With per-step IOSurface logging, the Playtest 8 release check (including the 1-worker WebKit step and the Tauri bundle) stayed at 293–327 surfaces throughout. So the 250 → 32k jump on 2026-10-05 at 23:57–00:03 wasn't the release check. The other candidate in that window is the ChatGPT app's computer-use helpers (started 00:00:13), which capture the screen. The 46k crashes were also preceded by heavy parallel WebKit tours, so host WebKit stays limited to the release check, and the watchdog's 10k alert stays on.
- **Search overhead isn't the rollout cost.** Per rollout: copying the seat's brain 1 µs, determinizing 0.8 ms, cloning the state 48 µs, against 260 ms for a 20-turn rollout (turn 40, 8 seats). All the cost is the engine turn itself (views, scripted planning, resolution), which the perf work targets; a slim rollout brain isn't worth building.
- **A 1v1 win-chance fit differs sharply from the 8-player one.** On 512 Small-map duels, held out on 256: Brier 0.1858 with the 8-player weights, 0.1656 with refitted ones. In 1v1 the army and science shares get almost no weight (−0.07, −0.18) and economy, late VP progress and cities carry it. Each player count needs its own fit (`winchance::WEIGHTS_DUEL`).
- **All search options together are expensive.** The 50-game 1v1 smoke with `t,e,o,oh=80,p,v=3` passed (0 panics, every game ended) but averaged 25 searches per game and 1.1 s per search-seat turn, peaking at minutes on turn 1 (5 openings × 2 samples × 80 turns, on a loaded machine). Posture searches are most of the count (825 of 1,277).
- **Improvements UI review fixes (2026-10-06).** "Dormant" is one check (`isDormant`) behind the picker count, the faded medallion and the tile card; the review's "6 dormant but 5 faded" came from a fixture whose fogged ring-3 tile had no remembered owner (`TileView.owner` is the last known owner), so test fixtures must fill the team's `knownOwner` for tiles it can't see. The zoomed-out improvement is a solid ring on the resource marker (dashed = dormant), not a second glyph beside it. The Improvements group moved after Buildings, rows are two lines ("Farm" over "Wheat · 1 tile W", with non-breaking spaces so distance and bearing never split), the hovered or picked tile's ring is drawn above unit counters, and a captured city keeps its improvements.


## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increment 1 (claude/sim-speed): where a game's time goes and what was waste
- **Measure with instructions, not seconds.** At load 30-90, timings move ±30% and a single-threaded run lands on E-cores. `/usr/bin/time -l` reports instructions retired and cycles; instructions move under 1% run to run. `crates/sim/examples/sim_bench.rs` prints a digest of every final state hash (and `views` a digest of every seat's PlayerView JSON), so an optimization is proven output-identical before it is timed. Ledger: `docs/sim-results/perf/`.
- **State cloning isn't the rollout cost.** `GameState::clone` takes 10 µs (duel) to 60 µs (eight players, turn 100), against 2 ms to 40 ms per rollout turn. The time is the per-turn loop: AI planning (about 65%), building a PlayerView per seat (20-30%), and resolve_turn (12-16%).
- **AI "what if" projections were most of the waste.** `diplomatic_campaign`, `choose_strike`, `forecast_siege` and `city_risk` clone the whole PlayerView to flip one relation or move a few units. `diplomatic_campaign` then builds a complete second Planner, which scored every tile for settling. Copy-on-write view parts (`view::Shared`: research, economy, city detail) and settling scores computed on first read (`analysis::LazySites`) removed most of it.
- **Hot small lookups were linear scans.** `can_enter`, `allied`, `team_of` and `faction_of` scanned players and relations per tile inside A*, and `squatted_near` scanned every unit for each of thousands of yield previews. Now they use `EntryTable`, a direct index, and one squatted list per city.
- **`find_path` only reads the map's geometry.** AI callers built a fresh `Map::new` per path; the view's cached terrain map gives identical routes.
- **Line of sight is translation invariant.** The nudges (±1, ±2, ±3 out of 1024) never produce an exact half, so `rounded_step` commutes with whole-tile shifts and the steps come from a table (test `line_table_matches_rounded_steps`).
- **Dead ends:** memoizing `movement_path` across projections had about 0% hits (each projection asks different routes). A reachability prefilter for staging spots saved nothing (the failing searches weren't the cost).
- **Result of increment 1, single-threaded instructions against master a59968be:** eight-player Standard Hard 143G → 70G per 2 games (2.1x fewer); Quick duel 103G → 55G per 16 (1.9x); Small-map Standard duel 33G → 19G per 4 (1.7x). Tapes, pins and views are byte-identical.
- **Search levers in 1v1 (2026-10-06, 512 paired games each, base k3 h20 d2 w = +3.1 points over Hard):** information triggers +0.2, rival economy calibration −0.9, postures +0.4, rival policy variants −0.3, 4 samples −1.0, horizon to the end −1.6, opening search −3.5 [−7.5, +0.5] (worse), and the omniscient upper bound +0.1. None beat the base. Since perfect information adds nothing, the determinization isn't what limits search. What limits it is the choice set (the plan layer's few candidates, with tactics still scripted) and the noisy 2-sample horizon estimate. More rollouts of the same choices don't help; better choices (tactical search, step 2) are next.
- **Opening search picks no stable book.** Over 512 games it chose Default 153, Snowball 137, Rush 113, Economic 87, GreedyExpand 22, with no faction or matchup consistent enough to distil, and it lost 3.5 points against Default-only. The 40-turn opening rollouts mislead (a strong-looking early economy that loses later). No opening book proposed.
- **Hard+Search is cheap in 1v1 once rollouts have their own pool.** `examples/search_cost.rs` (games one at a time, rollouts on the dedicated pool, load average about 35 from other work): k3 h20 d2 w over 6 games, 682 turns. The search seat averages 3.3 ms per turn; the 24 turns with a search averaged 73 ms (p95 106, max 119). Both seats together: 4.4 ms per turn, max 121 ms. That's far inside pillar 7's budget.

## 2026-10-06 — Search gains are capped by the candidate set, not by information or evaluation
In 1v1 (512 games each, paired against search h20 with 1v1 weights, which is +3.1 pp over plain Hard), four levers gave no gain:
- information triggers: +0.2;
- rival economy calibration: −0.9;
- posture search: +0.4, at about 4× the cost;
- rival policy variants: −0.3.

Search can only pick among the plan layer's few candidates (Economy, Campaign per target, Contain, Snipe), so re-planning sooner or evaluating more precisely changes little. The next gains must come from widening what search chooses: production, settle sites, where to defend, and tactical combat (step 2). Rival variants can't show a benefit when the opponent is always plain Hard.

## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increment 2: battle forecasts were quadratic, and LTO pays
- **`forecast_attack_mode` looped over all 41×41 roll pairs.** Damage to the defender depends only on the attacker's roll and vice versa, so every pair total factors into one sum per side times the other side's roll count (exact integers). That alone was about 8% of an eight-player game.
- **Memoize per planning pass what reads only fixed facts:** `exposure_key` and `safe_campaign_tile` read only the view and the planner's enemy lists, and A* asked them for the same tiles hundreds of times.
- **A distance bound beats a smarter search:** a step costs at least 1 and a turn walks at most `max_moves` steps, so a strike tile further than `approach × max_moves` can skip its A* with an identical result.
- **Build profile:** `--profile sim` (fat LTO, one codegen unit) is about 10% fewer cycles; `-C target-cpu=native` adds nothing on Apple silicon. mimalloc is about 15%.
- **Timed against master on a quiet machine (load 6):** Quick duel 32 → 108 games/s on 16 cores; eight-player Standard Hard 3.5 → 1.23 CPU seconds per game.

## 2026-10-06 — Some 8-player games stall at the 1,200-turn cap in a VP standoff
In a 50-game Standard Hard smoke, one game hit the 1,200-turn safety cap with scores 9, 9, 8, 8, 8, 7, 7, 2: several empires sat one VP short of 10 and nobody could close. Master alone does the same on other seeds (7700042), so the improvements branch didn't cause it. It's a pillar 11 and game-length issue, a leader that can't finish rather than a runaway. Track it under the supervisor's game-length metric, and look at it once the AI is competent (titles that never flip? coalitions blocking every leader?).

## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increment 3: a simulation-only fast path (user: build only what decisions read)
- **The AI seats' views are now decision-only (`ViewDetail::Decisions`) on every target.** They're never shown to anyone, so `Session::ai_orders` (native and WASM alike, which keeps cross-target tapes identical) and the runner skip the labelled yield sources and focus reasons, tech unlocks' growth forecasts, income-change previews, buy quotes, upkeep previews and population unlocks. Humans' views stay `Full`. Measured share of a sim turn: 5–8%.
- **The engine built a whole PlayerView (city details, research, economy, forecasts) per player per turn just to check move orders against that player's map.** `ViewDetail::Map` (tiles, units, cities, players, diplomacy) does the same checks: 2–4% of a turn.
- **Some human-looking text is a decision input; audit before cutting.** The Byzantium salvage planner parses the "Tech diffusion (N ...)" label of tech costs, and buy and production blockers are read as conditions. The rule for any new cut is in `view::ViewDetail`'s docs: grep `crates/ai` for every read, then check the bench digests and repin.
- **The runner's telemetry costs under 1%** (legatum-sim and the bench retire the same instructions for the same games); nothing to gain there.
- **Labels formatted for zero lines:** `promoted` and terrain defense formatted a label before `Breakdown::add` threw the zero line away, on every combat forecast (2.4%).
- **Merging master's tile improvements (e3794ba8) kept every digest and view identical to master**, and the fast path is 17–19% fewer instructions on top of it.
- **AI speed, first pass (claude/ai-speed, 2026-10-06; decisions byte-identical, same bench digests).** On top of sim-speed increment 3, eight-player instructions fell 30.22 → 25.25 G and the Small duel's 19.0 → 14.1 G (Quick duel 17.2 → 16.3). The big wins:
  - *Failed path searches.* A* to an unreachable goal explores the whole component, and campaign staging asked for up to 18 spots per unit, 66% of calls returning nothing. Once a search for a unit fails, the tiles it can reach (with and without the safety filter) are flood-filled and kept for the planning pass. That's exact because reach only shrinks within a pass: reserved tiles only grow, and the occupancy tables and safety memo are fixed.
  - *Prospective planners.* `diplomatic_campaign` built a whole planner (analysis, hard-defence routes) for every rival every turn. It now returns None first when that planner's answer must be None: a faction that never sieges, no known city of theirs, no city of ours, fewer than two units that could march.
  - *Strike tiles in lower-bound order.* ceil(distance / moves) bounds a tile's turns, so tiles are tried in (bound, distance, tile) order and the search stops once no tile can beat the best.
- **The AI no longer parses view labels.** The Great Work reserve read "Not enough gold:" from `buy_blocker`, and the Byzantium salvage planner read the count from "Tech diffusion (N empires know it)". The view now carries `ProductionOption::buy_block` (`economy::BuyBlock`, from the same rule check that words the blocker) and `TechOption::known_by`. Every AI blocker check reads `buy_block`. Presence-only string reads remain in `DiplomaticAction::blocked_reason` and `ProductionOption::resource_blocker`; `diplomacy.rs` also copies `blocked_reason` text into reply explanations, which are part of the orders.

## 2026-10-06 — The AI reads some human-facing text, which blocks cutting it from sims
The sim-only fast path (`ViewDetail::Decisions`) found that the Byzantium salvage planner parses the "Tech diffusion (N" label of tech costs, and that buy and production blocker strings are AI inputs. Labels can't be dropped from AI views until those reads become structured fields. Rule: the AI reads only typed data, never display strings. Grep `crates/ai` before cutting any view field. Measured split of an 8-player sim turn after the cut: about 70% AI planning, 15% AI-seat views, 13% resolution. Human-only work was 10–13% before it was cut.

## 2026-10-06 — Append-only logs use git's union merge
Parallel branches each appending to `docs/learnings.md` or the perf and AI-quality ledgers caused a merge conflict at almost every landing (five times on 2026-10-06), and each one cost a gate re-run. `.gitattributes` now sets `merge=union` for these append-only files, so both sides' new lines are kept automatically. Check the order after a merge if it matters (the ledgers sort by date anyway).
- **AI speed pass 2 (claude/ai-speed-2, 2026-10-06; byte-identical).** On increment 5, the eight-player run takes 23.26 → 22.35 G instructions (−4%) and the Small duel 13.24 → 13.04 G. The planner now memoizes strike forces per (city, window, war state) and each city's attacks and unit arrivals for its defence risk. Prospective planners share the main planner's hard-defence declaration reach: reach depends only on each rival's own relations, which a prospective war with another team doesn't change. Movement reconciliation reads bitmaps instead of tree sets. Little AI-side hot work is left. What remains of planning is mostly core work it calls: `settlement::settlement_candidates/analyze_sites` (about 18% of planning, through `plan_settlers`), site scoring in `count_good_sites`, `route_around_friends` A*, and building `Ctx` for prospective planners. 91% of `diplomatic_campaign` calls are against a team we're at peace with, so sharing the main planner's analysis (possible only at war) isn't worth it.

## 2026-10-06 — A mark true of every tile in a set is noise (8.1 polish)
The white diamond drawn on every worked tile while a city was selected was unexplained in the 8.1 review. Cause: every owned tile inside the work radius is worked (`worked_tiles_squatted` in the core: all owned tiles within `CITY_WORK_RADIUS`, except tiles a foreign Byzantium unit squats on), and tiles beyond it already carry the hatch, so the diamond duplicated the absence of hatch. Removed rather than explained. Related: the tile card said "+1 Production" (base bonus) where the picker said "+2" (preview gain at current tech); both now read `improvementGain`. The gain function should be the only source for a per-tile improvement forecast.

## 2026-10-06 — New core view fields need the mock in the same change (parity break fixed)
AI speed pass 1 added `ProductionOption::buy_block` and `TechOption::known_by` and was gated on cargo only, so `app/src/mock/diplomacy.wasm.test.ts` (2 parity tests) failed on master. The mock now produces both through the same rule order as `economy::buy_block_with_reason` (`buyCheck` in `mock/rules.ts`, with `buyBlocker` as its wording) and `economy::tech_known_by` (`techKnownBy`); `buy_block` is omitted when unset, like core's `skip_serializing_if`. The parity comparison was not relaxed. Any change to `crates/` view structs needs the WASM build plus full vitest before landing.
- **Search step 2, skateboard (1v1, paired against base search k3 h20 d2 w on the post-tile-improvements game, 512 games each): none of the wider choices helped.**
  - Hold-city candidates (`g`): +1.7 [−0.8, +4.1].
  - A target per front (`f`): +0.0. Only 53 of 2,249 searches had two feasible campaign targets, so in 1v1 there is rarely a second front to choose.
  - Settle/defend and build-ratio postures (`p` with Ranged/Melee): +0.3 [−2.3, +2.9].
  - Tactical stance (`s`, attack-threshold ×0.8 / ×1.25 on engaged turns): −0.1 [−2.0, +1.8]. In 86% of stance searches all three stances ended with identical material.
  Knob-level levers barely change what the scripted army does. The next gain needs real move-level choices (per-engagement attack, wait, pull back, focus fire), not more plan or knob variants.
- **The tile-improvements merge changed the game:** the same base search on the same seeds changed 104 of 512 outcomes. Ledger rows before it are a different game version; the chart marks the break.

## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increments 5–6: cheap view clones, shared settlement facts, PGO
- **A PlayerView clone is now mostly reference counts.** Units, cities, diplomacy, players, titles, milestones and standings joined research, economy, city detail and tiles as copy-on-write `view::Shared` parts, so an AI projection copies only what it edits. Test code that edits views needs `.into()` for whole-field assignments. `DerefMut` gets no two-phase borrows, so `v.diplomacy.relations.push(Relation { b: v.team_of(x), .. })` must read `v.team_of(x)` first.
- **Every settler re-derived the same per-view settlement facts**, and so did the planner's site scores: border risks, enemy reach, and each tile's legality and potential. `settlement::SiteBasics` computes them once per view. Potential comes from per-tile contributions, and legality from a city-spacing mask. `debug_assert`s compare both against the direct functions, so a stale or wrong cache fails the debug test run.
- **PGO** (`tools/sim/build-pgo.sh`: instrumented build, training on the bench, `xcrun llvm-profdata` merge, which reads rustc's LLVM 22 profiles fine) is 3–8% fewer cycles on top of fat LTO. Duels gain most and eight players about 1%. `-C target-cpu=native` adds nothing on Apple silicon.
- **Where it stands:** allocation plus memcpy is about 10% of self time even with mimalloc. Most of it is per-turn view and AI planning allocation, so pooling game states across games wouldn't move it. Movement reconciliation's A* (`route_around_friends`) cost is the search itself: tile masks for its set lookups saved about 1% and were dropped.
- **Move-level tactical search skateboard (`x`, 2026-10-06): it really chooses, but doesn't win more yet.** At a city attack with a visible garrison, the engaged units' orders are default / focus fire / spread / wait. Each is resolved this turn, played 3 more turns, and scored by material. Over 512 1v1 games (paired against base search): +0.4 [−1.6, +2.3]. The choice differs from the scripted default in 36% of 4,666 decisions, and only 15% had all four options equal (the stance lever had 86%). The search seat lost 11% fewer cities (354 against 400) but captured fewer (396 against 417). The 3-turn material score values keeping units alive over taking cities. Levers to try next: a capture-weighted score, a longer tactical horizon, more samples.
- **Tactical levers (2026-10-06, 512 paired 1v1 games each): none makes the city-attack choice pay.**
  - Capture-weighted score (`xc`): +0.6 [−1.4, +2.6].
  - 6-turn tactical horizon (`xh=6`): +0.4 [−1.8, +2.6].
  - 4 samples (`d=4`): +2.7 [+0.3, +5.2] against the d=2 base, but the d=4 base search without tactics gets +1.8 [−0.2, +3.7] on its own, and tactics on top of d=4 adds only +1.0 [−1.0, +3.0].
  The tactical choice keeps diverging from the default (36–41% of decisions) without converting into wins, so the move generator isn't generalised yet. More plan samples (d=4) is the most promising cheap lever left, worth a larger-sample check.
- **d=4 confirmed (2026-10-06):** on 1,024 paired 1v1 games, 4 determinizations beat 2 by +1.6 points [+0.3, +2.9] (31 games better, 15 worse), for a search seat at 5.9 ms per turn instead of 4.3. It's now the search default.
- **The exploiting-opponent check is inconclusive.** The human-playbook proxy only runs with 8 players (`--proxy` requires `--players 8`), so the scripted full-game Rush stood in (`--duel-opponent rush`). It doesn't exploit plain Hard: Hard beats it 83.8% in 1v1 (Sparta only 48%). Against it, search scores −1.2 points [−3.5, +1.2] and search with tactics −2.2 [−4.7, +0.4] (paired against plain Hard). With nothing being exploited, search has nothing to defend against. A real exploit check needs a 1v1 human-playbook opponent, or the proxy extended to 2 players.

## 2026-10-06 — Hard + Search in the app (claude/difficulty-search): the engine is WASM, so the budget shrinks with seats
- **How the app runs AI turns:** the Tauri app is a shell around the web UI, and the whole engine (`Game::end_turn`, every AI seat's planning) runs as WASM in the webview on one thread. Tauri has no native engine (only archive commands); `legatum-server` (LAN) is native but runs the same `Session`. Rayon rollouts exist only with `parallel-search`, which only the sim enables, so in the app a search costs the sum of its rollouts, not the 6 ms the 16-core sim reports.
- **One budget for every path.** Determinism across native and WASM needs the same search config everywhere, so a faster native path can't have a bigger budget. `SearchConfig::in_app(players)` depends only on the player count: 2 players k3 h20 d4 w, 3-4 players k3 h20 d2 w, 5-8 players k3 h10 d2 w, all with `z` (stagger). The choice is stored as `PlayerSetup::search` on a Hard seat (omitted when false; plain Hard's tapes and pins are byte-identical, `repin.sh` shows no diff).
- **At 8 players the spike was the problem, not the mean.** One native thread, CPU time (`crates/wasm/examples/app_search_time.rs`, 7 search seats): the full 1v1 budget costs 1.5 s per search (max 3.2 s), 446 ms per turn on average, and p95 2.7 s, max 8.8 s, because the first plan's review is on the same turn for every seat, so 5 searches coincide. h10 d2 cuts a search to 360 ms but 6 still coincided (max 2.6 s). Staggering the first review by the seat number (`z`: `plan.rs::review_stagger`, search seats only) keeps it to 2 searches per turn: max 530-690 ms. Wall time on a loaded machine is useless here (load 95 inflated it 3-6x); read thread CPU time.
- **1v1 is cheap:** a search takes 150-490 ms (max 874) at the full budget, 1-5 per game.
- **WASM is about 1.4x slower than native** on one thread (Node, 8 players all-AI, full budget: mean 189 ms, p95 1.1 s, max 4.6 s, against 133 / 833 / 2,757 native on the same seed).
- **In the actual app** (Chromium, 1000x600, `e2e/search-timing.spec.ts`, human idle after founding, load average 25-35; the ~0.4 s mean of plain Hard is the harness's own round trips): re-run on the merged build (master's engine speedups included): 1v1 mean 472 ms, max 599 (Hard 438 / 577); 4 players 298 / 614 (Hard 287 / 608); 8 players mean 399, p95 651, max 1,065 (Hard 444 / 566). One turn in 100 passed 1 s, none passed 2 s. The pre-merge run was about the same (8 players max 1,091).
- **Cross-target check:** `scripts/wasm-search-check.mjs` plays a 1v1 and an 8-player Hard+Search game through `Game` natively (`examples/search_hashes.rs`) and in WASM (Node), reloading the WASM game from its save halfway, and compares the state hash after every turn. The existing tape check can't do this, because tapes replay recorded orders and never run an AI.
- **Playwright traps found on the way:** a war declared on the human puts a "To arms" card up and an idle End Turn loop waits on it for the whole test timeout (click actionability has no timeout by default), so a long turn loop has to dismiss cards and carry a watchdog. And a Segmented option named "Hard + Search" matches `/^Hard/`: any selector for the Hard level must exclude it.
- **Not done:** a spinner or "thinking" note during End Turn (the main thread is blocked for up to about a second in an 8-player search turn), and a Web Worker (it would unblock the UI but not shorten the wait).

- **Cheaper rollout policies cost more quality than they save (2026-10-06, 512 paired 1v1 games each against the default search k3 h20 d4 w).** Single-thread rollout speed against quality, in points:

  | Level | What it skips | Speed | Quality |
  | --- | --- | ---: | --- |
  | r=1 | prospective war planners and strike forecasts | 1.04× | −0.4 |
  | r=2 | also rivals re-plan every other turn | 1.27× | −3.1 [−5.3, −0.9], significant |
  | r=3 | rivals re-plan every third turn | 1.38× | −1.6 |
  | r=4 | also the seat every other turn | 1.94× | −1.4 |

  Spending r=4's speed on 8 samples or a 40-turn horizon gives −0.7 each; 5 candidates gives −1.4 (the 1v1 pool rarely holds more than 3). Rival planning is a modest share of a duel rollout (views are about 40%, planning 40%, resolution 17%), and lazy rivals make the what-if worlds less faithful. The search default stays r=0. Engine speed (the perf agent's work) is the way to buy search budget.

## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increment 7: cross-turn city yields cache
- **`yields_cache::ViewCache` keeps each city's yield results across view builds while an explicit key holds:** position, owner, faction, population, resistance, buildings, the owner's techs, terrain/resource/improvement of the centre and the city's own tiles within 3, squatters, Byzantium Salvage Works camps, and the owner's city layout (capital, Japan network). Keys are compared by value, never hashed or versioned, so one cache can serve every turn, seat and even unrelated states (rollouts) without going stale. Session, the runner and the bench hold one per game.
- **The hit rate is about 80% per city build, and the gain is smaller than the profile promised: 3–5% of a game.** Most misses are real changes: border growth, population growth and new techs. What a miss costs (recomputing all ~30 building previews) outweighs what a hit saves. The key is checked without allocating, and only once per view build (a build counter).
- **Debug builds recompute every cached read and assert equality.** The full debug test suite and a 60-game debug sim batch (duels, small maps, 4-player mixed brains, 8-player Hard and rotated difficulties) passed with 0 panics. Leaving `population` out of the key on purpose made 2 of 4 debug duels panic, so the cross-check does catch a missing input.
- **What city_detail still costs is per-item and depends on gold:** buy checks and quotes, production blockers and tile claims. It can't be keyed cheaply; making those checks cheaper (booleans without formatted reasons) is the next lever.
## 2026-10-06 — The human-playbook proxy now plays 1v1: it beats plain Hard 75%, and search doesn't defend against it
`--duel-opponent proxy6` seats Proxy v6 as side B of the 1v1 duel matrix (512 games, Small map, seeds 7100001+, all 64 faction pairs from both seats; metric `duel_1v1_vs_proxy`, an exploiting-opponent benchmark, not the primary metric). The proxy still reads only its own view; no bonuses.
- **As shipped for 8 players it only wins 59.0%** (302 of 512, CI 54.7–63.2%). Its 8-player schedule misfires with one rival: (1) it consolidates into peace after about turn 80 (cities ≥ 7, 3 held captures or a turn limit) and then sits on 1,000–3,000 Gold; (2) it only targets cities within 14 tiles of one of its own, but a 40×26 map often has none, so the army waits for a target that never comes; (3) the staging ring (radius 3) fills with 3 shooters and a soldier, and the rest of the army can't path to the far-side spots and idles for 100 turns (the game that taught this: full-health 200 HP walled city, 5 shooters and 3 infantry, no capture in 100 turns); (4) scouts die to barbarians by turn 40, nothing else explores, and the rival is never found (36 of 114 Conquer turns with no known rival city in the traced losses). Half of its losing games had no capture at all, and its win rate by captures was 48% with none, 75% with one, 89% with two.
- **Duel mode** (`view.players.len() == 2`, so 8-player proxy games are unchanged; 64 rotated 8-player games each of v5 and v6 give identical seeds, turns, outcomes, winners and captures before and after) fixes those: never consolidate, any known rival city is a target, staging spills onto ring 4, one infantryman (or a rebuilt scout) explores while no rival city is known, and the army goal is 8 shooters and 4 infantry (stop buying at the goal, as before). Tuned on seeds 8100001: 58.7% as shipped, +9.4 from no consolidating and far targets (68.1%), +0.3 from the ring spill; then scouting (seeker +2.7, rebuilt scout +2.0) and the bigger army (+1.7) each add 2–3 points and together 73.8%. Bigger armies beyond 8/4 (10/4, 10/5, 12/6) were noise.
- **Measured on the standard seeds 7100001: plain Hard scores 24.9%** [21.4, 28.8] (the proxy wins 75.1%, [71.2, 78.6]) against 50% for Hard against itself, so the proxy does exploit Hard. Hard's score is highest as Japan (51.6%) and lowest as Sparta (12.5%), Celts (15.6%) and Switzerland (17.2%). Seat bias 50.2%.
- **Hard+Search doesn't defend against the exploit (paired on the same 512 games):** default config (k=3, h=20, d=4) 24.4%, −0.5 points [−2.0, +1.0], 7 games better and 9 worse; with the fitted plan (`w`) 24.0%, −0.9 [−2.7, +1.0], 10 better and 14 worse. Only 16 and 24 of 512 outcomes change. Search picks plans by rollouts that model the opponent as scripted Hard, so a human-style conquest isn't in its rollouts; it needs the proxy (or a campaign-aware opponent model) in the rollout opponents before it can learn to garrison against it. It costs 8–9 ms per turn at the search seat.
- **Rare stall:** one of 512 games ended at the 1,200-turn safety cap, with the proxy at 33 cities and 9 VP and the rival at zero cities but not eliminated (a hidden unit). The proxy has no hunt for the last enemy units. It scores half a point for both sides.

## 2026-10-06 — Search can't defend against an opponent it doesn't model
With Proxy v6 now playable in 1v1, it beats plain Hard 75.1% [71.2, 78.6] over 512 games. Hard+Search does no better (−0.5 pp, only 16 of 512 outcomes change). The search's rollouts model the rival as scripted Hard, so a human-style conquest never appears in its imagined futures. This also explains why search gained only about +3.5 pp against Hard: Hard never exploits the weaknesses that cost games against a human. The levers are defensive play (garrison warned cities, recapture, hunting raiders) and modelling an exploiting rival inside rollouts, measured against the 1v1 proxy on held-out seeds.

## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increment 8: checks that don't word their answer
- **Blocks were worded even when only their existence mattered.** Production, building, improvement, buy, resource and tile-purchase checks are now generic over `economy::Reason`: `String` words the block, `Blocked` only reports it. AI seats' views keep every block present but empty: `buy_blocker`, `resource_blocker`, unavailable reasons, `buy_tile_blocker` and unlock blockers. The AI reads `buy_block` and presence only. The resource shortage text alone had been computing the empire's resource income for every unaffordable item.
- **`buy_cost_for` built the whole labelled buy quote to read its total.** The quote's "Game speed" line brings the total to the full cost, so the price is `(full + first-Settler discount) × percent`. A debug assert compares it with the quote. Each build option now computes its buy cost once instead of twice.
- **Result:** 4–5% fewer instructions in every scenario. Digests and full views are unchanged, and the debug suite and the debug sim batch passed.
- **Dead end:** computing a city's claimable tiles from the territory the view already gathered (instead of scanning the map three times) changed nothing measurable.
- **AI speed lever B (2026-10-06, byte-identical).** Building the analysis context (`Ctx`) was about 4.7% of an 8-player run: land regions 2.5%, the frontier 1.2%. A prospective planner (`diplomatic_campaign`: our relation with one team set to war) now copies its parent's frontier, and copies its land regions when every tile's move cost is unchanged. Passability changes only if that team owns tiles we couldn't enter at peace, and then the regions are recomputed. Regions, frontier and the Dijkstra distance fields walk a per-map neighbour index table, and `Ctx::move_cost` reads a per-tile cost table. On master 51188132, instructions fell: eight players 19.65 → 18.96 G (−3.5%), Small duel 11.02 → 10.42 G (−5.4%), Quick duel 12.92 → 12.29 G (−4.9%). Sharing alone gave only −1.4% at 8 players, because most prospective wars open the target's territory and force a recompute.

## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increment 9: sight remembered per map
- **Native sight was recomputed for every team on every query:** each movement round's `update_explored`, remembered threats, the views and combat. `visibility::SightMemo` lives on `Map` (serde-skipped, ignored by equality, shared by clones). It reuses a team's sight when the source list (tile and radius) is unchanged, and each source's line-of-sight footprint by (tile, radius). It holds a copy of the terrain and starts over if any terrain differs, so it is exact even in tests and scenario builders that edit terrain.
- **Checking terrain per source cost almost as much as line of sight itself** (it gained 1%). A whole-map terrain compare per update, shared by all teams, plus whole-team reuse, gained 3–4% of a game.
- **Debug builds recompute every reused team sight and footprint.** Sabotage check: matching team sight on source count alone made debug duels panic.

## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increment 10: AI seats plan in parallel within one native game
- **Each seat decides only from its own view and brain**, so `Session::ai_orders` plans the seats on the rayon pool and gathers orders by seat id. Each seat now has its own yields cache. The game is unchanged: `sim_bench latency` plays games both ways and asserts the same final state, and the determinism, WASM tape and pin checks pass.
- **It runs only when called from a plain thread** (a server, a single game, tests). Inside a rayon worker (batch sims, one game per core) and on WASM it stays sequential. Nested parallelism in batch runs only adds work stealing across games (the search agent hit stack overflows that way) and no throughput.
- **One 8-player game's wall time drops about 2.4x** (0.51 → 0.20 s and 0.90 → 0.38 s at load 30). A duel gains nothing. A search seat's own decision is unchanged: its time is its rollouts, which are already parallel. With other seats beside it, a turn now takes about as long as the slowest seat instead of the sum of all of them. The desktop app runs the engine in WASM, so it doesn't get this; the native LAN server and the simulator do.

## 2026-10-06 — Plain Hard lost cities to the proxy with its reserves holding out of reach
`duel_loss_audit` (`crates/sim/examples/`) replays duel tapes and describes every city side A lost. Plain Hard against Proxy v6 (512 tune games, 875 lost cities):
- **Warning and garrisons were not the problem.** 84% of lost cities had a visible enemy army within 3 hexes for 3+ turns, and 84% had a garrison when the losing turn began. Recapture isn't the lever either: only 10% of losses left Hard locally stronger in the three turns after (and it retook most of those).
- **Concentration was.** Overall armies were even (9.3 against 9.5 military units at the loss, 15.7 against 14.3 units lost per game), but locally the proxy had 5.5 units within 3 of the city against Hard's 2.7. On the losing turn Hard's healthy units 4–6 hexes away mostly *held*. The cause: `defend` only sends a unit to a safe tile within 2 of the city (`can_defend_at` refuses any field tile in enemy reach) and holds it otherwise, and `avoid_free_damage` pulls back any unit that does stand in reach. With the besiegers around the city there was never a safe tile, so the reserve waited out of reach while the city fell.
- **The fix needed three parts together.** Aiming each army's threat at its nearest city plus a 2x defence margin: +2.3 on tune seeds, but −1.0 held-out. Relief alone (stand in reach near an attacked city, close in when no safe tile is left): +3.3 tune, +0.2 held-out. All three: +7.0 tune (2048 games), +7.6 report, +6.3 held-out, and 51.2% against the old Hard. The margin pulls more units to the city and relief lets them reach it; neither helps much alone.
- **Dead ends:** sizing the standing army to the rival's (by visible units or the public Strongest Army value) changed almost no games; Hard's army count isn't the binding constraint. Counterattack odds genes (`counterattack_win_percent`, `counterattack_loss_percent`) changed zero outcomes. A bigger defend radius, `recover_hp` 40 or more military production gave +1–2 points at most.
- **Native engine in the Tauri backend: assessed (2026-10-06), not built.** Measured on master c42d4374 plus this branch, same seeds, whole `end_turn` for an all-AI game (`examples/app_turn_time.rs` native, a Node script for WASM), load average about 10:

  | game | WASM (one thread) | native, seats in parallel | native + parallel rollouts |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 8 players plain Hard | 6.5 ms/turn | 2.7 | 2.8 |
  | 8 players Hard+Search (in-app budget) | 51 mean, 211 p95, 313 max | 48 / 216 / 255 | 16 / 59 / 68 |
  | 4 players search | 28 / 211 / 537 | 8 / 60 / 117 | 4 / 22 / 67 |
  | 1v1 search | 15 / 37 / 390 | 10 / 2 / 251 | 3 / 2 / 51 |

  The seat-parallel planning on master helps plain Hard (2.4x) but not search turns, because a search seat's rollouts only parallelise with the `parallel-search` feature. At the full 1v1 budget (k3 h20 d4) in 8 players one search turn peaks at 3.5 s on one native thread (about 4.8 s in WASM) and at 0.5 s with parallel rollouts: the native path's real value is affording the full budget, not speeding the shipped one (master's engine speedups already put the in-app budget well under a second).
  - *Shape that works (hybrid):* WASM stays the engine for every synchronous read (`view`, `validate`, `forecast`, settlement sites), and only `endTurn` goes native: a Tauri command takes the save and the human orders, resolves in `Game::from_save_json` + `end_turn_json`, returns the result and the new save, and the page `load`s it. `client.endTurn` has exactly one call site (`store.ts`, already inside a `setTimeout` behind the "Resolving turn" overlay), so making it async is local. Reload equivalence for plain Hard and for search seats is already tested.
