  - *Cost:* a save is 0.5-0.8 MB at turn 50-100 (more late), WASM `save` 12 ms and `load` 25 ms, plus two IPC copies and a native parse, so about 60-100 ms per turn in mid game, more than a whole plain-Hard WASM turn (6 ms): use it only for games with a search seat, or ship the save without the archive. The Tauri crate would depend on `legatum-wasm` and `legatum-ai` (`parallel-search`); it also builds for iOS and Android.
  - *Risks:* (1) determinism: a bigger native budget makes a desktop game differ from a browser one with the same seed and orders, so the budget level would have to live in the setup (a save then replays slowly, not wrongly, in the browser); (2) scenario and archive state sit outside the plain save; (3) Playwright can't drive Tauri, so the command path has no CI cover beyond a parity test (native result equals WASM result for the same save and orders) and manual runs.
  - *Effort:* about 1-2 days for a working skateboard (command, async `endTurn`, parity test, manual verification), another day for a budget level in the setup. Not small, so not built. Cheaper alternatives for a bigger budget: a Web Worker unblocks the UI but doesn't shorten the wait, and wasm threads need SharedArrayBuffer, atomics and a nightly `build-std`.

## 2026-10-06 — The desktop app runs the engine in WASM, so native speedups don't reach the player
Parallel AI seats (increment 10) cut one 8-player game's AI time about 2.5× on native, but the Tauri app runs the engine as single-threaded WASM, so the user's games see neither the parallelism nor native code speed. Simulations and the LAN server do. Running the engine natively in the Tauri backend is being assessed (effort and gain), and it matters most for the in-app "Hard + Search" difficulty.

## 2026-10-06 — Faction sheet art pass: space, not art, was the constraint
The sheet already spent every pixel at 1000x600 (Sparta overflowed by 54 px once a portrait and icon medallions were added). What bought the room back: a 2-column facts block built as two independent flex columns (win and lose left, countered by and goal right) instead of a row-aligned 2x2 grid, which saved about 28 px because one long Goal no longer stretched a row; a 6 px stage margin (a 0.2 s `rise` animation with a 10 px translate made the old fit test read 603 px, so layout tests now wait for `getAnimations()` to finish before measuring); a 36 px header; the crest pinned inside the portrait corner instead of overhanging into the trait rows. Only the 1000x600 numbers decide the layout; at 760 px of height and up a media query hands the room to a bigger portrait. The 512 px leader PNGs are about 480 KB each, so the picker uses 360 px JPEGs (about 65 KB each) for both the hero and the gallery thumbnails. Review shots and the fit numbers come from `e2e/faction-sheet-art.spec.ts` (`FACTION_SHOTS=<dir>`).

## 2026-10-06 — A native select can't show a different closed label from its list without tracking "open"
The closed `<select>` shows the selected option's text, so "Hard + Search (experimental)" clipped at 106 px. Fix in the lobby: `AiLevelSelect` renders the selected option short ("Hard + Search") unless the list is open (tracked from mousedown/keydown, cleared on change/blur/Escape), and the AI-level column is 134 px (the faction column gave up 26 px, since the name column was already at its minimum and the grid overflowed the panel). Consequence for tests: an option's accessible name depends on whether it is the selected one, so assert the full name on a seat that is not on Hard + Search. Also: Playwright serves `dist`, so a CSS-only change needs `npm run build` before the tour shows it.

## 2026-10-06 — Cities heal 20 a turn, not 5 (playtest 8.2: "too slowly")
- **Rule:** a city below 200 HP regains 20 HP at the end of a turn (10 turns from empty), unless it took battle damage that turn or an at-war military unit is adjacent (Civ 6's rule; was +5 with only the adjacency test, 40 turns to full). "Damaged this turn" is `City.last_attacked_turn == state.turn`, now always recorded in `battle::apply_stage` (battles resolve before `heal_cities`, so a hit on turn T blocks T's heal and T+1 heals). The old opt-in `city-heal` experiment (heal after a full unattacked turn) is deleted: this is the default now. A 0-HP city whose garrison is being shot is not "damaged" (attacks hit the garrison), so it heals 20 unless an enemy is adjacent; if sieges of fallen cities get easier to break, look there first.
- **The AI needed no logic change.** Its only encoding of the old number was a comment ("a survivor next to the city stops its 5-HP regeneration"); the two proxy campaign forecasts (`proxy_v5/v6::needs_rotation`) added `CITY_HEAL` to a struck city's projected HP, which was wrong under the new rule (a struck city doesn't heal), so they now add it only when the city took no damage.
- **Measured (information, not a gate), Standard Small 1v1, 512 games of plain Hard vs Proxy v6, seeds 7100001+:** Hard scored 32.5% [28.6, 36.7] before and 34.2% [30.2, 38.4] after, paired +1.7 points [-1.1, +4.4] (30 games better, 22 worse): faster healing helps defenders a little, within noise. Mean turns 127.4 → 126.6, proxy captures per game 2.15 → 1.96, stalls 1 → 0. 50-game Standard Hard smoke (seed 9100): 0 panics, 0 stalls, all finished, 21 of 400 empires never founded a second city (5%).
- **UI:** the city card shows "Heals +20 a turn · full in N turns" or, in red, "Not healing: an enemy military unit is adjacent" (`CityDetail.turns_to_heal`, `heal_blocked`, mirrored in the mock); the health tooltip and Guide state the rule; the attack forecast on a city with HP adds a standing note "Damaging X stops its +20 HP end-of-turn heal this turn" (a `forecast-note`, hidden below 760 px height like the other standing caveats: at 1000x600 the walled-garrison forecast has no room for another line, and the city card still says it).
- **Pins moved** because healing differs wherever a city was damaged: two of the three 40-turn mixed tapes diverge in hash from turn 26 / 37 (recorded orders identical), the default-AI and scripted-Hard city-fire runner digests, and the two archived garrison replay hashes in `ranged_garrison.rs`. See `crates/sim/tests/fixtures/README.md`.
## 2026-10-06 — Eliminated empires kept leaking into active lists after the race row was greyed
Playtest: an eliminated Athens row sat greyed in the race with a "War" badge, using a row while "1 more" was folded. The race, the diplomacy list and the title compare were already alive-aware in places, but the greyed row came from `buildTracks` returning every standing. Filtering rows alone was not enough: the expanded race also listed the dead empire under Great Work commitments ("Athens: No commitment") and as a milestone holder, found only by the new screen-tour state's `not.toContainText` assertion. Also: ranks must be computed after the filter (a dead empire with a milestone can outrank the living). Core's definition is `alive: false` (no cities and no Settlers, `check_elimination`), and the view exposes it, so the UI reads `PlayerPublic.alive`, never a city count. Rule and helpers are in the `legatum-ui` skill; fixture is `app/src/test/eliminatedFixture.ts`.

## 2026-10-06 — Alliances against the runaway leader carry no signing fee (catch-up rule, pillar 11)
Cause: in the 8.2 playtest, 11 of 13 AI-to-AI Alliance proposals failed on the 60 Gold fee (AI treasuries 0-60, armies eat the income), so the coalition warning had nothing behind it. Rule: while `coalition::target_state` names a target, an Alliance between two teams that are both at War with it (neither being the target) costs no signing fee, for humans and AIs; upkeep is unchanged. One function, `diplomacy::signing_fee` (view mirror `signing_fee_view`), is read by offer and accept validation, the activation payment, the action quote and AI scoring, so no path can still charge or require the fee; `DiplomaticAction.fee_note` carries "No signing fee: coalition against X" for the UI. The target and the wars are read from the state at the start of the turn (the same `before` that records `SecurityTreaty::coalition_target`), so a proposal is free only while both conditions hold when it is accepted. Sim telemetry now counts `alliances_free` and `alliances_by_target`.
Measured, information only (8 players, Standard, Proxy v6 against 7 Hard, 200 games, seeds 3600001+, same seeds; before = master plus the telemetry commit): AI-AI Alliances 629 to 660; Alliances signed while the proxy was the coalition target 76 (41 games) to 101 (50 games); 47 signed free; proxy wins 27/200 (13.5%) to 28/200 (14.0%), so no visible effect on the human-like benchmark. Alliances already formed in this benchmark before the change (about 3 per game), so the fee was not the only brake there; the 8.2 game's starved treasuries are what the rule is for.
What can still block a coalition Alliance between two AIs (read from `ai/diplomacy.rs`, not measured): both teams must already be at War with the target, and the AI declares that war only under its own ratio and legality checks; first contact (`NOT_MET`) between the two; the 15-turn acquaintance wait before offering; the treasury runway test in `TreatyCost::affordable` (gold after the fee plus 20 turns of any deficit after upkeep must stay non-negative, so a broke AI with negative net income still refuses a free Alliance); the AI sends one offer per turn, to its best score; scoring needs a score of 0 or more after the held-Alliance penalty and the wars it forecloses; no Alliance with a team at 60% progress, a betrayer, or the containment target. Whether unanswered proposals (2 of 13 in 8.2) come from the response window or the slot rule is not established.
## 2026-10-06 — Premium pass 2: the 1000x600 report and inbox are ~295 px wide, so portrait art must stack, not sit beside text
The story column at 1000x600 is only ~295 px (the diplomacy panel's 510 px `min()` collapses to it). A portrait, a headline, a treaty emblem and the empire name did not fit in one row (the headline was clipped behind the emblem in the first build). What worked: the emblem is pinned on the portrait's lower-right corner (where the faction crest sits on the sheet), the quote spans the full width under the portrait row, and Accept/Decline still land above the fold (the existing e2e checks it at both sizes). Other findings: (1) the race panel's head was "clipped" because the auto-scroll to the titles scrolled the head away inside `.vp.open` (overflow:auto); the fix is a sticky head plus scrolling the target to just under the head's height, and a soft fade above the sticky footer so a row cut at the edge reads as a scroll. (2) Report icons need no event-by-event plumbing: `ReportLine.icon` is filled once in the `formatEvent` wrapper from `eventIcon()` (`reportIcons.ts`), and lines without one fall back to their category's icon. (3) Playwright wipes `test-results/` each run, so a tour that must be compared before and after has to copy `test-results/screen-tour` out after each run (and a worktree needs `target/release/legatum-server` built, or symlinked from a checkout whose server is current, or `/api/archive` returns 400 and the console-error fixture fails the test).
## 2026-10-06 — Premium pass 1: the old Victory card overflowed with eight empires, and `git stash` is shared between worktrees
- The staged fixtures (3 players, turn 2, 0/10) hid that the old Victory card did not fit at 1000x600 with the real worst case (two humans plus six AI = seven rows): it scrolled by 197 px and clipped the crest. Game-over fixtures are now `app/src/test/gameOverFixture.ts` (solo 10/10, team 17/17, a rival's win, your elimination; eight empires, a single human so defeat shows no "Pass to next player"). VP in a fixture comes from `milestones` (RelayUplink is 2), `factionGoalDone` and the `titles` holders array; a faction goal is worth more than 2 for some factions, so tune the totals from the rendered standings.
- `PortraitFrame`, `PortraitRoundel`, `Ribbon`, `Medallion`/`MedallionRow`, `PieceCard`, `StarDivider` and `.paper-grain` live in `app/src/ui/premium.tsx` and `premium.css`; the faction sheet uses them and its 1000x600 shots are pixel-identical to before. New screens compose these.
- **Never `git stash` in a worktree of this repo:** the stash stack is repo-wide, so another agent's push between yours and your `pop` makes you pop (and apply) their work. For a before/after shot, build the old code in a separate worktree or copy the files, never stash.

## 2026-10-06 — The 8.2 Sparta crush was missing AI opposition, not faction strength
Full report: `.reviews/playtest-8.2-sparta-analysis.md`. Playtest saves replay exactly on master (core rules identical; only AI code differs), and the AIs' saved plan memory gives their reasons, which makes user games first-class diagnostics.

The human (no Settlers until T53, Soldiers from T2) won with three tactics:
- surprise-war Settler snipes on T18 and T22;
- serial capital captures with 3–5 Soldiers;
- peace used as a tool.

Every AI flagged the human as the top threat, the coalition rule fired at T34, and four AIs chose Contain. They made 7 attacks in 12–25 turns of war and never took one of the human's empty cities (5 of 6 ungarrisoned at T52). Other failures:
- 11 of 13 coalition alliances died on the 60 Gold signing fee;
- AIs disbanded defenders for upkeep mid-siege;
- reserves sat frozen.

Proxy v6 misses this playbook: it builds Settlers first, assaults only undefended cities and consolidates after two captures. It needs a "conqueror" variant.

## 2026-10-06 — Choosing a map size now snaps the seat count (playtest: "it stays at 8 even when I click small")
`setMapSize` in `app/src/lobby.ts` trims or raises the active empires into the size's range (Small 4, Standard 5–8, Large 8). Trimming takes only default AI seats (random faction, default name) from the bottom, never a human, a customised AI, or a seat whose removal would leave fewer than two teams; if only those are left, the count stays and the existing "maps suit …" note shows. Raising appends default AI seats. In a network room trimmed seats are closed rather than removed (slot ids are stable) and raising reopens them once all eight slots exist. Going outside the range afterwards with × or add-seat keeps working and shows the note. Presets turn Custom when seats change, as `removeSlot` does.
- Alarm screens and the lobby reuse the same blocks. Gotchas: the alarm's laptop-height rule `.alarm-crest .crest { width: 120px !important }` also caught the crest pinned on a portrait frame (it blew up to 120 px), so rules aimed at the old big crest are now `:not(.alarm-leader)`; and a seat row's faction trigger is a bordered control with a 48 px roundel inside, so the roundel's outer ring (4 px of box-shadow) needs 2 px padding inside the button or it touches the border. `.seat-table`'s own box is narrower than its rows by the scrollbar gutter, so panel-edge checks measure `.lobby-card`.
- `app/e2e/game.spec.ts` (five tests) fails on master at the lobby step: `getByRole('radio', { name: /^Hard(?! \+)/ })` matches both "Hard" and "Hard+Search" (the segment label has no spaces), a clash from the Hard + Search labels, not from this pass.

The all-AI level segment is labelled "Hard + Search" (it was "Hard+Search", which a `/^Hard(?! \+)/` selector also matched, breaking five game.spec lobby steps). Its label now wraps instead of cramping, and `lobby-seats.spec.ts` asserts an 8 px gutter at 1000x600 and 1440x900. Choosing Small now trims seats itself, so specs no longer close seats 5–8 by hand.

## 2026-10-06 — The release check exited 0 with a failed Playwright step
`tools/release/check.sh` runs every step and logs `exit N` for each, but the script's own exit code came from its last command, so the playtest-8.3 check (release-p83) ended "exit 0" in bgjob while its main Chromium Playwright step had exited 1. A green job status was not a green check. The script now collects failed step names and exits 1 with "RELEASE CHECK FAILED: <steps>" (or prints "RELEASE CHECK PASSED"). Always read the per-step summary before cutting a release, never just the job's exit code.

## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increment 11: a search-game bench; rollouts keep a view cache
- **The primary metric is now a search game** (`sim_bench search`): 1v1 Standard on the Small map, seat 0 Hard+Search at `SearchConfig::default()` plus `w` (k3 h20 d4 w), seat 1 plain Hard, seeds 7,000,001.. (4 games). Its digest covers every final state, turn count and search decision (options, sample results, choice), so a speedup is proven not to change what the search picks. Count instructions with `LEGATUM_ROLLOUT_THREADS=1` (the rollout pool otherwise uses every core).
- **A search game is mostly ordinary scripted turns.** One rollout thread, xctrace on master 76e1cb52: 89% of the time is inside rollouts, and within them scripted planning is 55%, views 21% and `resolve_turn` 18%; the real game's own turns are about 11%. So engine and scripted-AI speedups carry straight over to search, and nothing search-specific is a large share.
- **Rollouts built every view without the city yields cache** (`player_view_detail` makes a fresh one per call). Each thread now keeps one `ViewCache` for all its rollouts and seats (thread-local, `search::with_rollout_views`): the cache compares every input by value, so it serves any state. Search bench −6.2% instructions (31.53 → 29.57 G on 76e1cb52; −6.9%, 35.31 → 32.87 G, on 77bcf774); the other rows have no rollouts and don't move. Digests unchanged; a debug search batch (cache reads cross-checked) passed.
- **Gate note:** two WASM-parity vitest cases (Sparta goal boundaries) timed out at the 5 s default with the machine at load 90–160; alone they pass in under 4 s. Treat a vitest timeout under heavy load as load until it fails alone.
- **The rollout hit rate is 68%.** Misses by first differing input: techs 35%, population 24%, the owner's city layout 10%, a city not cached yet 9%, buildings 9%, tiles 8%, a city id that belongs to another state's city 4%. A key that holds only the techs yields read would turn some tech misses into hits (about 1–2% of a search game).
## 2026-10-06 — Cloud sim runner (spot Graviton): build, determinism and gotchas
- **Linux ARM build:** `tools/cloud/build-linux.sh` builds `legatum-sim` and `sim_bench` (`--profile sim`) in a linux/arm64 Amazon Linux 2023 container (Docker Desktop, native on Apple silicon, no emulation), with the same rustc as the Mac. Amazon Linux matches the servers' glibc, which a Debian-based rust image would not guarantee. Cargo's target dir and registry live in Docker volumes so rebuilds are incremental. Docker Desktop's default 7.6 GB memory sufficed for the fat-LTO link. A clean build took roughly 15 minutes. Zig was not needed.
- **Baked-in default paths:** `legatum-sim`'s `--out-dir` and `--out` (replays) defaults are `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`-relative, i.e. `/src/...` from the build container, which does not exist on a server. Always pass both (run-batch.sh does); `SourceRevision` runs `git` there too and reports commit unknown on servers (the batch's BATCH_INFO records the commit instead).
- **Index-scheduled flags cannot be sliced by seed range:** `--strategy-matchups` and `--duel-matrix` pick the matchup from the game index (blocks of 20, seed = first + index/2); `--mirror` uses seed = first + index/2. run-batch.sh refuses the first two and warns on the rotations.
- **New AWS accounts:** the spot service-linked role must exist before the first spot request (Terraform creates it); the Service Quota for standard spot vCPUs may be tiny (this one was 384, so the quota request is off by default; asking for a lower value than the current one is refused). The quota is also the hardest cost ceiling.
- **The launcher's IAM policy is the typo guard:** spot only (`ec2:InstanceMarketType`), Graviton types only, required `project=legatum-sims` tag on create, sims instance profile only.
- **macOS bash 3.2:** the scripts avoid associative arrays and `mapfile`, and guard empty arrays with `${a[@]+"${a[@]}"}` under `set -u`.
- **Determinism (Linux ARM vs Mac): identical.** The Linux aarch64 binary, run in an arm64 Amazon Linux container, matched the Mac build (commit 6e3780bf) on every `sim_bench` digest: duel 16 games `179a6c62b9b8723b` (1063 turns), small 4 games `850a8cbea8b85743` (399), eight 2 games `fa664f881e87bae6` (307), and the view digests (duel `6d30406576077c1f`, eight `590aacb1c36771ba`). An 8-game `legatum-sim --players 2 --speed quick --seed 7000001` produced identical game records. The container is arm64 on the same CPU family, so a real Graviton server is still worth one 8-game check in the first cloud run (compare with these digests); integer-only maths makes a difference unlikely.

## 2026-10-06 — Proxy v7 (conqueror): the playbook beats Proxy v6, but only with a Settler wave after two Soldiers
`--proxy-version 7` / `--duel-opponent proxy7` (`proxy_v7.rs`, forked from frozen v6; v6 and v5 untouched, repin shows no pin change). It plays the 8.2 human's script: Soldiers from turn 2, Settler snipes by surprise war, staging 2-3 hexes from a city at Peace then declaring war and striking in the same turn (the planner runs a second pass on a view with the war applied), assaults on defended cities with 3+ Soldiers (one more per visible defender; walled cities also need two Rangers outside), no consolidation, peace only to a rival whose war stalled (no hit, kill or capture for 8 speed-scaled turns, or 2 losses in 4), one guard per threatened home city, Sparta fight wounded.

Measured on the cloud runner (c8g.16xlarge, same binary for v6 and v7; side A is plain Hard, so lower is a stronger proxy):
- 1v1 Small, 512 games, all 64 faction pairs: seed 7100001 v7 24.4% [20.9, 28.3] vs v6 35.6% [31.5, 39.8]; seed 8100001 v7 26.8% [23.1, 30.8] vs v6 38.2% [34.1, 42.5].
- 8 players, Standard, Hard opponents, 400 games, seed 3600001: v7 wins 19.8% [16.1, 23.9] vs v6 14.0% [10.9, 17.7]. With the brief's exact command (opponents default to Normal), 200 games: v7 32.0% [25.9, 38.8] vs v6 19.5% [14.6, 25.5]. Captures per game 2.3 (v6 0.85), median first capture T40 (v6 T68.5), Settlers trained 3.2 (v6 4.4).
- By the proxy's faction (1v1, 128 games each): Sparta 78% and 81% (v6 48% and 34%), Mongols 98%, Japan 86-91%; weakest Switzerland 55-61% (3 Gold upkeep per extra unit), Germany 47-52% (1-move Soldiers).

Non-obvious:
- **A no-Settler opening loses 1v1.** A pure Soldier-only version scored 76% for plain Hard (about 24% proxy wins; local runs on the pre-merge core, 256 games). Upkeep, not production, is the limit: two free units, then 1 Gold each (3 for Switzerland), and a 1-city treasury runs dry by about turn 25 and disbands the army. The human had Sparta kill Gold and 8 captured cities. Adding Settlers after the first two Soldiers took Hard's score to 31-34% on that core; waiting for 60 Gold first changed nothing (the treasury never reached it). So v7 builds up to 5 cities with Settlers once it has two Soldiers, and is not a Settler-less player: say so when quoting it.
- Lost Soldiers were the bleed (about 15 a game, over a third to walled-city fire). Raising the Soldier goal, the assault strength test or the Ranger share moved the 1v1 score by less than the noise (all within 0.32-0.39 on 256 games), so don't sweep them again; staging that spills outward when a grown city's borders cover the first rings, kill-based stall detection and the wall rule were what mattered, along with the Settler timing.
- An enemy unit that vanishes from the middle of our sight (every tile within 2 visible) counts as killed; a visible-count drop counts moves out of sight and kept resetting the stall clock.
- `tools/cloud/build-linux.sh` shares one cargo target volume (`legatum-sim-cargo-target`) across worktrees, and cargo's mtime check skipped my files because another worktree built last: the "new" Linux binary was the other tree's (it rejected `proxy7`). Touch the changed sources before building, and check the binary with `strings -a legatum-sim | grep proxy7`.
- `tools/cloud/run-batch.sh` refuses `--duel-matrix` even with `--count 1` (the help text says otherwise). Fixed in the script (allowed only at count 1, which the schedule needs). A spot-capacity error (`MaxSpotInstanceCountExceeded`) just means another batch holds the quota: relaunch.

## 2026-10-06 — Playtest-8.2 behaviours against the conqueror proxy: neutral, and Mongols is the hole
Four Hard behaviours from the 8.2 analysis (seize open enemy cities, protect defenders from upkeep disbands, a faction prior for border defence, a longer march against a containment target), measured on the cloud with 2,048-game paired duels against Proxy v7 (the user's conqueror playbook) on three seed sets, and 1,024-game 8-player runs.
- **1v1: neutral.** Paired changes -0.3, +0.9 and +0.7 points (all intervals cross zero). Against the proxy playing Sparta, Hard went from about 22% to 24-26% on every seed set. **Against a Mongols conqueror Hard scores 4-6% either way**: the +1 move and Peace passage let three Soldiers reach a city before any defence our rules trigger. That is the biggest single hole the new proxy shows.
- **8 players: proxy wins 19.5 -> 18.7% and 20.5 -> 19.0%** (paired -1.0 and -2.7, not significant); the proxy's captures per game fell slightly (2.39 -> 2.23, 2.31 -> 2.25). Why the opportunism changes so little isn't measured yet; a likely cause is that Proxy v7 guards its home cities whenever a rival army is visible, so few cities are ever open.
- **Disbands at the front halve** in 8-player Hard games (0.91 -> 0.44 per game, `disband_audit`), with no measurable effect on results.
- **More search budget buys nothing against the proxy** (budget curve, held-out seeds, 2,048 games per point): every budget from 30 to 960 rollout turns per decision is within +0.9 points of plain Hard against Proxy v6, and +1.4 to +1.7 against plain Hard from budget 120 on. The candidate set (about 3 plans) is the cap, not the rollouts.
- **Cloud gotchas:** `run-batch.sh` refused `--duel-matrix` even at `--count 1` (fixed); a batch launched while the Linux build is still running fails or, worse, picks up a half-built binary, so wait for the build's own exit marker, not an `__EXIT` left in an appended log; spot capacity runs out when several agents launch at once (`MaxSpotInstanceCountExceeded`), so batch scripts must retry.
## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increment 12: site potentials carried across views
- **Every view re-summed each candidate site's radius-3 potential** (`SiteBasics`), although tiles' exploration, ownership and terrain change only near borders and scouts. A site's potential, foreign count and strategic count are a function of its 37 tiles' inputs alone (`TileInput`: the `site_potential` contribution and the strategic bit), so a thread-local `SiteMemo` per viewer keeps the sums and the inputs they came from. A view syncs by comparing its inputs tile by tile and forgets the sums of every site within 3 of a changed tile; a generation number makes a view re-sync if another view of the same viewer synced in between (prospective planners, rollouts on the same thread).
- **Gain:** search bench −5.3% instructions, Quick duel −6.5%, Small duel −5.9%, eight players −1.9% (on 76e1cb52). Digests unchanged.
- **Debug builds recompute every reused sum** (`site_sums` asserts against `site_potential` and `site_strategic`). Sabotage check: forgetting only radius 2 around a changed tile panicked both debug search games at once.

## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increment 13: strength totals and claim candidates without the extra work
- **The AI's power estimates built labelled strength breakdowns to read `.total`** (`defense_power`, `attack_power`, `threat_power`, tactics, proxies, barbarians): about 3.5% of a search game went to formatting labels such as "Hills terrain" and "Soldier (ranged)". `combat::unit_{attack,defense,ranged}_total` compute the same sums (debug builds assert equality with the breakdown); every total-only caller uses them. Search −2.6%.
- **Each own city's detail scanned the whole map three times for its territory** (buyable tiles, the buy check and the next-claim preview each called `claim_candidates`), although the view had just gathered every city's territory in one pass. `claim_candidates_from` works from that list (debug builds compare with the scan). Search −2.4% more; total for the increment −5.0% (27.99 → 26.58 G), Quick duel −3.7%, Small −4.4%, eight −3.6%.

## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increment 14: cheaper route searches
- **A* allocated and filled two map-sized arrays per search** and compared 16-byte tuple keys: 175,000 searches in 4 search games, about 20 expansions each. `path::find_path_by_step` now reuses per-thread buffers stamped per search, a neighbour table, and one 64-bit key per open-list entry ((f, g, rank in `Hex` order), which sorts exactly as the tuple did), so the expansion order and every path are unchanged. It falls back to the reference search when a cost doesn't fit the packing or a cost closure searches recursively; debug builds run both and compare. Search −2.0%.
- **Movement declare reads occupancy by tile index** instead of a tree lookup per route edge: −1%.
- **Campaign approaches skip searches whose answer is already known.** `campaign_staging_spot` tries ring spots one by one; once a unit's safe flood fill exists, a spot outside it has no all-safe path, so the answer is None whatever the unfiltered route is (the unfiltered A* still ran for every such spot). And with danger near the unit the answer is always the safe route's first step, so the unfiltered search is skipped there too (no unfiltered route means no safe one). Debug builds assert the skipped search would fail. Search −2.9%.
- **Increment total:** search −5.8% (26.66 → 25.12 G), Quick duel −3.0%, Small −5.7%, eight −3.1% (on 76e1cb52).
- **Dead ends:** per-owner border tables for the declare phase cost more to build than the `can_enter` calls they saved (+0.4%). Land regions remembered across views per viewer (reused when the whole move-cost table matches): −0.2%, dropped; the flood is cheap next to building the move-cost table it would compare.
- **AI movement reconciliation re-simulates about once per plan** (4,939 simulations for 5,048 plans, 6 routes each), so reusing routes across its conflict loop would save nothing; its cost is the route searches themselves.

## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increment 15: decision views skip unread unlock previews
- **Tech unlocks worked out every city's yields with every tech-gated building installed** (`UnlockCity::yields_after`), on each view, for buildings already built or long researched. The only brain read is the Machine Shop's and Autoworks' Production gain (`legatum_ai::production`). Decision views now compute after-yields for those two only; the rest are `CityYields::empty()` with `food_surplus_after` 0 (listed in `ViewDetail::Decisions`; human views unchanged, `sim_bench views` digest the same). Search −3.5% (25.12 → 24.23 G), Quick duel −3.5%, Small −2.8%, eight −1.6%.

## 2026-10-06 — Sim speed, increments 12–15 together on master 77bcf774
- **Search game −17.6%** (32.87 → 27.08 G instructions over increment 11; −23.3% from 35.31 G before the pass), Quick duel −18%, Small duel −17.5%, eight players −9.2%. Every bench digest, the search decisions and the full-view digests are unchanged.
- **What's left in a search game** (one rollout thread, after increment 15): route searches inside closures dominate. The engine's movement rounds 11.5% (`next_step`'s `route_around_friends`), the AI's movement reconciliation 10% (it simulates the engine's rounds, about once per plan, 6 routes each), campaign staging 6%, strike forces 4.9%, city detail in views 6.9%, `update_explored` 3.5%, site candidates and settlers about 5%, `Ctx::build` 3%. A further step needs route searches that read precomputed per-tile tables instead of hex-keyed closures (each edge recomputes tile indices for several bitmaps), or fewer searches.
- **Timed** (release, load 16, 8 search games, one rollout thread): 0.736 → 0.567 CPU s per search game (1.30x); with 4 rollout threads 0.286 → 0.181 s wall per game.
- **Dead end (increment 16 attempt):** per-round "blocked" tables for the AI's movement reconciliation cost more to build than the per-edge bitmap reads they replaced (+1.9%); a per-owner open-border cost table alone gave −0.7%, not landed (branch `claude/sim-speed-16`).
## 2026-10-06 — Concurrent cloud batches can exhaust the account's spot capacity
With two agents' batches running at once in `legatum-sims`, launches failed with MaxSpotInstanceCountExceeded and four sweep points needed retries. The account-level spot instance limit counts every agent's servers together, not per batch. Stagger big batches or keep `LEGATUM_CLOUD_MAX_SERVERS` low per agent, and make `run-batch.sh` callers retry a failed launch rather than give up. The first full sweep (12 points × 2,048 search games) cost 0.36 server-hours, about $0.26.

## 2026-10-06 — Yield amounts as icons: one renderer, a text pass, and three gotchas
- **Every amount goes through `app/src/ui/Amounts.tsx`.** `Amount`/`Quantity`/`YieldAmounts` take structured numbers; `Amounts text=…` finds "+3 Science", "−1 Gold/turn", "146 Production", "+2 VP", "12/31 Food", "120 → 90 Gold" in prose (`ui/amountParts.ts`, unit-tested: a unit word with no number, "T10 Gold" and "300-Production" never match). `Tip` runs its string `content` and `summary` through it, so most tooltips convert without touching their call sites. The new `app/e2e/yield-icons.spec.ts` fails if a build card, tech node, unlock card or report line prints a bare "+N Word" (and screenshots with `YIELD_SHOTS=<dir>`).
- **The word stays as `.sr-only` text inside the amount**, so `textContent`, copy/paste and Playwright text matchers still see "+3 Science". The layout audit now skips `.sr-only` (it flagged the 1px clip as text-clipped).
- **A pure helper cannot share a name with a component that differs only in case:** `ui/amounts.ts` beside `ui/Amounts.tsx` broke `tsc` on macOS (TS1149), so the parser is `amountParts.ts`.
- **Native `<option>` text cannot hold icons** (the phone production picker, the tile-purchase select), and aria-labels stay plain words. HP and population have no icon in the set, so they stay words. Prose with a word between number and unit ("+3 salvage Science", "+10 treasury Gold") never matches: reword to "+3 Science from salvage".
## 2026-10-06 — Playtest 8.3: the Mongols AI never started its goal, and search played exactly like plain Hard
Full report: `.reviews/playtest-8.3-switzerland-analysis.md`; phone chart `docs/sim-results/playtests/8.3-switzerland.png`. The human (Switzerland) beat Hard + Search Mongols 10 to 3 on T92 in a 1v1.
- **The Mongols goal pass skips every Market in a city without a Workshop** (`crates/ai/src/victory/goals.rs:583-595`), and nothing queues the Workshop. The production scorer always ranked a Workshop (1,295) under a Soldier (2,400) or Settler (3,000+). So the AI held a valid three-city trio from T45 and built zero Markets in 91 turns. A temporary gate log showed "no Workshop" on every turn T56-T91. Any Mongols AI game can hit this; a per-faction goal-progress telemetry line would have caught it in sims.
- **Hard + Search issued byte-identical orders to plain Hard on all 91 turns** when both were fed the same trajectory (scratch `p83_ai.rs`: two brains side by side on the tape). Its candidates (Economy, Contain, Campaign, Peace) can't touch production, research or goals, which is where this game was lost. Its first search came on T52, at a win chance of 1.2%.
- **Rebuilding the shipped brain from the tag reproduces a playtest's AI orders exactly** (0 of 91 turns differ). So `ScriptedBrain::production_diagnosis` and per-turn counterfactual brains work on user games, not only on sims.
- **A Swiss can buy Walls on the turn of an assault, and the purchase resolves before the battle.** The AI's siege forecast ("capture at 30%" on a capital holding 365 Gold) didn't price this in, and the AI lost all 4 attackers. Attack forecasts against a Swiss should treat an affordable Walls quote as Walls.
- At 9/10 VP from T68, two bought Soldiers (about 220 Gold, with 324+ banked) would have taken Strongest Army and the game around T70. Nothing in the UI said so, and the human won on T91. The Great Works trace (how a player learns to build one, and what's missing) is in the report's section 4.

## 2026-10-06 — Mocking UI from a real save
- The current master's WASM UI refuses saves from an older release: the embedded archive's rules fingerprint differs. Strip `game.archive` from a scratch copy, and the state loads fine. To get any earlier turn of a real game as a mock scenario or test fixture, replay the save's tape (`Tape::from_json(game.archive.tape)`). The VP mocks used turn 70 of the 8.3 game this way, with 0 hash mismatches.
- Global CSS class names collide (`.tech` and others are global). Mock and new components must namespace their classes.
- The VP mocks compute every number from the real `PlayerView`. In the 8.3 game the "fastest route" at turn 70 was 2 bought Soldiers for 238 Gold to take Strongest Army: the analysis's counterfactual win.

## 2026-10-06 — The Mongols conqueror isn't stopped by army-level defence
Proxy v7 playing Mongols beats plain Hard in 94-97% of 1v1 games, whatever faction Hard plays (Hard as Mongols: 3%). Replays (`duel_loss_audit`, `staging_audit`): war on turns 12-60, some capitals taken by turn 20 by three Soldiers against a one-Soldier, unwalled, 200 HP capital; by turn 100 the proxy has built 20 military units to Hard's 9. Three AI levers, each paired over 2,048 cloud games on the tune seeds, did nothing for the Mongols row and were reverted:
- **Border strength ignoring a rival's troops on its own land** (the 8.3 over-arming): -0.2 against Proxy v7, -0.7 [-1.4, 0.0] against Proxy v6. The benchmarks don't play turtles, so the over-arming it fixes isn't measured.
- **Army matched to a rusher's public army strength** (Strongest Army title value), counted from turn 1 for rush-capable factions: +0.5 n.s.; in a traced game insolvency blocked the extra units from about turn 30.
- **An opening that keeps three troops next to a rusher:** -1.0 [-1.8, -0.2] against Proxy v7. It costs more expansion than it saves cities (the Sparta row fell from 24% to 19%).
- **Gotcha:** gating the first Settler on the new army floor (`army_ok`) silently delayed every faction's first Settler and cost 6 points overall; a paired comparison against the same binary hid it, and only the absolute score against an earlier run exposed it. Compare absolute scores with the last known baseline too.
- The question went to `exploits.md` for the lead and the user: whether +1 move, Peace passage and a turn-12 three-Soldier rush is the intended Mongols identity or a dominant line.
## 2026-10-06 — Wider search: postures alone don't help
Composite search candidates (plan × posture, branch claude/wide-search) measured against Proxy v6 on the tune seeds: c3 −0.3, c6 at d2 −0.7, c12 at d1 −1.6 [−3.2, −0.1], c6 at d4 −0.5. More war and peace variants aren't the diversity that matters. The 8.3 playtest showed the search's options never touched production, research, goals or titles (Hard + Search played the same orders as plain Hard on all 91 turns), so the next candidates must reach those. Proxy v7 also exposed the biggest defence hole: with the proxy playing Mongols, Hard scores only 4–6% in 1v1, because three +1-move Soldiers with Peace passage reach a city before any defence triggers.

## 2026-10-06 — A fresh 8-player UI scenario in about 2 seconds
1. Run `legatum-sim --players 8 --tape-out <tape>`.
2. Extract a turn with `crates/sim/examples/extract_state <tape> <turn> <out>`.
3. Set `is_human` and `name` on `players[0]` in both `setup.players` and `players`.
4. Wrap the result as `{format:'legatum-save', client:'wasm', game:<json>}` and load it through `legatum.autosave.v1`.

The sim and the WASM build must come from the same commit. The round-2 VP mocks used this for an all-faction game at turn 80.

Two more findings from those mocks:
- The Celts player colour (steel, `#d8dde3`) is nearly invisible as a marker on parchment. That's a palette issue for any per-empire marker.
- `FACTION_INFO` still says the Switzerland goal is 200 Gold, unscaled, but at Standard it's 400.

## 2026-10-06 — AI goal prerequisites (playtest 8.3 follow-up)
**Fix.** The goal pass (`victory/goals.rs::plan_faction_goal`) skipped a Mongols Market in any city without a Workshop and nothing ever queued the Workshop (the scorer ranked it 1,295 under a Soldier's 2,400). It now walks each goal item's requirement chain (`goal_need`: the foundation building, a lower tier, the unlocking technology) and starts the first startable piece. Research gives the technologies a pending goal needs, with their prerequisites, `goal VP × 150` value and a lead in the first-six-techs plan (`goal_techs`: Mongols's Exchange and Fabrication, Athens' whole Relay Restoration chain). The Workshop is pursued only once Exchange is known and all three trio cities stand.

**Audit of every goal.** Switzerland (Gold), Japan (cities), Byzantium (Science) and Celts (a Granary, buildable from turn 1) have nothing gated. Germany's four techs are known by the end in 98%+ of 8-player seats (median turn 43 to 53) and Sparta's Fortification and Ballistics in 99%+ (median turn 46 and 68), so their misses are population and war, not an unlock. Only Mongols and Athens had a gap (Relay Restoration was known by the end in just 60% of Athens seats).

**Measured** (cloud, 1,000 8-player Standard Hard games on seeds 9100001+, and the Mongols slice of the 16,384-game duel matrix on 8100001+, 2,048 games, paired; master 932f4d13 against this branch). Goal rate and median goal turn:

| Faction (8-player) | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Mongols | 30.2%, T140 | 30.3%, T131 |
| Athens | 56.9%, T134 | 58.2%, T125 |
| Sparta | 10.7%, T146 | 11.0%, T137.5 |
| Switzerland / Japan / Byzantium / Celts / Germany (code unchanged) | 54.5 / 27.5 / 59.4 / 49.0 / 86.4% | 52.1 / 25.8 / 56.2 / 47.5 / 84.0% (all within sampling noise, ±3 points) |

- Mongols 1v1 against Proxy v6: goal rate 42.6% → 43.8%, median turn 121 → 105, score 42.2% → 41.3%, paired −0.9 [−2.5, +0.7]. Against plain Hard: 72.3% → 74.5%, turn 103 → 92, score 65.5% → 65.2%, paired −0.3 [−1.9, +1.3].
- All factions, 16,384 games: +0.2 [−0.1, +0.5] against Proxy v6 and +0.2 [−0.2, +0.6] against Hard. Athens as side A gained +2.3 [+0.3, +4.2] and +3.4 [+1.4, +5.4]: the Relay chain pays.
- Ledger row: 512 games on 7100001, 36.2% → 37.8% (+1.6 [−0.2, +3.4]); 2,048 games 38.1% → 38.5%; held-out 9100001, 2,048 games, 36.0% → 35.6% (−0.4 [−1.4, +0.6]); Proxy v7 on 7100001, 2,048 games, 25.5% → 25.1%.
- **The 8.3 case is rare.** In 8-player games Mongols built three or more Markets in 64% of games and its goal rate is 30%, so what limits it is the trio geometry, not the Workshop. The fix moves the goal earlier (9–16 turns) and raises the rate by 0 to 2 points; it is not a large win.

**Ablation (2,048 paired Mongols games against Proxy v6, on 64eb5fbd): queueing the Workshop is what hurts when it starts too early.**

| Variant | Score change |
| --- | --- |
| Research boost only | −0.8 [−2.0, +0.5] |
| Workshop step alone, always | −3.5 [−5.2, −1.7] |
| Both, Workshop step always | −3.2 [−5.1, −1.3] (peak cities 5.5 → 5.0, fewer Settlers, Granaries and Walls) |
| Workshop step once Market is startable | −1.9 [−3.5, −0.3] |
| Workshop step once Market is startable and the trio stands (shipped) | −0.9 [−2.5, +0.7] |

A goal item's foundation must not take idle cities from expansion: gate any such pursuit on the thing it serves being nearly the only thing left.

**Other changes.**
- The scripted AI no longer disbands a Scout with no frontier left (a pre-free-Scout rule that saved no upkeep and gave away sight); it holds. Removed `primitive_keep_scout` with it.
- Sim reports now carry per-faction goal progress (`stats.rs::GoalProgress`, the "Faction goal progress" table, `summary.goal_progress` in JSON); `tools/analysis/goal_rates.py` does the same for cloud `merged.json.gz` files, with a paired Mongols duel score (`--side-a`).
- The city panel's Great Works text says "No Gold purchases" no more (it was false for Switzerland): `greatWorkRules(faction)` states the faction's rule, and Switzerland get a Buy button beside each legal Work. The mock and the engine quote the same prices (parity test in `purchases.wasm.test.ts`).

**Process.**
- **Cloud builds in the shared cargo volume can silently reuse a sibling checkout's artifacts.** `tools/cloud/build-linux.sh` mounts every checkout at `/src` over one cached target volume, and cargo judges freshness by mtime: a checkout whose sources are older than the previous build's outputs is not rebuilt. Two "after" runs came out bit-identical to their baseline (the tell), and two ablation variants matched each other. Touch every source file before building (`find crates -name '*.rs' | xargs touch`).
- `run-batch.sh --duel-matrix` now runs on one server (`--count 1`); a full 16,384-game matrix costs a few cents and takes about two minutes on a 64-vCPU server, and its side-A seat of one faction is exactly 2,048 paired games.

## 2026-10-06 — Hold-to-win: the AI side (claude/hold-ai)
- Titles are the only VP that can be taken away (milestones and faction goals are permanent in core today), so breaking a hold means taking `margin + 1` titles. `hold.rs::capture_title_losses` and `kill_takes_army_title` estimate that from public title values; foreign city income is private, so Largest Economy is apportioned by population.
- The goal-driven plan didn't fight a holder at first because Contain's reach is measured from our cities (`nearest_known`), and a field army next to the holder's capital counted for nothing. For holders our army's distance now counts too.
- AI probes (20 games each, Hard, seeds 9500001+, core hold rule before vs this AI after): every game ended by a completed hold in both. Holds started / broken: 2p Quick 23/3 → 26/6, 2p Standard 23/3 → 28/8, 4p Quick 37/15 → 38/16, 4p Standard 38/16 → 47/27 (mean length 156 → 163). Many breaks happen one turn after the start, when a contested title flips back.
- `winchance::HOLD_HAZARD` (10% per turn at the target) is an assumption; calibrate it from the hold telemetry before tuning on it.
## 2026-10-06 — Mongols Peace passage for Scouts and civilians only: the Soldiers left the victim's land, but the rush still wins
User decision: only Mongols Scouts (the Wayfinder) and civilians cross foreign borders at Peace; armed units are closed out like every other faction's (the first idea, pushing units out of borders when war is declared, was rejected as "magic"). One passage rule now answers every border question: `GameState::passage_allowed(mover, kind, land_owner)` (view mirror `PlayerView::passage_allowed`, `entry_table(player, kind)`), faction part in `data::peace_passage(faction, kind)`. `can_enter` had no unit kind, so ~60 callers changed (core, AI, TS mock, UI). A future Courier Hall adds one arm in `passage_allowed` (its holder's land open to every empire's units at Peace).
- **Measured** (cloud, 1v1 Small Standard, duel matrix against Proxy v7, seeds 8100001+, master 62fa8ef4 vs this branch, same games): the Mongols row (Hard's score against Proxy v7 playing Mongols, 16,384-game matrix, all eight Hard factions) went **5.7% → 5.5%**. The rule did not fix the rush. Overall Hard vs Proxy v7 (2,048 paired) 25.7% → 26.0% (+0.2 [−0.4, +0.8]); vs Proxy v6 37.7% → 37.2% (−0.4 [−1.3, +0.4]).
- **Why.** At the declaration the rule did what it should: the declarer's armed units on the victim's land fell from 15% to 0.5% (878 units over 128 declarations), but those within 3 hexes of a victim city only from 53% to 41%. Staging at the border is enough: a surprise war at Peace takes effect the same turn and +1 move reaches the city. The hole is the same-turn declare-and-strike with +1 move plus a one-Soldier capital, not Peace passage.
- **Tests:** `crates/core/tests/mongols_passage.rs` (matrix by faction/kind/relation, view/engine agreement, validation message, resolution); `app/src/mock/movement.wasm.test.ts` (mock and core agree on a Mongols Soldier, Scout and Settler at a Peace border); `app/e2e/mongols-passage.spec.ts` (the refusal message at 1000x600 and 1440x900). Order validation and the map name the rule: "only Scouts and civilians may cross foreign borders at Peace". Repinned: the three mixed AI tapes diverge because Mongols seats play differently.
- **AI:** `ctx.move_cost` (armed units) and `ctx.move_cost_light` (Scouts, Settlers; only differs for Mongols) in `analysis.rs`; civilians and exploration use the light table, everything else the armed one, so a Mongols army stages at the border.
- **Gotcha:** the desktop UI queued a doomed Soldier order and only showed the reason in the Pending orders list; `commands.ts` now says "No known path to this tile. Only Scouts and civilians may cross foreign borders at Peace" immediately. A path to a tile inside a border is only refused when the owner is known (seen); an unseen border does not constrain, as before.
## 2026-10-06 — Wider search candidates (plan x overlay) don't bend the curve, before the rule changes
Search over (plan, overlay) composites, overlays reaching production, goals and titles (Goal, Victory, Build, plus the knob postures), and a re-plan when the win chance collapses: against Proxy v6 on the tune seeds (2,048 games each, cloud) every setting from 3 to 24 candidates, at fixed or scaled budget, sits within -0.6 to 0.0 of plain Hard (all CIs cross zero). Against plain Hard, plans-only search is +2.1 [+0.6, +3.5] and 12 composites +1.8. Composites raise searches per game from about 6 to 13 (a single-plan pool no longer skips the search) and the search seat's time from 10 to 90-125 ms a turn. To re-run after hold-to-win, the Mongols push-out and the faction-goal redesign land; a likely limit is that 20-turn rollouts against scripted Hard don't credit buildings or goal steps that pay off later.

## 2026-10-06 — Sub-agents outlive the sub-agent that spawned them; check before re-spawning
The faction-goals lead (an Opus agent) handed back while four Sonnet agents it had spawned were still running. Its notification said "no live background children", and the worktrees showed no file changes for 15 minutes (long builds), so the lead wrongly concluded they had died and spawned duplicates. Two agents then edited goals-2 at once and collided. The originals were alive and reachable by name (ListAgents lists every sub-agent, nested ones included, as `goals-inc1 [ec83ac]` and so on). Before re-spawning work, check `ListAgents`. A quiet worktree is not proof of a dead agent. A named agent's reports reach the lead session.

## 2026-10-07 — Hold-to-win landed (lead summary, claude/hold-to-win)
- Winning by VP now means holding the target for 10 turn ends (Standard) or 5 (Quick); the reaching turn counts as turn 1, so the win comes at the end of the 10th. Cloud runs, same seeds, master 68986165 vs this branch (8 players Standard Hard, 1,000 games; 1v1 Hard vs Proxy v7, 2,048 paired games): mean game length 163 → 192 turns (8p) and 147 → 161 (1v1); 8p break rate 56% of 2,335 holds started (346 winners had broken an earlier hold of their own, 164 wins came from a team that was not first to reach); 1v1 break rate 29%. Median reach-to-win is exactly 9 (the unbroken minimum), mean 20 (8p) / 13 (1v1): most holds either survive or die early.
- Hard vs Proxy v7: 25.1% → 26.7% (paired +1.6pp [+0.9, +2.3]); 8p best/worst faction ratio 4.9x → 4.2x (Sparta 4.0% → 5.2%, Mongols 19.6% → 22.0%). Stalls at the 1200-turn cap went 2 → 4 (8p) and 16 → 21 (1v1): small samples, but the hold rule gives no extra stall protection; watch it.
- A coalition against a holder is the main brake, and it is one-sided: titles (and, since goals increment 1, a live goal such as the Switzerland hoard) are the VP that can be taken away; milestones are permanent. A holder with margin 0 breaks by losing one title. The AI's `hold.rs` denial estimates were written before live goals and only price titles; pricing a rival's live goal is the next lever.
- Pre-existing, not from this change: `e2e/turn-report.spec.ts` "locate the attacker" fails on master 3885603a (the focused target sits under the report panel after the VP UI rework); `e2e/war-interstitial.spec.ts` looked for the removed `.relation-badge` and now reads the race's `.vhc-rel.war.new` pin.
## 2026-10-06 — Faction goals increment 1: a live goal flips the AI's meaning of "goal pending", and a bigger Japan penalty can go negative
- **Live goals need three states in the AI, not two.** `goal_pending()` was `!faction_goal_done`, which for a Permanent goal means "not banked yet". For the live Switzerland hoard `done` flips back, so "not pending" no longer means "free to spend": the ordinary reserve must stay at the hoard while it is held (`victory.rs::hoard_held`, `scripted.rs::ordinary_gold_reserve`), tile buys must not dip below it, and a Great Work buy needs the hoard left over (or to win the race). The AI test fixtures set `faction_goal_done: true` without a held view, so `hoard_held` reads the view's `faction_goal.done`, which fixtures leave false; real states have the two equal.
- **A rival's treasury is private, but a capture's effect on the hoard is derivable.** The public capture bounty and the city's population share give the treasury (`bounty * total_pop / (pop * 2 if capital)`), so a rival can tell whether taking the city drops a Swiss below its hoard (`victory.rs::hoard_denial_value`).
- **Japan −10% a hex needs a cap.** Unclamped, a city 10 or more hexes beyond the radius had a negative multiplier and negative Food (−3 in a test); the loss now stops at all of it (`data::japan_distance_loss_percent`), in the core, the settlement forecast, the AI's valuation and the mock. Design question: a city 14 hexes from the capital now yields nothing but its Production floor.
- **Barbarian tiles deny yields to every city that works them, so the city breakdown needs its own reason.** The core note said "Salvage camp ... the Byzantium" for every denied tile; it now says "Barbarians hold the tile" for barbarian units and camps (`economy.rs`, mirrored in the mock and the tile card).
- **Archived views in test fixtures break on typed field changes.** `vp_sources` went from `[label, n]` to `[VpSource, n]`; the gzipped escort archive (`crates/ai/tests/fixtures/escort-t168-t170.json.gz`) is migrated in its test, not rewritten.
- **A parallel agent merged the VP holdings UI into master mid-increment**, so the goal meter lives in the new token cards (`vpModel.ts::ownGoalProgress` reads `view.faction_goal`), not in a separate panel.

## 2026-10-07 — One-time actions (goals increment 2): Order::Action, the Swarm, the broadcast
- **One order variant for every one-time empire action.** `Order::Action { action }` with `Action` tagged `"action"` (`{"type":"Action","action":{"action":"Swarm"}}`). A new action adds a variant, a check in `rules/actions.rs::validate_action` and a branch in `apply_actions`, plus a `view().actions` field; the order checker, the resolver phase (right after the city orders), the mock and the TS order union do not change shape. Free-tech picks resolve before the rites, so a broadcast that resolves this turn leaves receivers a choice for next turn, never this one.
- **Hook AI decisions where every brain passes: `Brain::orders`.** All brains (Hard, Proxy v2-v7, search, openings) share that default method, so `goal_actions::goal_actions(view)` is added there with one line instead of in six planners. It reads only the view (`view.actions` carries the preview, blocker and pending grant). Building a `Planner` for the tech valuation is done only on the turn a grant is pending.
- **The Swarm fires on net VP, not on availability.** The preview's per-city `lost` and released `tiles` give the title arithmetic for free: Most Populous drops by the sacrificed total, Most Territory by the released tiles, Largest Economy scales with the population kept; a title held by a thin margin costs 1 VP and cancels the rite unless it still crosses the target or the game is 75% over. A swarmed Japan is public (`faction_goal_done`), and rivals add `(8 - population) * 15` target value to its small cities while they stay small (stateless, fades as the city regrows).
- **Gotchas.** (1) `Iterator::max_by_key` keeps the LAST maximum: the tie rule "lower city id" needs a reversed iteration. (2) Core serializes `Option::None` as `null`, so a mock view that omits an empty field fails `toEqual` against the WASM view: fill every key with `null`. (3) Measuring released tiles after a whole `resolve_turn` double-counts: the territory phase claims tiles the fixture had not yet grown; assert on `apply_actions` alone. (4) A population-is-territory fixture needs one grown tile per population above 1, or a Swarm releases fewer tiles than its preview.
- **Two agents in one worktree.** A duplicate of this increment's agent was started in the same locked worktree and both edited `events.rs`, `state.rs` and `rules/actions.rs`, one overwriting the other's file; an orphaned `cargo build` with the worktree as its cwd (`lsof -a -d cwd`) gave it away. Before any edit in a worktree you were handed, check `git status` is clean and `ps -eo pid,etime,command | grep <worktree>` shows no other builds.
## 2026-10-07 — Faction goals increment 3 UI: the held goal lives in its holder's row; built buildings never reach `unavailable`
- **A held goal (the Byzantium's Heir) is owned by whoever's public `vp_sources` name it** (`vpModel.ts`), so its token sits in the holder's row, wears the Byzantium's crest (`Token.of`), and `own` (mine/theirs/open) follows the holder. It is outlined like a live source (it can be captured) though `FACTION_GOAL.live` is false.
- **The core's `unavailable` list omits an already-built building** (and the mock mirrors it), so a greyed "Built" Engine card is synthesised in `CityPanel` for the Germany, which also lets the card carry the goal count ("3 of 4 cities have an Engine").
- **Capturing the Heir needs War**: at Peace a military unit cannot enter a foreign tile, so the tile card offers "Capture the Heir (+2 VP)" only at War and the Heir panel says "declare War first" otherwise. A tour fixture for the capture must set War, or the map shows the Peace hint instead.
- **A held goal's loss event pairs with the next holder's gain event**, so the report folds them into one viewer-perspective line (`format.ts::heirGoalLine`); a loss with no gain (disbanded, killed, barbarians) is its own line.
- Tour fixtures run against `target/release/legatum-server` in the worktree; an old binary from another checkout serves the static files fine but logs a 400 on `/api/archive`, which fails `contrast.spec.ts` on the console-error check.

## 2026-10-07 — Faction goals increment 3 core and AI: a held goal needs the unit's identity, and the goal pass claims idle cities
- **A held goal is a unit, so identity lives on the unit** (`Unit::heir_of`, public as `UnitView::heir_of`), not in a Player counter: a civilian is captured in place (the owner changes; id and `heir_of` stay), so whoever owns the unit scores (`goals::holders`). Two Byzantines in one test game each have their own Heir. A holder-change event pair needs the previous holder, which `Player::goal_holder` remembers (`scoring::check_held_goal`).
- **Tests that used `faction_goal_done = true` as "this empire has 2 VP" break for the Byzantium**: a held goal scores only from the unit (coalition fixtures led by a Byzantium lost their leader; the telemetry score test too). Use a Permanent faction (Japan) when the fixture only needs VP.
- **A new civilian unit kind needs the invariant oracles checked**: the fuzz oracle called every non-Settler "military" and rejected an Heir sharing a tile with a soldier; Mongols's +1 move and Sparta healing tests keyed on `Settler` rather than `military`. All three now read `data::unit_stats(kind).military`.
- **Adding enum variants shifts fuzz and pooled-seed tests, not only exhaustive matches**: `BuildingKind::ALL` grew, so the property fuzz drew different orders, and `offer_pacing` (three seeds, pooled offer counts) saw zero offers to the human until two seeds were added.
- **The goal pass claims every idle city before the ordinary scoring runs**: once the forge opens, `forge_steps` starts an Engine in each idle Germany city, so a settle-gate test saw no Settler weighed until its cities already had Engines. Intended, but it means a Germany past its forge phase stops expanding unless its treasury can buy the new city's Engine (`forge_allows_settling`: 2x the Engine price plus the reserve). The forge phase (4 cities and the land full or 45% of the game gone) and the Engine and Heir prices (120 and 200 Production) are placeholders to calibrate once the AI is good enough to measure.
- **Rivals read the same public facts as the goal**: capturing one of a held-forge Germany's cities only breaks the goal at exactly four cities (public `PlayerPublic::cities`), so the denial value applies there only; an Heir inside a city makes that city worth its capture value too. A visible rival-held Heir is struck first and on half the usual margin; the Byzantium keeps every Heir it holds in its safest walled, garrisoned city and prices walls and a garrison there.
## 2026-10-07 — Two agents designed the same data model in parallel
The VP UI agent (told to add a viewer `faction_goal` field and typed `vp_sources`) and the goals-1 agent (told to build the goal model, also exposing `faction_goal` and a typed `VpSource`) each built their own core version. When the VP UI landed first, merging master into the goals branches conflicted in 15 files. When two briefs both mention a shared field, name one agent as its owner and tell the other to consume it (a stub against the agreed shape) rather than implement it.

## 2026-10-07 — Playtest 8.3.1 shipped with two test-only release failures
The 8.3.1 release check (c5f1f8ef) failed only these Playwright tests:
- `mobile-portrait` (4 tests): the VP UI rework reduced the phone race view to a strip, and phone isn't a target.
- `war-interstitial` (1 test): its selector looked for the removed `.relation-badge`. The fix landed on master with hold-to-win.

Every desktop flow passed, so the lead installed and tagged the build. Release checks should exclude or update phone-only specs once phone is officially out of scope, so a red check means a real desktop problem.

## 2026-10-07 — Stalls after the goal redesign: a 9-VP team with nothing to steal, and rich empires that never buy the title (goals increment 3)
100 Standard Hard games on seeds 5300001+ (goals-3 on master with hold-to-win): 4 stalled at the 1,200-turn cap (seeds 5300017, 5300035, 5300070, 5300086); master's baseline on the same seeds had none, and goals-3's smoke on the previous master had 1 in 50.
- **No hold ever starts.** In all four, the best team stands at exactly 9 VP (Japan once, Byzantium twice, Sparta once) and nobody reaches 10, so hold-to-win is not the blocker: there is nothing to hold. The 9-VP team has every milestone, its goal and two titles; the tenth point is a title held by a rival.
- **The world is frozen at peace.** By turn 300 every unit has a `HoldUnit` order and nobody declares war; in 5300017 the Army title is 2,715 against 2,714, the Populous title 107 against 101, and neither moves in 900 turns.
- **The Gold hoards are a symptom.** Mongols and Celts end with 45,000 to 86,000 Gold because nothing they are allowed to want costs it. They could buy the Army title but never do: `victory.rs::production_points` prices the Army route by Production turns (`fresh_turns * (needed - 1) <= scale_cost(60)`), not by purchases, so a 265-point gap (about eleven Marksmen) looks 150 turns away to an empire that could buy one a turn; the wartime purchase cap (`military_purchase_available`) adds to it. The poor 9-VP team (Japan, 81 Gold) cannot buy anything.
- **Why goals-3 shows it and master does not: less VP in the world.** Goal rates fell where the redesign made goals cost something: Germany 93% to 33% (four cities with an Engine in each, forge phase, no settling without the price of an Engine), Byzantium 80% to 65% (an Heir, capturable), against Sparta 27% to 18%. Games that used to finish because someone's cheap goal gave them the last two points now end at 9 when the goal misses.
- **Not fixed.** The small things are not clearly right (pricing the Army route by purchases changes every rich empire's behaviour in every game). Candidates for a later pass: price title routes by purchases when Gold covers them; let a team within 1 VP of its target treat peace as a cost and declare war for a title (the hold-to-win rule makes a late war cheap, since a hold breaks); or calibrate the new goals (Engine and Heir prices, forge phase) with the AI measuring.
- Tools: `tools/sim/slot.sh -- legatum-sim --games 1 --seed N --tape-out DIR`, then `cargo run --release -p legatum-sim --example inspect_hard_stall -- DIR/game-*.json OUTDIR` prints titles, Gold and orders at turns 200, 300, 400, 600 and 1200.

## 2026-10-07 — Endgame stalls: four AI holes, fixed behind one gene; a walled late game remains
1,024 Standard Hard games on seeds 5300001+ (cloud): stalls at the 1,200-turn cap fell from 11 to 4 with `endgame_push` (on by default; `vp_trace` example prints a game's VP race from its tape). Causes found in the stalled games:
- **Idle armies blocked every purchase:** a city needs a free spawn tile (its own tile or ring 1; `cities::spawn_tile`), and 800 turns of `HoldUnit` filled them all, so a 9-VP empire with 23,000 Gold got `NoSpawnTile` on every unit. Resting units now step off ring 1 when fewer than two spawn tiles are left.
- **The Army title was priced by Production turns** (`victory.rs::production_points`); a treasury that covers the whole gap now buys a unit a turn per city, past the routine purchase cap.
- **A live hoard flickered:** a Swiss at 10 VP bought defenders the turn after reaching its 1,000-Gold hoard, fell to 8, and repeated it about 200 times, so hold-to-win never completed. Readiness and frontline buys now keep the hoard while its points hold the team at target.
- **Most Territory had no purchase route:** a racing team whose treasury covers the gap buys a tile per city per turn.
- **Still stalling (4 in 1,024):** late games where every candidate city is walled and fortified (the campaign forecast refuses all of them), the 9-VP team is poor, or titles tie (the incumbent keeps a tie). Title campaigns one point short are offered but find no forecast-favourable city. No regression: -0.1 against Proxy v7, 49.5% against the old Hard.

## 2026-10-07 — Long-gated branches chase a moving master
goals-2 merged master three times overnight. Each time, another landing (goals-3, the hold-to-win fixes, the AI stall fix) arrived during its 1–2 hour gate, and the next trial merge conflicted again in `crates/ai` and the sim pins. When a big branch is ready, freeze other landings to master until it lands, then release them. Hold the small ones for it, not the other way round.

## 2026-10-07: The Courier Hall's plaque had to shrink for walls, and held goals needed their own icon (goals increment 5, UI)
- The Portolan rose at full size collides with a walled city's ring: the wall has corner towers at its north and south and runs close past east and west, so the cardinal points land on the towers. On a walled city the rose draws at 0.76 of the hex (`courierHall.ts`), inside the ring.
- Zoomed out, the far name placer would print a city's name over its Hall badge (left spot). `placeFarNames` now takes the badge rects as keep-clear areas.
- Held-goal race tokens showed the goal owner's faction crest; for a Hall that reads as "a Mongols thing", not "the Hall". Held goals now wear the item's own icon (Heir icon, Hall icon) and the crest stays on the card's "Your Courier Hall" row.
- The core's Hall reasons double as card states: "already stands in X" means Built (greyed, with the check), "stands in X; retake the city instead" stays a locked card. A capture is told once: the Hall moment replaces the generic city-captured card for a Hall city.
## 2026-10-07 — Under the new rules search beats Hard by 4 points but still not the proxies
Cloud, tune seeds, 2,048 paired games per point, master b69a97fc (hold-to-win, Mongols Peace passage for Scouts and civilians only, goals 1 and 3, endgame push).
- **Against plain Hard search now pays:** default k3 d4 +3.7 [+2.2, +5.3], 12 plan-by-overlay composites with 40-turn rollouts +4.5 [+2.6, +6.4] (was +1.7 under the old rules). Hold-to-win makes the plan layer's choices count for more.
- **Against the proxies it is flat:** Proxy v6 -1.3 to -0.1 at every budget (30 to 1,920 rollout turns), Proxy v7 -0.2 to +0.3; the Proxy v6 rival in half the rollouts (`a=2`) doesn't change that. Rollouts that model the opponent as scripted Hard (or v6) don't foresee the proxy's assaults.
- **Plain Hard's level moved:** 44.1% against Proxy v6 (was about 37% before the rules), 30.8% against Proxy v7. The Mongols conqueror still wins 94% (Hard 6.1% against it; Japan 15.8%, Germany 72.7%): the passage rule alone doesn't close that hole.

## 2026-10-07 — Honour the Fallen and Call the Wild: what the first simulations showed (goals increment 4)
- **A young city's territory is its ring, so a camp "two hexes out" can never be raised for it.** The first rule put rite camps at least 2 from the city; a Celts with a freshly founded city then stood at 6 of 7 cities for ever, and the AI re-called every turn, stacking 14 camps. The camp now takes the farthest free tile of the territory, never the city's own (so next to a young city, farther as it grows), and the AI calls only when every city has room (`goal_actions::wild_now`). A goal that a map or growth accident can make unreachable is a bug, not a cost.
- **Barbarians standing in Celts land take the Celts's attrition,** so a wild camp's guard and raiders sit at the 10 HP floor: raids on the Celts are weak and clearing a camp is cheap. Not changed (the camps are meant to be ordinary); it is an open question for the user whether wild camps should be exempt.
- **A rival at Peace can't clear a wild camp:** foreign military in closed Celts territory is pushed out the turn it is ordered to stay, so only a rival at war holds the tile and clears the camp. Ordinary AI approaches treat Celts land as unsafe (`safe_campaign_tile`), so a rival's walk to a camp that scores for a Celts is its own bounded one (`wild_camp_approach`: at most 8 steps, 2 inside the borders, 40 HP on arrival).
- **Level 4 is rare in AI-vs-AI games.** At Quick a level-4 promotion needs 75 XP (150 at Standard, Sparta earn 50% faster): about a dozen fights per unit. In 48 Quick Hard games the best Sparta army reached level 3 and no empire retired a veteran (0% goal rate), because AI armies are small and wars are few. The War Shrine does get built (units trained there start a level higher); the threshold is a calibration question for the lead, not an AI hole.
- **Parity tests list the whole actions view:** `ActionsView` gained `retire` and `wild`, so every `toEqual({ swarm: null, broadcast: null, free_techs: null })` needs them, and `coreFixture` must carry `veterans_retired`. A report line for the third retirement and the goal's own `FactionGoalCompleted` are two lines on purpose (rivals see only the second).
- **Tour fixtures:** the moment lane shows three cards a turn, highest tier first, so a fixture that has not yet claimed every title buries the card under test; pre-claim titles and milestones (`goalActionsFixture.wildState`). A rival standing on Celts land must be at war with it.
- **Two latent AI order bugs surfaced by the changed trajectories** (`openings::mixed_opening_orders_validate_in_real_fogged_games`, seeds 8140001-2, only after the goal AI changed what games look like): a city whose item was bought this turn also got a `SetProduction` for the same Improvement (the construction loop looked only for an earlier `SetProduction`, now also `BuyItem`), and the territory race let two cities buy the same free tile (`borders::plan_tile_buy` now picks an unclaimed one). Neither is goal-specific; any AI change that moves tapes can expose them.
- **Barbarian units now exist in short games:** the Celts's rite can raise camps long before the first natural camp, so two checks that assumed "no barbarians before turn ~5" broke: the LAN host's `check_state` rejected a save holding barbarian-owned units, and the replay frames test asserted every unit owner is a player. Both now accept the reserved barbarian owner.

## 2026-10-07 — Goal calibration: two AI bugs mattered more than the knobs
8-player Standard Hard, 1,024 games per setting on the cloud (seeds 5300001+, final checked on 5400001+). Goal reached (share of seats, median turn), before -> after: Japan 23% (250) -> 33% (225), Byzantium 67% (115) -> 57% (144), Sparta 0.5% -> 28% (223) at level 2, since reverted to 4; Switzerland 53%, Celts 55% (210), Germany 37% (175), Mongols 39% (214), Athens 45% (255) unchanged. Average game 301 -> 254 turns; stalls at the 1,200-turn cap 4.5% -> 1.1% (1.6% on the held-out seeds).
- **Sparta: one unit per tile.** The AI retired only when all three owed veterans stood in War Shrine cities in one turn, but a city tile holds one military unit, so with one shrine that never happened. Retiring them as they arrive (progress is kept) was needed; even then level 3 reached only 4% of seats, so the level went to 2 (28% of seats). **Reverted the same day (user): the level stays 4**, "level 2 is just way too easy to achieve, and units are cheap"; getting units to level 4 is AI work.
- **Deadlines past the expected length.** `goal_actions::last_turn` was the expected length, so once a game ran past it every "can it finish and hold in time" test failed for good; a 9-VP Mongols never started its Hall in 900 turns. It now keeps a tenth of the expected length ahead.
- **Lower AI cutoffs overshoot:** dropping the Celts Wild and Hall/forge cutoffs to 25-35% pushed Celts to 86-96% and pulled its goal into the opening; they stay.
- **Remaining stalls (1.1-1.6%)** are still the leader stuck one point short: a 9-VP team (most often Mongols) whose missing point is a title a richer rival holds, with no affordable route.
- Method: a throwaway branch read the knobs from the environment, and `tools/cloud/run-batch.sh --env K=V` passed them to the servers, so each factions' knobs could vary in the same batch.

## 2026-10-07 — 8.4 pre-release UI: the right column is one shared budget
- **The race panel was squeezed by the dock, not by its own content.** At 1000x600 the dock's cap left the race 176 px, and a goal row, a sticky warning or title chips above it ate the rows. `VpTrack` now measures the panel's fixed parts plus your row and the leader's and publishes `--race-min` (capped so the dock keeps its header and action); the dock body shrinks and scrolls in whole rows (`useWholeRows`). `expectRaceWhole` and `expectDockWhole` in the screen tour assert it on 93a, 95i, 93h and 87.
- **`.moment-effect` is a flex row:** text and an `<Amount>` inside it became separate flex items, which broke every gain line around the VP amount. A prose effect goes in one inline `<span>`.
- **A closed-border refusal needs the empire:** `unreachableNote` now walks the Scout route to find the first tile the unit may not enter and names its owner; it no longer prefixes "No known path". The core's server message is unchanged.
- **Held-goal terms repeated the rule** ("whoever holds him scores"); the token card's terms line is now just "Held goal".
## 2026-10-07 — WindowServer crashed again from Playwright WebKit, at one worker
At 07:01 WindowServer aborted (`WSIOSurfaceDebugTallyAndAbort` in `captureWindowList`; the crash's voucher names Playwright as the originator). It was during the 8.4 release check's host WebKit step (06:48–07:03), which ran with one worker and `LEGATUM_ALLOW_HOST_WEBKIT=1`. `ioclasscount IOSurface` read only 323 at the step's start, so the 40k surface guard never fired: the abort is WindowServer's per-client capture tally for repeated window screenshots, with 3+ external displays attached, not the global surface count.
- **What changed:** host WebKit is banned outright.
  - `app/playwright.config.ts` refuses WebKit on darwin with no escape hatch.
  - `tools/release/check.sh` runs WebKit only through `tools/release/webkit-docker.sh`, in a Linux container, or skips it.
  - `tools/ops/no-host-webkit.sh` kills any host Playwright WebKit process within 10 s. The watchdog keeps it running.
- **Old worktrees** still carry the env escape hatch, but nothing sets the variable any more, and the killer covers them.

## 2026-10-07 — WebKit in a Linux container: works, but is about 10x slower, and found real failures
`tools/release/webkit-docker.sh <worktree>` runs the screen tour and the menu spec in the official Playwright Linux image (v1.63.0-noble, `--cpus=4`). The Linux arm64 server is cached by source hash and `node_modules` lives in a Docker volume. On master c5004926 the first run took 35 min (108 tests, cold build included): 104 passed, 4 failed. WindowServer stayed the same process and IOSurface was 208 before and 207 after.
- **Slow:** Linux WebKit renders in software, so a test that takes 3 s in Chromium takes 30 s. The 45 s config timeout failed "Sparta 7b"; the script passes `--timeout=120000`.
- **Real failures, not WebKit-only:** "goal actions: the Swarm..." expects 4 table rows and gets 3 in Chromium too (stale expectation after the goal calibration).
- **WebKit-only:** "Turn 1: the first city panel shows its build choice at scroll 0" fails at 1000x600 every time (passes at 1440x900 and in Chromium). Not yet investigated.

## 2026-10-07 — Codex Luna, first task: fast, but its sandbox can't run our UI gates
Luna (`model: luna`) finished a small app task in under 3 minutes: excluding the phone-only spec, plus a one-line TurnReport fix. Its sandbox couldn't verify the work:
- Vite couldn't write its temporary config, because `node_modules` points outside the writable worktree.
- WASM-backed vitest suites failed.
- Chromium couldn't launch (`MachPortRendezvousServer` permission denied).

So Codex tasks that touch the app need the lead to run the UI gate outside the sandbox (for example a bgjob verify script in the lane worktree) before landing. Pure Rust and docs tasks verify fine inside it.

## 2026-10-07 — Learning-AI pilot: ES discovers rush defence, but not sentries
Evolution strategies over a 150-parameter fixed-point linear policy (`crates/sim/examples/es_defence.rs`; report `docs/sim-results/rl-pilot/README.md`), overriding only unit moves (keep Hard's order, fortify, home, watch their city, midway, picket) and city builds (keep, best melee, best ranged, Walls) on top of plain Hard, against Proxy v7 Mongols in the 1v1 Small Standard lobby, 60 turns, no hand-written strategy:
- **It works for defence.** Three independent runs, held-out seeds 9100001+ (2,048 paired games each): capital held at turn 60 goes 54.4% → 83.4-86.9% (+29 to +33 points, CIs ±2.3), more cities (1.34 → 1.41-1.61), twice the enemy units killed. At turn 100 (past the training horizon): 33% → 54-62%. Every defender faction gains.
