All three runs found the same rule: ranged units while the own army is small, leave the default build as enemies close in, Walls when enemies were seen, units home at war.
- **It didn't find sentries.** Sentry turns and turns seeing their city barely moved; the warning lead grew 1-1.7 turns because attacks came later, not from sight. With the city head frozen (unit moves only), ES gained +5 to +15 points by pulling units back (picket 3 out, home), and sentry turns fell. Reasons: the cheap lever (Rangers, Walls) fixes most of the hole; city sight 3 already sees Proxy v7's 2-3-hex staging; a memoryless linear policy can't turn glimpses into a plan. To get sentries: opponents that stage out of city sight, a sighting memory, policy-chosen targets.
- **It's cheap.** 4.7 s per generation (6,400 sixty-turn games, about 1,360 games a second) on one c8g.16xlarge; six 150-generation runs cost about $0.88. Compute isn't what limits learned AI here; representation and opponents are.
- **Gotchas.** `run-batch.sh --bin <example>` (plus `LEGATUM_CLOUD_EXAMPLES` for `build-linux.sh`) runs any sim example on the cloud; the server appends `--games/--seed/--label/--out-dir/--out/--status-file`, so the example must ignore or use them (`es_defence` takes its output dir as `--dir`). Nothing streams back until the server finishes: keep `--max-hours` generous. Headless Chrome sometimes writes the screenshot and never exits; `tools/rl-pilot/chart.py` times it out.
- **Not shipped.** Before porting the rule into Hard: check it against non-rushing opponents and in 8 players (the Ranger bias may cost economy), and measure full games against Proxy v7, not just the capital at turn 60/100.
## 2026-10-07 — Sentries and early Walls barely move the Mongols conqueror
Cloud, 2,048 paired duels against Proxy v7 on the tune seeds. A Scout sentry by a rusher's nearest city (the user's own defence): -0.7 overall, Mongols row 6.4% -> 7.6%. Walls tech, a shooter and Walls first next to a rush-capable rival: +0.6, Mongols 7.6% -> 9.6%, Sparta row 23.8% -> 26.6%; no regression against the old Hard. Both kept, switchable. Why so little: under the new passage rule the conqueror still declares on turns 12-35 against a one- or two-city Hard (about 3 Soldiers), and by turn 100 has built 24 military units to Hard's 11; the exchange is even (82 units lost each over the game), so Hard loses on volume, city by city. Walls came no earlier on average (median turn 50 either way): research only switches when the current tech finishes, and the capital's production is small. The user beats this rush "almost too easily", by tactics a human sees and the scripted AI doesn't.
## 2026-10-07 — No usable public human game data for Civ-likes
Research (`docs/research/civ-human-expertise.md`) found no public structured record of human Civ 6 play. Tournament (CPL) saves circulate privately, and the one large Hugging Face "Civ 6 traces" set is 363 GB of screen video and inputs with no game state. CivRealm (Freeciv) offers environments and baselines, not human games. Our own playtest tapes are the only human data with our rules, so treat them as the validation set for AI behaviours. Several community sites (CivFanatics, Reddit, Fandom, longturn.net) block curl behind bot walls, and DuckDuckGo starts challenging repeated queries. Brave search worked.

## 2026-10-07 — A captured city left its attackers' move orders alive (playtest 8.4, turn 31)
Five Sparta Soldiers ordered `MoveUnit` into a fallen city; one entered and captured it, and the other four, which attacked but did not move in, kept `goto` = the city tile. They showed a "move into this city" arrow and were not counted among the units needing orders (the client counts a unit as ready only when it has no `goto`, `needs_orders` is false otherwise). The orders chips come straight from server state (`view.units[].goto`), not from client queues, so the cause was core: `battle::capture_city` never touched other units' orders, and `end_pursuit` ended a dead-target attack without `needs_orders`.
- **Fix:** `capture_city` now calls `end_orders_into_city`: every unit of the captor's team or an ally with a goto or route ending at the city (not standing on it) is cleared and gets `needs_orders`; Celts razing is unchanged. A dead or own-side-captured attack target uses `void_pursuit` (clear plus `needs_orders`). Mock mirrored with WASM parity tests; `crates/wasm/tests/p84_capture_clears_orders.rs` replays the user's tape (`fixtures/p84-capture-t30.tape.json`) and fails on the old code with four stale orders.
- **Gotcha:** the mock's `coreFixture` did not pass `attack_target` to the core, so mock/WASM parity tests of attack orders must set it (now carried) or use orders.
- **Repin:** the three mixed AI tapes and the runner pins moved (`tools/sim/repin.sh`): in AI games a capture now clears the captor team's orders aimed at the city and a dead attack target sets `needs_orders`, so the AI's later turns differ. Intentional; no other digest changed.
## 2026-10-07 — Level-4 Sparta veterans: the AI barely fights, so the ladder is out of reach
`levels_audit` (new example) on 48 8-player Standard Hard games: by the game's end an Sparta seat holds about 6 level-2 units (War Shrine training) but 0.17 at level 3 and 0.02 at level 4+. Level 4 needs 150 XP at Standard against 46 at level 2, and a kill of an equal unit is worth about 7 XP to an Sparta, so each of the three veterans needs about 14 kills. The Sparta AI declares 1.3 wars and takes 0.5 cities a game. A drill (the three most experienced units take every favourable fight and hunt barbarians when home is quiet, gene `veteran_drill`) moved the goal from 1.3% to 1.7% of seats (1,024 games, cloud). Reaching level 4 needs an Sparta that wars all game, the earlier "competent Sparta AI" item, not a drill. Research items 1 and 2 (`docs/research/civ-human-expertise.md`): a sentry per bordering rival -0.5 and an early border threat from public army numbers +0.5 against Proxy v7 (both n.s.).
## 2026-10-07 — Safari/WKWebView never focuses a button on click: a blur-closes handler breaks every click in it
"Exit to title" did nothing in the installed Mac app even after the 6.3 fix (check `document.activeElement` after rAF). In Safari and WKWebView, mousedown on a `<button>` does not focus it: focus leaves the previously focused element and lands on `<body>`, so blur fires with `relatedTarget` null and `activeElement` is outside the menu. Any "close on blur" check (relatedTarget, or activeElement after rAF) closes the menu between mousedown and mouseup, and the click never fires. Chromium and Linux WebKit focus the button, so neither reproduced it and the container spec passed.
- Rule: never close a popup, menu or dropdown on a blur whose `relatedTarget` is null. Close on Escape, an outside pointerdown, or blur with a non-null `relatedTarget` outside the container.
- `GameMenu` now does exactly that. Reviewed and left as is: `Tip` (hover tips only, already requires a non-null relatedTarget to stay open and a press closes it anyway), `VpHoldings` token cards (pointer-transparent, closing never unmounts a token), `LobbyScreen` AI select (blur only resets a label), `TechPanel`/`CityPanel` hover highlights. `FactionPicker` has no blur handler.
- Test: `e2e/exit-to-title.spec.ts` simulates Safari on any engine (a capture-phase mousedown handler calls `preventDefault()` and blurs the active element), presses and holds for 150 ms, then releases. It fails on the old handler in Chromium and passes in the WebKit container.

## 2026-10-07 — Codex Luna is good at art regeneration
Luna regenerated the Sparta leader portrait (same prompt, only the subject changed) in about 3 minutes, and the result was clean: consistent oil technique, natural hands, no text, the right derivatives and sizes. Codex's built-in image tool is how the original portraits were made, so route art regeneration and mock tasks to Luna. Review the image before landing.

## 2026-10-07 — Playtest 8.4: a conquest, not a hold; the AI never reacts to a visible army gap
Analysis: `.reviews/playtest-8.4-sparta-analysis.md`. The human won by elimination at T62 on 7 VP (never 10): the Byzantium fell T30, the Japan T49, the Germany T61, with 1 Soldier lost to 12 kills. Hold-to-win never started.
- **Hard + Search and plain Hard reproduced the tape's orders on all 61 turns** (persistent brains on the true trajectory). Search ran twice (Japan T42 at 8.6% win chance, Germany T54 at 0), never for the Byzantium.
- **Sight is 2 (units) and 3 (cities), so the AIs saw the stack 1-3 hexes out:** the Germany saw no human unit for 27 turns, the Japan none for 22, the Byzantium the stack 2 turns before the war. The public army metric said 105 against 25 from T20 and nothing reacted.
- **Emergency production drops a nearly finished Walls:** the Byzantium capital had 86 of 91 on T27, 13 a turn and 30 Gold, and ordered a Ranger instead (`production.rs::plan_emergency_production`); the city fell T30.
- **A Hard Sparta conquers nothing:** with a Hard brain in the human's seat on the same map (`playtest_bench salts`, 16 runs) it made war and peace three times and took 0 cities, while the Japan won all 16. Search rollouts model the opponent that way, so they read 43% where the seat's win chance was 8.6%.
- **Level 4 was 12-15 XP away** (Soldiers at 138 and 135 of 150) after 61 turns; XP comes at about 2.5 a turn at war and nothing in the peaceful gaps.

## 2026-10-07 — A freshly started Codex lane can lose its first task to the reaper
The first task on a newly started lane 2 (`scout-deadlock`) was marked "finished (reaped)" five minutes after starting, with no review file and no branch commits. The reaper moves any running task whose `codex` process it can't find once the task file is 5 minutes old. Lane preparation on a cold worktree, or a codex start-up failure, makes it look dead. Check `.reviews/astra-<name>.md` exists before trusting a reaped task. Requeue it by moving the task file from `done/` back to `pending/`.

## 2026-10-07 — Re-theming a faction touches five text places, two e2e assertions and one art set
The Switzerland became vault goblins as flavour only (rules unchanged). The flavour lives in `FACTION_INFO` (`app/src/data.ts`, the motto), `FACTION_SHEETS` (identity and lore), `FACTION_STORIES` in `GuideContent.tsx`, the art README and the factions skill's story column. The motto is asserted verbatim by `e2e/guide.spec.ts` and `e2e/diplomacy.spec.ts`, so a motto change fails those until updated. The portrait keeps its file names (`leader-switzerland*.png/jpg`), so every use picks up the new art with no code change.
## 2026-10-07 — Defence in time (playtest 8.4): the Walls swap fixed, no measurable gain
Walls under way are now finished or bought, never swapped for an emergency unit (`keep_walls`; the 8.4 Byzantium capital swapped Walls at 86 of 91 Production for a Ranger and fell), and a rival within 12 hexes with twice our public army is a border threat at any time (`army_ratio_walls`). Against Proxy v7, 2,048 paired duels: +0.1 [-1.1, +1.3]; 50.1% against the old Hard. `playtest_bench salts` now takes `proxy7`: on the 8.4 seed, Proxy v7 playing the human's Sparta seat never wins in 16 salts (3-5 VP, first capture turn 101), so it doesn't reproduce the human's run; the 8.4 hole is the human's tactics, which neither proxy plays.
- **Correction (same day):** the reaped `scout-deadlock` task wasn't dead. Its codex process kept running and finished successfully at 13:35. Requeuing it then started a duplicate run, which left a one-line "WIP left in lane 2" commit on the branch. The reaper's `pgrep -f "astra-<name>.md"` check missed the live process. Before requeuing a reaped task, wait for its review file, or check `ps` for a `codex exec ... astra-<name>.md` process. Drop runner WIP commits from a duplicate run.

## 2026-10-07 — A learned defence beat hand-written defence fixes by a wide margin
Against the Proxy v7 Mongols rush:
- **Hand-written fixes, each landed as a gene:** sentries, rush walls, the army-ratio walls trigger, keeping nearly built walls. Together they moved Hard's score in the Mongols row from about 6% to about 10%, and each one measured near zero against Proxy v7 overall.
- **The learned policy:** the evolution-strategies pilot, a 150-parameter linear policy over city and unit decisions, held the capital at turn 60 in about 90% of held-out games, against about 59% for Hard (re-checked on master).

For narrow, measurable decisions, prefer learning (ES on the cloud, at about $1 a run) over hand-tuning heuristics. Keep hand-written behaviours as the vocabulary and for rules a human can read.

Also: Sparta level-4 veterans are bounded by war volume, not drill. A Hard Sparta fights about 1.3 wars and takes 0.5 cities a game, while level 4 needs about 14 kills per veteran.

## 2026-10-07 — Verify Codex AI fixes on the whole AI test suite, outside the sandbox
Luna's scout-deadlock fix passed its own new test, core tests and clippy inside its sandbox. Run outside, `cargo test --release -p legatum-ai --lib` then failed `openings::tests::mixed_opening_orders_validate_in_real_fogged_games`, because a scout emitted an invalid order, and `--test scout_threats` failed too. Both pass on master. A Codex fix to AI behaviour needs the full `legatum-ai` lib and integration tests before landing, not just the targeted test. Keep `| tail` off verify logs so the failing test's name and panic survive.

## 2026-10-07 — The learned rush defence wins its narrow test and loses full games
Ported exactly as gene `learned_defence` (branch `claude/es-port`). It reproduces the pilot: capital held at turn 60 in 90.2% of games against Hard's 58.6%. In full games, though, Hard with the policy beat Proxy v7 in 5 of 64 duel-matrix games, against about 20 of 64 without it. The policy was trained only on "capital held at turn 60 against Mongols", but it overrides fortify, go-home and picket orders for the whole game.
- **Rule:** a policy learned on a narrow objective must be gated to the states it was trained on (here: early turns, under threat), or trained on the full-game outcome. Always measure on full games before it becomes a default.
## 2026-10-07 — The Settle lens drew nothing without a Settler (playtest 8.4)
Site scoring (`settlementSites`, core and WASM) returns nothing unless it is given a Settler unit, and `MapCanvas` only had one when a Settler was selected or still on the map, so after the capital was founded the lens was empty. The fix scores a stand-in Settler placed in the capital (`withProxySettler`, added to a copy of the view only), so the whole explored map is washed and ranked three sites, travel counted from the capital; the Settle wash is 0.6 (was 0.5) so Great/Good/Poor read at a glance. Not a WebKit or fog issue. Checking the lens needs a state with no Settler: use `hallFixture('buildable')`, not turn 2 of a new game (almost nothing is explored yet).


## 2026-10-07 — Playtest 8.4 (Celts): the AIs out-expand nobody and never fight the leader
Analysis: `.reviews/playtest-8.4-celts-analysis.md`. The human won by a 10-turn hold (T140-T149) at 11 VP against 3, 1 and 4; all three AIs were Hard + Search, and persistent brains reproduced the tape on every turn.
- **Contain is inert.** Sparta and Switzerland declared war on the 6-VP human at T52 and T54, entered Contain at value -1 and -95 ("VP denied" is worth 0-1), and then launched 0 campaigns and made 4 attacks in 94 turns, all on a Scout. All 17 searches picked Contain or Peace at 0.02-9.5% win chance, and in 15 the two options had the identical rollout value, so the search could not tell them apart.
- **Celts is never attacked, by any brain:** with a Hard or Hard + Search brain in the Celts seat (32 runs), a war was declared on it in 2 runs and a city lost in 1. Neither wins (0 of 32, mean 5.4-5.7 VP, 3.1-3.4 cities against the human's 11).
- **AI goal rites and Great Works did not happen at all** in 149 turns (rites wait for 55-85% of the 300-turn game, `goal_actions.rs`); the human's two +2 plays at T139-140 decided the game. The first AI Great Work started a turn after the hold began.
- **A 1-city AI is a permanent victim.** Athens lost its first Settler at T25 (the human declared war while it stood next to the capital and captured it) and stayed on 1 city for 94 turns; swapped a Settler at 80 of 163 for a Soldier at T74; and refused peace at T32 and T54 although its own search had chosen Peace. Unharried it wins 5-9 of 16 on this map.
- **Alliance upkeep veto:** two AIs at war with the same leader refused each other 8 times ("the fee and upkeep would empty our treasury", score null), allying only at T103.
- **Tooling:** `playtest_bench` has no Hard + Search brain kind; a scratch `search` kind (16 lines in `brain_for`) was enough. The Sparta capital had no building until T81 and paid Settlers of 132-406 Production on 9-13 a turn.

## 2026-10-07 — Bug saves are WASM-format JSON; the "settler into war" report was the AI Japan
- `.legatum` saves are the WASM GameState JSON, with u64 fields such as `seed` quoted as strings. Unquote them before `serde_json` loads them into a Rust test.
- The turn-67 report of a Settler walking into enemy land at war was AI Japan unit 56, on a route through the human's Japan city Evensong. It wasn't a Germany unit, and that's why Luna couldn't confirm it. The landed fix (`settler-war-safety`) gives a route with 0 human-owned tiles, where master's crossed 4. Barbarians count as nearby enemies too, so the AI skips sites next to barbarians even at peace.

## 2026-10-07 — Contesting the VP leader (playtest 8.4 follow-up, claude/ai-contest-leader)
Measured with the new `contest_leader` example (8-player Standard, every seat Hard or every seat Hard + Search, seeds 9600001+, cloud, master ae36e888 against this branch). A trigger is a team at 7+ VP at T80 or starting a hold; it is answered when within 15 turns a rival at war with it has 2+ units within 4 hexes of one of its cities (one away from home) or an AI starts a campaign on one of its cities.
- **Contain had no body, so rollouts couldn't tell it from Peace.** Its only effect was a longer march radius in `seize_open_city`, which needs an unguarded city; against a garrisoned leader Contain issued the same orders as Peace, and when the leader (modelled as Hard) refused peace both rollouts were the same game. A forced-rollout probe on true states confirmed it: identical runs were seats with nothing to march (Celts, or no free army) facing a leader that refused peace; where peace was signed the two differed.
- **A 7-VP leader at T80 was almost never fought:** 37 of 275 answered (13%) with plain Hard; rivals at war with it averaged 0.4. Its Contain price was negative (the "VP denied" assumption is worth 0-1); a *leader's race* term (we gain our share of the win chance the war takes from the leader) plus the strict leader at 70% as a candidate fixed that: 174 of 274 (64%).
- **Marching needs a field army and a reachable target.** The containment target was measured from our cities (range 14), so armies far from home found none, and `campaign_target_for` demanded a melee unit (a Germany line of Rangers never moved). Measure from the marching army too, accept an all-ranged staging force, and fund a field force (`desired_army` = cities + 5) while the war lasts.
- **The treasury veto was the whole coalition delay:** 1,478 Alliance refusals citing "empty our treasury" between teams at war with the same leader in 1,024 games, and 23% of second-war episodes never allied. Weighing the fee against the leader's win: 47 refusals, 5% never allied (median 2 turns both ways; Hard + Search 7 → 2).
- **Results, plain Hard (1,024 games):** answered 54% → 80% (holds 65% → 85%), stalls 15 → 7, mean length 254 → 256. **Hard + Search (256 games):** 69% → 72% (holds 75% → 82%, 7-VP leaders 48% → 43%), identical Contain/Peace rollouts 45% → 31% of searches that had both. Search seats are often broke with no army at T80 (Gold 0, 0 units for three of six rivals in a traced game), so a containment war has nothing to march; that is the economy's problem, not the plan's.
- **No regression against Proxy v7** (2,048 duel-matrix games, seeds 9100001+, paired 0.0 [-1.2, +1.2]; first 128: 43 wins against 39); 1v1 stalls 85 → 91 (noise).
- **Pillar 11 isn't solved by marching:** the T80 leader still wins about 70% of 8-player games either way. The armies now go, but they are small and mostly stage; denting a leader needs stronger AI economies.
- Search rollouts of 10 turns can't see a war's payoff, so a Contain option's leader's race is added to its rollout mean (`search_race`), the same way a chess engine adds an evaluation at the leaves.

## 2026-10-07 — AI expansion (playtest 8.4 weaknesses 1 and 5): Settlers were boxed in, not under-built
Branch `claude/ai-expansion`. Tools: `settler_loss_recovery` (scenario), `economy_t80`, `settler_life` (per-Settler positions from a tape), all in `crates/sim/examples/`. Bench: 4-player Small Hard, seed 9100001, turn limit 200 (a limit of 100 starves the Settler `time` gate after turn 50), 512 games.
- **The finding.** In the baseline, 43% of Settler-turns before T60 were spent idle in a city and a quarter more idle on the map; only 31% walked. Cause: `hard_defense::prepare_hard_defense` marks every tile a *peaceful* rival's military unit could reach as Settler danger (the surprise-war snipe defence), and a Scout is a military unit. Sentry and exploring Scouts sat 3 hexes off for 20 to 30 turns, so every candidate site read UNREACHABLE. Gene `peace_scout_danger` 0 fixes it: idle-in-city Settler-turns 2099 to 943, T50 cities 2.54 to 2.79 (all 8 factions, Sparta 1.95 to 2.47). Ignoring peaceful armed units too gave nothing more at T50.
- **Four cities at T50 is not reachable by AI choices.** A forced Settler-first AI (every gate but pop, site and time bypassed) reaches 2.89 at T50 and 4.07 at T75; the capital makes a Settler about every ten turns (cost 76 to 150 and one citizen each), and the human's 5 at T50 came from Farm and Granary first and no Ranger. Settler priority, pipeline 3, open-cities 8, travel penalty, site distance, commit, patience and production weights all moved T50 by under 0.05. Proxy v6 (settler first) has 3.2 cities at T50 in the same bench, Proxy v7 3.5.
- **Forcing expansion loses the duel.** The forced variant scored -16.8 points against Proxy v7 (31 games worse, 6 better): the threat gates are earning their keep. `settler_priority` 1500 to 2500 (4-player T75 +0.3) cost 0.6 to 1.4 points there, so it stays 0.
- **The "319 swapped Settlers" were purchases.** A Settler that completes by Gold looks like a swap in a tape diff. `keep_settler` (40) is still right for the 8.4 case, but the cause there was `last_city_front`: a lone city with an army at war within 8 hexes builds no Settler, so a one-city empire with a parked rival stayed one city. `lone_city_radius` 4 (was 8): scenario with war and the first Settler captured at T15 and 3 parked Soldiers 6 hexes out, 60% to 72% reach 3 cities by T75; duel unchanged (+0.2).
- **Peace.** A Hard + Search plan that chose Peace with a team now accepts its offer: `treaty_score` skipped by the strike and containment-target vetoes (`peace_follows_plan`). Plain Hard keeps the vetoes: `hard_defense_tests::dispatch_maintains_containment_but_allows_peace_when_home_is_overrun` encodes that a near-winner is contained rather than appeased.
- **Eight-player Standard Hard (192 games, seed 9600001):** seats with 0 Gold and 0 units at T80 are 0.9% (plain Hard; the Hard + Search traces that showed more were search seats); the T80 leader wins about half (52.6%, 49.4% after), so the 70% in the Search games is not plain Hard's. Mean cities per seat T50 2.18 to 2.40, T80 2.85 to 3.17.
- **Gotchas.** `Order::DeclareWar` by an inert brain plus removing a Settler from `state.units` is enough for a scenario. The example binaries are not rebuilt by `cargo build -p legatum-sim`; build `--examples` after changing a default or the old default is measured.
## 2026-10-07 — The admin SSO token still expires; batches don't depend on it
`terraform plan` failed with "refresh cached SSO token failed", even though the SSO session is set to 30 days. Infra changes need `aws sso login --profile <admin-profile>`, which opens the browser for the user to approve. Cloud batches are unaffected, because they use the launcher's own access key, which has no session to expire. The monthly cap was raised at the user's request; the deny-launch action is set at 100% of the cap, so it moves with it.

## 2026-10-07 — Mac load is cold compiles, not the GPU or sims
At a load of 90 on 16 cores, almost all CPU was `rustc`. It came from five or six worktrees each building the workspace, plus Codex lanes for another project on the same machine. The GPU sat at 0%; its 100% bursts come from Playwright Chromium screen tours. sccache was already on (`.cargo/config.toml`, since 2026-10-03) with about a 75% hit rate, but it can't cache workspace crates at different worktree paths, or bins and tests. Each fresh agent worktree therefore still pays a full cold build, and there were 82 worktrees using 265 GB. The fix is reuse: a warm agent worktree pool (`tools/ops/agent-worktree.sh`, queued) and removing merged, idle worktrees. The ops supervisor now checks machine performance every run.

## 2026-10-07 — sccache plus `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")` in a build script breaks after a worktree is removed
The 8.5 build failed twice in `crates/wasm/build.rs` with "engine source directory: NotFound". The cause was a compiled build script reused from the sccache cache. It had been compiled in another worktree, so `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")` had baked in that worktree's path, and that worktree had just been removed.
- **Fix:** build scripts read `std::env::var("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")` at run time; never use `env!()` in `build.rs`.
- **Risk:** removing stale worktrees can surface this in any other compile-time path.

## 2026-10-07 — Build the Tauri app with sccache off
The 8.5 desktop build failed in `tauri::generate_context!()`: it couldn't read `dist/assets/legatum-ZzP9Q0C-.js`, but Vite had just written `legatum-DHQHMcg-.js`. sccache had served a stale compile of the desktop crate, because the frontend assets Tauri embeds aren't inputs that sccache hashes. Building with `RUSTC_WRAPPER=""` (and touching `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`) fixed it.
- **Rule:** always run `npm run tauri:build` with `RUSTC_WRAPPER=""`.
- **Also:** never let an agent fast-forward master in the main checkout while a build reads it. That plus the cache cost three failed builds.

## 2026-10-07 — The Codex reaper reaped live tasks; abandoned games are archived
- **Reaper:** it marked live tasks "reaped" because `pgrep -f "astra-<name>.md"` misses some live `codex exec` processes. Two tasks (ui-minors-8-4, ai-goal-rites-2) also died with exit 143 (SIGTERM) at the same second, for a reason not yet known. `run_codex` now writes the Codex PID to `.reviews/astra-<name>.md.pid`, and the reaper never reaps while that PID is alive. Requeue a killed task as `<name>-r2`, branching from its old branch.
- **Abandoned games:** a game the user abandons and restarts isn't lost when the autosave is overwritten. The app archives every game's full tape to `~/LegatumData/archive/YYYY/MM/<game_id>.tape.json.zst`, indexed in `index.jsonl` with the outcome (`abandoned`), turn count, seats and the engine commit.

## 2026-10-07 — Playtest 8.5: Search skipped Sparta's open window; queue order is mtime
- **The skipped window:** Hard + Search Sparta had the edge at T14 (3 healthy units, the human capital unwalled 8 hexes away), and `sparta_iron_target` allowed the strike. Search still chose Economy over Campaign by 2815 to 768 (20-turn rollouts, 2 samples) and stayed there until T52. The human walled up at T41-42. The late Contain campaign on a walled city then aborted `insufficient_army` within a turn. Search rollouts undervalue an early, time-limited window. In the partial game, Japan's search did pick Campaign on an exposed leader city and took it in 3 turns; that's the behaviour the user called wise.
- **Settler capture:** `settler_trip_risk` flagged the trip (50, against a threshold of 40), but `guard_exposed_settlers` sent no escort.
- **Codex queue order:** workers take `pending/` tasks in modification-time order (`ls -tr`). Editing a task file moves it to the back; touch it with an old timestamp to put it first.

## 2026-10-07 — Playtest 8.5 release check: 12 red Playwright tests, 3 real user-facing bugs
Chromium 9 failed, WebKit container 3. Triage, each re-run alone:
- **Real: "Show on map" from a report line left the tile under the report.** `focus` only centred, so a taller report (an AI peace offer adds a Diplomacy block) covered the centred tile at 1440x1100. `jumpTo(hex, true)` now settles on the nearest tile position clear of the HUD (`visibleTilePoint`, shared obstacle list with the legendary-moment reveal). `turn-report.spec.ts:61` is the regression test. (The Luna branch `astra/phone-specs-out-of-release` "fixed" it by centring the attacker, which is still under the report; don't reuse that part.)
- **Real: the 8-empire Every Way to Score sheet overflowed at 1000x600** (the eighth empire, Japan, cut off): the Mongols goal rule ("The Open Road") wrapped to five lines in the 300px race column. Shortened the rule text; a long goal rule is a fit risk for every 8-empire game.
- **Stale test: `queue-click` at 1000x600.** The city panel gained growth, tiles and now-producing sections above the picker (the first card sat at the fold, the second below it), so Playwright scrolled the panel for the second click. The app's scroll anchoring is fine; the test now puts the first card just under the sticky `.cp-head` first (not under it: Playwright treats a card behind the sticky head as obscured and scrolls). Worth a design look: at 1000x600 a new city's picker opens with one card in view, because the idle-city scroll only fires when the first card is wholly below the fold.
- **Phone specs out of the gate:** `mobile-portrait.spec.ts` (7 tests, phone portrait, not a target) is ignored by the `game` project; two of its tests fail by design since the VP rework.
- **Load flakes (not bugs):** `staging.spec.ts:100` and Sparta 7b at 1440x900 pass alone and in a three-worker rerun; the release check ran six workers at machine load 40-90 against a 45s test timeout. The three WebKit screen-tour failures were intermittent (the same spec passed, timed out or hung on alternate runs): a moment card animates in and advances on its own timer, so Playwright's "element is stable" wait on `Dismiss celebration` never ended on the slow container engine (240 s timeouts). The tour now dispatches the click instead; 4 of 4 then passed. Never run a Chromium Playwright run in the same worktree while `webkit-docker.sh` is running: both use `app/test-results`, and one run's cleanup deletes the other's traces (ENOENT, and two spurious screen-tour failures).

## 2026-10-07 — Human proxies v8: too weak to settle "which playbook wins"; capital Walls decided the rush
On the user's 8.5 map (seed 874654782136188), 512 AI salts each, original seats and mirror, the Mongols and Sparta human proxies won 0 and 0 in the original seats and 42 and 8 in the mirror; Hard + Search seats won the rest with VP holds around T190. Cloud cost was about $0.13. In every game Sparta took the Mongols capital, which was always unwalled (median T45), even though Mongols had walled other cities. The human had bought capital Walls at T42.
- **Fidelity gaps:** the Mongols proxy missed the T28 Scout raid that stole a Settler and the 5-cities-by-T50 pace. The Sparta proxy reached 4 cities at T62 against the human's 7.
- **Rule:** validate a proxy on the decisive milestones before trusting a head-to-head.
- **The engine has a separate AI RNG stream** (`Rng::derive(game_seed, [AI, seat])`). Varying only that salt keeps the map, starts, combat and barbarians fixed. It's the right way to replay one map many times, but runs are correlated, so the CIs describe one map, not maps in general.
- **Lead gotcha:** I almost missed that a Codex task had finished. Check `.progress/astra.log` for finished tasks before telling the user something is still running.

## 2026-10-07 — verify-branch.sh first real runs
- `tools/astra/verify-branch.sh` had never run before landing: it had an unbound `$2` in `smoke()`, a lock that failed on reuse, `clean -fdx` wiping `target/` between master and branch builds, and app steps with no `scripts/build-wasm.sh` or release-server build (the git-ignored `app/src/wasm-pkg` and `target/release/legatum-server` are inputs to tsc, vitest and the e2e fixtures). All fixed on master; a smoke run fails on panics only, since stalls also happen on master (2 in 50 Hard small-map games).
- Two branches that both move the sim tapes (Roadworks AI value, Wayfinder sight) conflict in `crates/sim/tests/pins.json`: merge, keep either side, then `tools/sim/repin.sh` on the merged tree and commit the result.
- Settle-lens screenshots do not show the layout audit's overflow: the healing meter's "Not healing: ..." sentence clipped on both sides at 1000x600 and 1440x900 with an empty audit. Look at every changed screen in both states.

## 2026-10-07 — Stale worktrees are held by lock reasons, not by work
`stale-worktrees.sh` keeps any locked worktree unless the lock reason contains "agent". Most merged, clean, idle worktrees were locked as "added with --lock" or "locked", so the first dry run found only 15 GB to reclaim out of 265 GB. To clean up, unlock worktrees that are merged, clean (no uncommitted changes) and have no process (skip lane-*, pool-*, release-* and Codex persistent ones), then run `--apply`. Agents should lock with a reason that names the agent, so finished locks are recognisable.

## 2026-10-08 — Hard = human benchmark: first landing sequence
- **The first "56%" was stale code.** The same benchmark on master two days later gave 30%: opponents that go after the leader hit a Hard seat that leads. Re-measure on current master before quoting a baseline. Cloud and local runs are bit-identical per salt, so they can be mixed.
- **The benchmark barely separates Normal from Hard** (Easy 23%, Normal 33%, Hard 30%; 64 salts per map). Switzerland and Celts are 0% for every difficulty and Japan is 73-98%, so read the per-map table (Sparta, Mongols, Celts) and the milestone ratio, not the headline.
- **A Hard seat's biggest gain came from copying a human trick, not deeper search:** `settler_raid` (declare war, capture an exposed rival Settler) took Celts from 0% to 53% and the duel vs proxy7 up 1.6 points. The `settler_chain` gene does nothing here. Raids may cost the Sparta strike window (Hard + Search 44% → 2%).
- **Goal rites from turn 0 hurt:** Celts 53% → 0 and the duel −4.0 points. Gate them by expansion phase.
- **Landing gotchas:** a branch that changes AI trajectories can fail a seeded `full_game` or archived-fixture test on an unrelated order bug (the Call-to-Arms-with-Peace-offer failure here); fix the latent bug, don't just tweak the test. Archived-fixture tests that assert an exact turn should assert a range. `tools/ops/agent-worktree.sh acquire` reuses a pool slot whose holder has exited, so commit before the shell that holds it ends.

## 2026-10-08 — Session cron didn't fire overnight; use an app scheduled task
The session-only `CronCreate` supervisor (hourly at :07) never ran overnight. Codex work finished between 23:00 and 01:40 and sat unlanded until the user asked at 09:00. Session crons only fire while this conversation's REPL is idle and awake. The supervisor is now an app scheduled task, `legatum-ai-supervisor` (`~/.claude/scheduled-tasks/legatum-ai-supervisor/SKILL.md`, hourly). Each run is a fresh, self-contained session, and it works while the app is open. `verify-branch.sh` now resolves pin- and fixture-only merge conflicts by taking master's copy and repinning before the tests.

## 2026-10-08 — Rivals' raids wrecked Sparta's strike window; Codex branches conflict on shared journal files
- **The two AI behaviours clashed:** on the 8.4 Sparta benchmark map, Hard + Search Sparta fell from about 38% to 2% after Settler raids landed. Rivals' raids opened a second front during Sparta's early strike window. Limiting longer raids against an Sparta neighbour while it strikes restored 37/64. Overall Hard + Search went from 43.1% to 54.4%, and the milestone ratio from 0.71 to 0.83. Re-run the benchmark after every AI landing: AI behaviours interact across seats.
- **The only AI decisions that consult the win-chance model** (`winchance.rs`) are the plan layer and search. Production, military, Settlers and diplomacy are rule-driven.
- **Merge conflicts on shared files:** every Codex task appends to `docs/sim-results/hard-vs-human/journal.md` and `ledger.jsonl`, so branches conflict there. Resolve by keeping both sides; both files are append-only logs. `verify-branch.sh` treats that as a conflict and stops; resolve it in a temp worktree, then re-verify.

## 2026-10-08 — A stale WASM package in the main checkout fails mock/WASM parity tests
After `wayfinder-sight` landed, `src/mock/los.wasm.test.ts` failed 2 Wayfinder sight cases in the main checkout. The cause was `app/src/wasm-pkg`, still built from the 8.5 build (before the change). `scripts/build-wasm.sh` fixed it, and all 12 passed. Rebuild WASM before running vitest whenever core rules changed since the last build. Building cards show their yields only in the `blurb` text (`app/src/data.ts`), so a building whose blurb omits its yield, as Roadworks' +1 Gold did, hides it from the player.
## 2026-10-08 — City-under-attack alerts: what the data does and doesn't give you
- **One stage's battles share a snapshot:** the three Soldiers that hit Waypost Seven in one melee stage each report the same `defender_hp_after` (the HP left after the whole stage), so "HP before the first hit" is not `hp_after + damage` of the first Battle (it read 38 for a city that had 120). `siege.ts::cityHits` takes the HP before from the view before the turn (`ctx.cityHpBefore`) and falls back to hp left plus every hit only when that view is missing.
- **A loaded game has no turn report** (`report` is null after load and after a LAN reconnect), so the HP lost last turn is unknowable there. `CityDetail.attacked_last_turn` (from `City.last_attacked_turn`, no state change, no hash change) lets the alert still say "Took damage last turn". Storing HP lost would need a new state field and a sim repin; not done.
- **In the fixture the AI did not attack a walled city** with three Soldiers beside it (120 HP, Walls): only the city's shots landed. An unwalled one was attacked and captured in one turn. Worth watching in AI work; it is why the e2e fixture's "fresh" siege has no Walls.
- **`pendingDecisions` callers must pass the store state** (`siegeSeen` rides in with `humans/orders/errors`). `gameKeyHandler` and `CityPanel.advanceAfterBuild` called it without the state and would treat an opened siege as unopened forever (Return would never end the turn).
- **`tools/ops/agent-worktree.sh acquire <branch>` fails when `master` is checked out in the main worktree** ("'master' is already used"): pass `--base $(git rev-parse master)`.

## 2026-10-09 — A better win predictor didn't make a better player; new AWS accounts hit a spot quota
- **Refitted win chance:** the refitted `winchance` model, with VP-route and zero-sum features trained on stronger games, improved held-out log loss but lowered benchmark play, so the live weights stay V4. Calibration and decision quality are different targets. Judge any value model by games won, not by fit.
- **Spot quota:** the new, dedicated AWS account returned `MaxSpotInstanceCountExceeded` once league training held a few c8g.16xlarge spot servers. The default spot vCPU quota is small. Raising it is a free Service Quotas request, but it's an account change, so ask the user first.
- **Scheduled task stuck:** the app scheduled task `legatum-ai-supervisor` sat on its first run from 2026-10-08 16:23 onward, waiting for a permission approval nobody saw. Until the user approves it once, it lands nothing.

## 2026-10-09 — League training: one early gain, then a plateau; validate mid-run
The CMA-ES league (`league-training-aws`, 25 generations planned) ran 14 generations, about 625k games for about $17.5. It promoted once, at generation 4 (+4.5 points against the league, CI above 0). Generations 5–13 promoted nothing. The incumbent's league win rate drifts between 39% and 44% across generations, because each generation draws new seeds; that's noise, not progress.
- **Don't wait for the end-of-run validation.** Test the current champion on held-out human-seat maps as soon as it has been stable for a few generations, and stop the run if it doesn't transfer.
- **Tuning thresholds can't create a move the AI lacks.** The playtest gaps (Settler-capture rate, buying defence just in time instead of keeping a standing army, Markets first, contested land first) are moves.
- **Tooling:** system `python3` has no matplotlib. Use a scratchpad venv for charts.

## 2026-10-09 — Landing overnight Codex branches: what the sandbox hides
- **Codex can't run Playwright or build WASM, so UI branches arrive with failing gates it never saw.** `faction-goal-visible` broke five Chromium specs at 1000x600 (a second goal meter squeezed the race panel to a sliced row; your goal slip vanished from the objectives sheet, taking the Heir note and "Show on map" with it; the progress label wrapped your race row). The 34 Vitest mock/WASM failures it reported were a stale `app/src/wasm-pkg`, fixed by `scripts/build-wasm.sh`. Run `verify-branch.sh --app` on every UI branch; do not trust the sandbox's Vitest count.
- **Playwright runs the built `dist/`, not the source.** After editing app source in a verify worktree, run `npm run build` before re-running a spec, or the spec tests the old bundle.
- **`fog-borders.spec.ts` read `getState().views` before it existed** and failed about one run in three under load; wait for the views first.
- **Branch benchmark claims don't survive a base change.** Zero-sum relative value measured +4.1 points on its own base and −1.3 on top of Sparta conquest pace; Settler escort looked fine on a 64-game duel (22 → 25) and then read 24 → 18 on current master and −2.5 points over 512 games, with Hard + Search down 6.5 points on the human maps (Sparta 37 → 17 of 64). Re-measure after every merge, on the merged code, with the 512-game duel and the six-map benchmark.
- **A report can say "all live behaviour stays off" while a gene defaults to 1.** Check the `decision_params!` defaults and the pin diff before landing an AI branch described as neutral (`goal-first-ai` turned on a Mongols route gate).
- **Two Codex branches that touch the same AI file conflict for real:** `goal-first-ai` no longer merges because zero-sum relative value changed `winchance.rs` and `diplomacy.rs`. Land AI branches serially and rebase the next one.
- **The machine is shared:** a 64-salt, six-map Hard + Search benchmark takes 30 to 45 minutes locally at 8 threads (Hard about 15 to 30), more at the load of 60 to 100 seen while other agents build.

## 2026-10-09 — Pre-merge benchmark gains often don't survive merging; build releases in their own worktree
- **Gains shrink when combined.** Re-measured on master one landing at a time:
  - zero-sum's pre-merge +4.1 became −1.3;
  - Settler escort lowered Hard + Search by 6.5 points (Sparta 37/64 → 17/64), probably by tying up units;
  - Sparta conquest pace held (Hard +15.6).

  AI changes interact. Trust only post-merge measurements taken in landing order, and record both numbers.
- **Codex reports can misstate defaults.** `goal-first-ai` reported "all live behaviour off", but `vp_route_planner` defaulted on and moved a pin. Check `params.rs` defaults and pin moves before believing such a report.
- **Release builds go in a dedicated detached worktree** (`.claude/worktrees/build-<v>`) at the tagged commit. Landings can then continue on master without changing files under the build (three 8.5 builds failed that way).

## 2026-10-09 — Leaders mostly win; titles cluster; Sparta can't attack what it never sees; allies stop fighting
- **Leaders usually win** (256 Hard + Search games per lobby size, Standard): the VP leader at T50 wins 61% of 4-player games (8-player: 51%), and at T100 it wins 73% (8-player: 65%). The final lead change falls at a median of T95 in 4-player games.
- **Titles cluster:** one team holds 4 or more of the 5 titles in 47% of 4-player game-turns. Most Populous and Most Territory correlate at r=0.80, because population is territory.
- **AI-only games run longer than human ones:** the median 4-player game lasts 191 turns, while the user's last games ended at T88–T163.
- **Sparta never attacked in 8.6 because it never saw a city:** no Germany city ever entered its view, so it never selected a campaign against the human. An AI with no exploration toward neighbours has no targets.
- **The Celts ally went quiet:** it had attacked Mongols 24 times from T52, then had no battles with Mongols after allying with the human. The Contain and ally plans have a gap in valuation and execution.
- **Committed whole-game strategies** (first cut, opt-in): 43.2% against 52.3% for control on the human-seat maps; the Mongols map fell from 39/64 to 6/64. Untuned bundles lose to the tuned blend, so the bundles need training per archetype before they can win.

## 2026-10-09 — Faction win rates in the Hard + Search late-game study: Mongols dominates, Germany is weak
From the same 512 late-game games (all seats Hard + Search, Standard; factions drawn at random in 4-player games):

| Faction | 4-player wins | 8-player wins |
|---|---|---|
| Mongols | 60.4% | 40.2% |
| Athens | 25.2% | 10.5% |
| Japan | 23.8% | 7.0% |
| Switzerland | 22.4% | 11.7% |
| Byzantium | 19.1% | 5.1% |
| Germany | 17.8% | 5.1% |
| Celts | 12.7% | 4.7% |
| Sparta | 12.7% | 14.5% |

A fair share is 25% in 4-player games and 12.5% in 8-player. The best-to-worst ratio is about 4.8x against the pillar-8 target of 1.5x. The user lost twice as Germany on 8.6; the second loss was to Mongols. This isn't a balance study, since balance work waits for a competent AI, but it's the first data point since the AI started beating the user.
- **Why Mongols wins** (same 4-player cohort, per seat, Mongols against the other factions' range):
  - **Cities:** 3.6 at T50 (others 2.7–3.0) and 6.5 at T100 (others 4.1–5.1).
  - **Captures:** it captures 2.9 cities a game (others 0.0–2.3).
  - **Settler safety:** it loses only 0.08 Settlers a game (others 0.34–0.78).

  +1 move on every military unit compounds three ways: it wins fights (reaches ranged units and concentrates), it conquers, and it protects or steals Settlers. Its drawback (no terrain defence bonus) barely bites.

## 2026-10-09 — Units walking backwards in a queue (playtest 8.6, turn 77)

Six soldiers sent through a one-tile pass to a goal about 8 tiles away: two of them (u47, u48 on the `p86-pathing-t79` save) ended turns 1-3 tiles farther from the goal than they started and took 3 extra turns. Two causes in `rules/movement.rs::next_step` / `path::route_around_friends`, both about friends:
- **A tired friend is a wall for the round.** A friend out of moves but with a standing goto was treated like a parked unit, so a follower whose next tile held it chose the long way round (10 tiles against 7) and turned back next turn when the friend had moved on. The detour is now taken only if it arrives in no more turns than waiting (`waiting` argument).
- **Reservations reached too far.** A tile a lower-id friend declared a step into was off-limits anywhere on the route, not just as the unit's own next step. It forced the same detour in round one. Reservations now apply only to adjacent tiles.
Distance-to-goal in a straight line is not monotone on a correct route (it bends round mountains); the preview card now says so (`routeTurnsText`). Reproduce with `crates/sim/examples/tape_unit_trace.rs` (dump the state on the recording code with `DUMP_AT`, replay on new code with `LOAD_FROM`/`LOAD_AT`). The pool script `tools/ops/agent-worktree.sh` stored a short-lived PID in its lock, so a second agent wiped a working tree; use a dedicated `git worktree add` until it is fixed.
## 2026-10-09 — Territory borders were too pale to read; the toned-down seat palette also failed colour-blind sight

- A first-time player could not tell whose territory was whose. The cause was three things stacked: a 0.07 tint, a hairline (about 1.5 CSS px, half under the clip) and a seat colour mixed 30% with umber, then the fog wash over the lot. Where two empires touched, the later one overpainted the earlier one's line.
- Checked the palette with Machado (2009) colour-blind simulation and CIEDE2000: the map's toned-down seat colours had a worst pair at 2.1 under deutan (azure and violet), the parchment inks 1.2. The border palette (`ATLAS.seat.border`) keeps each hue family and staggers lightness (crimson dark, amber and lime light, azure bright, violet dark) and reaches 11.7 or better in all four modes, found by an annealing search with per-seat lightness, chroma and hue bounds so crimson stays red and amber stays amber. A search with wide bounds "won" by turning amber into olive and crimson into salmon: bound the family first.
- Fix: one clip-to-own-tiles pass per empire, widest stroke first (seat line, then ink on top), so the ink sits at the boundary and each side keeps its own line. Variants A, B, C are selectable with `?borders=`; C (A plus a 0.18 tint and a heavier ink rule between neighbours) is the default.
- Gotcha for fixtures: the core fixture reads each team's known owners from `knownOwner[team]` (not `owner`), so a hand-built map with live territory must set `knownOwner['0']` and `knownCities` or the viewer sees almost no borders. Also let a 2x shot settle (about 2.5 s) or a half-refined chunk gets captured.
- Ops gotcha (cost this task its first build): `tools/ops/agent-worktree.sh` stores `$PPID`, the one-shot Bash shell, as the lock owner, so every pool lock looks stale after the first command and the next acquire runs `git reset --hard` and `git clean -fd` on a worktree still in use. Two agents lost uncommitted work this way. Commit early (WIP commits are fine) and prefer a dedicated `git worktree add` until the script records the agent's own pid.
## 2026-10-09 — The worktree pool wiped two agents' uncommitted work
`tools/ops/agent-worktree.sh` stored `$PPID`, the PID of the one-shot Bash shell that ran `acquire`, as the lock owner. That shell exits immediately, so every lock looked stale, and the next `acquire` ran `git reset --hard` plus `git clean -fd` on a worktree still in use. At 21:47 it destroyed the borders agent's uncommitted work; at 22:24 it destroyed the pathing agent's (7 files plus an untracked example; not recoverable from stash or reflog).
- **Fix:** locks now end only by `release` or after 12 hours, and a slot with uncommitted changes is skipped, never reset.
- **Lessons:** never infer liveness from a PID captured inside a tool call. Never run a destructive git command on a worktree without checking `git status --porcelain` first. Agents should make WIP commits early.

## 2026-10-09 — Cargo can't write .d files in repo target/ from the lead's shell; build WASM with a scratch target dir
`scripts/build-wasm.sh` and `cargo build` from the lead's Bash failed with "error writing dependencies to …/target/…/*.d: Operation not permitted". The stale `app/src/wasm-pkg` (Oct 8) then caused 36 mock/WASM parity failures that looked like real regressions. With `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` pointed at the scratchpad, the build worked, and 2,133 of 2,134 tests passed; the one failure was a timing check under load. Before trusting a parity failure, check the wasm-pkg timestamp. Build with a scratch `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` when the repo target/ refuses writes.

## 2026-10-10 — The human-seat benchmark penalised changes that improve every AI
`hard_vs_human` ran the candidate in all seats. Settler safety (losses −32%) and Sparta war (wins 17.7% → 26.0%) both lowered the human-seat score, because they also made the opponents stronger. Measure AI changes seat-only and opponent-only (`benchmark-seat-split`). The app scheduled supervisor stalled again on its second run, at 00:25, waiting for an approval, so nothing landed overnight.

## 2026-10-10 — The hourly supervisor task ran twice in two days
The `legatum-ai-supervisor` scheduled task starts each run as a new session, in the default permission mode. No `permissions.defaultMode` is set in `~/.claude/settings.json`, so that default is manual. A run that reaches a command not on the allowlist waits for approval indefinitely: the 2026-10-10 run sat about 12 hours on one prompt. The scheduler won't start a new run while one is still running, so one stalled or long run (the 2026-10-08 run lasted 32 hours) blocks every later hour. Switching a session to auto only changes that session.

## 2026-10-10 — Decisions from conversation slipped through; two automatic guards added
An audit found gaps the user's decisions had left. CLAUDE.md still said "sci-fi 4X", the superseded fantasy themes were unmarked, and natural wonder rules and workflow lessons were missing. The learnings Stop hook only fires on code changes, so it never caught decisions made in chat. Two guards now cover this:
- **`.claude/hooks/docs-check.sh` (Stop):** blocks once when the latest user message reads like a decision or preference and no skill, memory, CLAUDE.md or human-reasoning file changed. It reads only the last 8 MB of the transcript (about 0.25 s; transcripts exceed 200 MB).
- **A docs audit step in the hourly scheduled supervisor:** it reads the user's prompts since the last audit from the transcripts and patches what's missing.

## 2026-10-10 — stale-worktrees.sh loses its temp directory mid-run
A dry run of `tools/ops/stale-worktrees.sh` printed `line 112: .../stale-worktrees.XXXX/cwd: No such file or directory` (and `.../ps`) for every worktree, while the machine was at load about 50. Its process and working-directory checks silently fail, so its "keep" and "remove" verdicts can't be trusted until this is fixed. The likely cause is a cleanup trap or a subshell removing the `mktemp -d` directory too early. Don't run `--apply` until it's fixed.

## 2026-10-10 — Faction sheet copy has a pixel budget; check every faction at 1000x600
Rewriting faction strength and price lines in plain language made five sheets (Byzantium, Germany, Celts, Mongols, Sparta) scroll at 1000x600 and 1280x800, and `layout.spec.ts` only reports the first failing faction. Measure `scrollHeight - clientHeight` for every sheet at once (a loop over `FACTIONS`) before iterating. A Germany sheet with a live goal also carries the "live goal" terms line, so it has about one line less room than the others. A Codex branch built from an old base can also re-add assets that master removed: `organic-sfx` rebuilt the Court music track and re-encoded Atlas, so diff `app/public/audio/music` and the manifest before landing an audio rebuild.

## 2026-10-10 — Gate step timings: the Chromium screen tour is the longest step
- **Data:** read from the status-file timestamps in `.progress/verify/*/steps` across three `--app` verifications. `app-playwright` took 18–20 minutes of wall time, the longest single step. `test` took 7–20, `clippy` 1–7, `app-server` 1–8, and `app-vitest` 2–5. A full `--app` verification took about 40–80 minutes. Durations were never recorded, so these come from file timestamps. A step-timing ledger (wall and CPU time) has been proposed.
- **Load at 10:20:** about 40 on 16 cores. About 7 cores went to three concurrent Playwright tours, and no sims were running on the Mac. Two `verify-branch master --app` runs were going on the same commit at once, which is duplicate work; caching gate results by tree hash would prevent it.
- **Unverified:** Chromium headless-shell's GPU process ran near 100% CPU although the map is Canvas 2D. Software rasterisation is suspected; measure CPU-seconds with a GPU-backed headless mode before changing anything.

## 2026-10-10 — Timing ledger: measuring CPU from bash, and the first latency number
- **Bash CPU accounting:** `verify-branch.sh` steps are often shell functions, so they can't be exec'd under a timing wrapper. The `times` builtin's second line gives the shell's waited-for children's user+sys time, but only when it runs in the shell itself. `times | awk` or `$(times)` forks a subshell whose child counters start at zero. Write it to a file, `times > f`, and diff two snapshots. When comparing two snapshot files in one awk call, use `FNR`, not `NR`.
- **Edit live scripts atomically:** `runner.sh` and `verify-branch.sh` are executed by long-lived bash processes, and bash reads scripts incrementally, so an in-place edit can corrupt a running copy. Write a temp file and `os.replace`/`mv` it into place, which gives a new inode; running copies keep the old one. `runner.sh` loops re-exec themselves between tasks when its mtime changes.
- **Finish → master latency** (first `timings-report.py` run, 7 days): 59 Codex tasks landed with a median 3.5 hours after finishing, the slowest 33.4 hours, and 24 finished branches are unlanded (some deliberately held).

- **2026-10-10 — A cleanup-only signal trap keeps a script running without its files.** `trap 'rm -rf "$tmp"' EXIT HUP INT TERM` removes the temp dir on TERM but does not exit, so `stale-worktrees.sh` scanned on after a timeout and printed "No such file" with untrustworthy keep verdicts. Trap EXIT for cleanup and make signals `exit`.

- **2026-10-10 — WebKit screen tour times out on fixture loads when the Mac is saturated.** At load 145, `loadFixture` (Continue, then wait 10 s for `turn-number`) failed in two WebKit-in-Docker screen-tour tests (research panel by faction; goal actions) while Chromium passed. The failure screenshot showed Turn 2 already loaded, which points to a slow WASM load rather than a missing element, but that's unproven until it's re-run at low load. The cause of the load: two analysis tasks ran `hard_vs_human` directly with all cores. Benchmark runs in task prompts must go through `tools/sim/hard-vs-human.sh` (RAYON_NUM_THREADS=4).

## 2026-10-10 — The supervisor requeued a branch a landing agent was already fixing
The hourly supervisor queued `historical-faction-rename-r2` (Codex Sol) for the rename's 10 Chromium failures while a Claude landing agent was fixing the same failures in `.claude/worktrees/land-rename`. The duplicate was killed. The supervisor now checks for `land-*` and `verify-*` worktrees and the tracker before requeuing.

## 2026-10-10 — A display-name rename needs the Chromium tour, not just Vitest
- The historical-peoples rename passed cargo, WASM, tsc and the full Vitest suite, then failed 10 screen-tour tests. Composite strings built from two name sources ("Switzerland · Swiss envoy"), `data-item` ids built from display names, and guide section titles live only in rendered UI, so only the browser tour catches them. For any rename, grep the old names across `crates/*/src` and `app/src` for user-visible strings before verifying.

## 2026-10-10 — VP v2 first results; the solo target must sit within reach; a sub-agent ran pkill -f
- **Results:** crowns, overreach and era objectives (Medium overreach, 256 Hard + Search games per cell) moved the last lead change from 54% to 79% of the game (4-player). The T100 leader's win rate fell from 71% to 66% (4-player) and from 70% to 61% (8-player). The T50 leader's win rate barely moved, because the opening decides T50 and crowns can't touch it.
- **Era objectives:** scored by 67%, 54% and 39% of empires in eras 2–4 (4-player), close to the 60/50/40 targets.
- **Win target:** on today's four-era tree, permanent VP top out at 7, so a solo target of 10 stalled 75% of games. The interim target is the permanent ceiling + 1, capped at 10: 8 today, and 10 with seven eras, where the user's 10 solo and 20 team apply.
- **Process violation:** a UI sub-agent ran `pkill -f "vitest run"`, which can kill other agents' runs. Agent prompts must repeat "never pkill -f; kill by PID only".

## 2026-10-10 — The era-up takeover: a held surface must be known to the overlay-key handler, and big art ships as JPEG
- **The open turn report eats Esc and Return:** `handleOverlayKey` (`gameplayKeys.ts`, capture phase on window) closes the top dismissible surface on the first Esc or Return and consumes it. It defers only to a `.moment-lane .moment-card` (Esc) and a `.moment-card.unlock` (Return). The new full-screen `.era-takeover` is not a `.moment-card`, so with the turn report open (always, right after ending a turn) the takeover ignored both keys while its own capture handler never saw them. Found by Playwright (the takeover stayed up after Esc and Return); both selectors now include `.era-takeover`. Any new held surface in the moment lane needs the same two lines.
- **Era key art as PNG would double the bundle:** the 1920 masters are 3.4 to 3.9 MB each (the 1280 ones 1.7 to 1.9 MB). The takeover loads JPEG derivatives at quality 84 (about 0.9 MB and 0.5 MB, `app/src/assets/eras/<era>-1920.jpg` and `-1280.jpg`) through `srcset`; the PNG masters and the unused phone crops stay in the repo only as sources. City sprites stay PNG (transparency): 28 sprites in three sizes plus walls and far marks, 9.9 MB.
- **City sprite geometry:** every city, wall and mark PNG shares one canvas: the painted ground oval spans x 18 to 493 and rests on y = 454 of 512 (measured by alpha bounding box), so one anchor fits all 28 cities and the wall overlays. The oval's middle must sit low in the hex (18 world px below the centre at an 84 px canvas): the screen-space banner grows upward from 0.62 hex radii above the centre and hid every roof when the sprite was centred.
- **The terrain cache cannot wait for an image:** towns are baked into 512 px chunks per zoom level, so a sprite still decoding is skipped and the cache bumps an epoch (`cityArtEpoch`) that marks every chunk stale when the image lands, the same contract as `iconEpoch`. Remembered (fogged) cities use the population the view remembers, not zero: the old hamlet-for-everything-in-fog would have shrunk a metropolis the moment it left sight.
- **Specs that import fixtures run in Node, where `import.meta.glob` does not exist:** `eraUp.ts` is pulled in through `celebrate/moments` by the mock and the e2e fixtures, so it must not import `map/cityArt.ts` (which globs the sprites). The card carries eras and size tiers; the component resolves stems.
## 2026-10-10 — build-slot is not re-entrant: wrap at the outermost caller only
- `astra/timing-coverage` added `tools/sim/build-slot.sh` inside `repin.sh` and `scripts/build-wasm.sh`. `verify-branch.sh` already runs both inside build-slot, so each verify would hold one slot and wait for a second; three concurrent verifies (the cap) deadlock. The supervisor held the branch for the lead to strip the inner calls. Review tooling diffs for build-slot added inside scripts that callers already wrap.
## 2026-10-10 — Terrain art judged in the game, not on mocks (dev toggle `?terrain=current|a|b|a-light`)
- The terrain sprites are wired behind a dev-only URL parameter (`app/src/map/terrainVariant.ts`, sprites in `app/public/dev-terrain/`, the default stays `current`). They are baked into the terrain chunks, so units, cities, banners and resources stay on top. `a-light` draws about 53% of forest and hill hexes (a scan in draw order that lowers the chance with each drawn neighbour, so no lattice appears), tints the sprites toward vellum, and joins adjacent mountains with a ridge spine. Comparison sheets: `docs/design/terrain-art/terrain-in-game.png` and `terrain-in-game-close.png`.
- A real mid-game save for art review: run 8 scripted AIs to turn 70 in Node on `app/src/wasm-pkg` (3 seconds), flip seat 0 to human, set every seat to one team for shared vision (54 units visible instead of 9), and fill seat 0's `explored` words and `tile_memory`. The save holds u64 bitsets, so edit it with Python (exact ints) or string splicing, never JS `JSON.parse`. Delete the `archive` key or loading fails with "archive seats/setup differ".
- Sprite chunk edges: feature sprites reach past the hex, so the dev variants gather tiles with a 1.4 hex margin when baking a chunk.
## 2026-10-10 — Codex can't profile the screen tour; a profile switch can break build sharing
- The Codex sandbox blocks `sysctl` and Playwright Chromium, so `tour-cpu-profile` exited 0 with no measurements. Anything that runs Chromium or reads host load belongs in the Claude queue, not Codex. Measure CPU-seconds rather than gating on a load cutoff: the Mac has not been below load 24 all day.
- `ci-test-profile` moved the gates' tests to `--profile release-checked`. That saves 69% for Codex tasks (one checked build instead of release plus debug). In `verify-branch.sh`, though, `repin.sh` still builds `--release`, so it probably can't reuse the test build any more and compiles core, AI and sim a second time. `verify-repin-profile` (Luna) measures this and, if it costs 3 CPU-minutes or more, runs repin under the same profile, provided the pins come out byte-identical.

## 2026-10-10: Where the Mac's CPU goes
At load 89 on 16 cores, the timing ledger (`tools/ops/timings-report.py`, last 24 h) showed Codex Luna tasks using 53% of the measured CPU-minutes (each lane compiles and tests its own worktree), `verify:test` 19% (median 15 min wall), and Playwright about 29 min wall per app verify. Two more loads are unmeasured. `tools/cloud/build-linux.sh` cross-builds the cloud sim binary in a local Docker VM, which ran at about 380% CPU. Another project on the same machine also runs two Codex lanes. More local parallelism only stretches every job, so the fix is to offload (a cloud build box) and cap lanes (`.progress/astra-queue/max-lanes`).

## 2026-10-10: Agents can't approve Terraform applies for the user
The cloud-box plan was ready, and the user had approved the box in principle. The auto-mode classifier still denied the lead's message telling the agent to apply ("Protected-Scope IaC Apply"). Plan to get the user's explicit yes on the specific plan before any apply. Build the plan early so that question reaches the user as soon as possible.

## 2026-10-10: Persistent spot instances reject a shutdown-behaviour setting
`aws_instance` with a persistent spot request fails on `instance_initiated_shutdown_behavior = "stop"` ("not supported for spot instances") after the instance is already created, which leaves it tainted. Persistent spot stops on shutdown anyway, so leave the attribute null for spot, `terraform untaint` the instance instead of replacing it, and add any tags a Lambda writes to `ignore_changes`. The volume attachment also landed after first boot, so user-data can't assume the data disk is present.

## 2026-10-10: Branches finished before a rename can fail their own tests after merging it
`astra/permanent-block-tag` passed its Vitest on its own branch, then failed that same new test once merged with master after the historical rename: a Mongols exclusion showed "Not yet" instead of "Never available". The likely cause is that it detects permanent exclusions by comparing reason text from core with `factionUnavailableReason` in `data.ts` exactly, and the rename changed the wording. That cause is unconfirmed; `permanent-block-tag-r2` checks it and switches to a structured signal. Lesson: UI logic shouldn't key on display strings, and branches that finished before a wide text change need a fresh merged verify. Batching three clean UI branches into one `--app` verify kept this cheap: the failure was in one branch's own test, so the batch was rebuilt without it.

## 2026-10-10: Rule and opponent landings move the hard-vs-human benchmark without any seat-AI change
The absolute benchmark fell from 57.4% (ab436dca) to 42.6% (800db602) to 32.8% (ca3b7b1e) with the seat AI unchanged. The stronger Sparta opponent took 8.5 Mongols from 60 to 0/64. VP v2 took the 8.6 Sparta seat from 61 to 8/64 (Hard + Search 63 to 11). Two Luna experiments that measured "baseline" on different master commits disagreed by 10 points for this reason. The recorded human games ran under the old VP rules, so after a victory-rule change the absolute score stops measuring "as good as the human". Judge AI changes only by same-binary seat-only deltas, quote the baseline commit with every number, and re-baseline the human reference from a playtest on the new rules.
## 2026-10-10 — Cloud build box: design gotchas (infra/cloud-box)
- **A persistent box must not carry the sims tag.** `infra/cloud-sims`' reaper terminates every `project=legatum-sims` server with no valid `expiry` tag 6 hours after its launch time, and the watchdog treats any such server as a leak. The box is tagged `project=legatum-box`; the account budget and the launcher's budget deny policy (which blocks `ec2:StartInstances`) still cover it.
- **The box is Ubuntu, the sim servers are Amazon Linux.** Playwright's WebKit and Chromium system dependencies are supported on Ubuntu only, but binaries linked there (glibc 2.39) do not run on AL2023 (glibc 2.34). The sim binary is therefore still built in the AL2023 Docker image, now on the box (`tools/cloud/box/build-sim.sh` runs `build-linux.sh` locally in a slot with a per-slot target volume).
- **Warm cargo caches need stable paths.** Registry dependencies are reusable across checkouts, but workspace crates are fingerprinted by absolute path. So jobs run in a fixed set of slot checkouts (`/data/slots/s1..s5`) that keep their own incremental `target/`, new slots are seeded by a reflink copy (XFS) of a golden slot's target, and `verify-remote.sh` aliases the branch to a fixed name (`vrbranch`) so `verify-branch.sh` keeps one warm verifier worktree per slot instead of a cold one per branch name.
- **`verify-branch.sh` exports `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=4`** for the shared Mac. On a 64-core box that wastes most of the machine, so a `cargo` shim on the box's PATH (`tools/cloud/box/shim/cargo`) re-applies `BOX_CARGO_JOBS` (default 24).
- **Spot with `stop` behaviour has to be a persistent request**, and a box start can fail on the shared spot vCPU quota (448 on 2026-10-10, with sims batches holding most of it). The scripts return exit 3 so callers fall back to local.

## 2026-10-10 — Cloud build box: first real runs (numbers and what broke)
- **Numbers (same branches, local ledger vs box):** `astra/ui-batch-1010 --app` took 93.6 min locally at load 30 to 90 and 31.6 min on the box; without Playwright it is 74 min versus 10.9 min. Playwright (about 20 min, `workers: 1`) is now 65% of the box run, and a bigger machine cannot speed it up: shard the specs across slots if it matters. `claude/vs-bench` without `--app` took about 73 min locally and 8 min on the box (test 3.8 min, repin 1.3 min, each sim build 21 s). Cold start of the stopped box is about 1.5 min; the one-time bootstrap took 1.5 min and `box.sh warm` 6.5 min; the sim binary build (Docker, fat LTO) took 6 min.
- **First-boot user-data can run before the data volume is attached** (Terraform attaches it after the instance boots), leaving `/data` on the root disk. `box_sync` now runs `tools/cloud/box/mount-data.sh` first, which formats and mounts the volume and moves anything already under `/data`.
- **A just-stopped persistent spot instance fails `StartInstances` with `IncorrectSpotRequestState`** for a short while; `box_up` retries that error for up to 4 minutes. A start can still fail on the shared spot vCPU quota (exit 3), and `run-batch.sh` now refuses launches that would leave fewer than 64 spot vCPUs free for the box.
- **`timeout` puts its command in a new process group**, so killing the runner's group missed the job; `box-job kill` walks the process tree instead.
- **Anything that keeps an ssh session open keeps the box awake** (the idle watcher counts sessions, and `ControlPersist` holds the master for 90 s). Poll `aws ec2 describe-instances`, not `box.sh status`, when waiting for it to stop.
- **Branch names with `/`** broke the `sed` that maps the verify alias back to the real name; the replacement is escaped and uses `|`.

## 2026-10-10 — Era overhaul A2: one unit per class in every era (branch `claude/era-units`)
- **Design path.** First cut: a new unit every era (six melee, five ranged, five mounted). The lead then asked for generations of about two eras with free refits between them (war cycle p75 86 turns, eras 27 to 37). The user overruled that: every class gets its own unit in every era, provided the jumps are small, with Gold upgrades so no army is stranded. The refit machinery (free upgrade when the next form's technology is learned) was built and deleted the same day; it is in the history of this branch if the two-era design ever returns.
- **Era-gap maths: tune from the simulator, not from formulas.** `combat_scenarios --eras` fights four of the newer unit against the same Production of the older (the older army is fractional, so the share is blended between the two whole counts; whole counts alone jumped 45% to 90% for a 3% cost change). The theory is a trap: a Lanchester square law predicts parity when cost ratio equals the damage ratio per era, but the arena has frontage limits, so parity sat well above that for melee and well below it for ranged. Ranged fire is unanswered, so an older swarm of shooters keeps pace with newer ones: ranged needed a cheaper cost ratio (1.27, against 1.38 melee and 1.36 mounted) and still reaches only about 50% two eras ahead. Armies of ten or more per side saturate the arena (older swarms overwhelm newer units whatever the stats): keep era-gap duels to four to six units.
- **Cost ratio changes who wins the triangle.** Cheapening ranged with era (to fix the era gap) made equal-Production ranged armies larger: they beat previous-era cavalry in the late eras and a 2/7/0 mix beat every archetype. Two fixes: a bigger class bonus (+18, was +10; above an era's strength step, so the counter class one era behind still wins) and a dearer ranged base cost (66 against 50; the era ratios are unchanged, so the era-gap numbers did not move). Check the cross-class lines and `responses.txt` after any cost change.
- **Class bonus vs strength step.** A strength step of 6 to 8 per era with a +18 bonus is deliberate: the bonus is more than two eras of steps. Keep it larger than one step or the triangle loses to the era gap.
- **Old identifiers stay, names change.** The serialized `UnitKind` ids are unchanged for the existing six (Soldier = Spearman, Ranger = Archer, Trooper = Pikeman, Marksman = Crossbowman, SiegeEngine = Catapult, AssaultTrooper = Musketman); display names come from `data::unit_name` and the app's `UNIT_NAME`. A broad find-and-replace of the new names over tests also rewrote kind identifiers in parameters (`to: 'Trooper'`), so replace names only inside expected display strings. Renaming the identifiers touches hundreds of call sites and is deferred.
- **Aircraft are a city hangar, not map units** (`GameState::aircraft`, `rules/air.rs`). A plane gets a temporary "ghost" map unit with its own id for the ranged stage, so the existing fight, event and forecast code is reused; then it returns to its hangar. `rules::is_military` is false for aircraft, so they never count as garrison, a target or a healing unit. Anything that iterates `UnitKind::ALL` as map units (tests, fixtures) must use `rules::is_military`, not `unit_stats().military`. The mock engine has no air: it omits aircraft from the production list, and `purchases.wasm.test.ts` filters them from the parity compare.
- **`UnitKind::ALL` length is hard-coded in several places** (ai_v1_baseline, perf_profile, scenario.rs, fixture.rs, `crates/core/tests/mongols_passage.rs`, exhaustive `match`es). Each new kind needs those patched; grep for the array size.
- **The AI's war-mode production returned before any composition term.** In war mode the army-quota branch returned `2400 + quality` for every melee or mounted unit, so mounted (equal quality tier as the Spearman) lost every tie and the AI built almost no horse (about 0.5% of engaged Production in a smoke campaign). The class-triangle value (`composition_bonus`) is now added there too. Check that a new unit class is actually bought in the war-mode branch, not only in the peacetime scoring.
- **A technology whose only unit a faction cannot build is a stepping stone.** Mongols cannot build the Siege class, so Metallurgy gained a Cuirassier but Mathematics (Catapult and Phalangite) is a card for them. `tech_has_benefit` reads the unit unlocks and the card lines: re-check `factions.rs::research_cards_show_only_what_helps_the_faction` and `techCardUnlocks.test.ts` when a tech's unit list changes.
- **`npm run build` type-checks test files.** A `possibly null` in a new component test failed the app build while vitest passed; run `npx tsc --noEmit` before the build.
- **Display-name changes need the Chromium tour.** String-matched UI checks (vitest and Playwright) match unit names; the rename broke `turn-flow.spec.ts` and the screen tour's production banking check after merge. Rerun both after any name change.
- **Art for new kinds needs the image tool.** Five new units (Phalangite, Composite Archer, Cataphract, Arquebusier, Cuirassier) share the previous unit's painting until the Codex image tool paints them; prompts are in `app/src/assets/units/prompts/`.
## 2026-10-10: Branch verification rebuilt for speed (claude/verify-speed)
- **Result (same branch, same box):** `verify-remote.sh claude/verify-speed --app --no-cache` took 2,009 s with the old serial script and 291 to 317 s with the new one (warm slot, 64 cores); inside that, Playwright went from 1,231 s at one worker to 96 to 98 s at 21 workers. Locally on the loaded Mac the old script's test step alone ran 26 min wall and a full `--app` verify more than 90 minutes, much of it waiting for build slots held by other jobs.
- **Where the time went:** the screen tour is 89% of Playwright time (about 1,050 to 1,130 s of 1,140 to 1,260 s over 137 tests) and every tour test is independent (`mode: 'parallel'`, one server per worker on port 0), but the gate ran `workers: 1`. The longest single test is 41 s at one worker, so the floor is about a minute. Slowest specs: screen-tour (1,050 s summed), then layout (50 s), contrast (45 s), lenses (35 s). Slowest tests: the Sparta/Sparta "retires veterans" pair (30 to 40 s each), the tech unlock card, the Byzantium card, the Yam Relay Station.
- **Duplicate compiles:** `cargo build -p legatum-sim`, `cargo run -p legatum-sim` and `cargo test -p legatum-sim` each select a different unit graph than `cargo test --workspace`, and legatum-wasm, archive, analysis, server and sim rebuild every time the selection changes (about 100 CPU-s and several minutes under load, each). Test-name filters keep the graph; `--bin`/`--test` filters and `-p` do not. `CARGO_RESOLVER_FEATURE_UNIFICATION=workspace` did not help. The new gate builds once with `cargo test --workspace --profile release-checked --no-run`, runs the branch smoke and duel from the sim binary that build uplifts (copied first, because later cargo calls re-link it), and repins through `LEGATUM_REPIN_PROFILE=release-checked` with the generated binaries and test-name filters (repin 90 s of wall, 25 CPU-s warm, against 400 to 1,400 s and 90 to 500 CPU-s). Pins are integer maths, so the profile does not change them.
