- **cargo-nextest:** the same 1,651 tests (8 ignored) in the same count, run as separate processes across all binaries instead of one binary after another: 173 s against 305 s of run time at load 60 on the Mac, bounded by the longest single test (`opening_league_twenty_quick_games...`, 105 s under load) instead of the sum of binaries. Doctests (all zero) run separately. The box does not have it yet, so it fell back to `cargo test` there (155 s); `cargo install cargo-nextest --locked` on the box would shorten that step.
- **Concurrency needs separate cargo locks:** cargo locks the profile directory per invocation, so release-checked (tests, smoke, repin), release (clippy, server) and the WASM target build in parallel. Only wasm-pack's host build contends with the release lock.
- **Master's side is cached by the sim code:** the master smoke and duel now come from `tools/sim/cached-baseline.sh --commit <sha>` (keyed by crates, Cargo.toml and Cargo.lock), not a rebuild of master in the verifier worktree on every run (about 200 CPU-s each time). `--build-only` was added, and it fetches the commit when another clone (a box slot) created the shared worktree.
- **Affected-only selection** (summary lists what was skipped and why): docs, skills and tools only skips builds (and runs syntax checks, plus the verify cache self-test when the verifier changed); app only skips cargo; crates only keeps WASM, tsc and vitest but skips the app build, server and Playwright unless `crates/core/src`, `crates/wasm`, `crates/server`, `app/`, `scenarios/` or the Cargo files changed. Unclassified paths, `master` and `--full` skip nothing.
- **Playwright on a loaded Mac:** with five workers at load 100, three tour tests exceeded the 45 s limit (they took 47 to 62 s). The gate now passes `--timeout=90000` (tests with their own timeout keep it; assertion waits are unchanged) and caps workers by free cores (`(cores - load) / 2`, at least 2, at most cores/3 and 24).
- **Process lessons:** a running bash script reads itself incrementally, so editing it mid-run garbled the tail of a benchmark run (run copies from the scratchpad). Benchmark runs share the machine-wide build slots with everyone else, so step wall times include queueing (a 2,182 s "sim-build-master" was a 100 CPU-s build); compare CPU-seconds, or give the benchmark its own `LEGATUM_BUILD_SLOT_DIR`. Until this lands on master, a verifier worktree has master's `repin.sh`, which ignores the profile variable and runs the old release-profile repin.
- **Disk:** verifier worktrees were locked forever (about 30 idle ones at 2.6 to 4 GB each). `verify-branch.sh` now unlocks on exit, and `stale-worktrees.sh --apply` removes `verify-*` worktrees (or ones locked "astra verify-branch" / "verify-speed benchmark") that no process uses and whose last commit is over 6 hours old.
## 2026-10-10 — sccache server serialised every local build
At 20:2x the scheduled supervisor found every local Cargo build crawling: verifies waited 57+ minutes on clippy, and `cargo test` holders sat at 0% CPU. The shared sccache server had been up 12 hours and ran exactly one rustc at a time (one child in every 5-second sample) while 25 clients queued. Requests went from 41,162 to 41,165 in 30 seconds. Its 10 GiB cache was also full. The cause is not confirmed: the server's environment had no `MAKEFLAGS`, so inheriting a jobserver isn't proven. After a restart from the supervisor's shell (`sccache --stop-server`, then `SCCACHE_CACHE_SIZE=30G sccache --start-server` with the cargo jobserver variables unset), it ran 6 or more compiles at once and served about 270 requests in the first minute. In-flight builds survived because `SCCACHE_IGNORE_SERVER_IO_ERROR=1` makes waiting clients compile locally. Load then spiked to about 150 while the backlog drained. `~/.cargo/config.toml` now sets `SCCACHE_CACHE_SIZE = "30G"`, and `tools/ops/snapshot.sh` alerts when 10 or more clients wait on a server compiling at most one. Slow verify timings from earlier today may partly be this stall, not the steps themselves. **Update 22:2x:** it recurred on the server restarted at 20:26 with no jobserver variables (15 clients waiting, one rustc child, 0 requests in 20 s), so the inherited-jobserver theory is wrong; the cause is still unknown. `tools/ops/watchdog.sh` now restarts the server itself when 10 or more clients wait on at most one child with no progress for 20 s, and logs each restart to `.progress/alerts.log`.

## 2026-10-10: An AI war cycle lasts nearly three eras
The war-cycle study (Hard + Search, Standard) measured p75 86 turns from building an army to a second city capture, and it recommended eras of about 130 turns. The era core's pacing gives eras of 27–37 turns (a median game of 220 turns, in line with Civ 6's 200–250). So "era length covers the war cycle" can't be met by stretching eras. The AI is also slower at war than the user, who took cities by T52. Instead, an era change must not strand an army: one unit per class per era with small jumps (one era ahead wins about 60–65% at equal cost) and Gold upgrades.

## 2026-10-10: Locked verify worktrees leak disk
`.claude/worktrees` had grown to about 650 GB (by du, which overcounts APFS clones) across 94 worktrees. `verify-branch.sh` locks each `verify-<branch>` worktree and never unlocks it, and `stale-worktrees.sh` skips locked worktrees, so the supervisor's hourly cleanup reported almost nothing to reclaim (9.5 GB). Removing 34 unlocked, idle worktrees (merged branches, plus clean worktrees of unmerged branches, whose code stays in the branches) freed 26 GB. About 30 locked, idle verify worktrees remain; force-removing locked worktrees is blocked by the auto-mode classifier and needs the user's say-so. The fix (unlock on exit, treat stale verify locks as removable) is assigned to the verify-speed branch.

## 2026-10-10: Playwright filters match the worktree path; Playwright rewrites screenshots; the shell defaults to Node 16
- A Playwright filter such as `npx playwright test era-takeover` is a regex over the full file path. Inside `.claude/worktrees/era-takeover/` every spec matches, so the whole suite runs. Pass `e2e/<file>.spec.ts` paths instead.
- The Playwright specs rewrite the screenshots under `docs/design/*/in-game/` as a side effect. Revert them before merging, or the landing carries unintended image changes.
- The login shell's default Node is 16, which can't run vite. Use `~/.nvm/versions/node/v22.14.0/bin`.

## 2026-10-10: A slow local verify can go stale before it lands
`astra/ui-batch-1010b` passed a full local `--app` verify, but by the time the result was acted on, master had moved 126 commits (era core, era takeover, natural wonders) and the medallions branch conflicted again in `GuideContent.tsx`. The pass was worthless. When master is moving fast, verify on the cloud box (`verify-remote`; the Settler harness took 6.4 min there) and land the moment it's green, before starting anything else.

## 2026-10-10: `pkill -f` keeps slipping into sub-agent runs
The Sakoku agent used `pkill -f` to stop its own smoke run, the second sub-agent this week despite the rule in every brief (it matched only its own process). Keep "never `pkill -f`; kill by PID" as the first ground rule in every brief. Consider a PreToolUse hook that blocks `pkill -f` and `killall` outright.

## 2026-10-10: The Sakoku UI review found copy and layout, not breakage
The UI reviewer (no blockers) caught what tests did not: the own race row's long goal note was `nowrap`, so in a narrow row it overflowed leftwards over the empire's name ("You" became "Y"); the reopen produced two contradictory report lines (a generic "lost until its condition holds again" beside the real one); the faction sheet stated the countdown twice with two different numbers because `goalLine`'s "N (M at Standard)" helper still scales for a Quick speed that was removed. Fixed here; left for later: the "holds" and "broken" moment cards still use the generic seal and the captured-city emblem (need Japan art), the rival-facing slip and token card still speak in the owner's "your land" voice, and Japan has no "Show on map" for the intruding unit.

## 2026-10-10: A passing box gate did not run the spec the branch's wording broke
`claude/overreach-v3` passed `verify-remote --app` while `e2e/vp-v2.spec.ts` failed 4 of 10 tests locally: the new decadence wording had changed strings the spec asserted, and the gate's Playwright selection did not include that spec. The UI reviewer found it by running the spec. For a UI wording change, run the specs that cover the changed surfaces by path before trusting the gate, and grep every surface for the old phrasing (the review found "every yield" left in the Gold hover, the sheet and the Guide). Also fixed: the growth tooltip's decadence note was cut off at 1000x600 by the 70vh popover cap.


## 2026-10-11 — Painted medallions passed every gate and drew nothing
`astra/ui-batch-1010c` passed the full cloud `--app` verify (Vitest, Chromium tour), yet the map showed no resource art at all: `resourceImage()` built its key from the capitalised `Resource` value (`Wheat-64`) while the files are lowercase, so the lookup returned null and the renderer skipped silently. The Guide worked because `resourceArtUrl()` lowercased. Only the legatum-ui-reviewer's before/after screenshots caught it. Lessons: route every asset lookup through one normalising helper, test it with real enum values, and keep the UI reviewer on every art-replacing branch; the screen tour had no framing that put resources on screen (now queued).

## 2026-10-11: The verify gate ran only 4 of 64 browser specs
`verify-branch.sh --app` ran only screen-tour, layout, contrast and lenses. Only the release check runs the whole `game` project. So `claude/overreach-v3` passed verify with 4 of 6 `vp-v2.spec.ts` tests failing at each size. On machines with 32+ cores (the cloud box, 21 Playwright workers) verify now runs every game spec; smaller machines keep the core four. `LEGATUM_PW_ALL=1/0` forces either.
- **`combat_scenarios` and `unit_matchups` write their report into `--out-dir`, which defaults to `.`.** A bare run (`--help` is not a flag, it runs everything) drops `*-local.txt` and `unit-matchups-study-*.json` in the repo root, where they are committed by `git add -A` and show up as "unclassified path" in the verify. Always pass `--out-dir`.
- **verify-branch now runs every game Playwright spec on the box.** Ten specs fail on master itself after the historical rename (diplomacy x4, lobby-seats x2, game, territory, war-interstitial, feel) and `staging` needs the debug server binary the box does not build; compare a branch's failures against `tools/cloud/remote.sh master --extra-ref master=master` on the same specs before chasing them. Two failures were this branch's: the contrast audit's first shot read a half-scrolled salvage chip (merge the card readings and the chip readings from the shot where each is in view), and a `hasText: 'Ranged'` locator now also matches the unit panel's Class row.
- **Process slip (A2 agent):** `pkill -f "playwright test"` was run once to stop a duplicate Playwright run. It can kill another agent's run; kill by PID.


## 2026-10-11: A hook now blocks `pkill -f` and `killall`
After three sub-agent slips in three days (Sakoku, A2, and a UI agent earlier), all despite the rule in every brief, a PreToolUse Bash hook (`.claude/hooks/no-pkill.sh`) denies `pkill -f`, `pkill --full` and `killall`, and points to killing by PID. Briefs alone don't stop it.

## 2026-10-11: Closed Japan could have been hired into a war
Sakoku's `closed_refusal` covered only `DeclareWar` and Alliances, so Byzantium's new Hire an army (a paid `CallToArms` to a non-ally) would have let a closed Japan be sent to war, which is the same war it cannot declare. It now refuses a `CallToArms` whose recipient's team is closed, and a closed recipient's acceptance of one already pending, in core and in the mock (`crates/core/tests/hire.rs`). The hire action stays listed (blocked, with the reason) so the row can say why it is missing. Lesson: a new order that makes a team go to war must be checked against every "can't go to war" rule, not only the order that names war. Also: the first all-spec box run on a branch shows master's 11 pre-existing e2e failures (stale mottos after the retheme and others); run `verify-remote.sh master --app` once to tell them from the branch's own.

## 2026-10-11: Hard's open-field stalls are a sight problem, not a planner objective

`tactical-close-to-contact` gave the tactical layer a visible-enemy objective. Of the 896 mutual all-hold episodes in the combat-scenario grid, 672 are open fights at distance four where neither army sees the other at the start, so a fallback that honestly reads only the player view can't act (896 → 832). Among episodes with an enemy in sight, all-holds fell 64 → 0. The next attempt uses the player's memory of last-seen units and known cities (`committed-army-last-seen`), and the harness needs a seeded "a scout saw them" memory to test it.

Separately, the timing ledger shows WASM builds on the cloud box take 52% of all CPU over 24 h: the golden-slot warm (`tools/cloud/box/warm.sh`, 33 runs, up to 5,664 CPU-s each) plus each verify's own `app-wasm` step. `box-wasm-cpu` (Luna) measures whether the warm actually seeds the verify slots.

## 2026-10-11 — Supervisor: the Playwright bridge was down, so a Luna UI spec shipped unrun
- `overreach-v3-ui-leftovers` (Luna) added `vp-v2.spec.ts` assertions it could not run ("Chromium `bootstrap_check_in` denied"); the box verify of `ui-batch-1011a` failed exactly those two tests (the staged scene has no own city with `detail`). The Playwright bridge (`tools/ui/pw-bridge.sh`, learnings 10-0x) exists for this, but its daemon wasn't running, and the task didn't fall back to it. Before queuing a Luna task that writes Playwright specs, check the bridge daemon (`pgrep -fl pw-bridge`) and say in the prompt to run the new spec through `tools/ui/pw-request.sh`; otherwise batch such branches through a box `--app` verify before counting them as done. Don't start the daemon during a release check (host Playwright load).

## 2026-10-11 — Supervisor: box WASM CPU was double-counted, and fake clocks can't pause in the past
- The timing ledger's `box-verify:app-wasm` row wraps `scripts/build-wasm.sh`, which writes its own `box-build:build-wasm` row, and `timed.py` counts child CPU. Paired rows match to the tenth of a second (4,430.5 vs 4,430.6 CPU-s), so "build-wasm 36% + app-wasm 20%" is about 36%, not 56%. Read nested steps as one cost until the report folds them.
- A second Luna UI spec failed unrun: `sakoku.spec.ts` called `page.clock.pauseAt(new Date())` after `load()` had already run the installed fake clock past the host's now ("Cannot fast-forward to the past"). Pause at a future time (`Date.now() + 60_000`), as `lobby-seats.spec.ts` does.

## 2026-10-11: 8.8.1: what the three review findings had in common
- **An opaque PNG loads fine.** The wonder B sprites were RGBA with alpha 255 everywhere and a pure-black matte, so `drawImage` drew black squares and no existing test noticed. `app/src/wonders.art.test.ts` now decodes each sprite's alpha and checks the corners. The cut: flood-fill the black from the border (plus any large enclosed black region), 1 px erosion, 0.8 px feather, premultiplied downscale. Check any new map sprite's alpha before wiring it.
- **Removing a mode leaves text in three shapes:** prose with the old number plus "(… at Standard)" (found by grep), prose under an explicit disclaimer ("figures use Quick speed", so the numbers were silently Quick once the disclaimer went), and engine-worded labels ("Game speed", "First growth (same at every speed)") in breakdown tables, which grep for the phrase in the app misses because the core writes them. The UI relabels the last kind in `plainBreakdownLabel`. The lobby selector is a fourth: it still offers Quick until the engine removal lands.
- **Buttons before prose on every decision card.** The offer card put the quote and a paragraph restating the headline above Accept and Decline. Decision first, then meaning, forecast and flavour.
- **Release-check gotchas (2026-10-11):** `cp -Rc` of a cargo `target/` into a worktree gives EPERM on `.d` writes from a sandboxed shell, and `check.sh` carries on through its other steps after a failed `build-sim`, so a half-broken run looks busy. Symlink the worktree's `target/` and `app/src-tauri/target/` to a scratchpad clone. The cloud box's daily 8 h cap can end a verify mid-run (exit 75, "the box is stopping"); `tools/cloud/box.sh status` shows the minutes used.

## 2026-10-11: The cloud box hit its 8 h/day cap overnight
The overnight landings (about six box verifies plus master e2e runs) used 505 of 480 box minutes, and the 8.8.1 verify was cut off (exit 75), falling back to the local release check. The cap and the stop Lambda work as designed. On landing-heavy nights, expect to need the local fallback after about 8 box hours, or ask the user about a higher daily cap.
- **After the cap, `verify-remote.sh` started the box anyway (fixed cf45750b).** `box_up` started the stopped box. The cap Lambda then stopped it, and ssh failed with exit 255, not 3, so callers never fell back to the local verify. `box_up` now reads the `cap-day`/`cap-used-min` tags and exits 3 when today's cap is reached (`LEGATUM_BOX_CAP_MIN`, default 480).
- **Local `verify-branch.sh` from a sandboxed scheduled run fails every cargo step with EPERM** on `.d` writes under `.claude/worktrees/verify-*/target`, and the summary then looks like a real red gate (sim-build, test, clippy and repin all FAIL in about 6 s). If several cargo steps fail in under 10 s, read a step log before trusting the result. `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` in the scratchpad fixes the build, test and clippy steps, but not smoke or repin: `slot.sh` and `repin.sh` hard-code `target/release-checked/legatum-sim`. Use the box, or a non-sandboxed shell.

## 2026-10-11: Running cloud-box Terraform after the build agent finished
- `infra/cloud-box/tf.sh plan` needs `-var "ssh_public_key=$(cat ~/.legatum-box/id_ed25519.pub)"`. The variable has no default, and the agent's tfvars lived in its deleted worktree.
- The admin profile is SSO and expires overnight. The user must run `aws sso login --profile <admin-profile>` before any plan or apply.
- A saved plan (`plan2.bin`) had been committed. `.gitignore` now covers `plan*.bin`.

## 2026-10-11: Content changes landed without an AI benchmark
A2 (21 new units, a new mounted class) passed every test gate and landed, but the human-seat benchmark wasn't run, and Hard's win rate from the user's seat fell from about 55% to 27.7% (bisected to the A2 merge). The test gates check correctness, not AI quality. Now: the AI-impact gate (legatum-workflow), the daily AI research director (scheduled task `legatum-ai-research`), and the method-level backlog `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ideas.md`.

## 2026-10-11: Most box:remote "flakes" were real failures, and stub runs polluted the timing ledger
Of 43 failed `box:remote` runs in 48 h, 33 were remote test or command failures, 4 missing-object checkouts, and 6 daily-cap or spot stops; none was an ssh timeout or rsync failure (`docs/ops/box-remote-flaky.md`). The flaky list also counted deliberate verifier-stub failures. Now setup steps retry boundedly, a terminated box exits 3 (unavailable) rather than as a test failure, and stub runs skip the timing ledger. Read "flaky" in `timings-report.py` as "check the logs", not "retry".

## 2026-10-11: The box cap lives in two places
The AWS-side Lambda cap comes from Terraform `daily_cap_hours`. The client also refuses early in `tools/cloud/box-lib.sh` (`LEGATUM_BOX_CAP_MIN`, which defaulted to 480). After the cap was raised to 16 h on AWS, agents were still refused at 528/480 until the client default was changed to 960. Change both together. Also, plans against the stopped spot box showed drift (public IP, volume tags) that would have replaced the instance; those attributes are now in `ignore_changes`, and `tf.sh` needs `-var aws_profile=legatum-sims`.

## 2026-10-11: Measure before blaming load on another project
I told the user that the Codex lanes of another project on the same machine added load. Measured, they were idle (empty queue, 0% CPU). That project's only load was one long-running background job at about 14% CPU. Attribute load from per-process CPU (`ps -Ao pcpu,command`, grouped by cwd), not from which processes exist.

## 2026-10-11 — The eight-map combined gate hides large opposite per-map effects
The capital-guard gene was rejected as "no effect" (Hard seat-only 27.73% → 27.73%). Per map, it moved 8.3 Switzerland 1 → 18/64 and 8.4 Sparta 30 → 11/64, which cancel in the equal-weight mean. Seat outcomes also reshuffle heavily between near-identical policies: Hard vs Hard + capguard flips 162/512 game outcomes (win Jaccard 0.27). So 64 salts per map can't resolve per-map effects, and pairing narrows the CI less than it seems.
**How to apply:** before calling a gene "noise", look at its per-map table (`gene-context-capguard` adds per-map CIs and a `HETEROGENEOUS` flag to `tools/sim/hard-vs-human-compare.py`). Run candidates that move a map at 256 salts per map (about $0.30 per 2,048-game arm). A gene that helps one situation and hurts another should become context-gated on PlayerView signals, not be discarded. Also: `aws ce` is denied for the `<launcher-profile>` key, so month-to-date spend can't be read from batch tooling.

## 2026-10-11: Regex-stripped prose leaves punctuation fragments in the UI
The faction piece cards built their rule text by regex-deleting the stat phrases from a prose description (`pieceTextWithoutStats`, app/src/ui/premium.tsx) and then patching punctuation with more regexes. Edge cases showed "152 ⚙,." on the Celtic Ambusher card, and the user saw similar fragments elsewhere. Build UI text from structured data instead, and guard it with a test that renders every player-facing text and rejects fragments (`,.`, ` ,`, `..`, `()`, `undefined`, `NaN`). The fix is in progress on the `text-artifacts` branch.

## 2026-10-11: Rewrites silently drop established interactions
The era rewrite of the research screen (250cbf77) dropped the card-level click (research now) and shift+click (queue) shortcuts from 3ada2ac6. No test pinned them, so every gate passed, and the user found it in a playtest along with a layout jump the 10-06 "clicks never shift content" rule forbids. When rewriting a screen, list its existing interactions first and keep a Playwright regression test for each one.

## 2026-10-11: A lead penalty should wait for a sustained lead, and only hit empires ahead
The user was paying crown upkeep (-3 Gold, 2 crowns) at turn 7, and the live game's one-city Germany at 0 Gold lost four units to crown upkeep from turn 32. Crowns are won in the opening by luck (one more ruin), and VP v2 charged them from the turn after they were won, whoever held them. Fix: a crown costs upkeep only after its current holder has held it for more than 30 turns (`state.title_since`; per crown, per holder, restarts on a change of hands), and only while the holder's Power is above the median (`scoring::ahead_of_median`, the same test decadence uses), so crown upkeep can never desert a unit of a below-median empire. First attempt, "no overreach before turn 50", was replaced by the user: "holding the crown for more than 30 turns triggers the upkeep. That feels more natural." Measured (Hard + Search, 512 games vs overreach v3 alone): T100 leader wins 47.7% to 50.9%, lead changes 11.4 to 9.7, desertions 4.5 to 1.9 a game; human-seat benchmark +0.5 pp (95% -2.1 to +3.0), no regression. Still true: decadence starts at full rate on turn 31 (0 to about 26% for the top empire in one turn), and a crown held since turn 1 is charged from turn 31 when its holder is ahead. Details: `docs/sim-results/overreach-grace/README.md`.
**How to apply:** a rubber band that scales with a lead should be tied to how long the lead has lasted and who is ahead, never to a bare turn number; give every such rule a "who pays" test that excludes empires at or below the median. When a rule has a gene for the AI, keep a way to switch the AI's knowledge off so the benchmark can separate the rule's effect from the AI's response (`crown_free_aware`, `hard_vs_human --rules-experiment crown-upkeep-after=0`).
## 2026-10-11: Codex lanes now run their heavy commands on the cloud build box
The Mac sat at 7-8% battery under a load of about 78. `runner.sh` now gives every write task `LEGATUM_OFFLOAD=1` and a PATH of shims (`tools/astra/lane-bin`): `cargo test|nextest|clippy|check`, `npx vitest|tsc|playwright` and `npm test|run build|e2e` go to the box through `tools/cloud/offload.sh`, `repin.sh` and `build-wasm.sh` offload themselves, and `hard-vs-human.sh` defaults to cloud. Same output stream and exit code; the non-ignored files a command changes (fixtures, `pins.json`, `Cargo.lock`) are applied back to the worktree by a before/after tree diff on the box (`tools/cloud/box/lane-run.sh`). The box being unusable falls back to a local run, once per command and then for 10 minutes. What we learned building it:
- **Codex runs every command through `/bin/zsh -lc`**, and a macOS login shell rebuilds PATH (`path_helper`, then `brew shellenv` from `~/.zprofile`), so a shim directory put first on PATH ends up behind `/opt/homebrew/bin` and the real `npx` wins (`cargo` only survived because it lives in `~/.cargo/bin`, which stays behind). A ZDOTDIR (`tools/astra/lane-zsh`) whose `.zprofile` sources the real one and then re-prepends the shim directory fixes it. Check with `zsh -lc 'type npx'`, not `which` from your own shell.
- **The Codex workspace-write sandbox allows what remote.sh needs:** network, the AWS profile in `~/.aws`, the key in `~/.legatum-box`, ssh control sockets in `/tmp`, and `~/LegatumRuns`. It denies `setpriority`, so `nice -n 15 cmd` inside it prints a warning and does nothing: the runner starts `codex` itself under `nice -n 15` and the children inherit it. A refused `codex sandbox -P :workspace` smoke test said the network was blocked when it was not; test with a real `codex exec`.
- **`box_sync` extracted tarballs over `/data/bin` in place.** `box-job` is a bash script and bash reads a running script incrementally, so a sync from a checkout whose `box-job` differed rewrote it under jobs waiting for a slot: two jobs died with exit 95 and a "checkout ... failed" line the current script does not contain. Sync is now extract-aside, rename-over. The same class of bug as editing a running script (see the workflow skill).
- **Re-running a commit the box already had left a fresh slot unable to check it out**: with nothing new to bundle, the job got no `refs/jobs/<id>/*` ref, and a slot cloned before that commit had no other way to fetch it ("reference is not a tree", exit 95). `box-job` now creates the ref itself. Lanes hit this constantly (many commands against one HEAD).
- **Not covered, by design:** `cargo build`/`cargo run`, binaries run by path, and `cargo fmt` (their outputs are macOS binaries or trivial). At the survey, the load was one Luna task's local `blunder_audit` sim (about 880% CPU), a Docker-VM Linux build and a few local `cargo` builds. The task prompts, not the shims, send sims to AWS spot; the runner's write-task suffix and the supervisor prompts now say so.
- **Wait for slots:** the box has 5 job slots shared with verify-remote, build-linux and every other agent; a lane command can wait minutes for one (155 s seen) and keeps its exit semantics. Each forwarded command also costs about 25 s of overhead (bundle, upload, checkout, artifact sync), so batch small checks.
- **Faction unit rules keyed on `UnitStats::military` catch Scouts (2026-10-11).** Scouts have `military: true`, so Sparta's no-rest-healing rule silently covered them. The exception is `kind != Scout` at every site (core `data::healing`, `units.rs::resting_heal`, mock `restingHeal`, UI `healsSlowly`); grep these together when the rule changes again. The AI needed nothing for Scouts: `plan_military` excludes them and `exploration.rs` already rests damaged Scouts for every faction.
- **Sparta's military rest-heals 2 HP a turn, not 0 (2026-10-11, user).** One number (`data::SPARTA_REST_HEAL`, exported as `RULES.spartaRestHeal`) now replaces the `HealUnit` refusal in core, mock and UI: HealUnit is legal and gives the same 2, so the Heal button and the "Rest here: heal up to 2 HP" forecast work like anyone's. `resting_heal` must test closed foreign land before the Spartan branch, or the 2 leaks into closed territory. Tests that used a Spartan Scout or Soldier as the "never heals" example (ranged_garrison replay hashes, sparta capture-heal parity, purchases forecast, the factions rest test) all needed new expectations; ranged_garrison's replay hashes depend on the whole Spartan healing path. The Sparta AI rests a wounded regiment only at peace (below 60 HP); at war it still keeps campaigning.

## 2026-10-11: The war scenarios show Hard defends like the user but can't attack like him
On the war-scenario suite cut from the user's playtests, Hard meets his goal 95–98% of the time on the two defences but 0–3% on the attacks (Hard+Search does better only on Sparta's First Blood). Three failures: plain-Hard Sparta never declares with 5 Spearmen at T29 and marches home; Hard proposes Peace the turn after it is attacked, even while winning; Search turns down a war Hard half-wins. The full-game benchmark's 27.7% hid that the gap is offence and commitment. The user's 8.8.1 Sparta game says the same: the AIs didn't treat him as a threat once he started winning battles.

## 2026-10-11: The Settle lens hid legal sites because it reused the planner's caution
The user (playtest 8.8.1, T46) saw only a few tiles tinted on the Settle lens while many visibly legal tiles had nothing. The lens listed exactly the sites the AI planner may send a Settler to: `settlement_scores` ran `analyze_sites` through `SiteBasics::facts`, which drops every site flagged by `border_city_risks`. That flag is a deliberate, conservative guess for the planner ("a visible foreign border is evidence of an unseen city within 3 hexes of it"), and it marks any tile that is not in sight right now, even an explored, remembered one, as a possible city centre. So at T46, with a rival's border in view and the land behind it fogged, a wide band of legal tiles (up to six hexes deep) disappeared from the lens. Founding there is legal (`known_legal_site` and `check_found_city` agree); it can only fail if an unseen city really stands nearby. Nothing else dropped legal tiles (unexplored, water, mountains, wonders, foreign land, city spacing and `failed_city_sites` are all illegal under the same rule).
- **Fix (core, `settlement.rs`):** the lens lists every `known_legal_site` tile; a risky one carries `border_risk: true` (dashed rim, and the hover says a foreign city may stand unseen nearby). The planner still skips risky sites (`settlement_candidates_with`, `rank_sites`, the top-three markers). `site_blocker(view, pos)` gives the reason for every other tile from the same rule, and a core test pins "tinted set = legal set = `check_found_city` ok" on a map with every obstacle.
- **The scale was relative, so it hid how good a site is:** the old tiers were "within 20% of the best reachable score" and "at least half", from a score that included travel, "found without moving ×110%", threats and the Settler's route. The same tile read Great on one turn and Poor the next. The band is now the absolute `SettlementSite::quality` (= `base_site_score`: Food ×4 + Production ×3 + Gold ×2 over the 37-tile work radius with the outer ring at half, plus 12 per known Iron/Coal, plus the wonder points, minus 8 per foreign tile). It ignores the Settler, travel and threats, so the same tile is the same band on every map. The AI keeps using the same `base_site_score` (and then the travel-adjusted `score`); there is one site value.
- **Calibration (2026-10-11, box):** 480 generated standard maps (60 seeds for each of the 8 factions as player 1), fully explored, no borders: 309,829 legal sites. Quality percentiles: p5 83, p10 102, p20 127, p30 146, p40 161, p50 175, p60 188, p70 201, p80 217, p90 241, p95 261, p98 285, p99 303, max 387. The factions differ by at most 2 points at any percentile, so one scale serves all. Floors 120 / 160 / 200 / 250 give **Poor 18%, Fair 21%, Good 30%, Great 23%, Exceptional 8%**. Re-run with `cargo test -p legatum-core calibrate_site_bands -- --ignored --nocapture` (on the box) before changing the weights in `base_site_score`, and change the floors only together (`SITE_BAND_FLOORS`, then `UPDATE_UI_RULES=1 cargo test -p legatum-ai --test ui_rules`). An in-game site scores below its map's fully explored value when part of its radius is unexplored or foreign, which the hover says in plain numbers.
- **Colour (superseded 2026-10-17, see "An agent overrode a stated user preference" below):** the blue-violet sweep and one-to-five pips chosen here replaced the user's requested green and were dropped. What stands: the colours are checked by CIEDE2000 under normal, protan, deutan and tritan simulation over the six land terrains (`siteBands.test.ts`), and the old green-yellow-red set collapsed under deutan.
- **Rule:** a lens that says "every X" must be driven by the rule that defines X, not by a planner's filter over X. If a planner needs extra caution, mark the extra cases, don't remove them.
## 2026-10-11: Box slot waits are not first-come-first-served
`tools/cloud/box/box-job` `pick_slot()` polls the 5 slot locks with `flock -n` every 5 s, so a newcomer that arrives just as a slot frees can beat a job that has waited longer. Seen: the `swiss-market-priority-on --duel` verify waited 34+ min while the later `box-wasm-cpu --app` verify got a slot in minutes and passed. With lanes forwarding every heavy command to the box, a verify (critical path to master) can starve behind lane jobs. Queued `box-slot-fifo` (Luna): ticket queue, head-of-line polls, dead tickets skipped. The user's pending decision on 5→8 slots would also ease it.

## 2026-10-11: Crown upkeep bankrupted a dying empire
In the user's 8.8.1 game, Germany (one city, 0 Gold, being invaded) held a crown from T1 and lost 4 units to crown-upkeep bankruptcy (T32–T42). Overreach is meant to rein in leaders. Crown upkeep had no "above the median" test, unlike decadence. Now crown upkeep applies only to holders above the median after 30 turns held (`overreach-grace`).

## 2026-10-11: Painted units got lost on the richer organic terrain
Adopting organic S2 as the default made the painted unit miniatures hard to see (user: "pretty hard to see, which is not ideal"): brown figures on dark woods and brown peaks, and a flat blue base disc that did not separate from busy ground. Four looks were shot on forest, range, hills, desert and grass at 1000x600 and 1440x900: bigger figure plus a vellum pool (A), plus a firm owner ring (B), a vellum coin base (C) and the old one. B won; the coin (C) let the owner colour wash out of the base, and the vellum pool is what lifts a figure off a dark wood or a brown peak. A dark halo or outline would also break the atlas look and does nothing on a dark wood.
- **Measure legibility, do not eyeball it:** `e2e/terrain-units.spec.ts` reads the map canvas pixels. A mean-luminance contrast of the figure body against the surrounding art barely moved (1.34 to 1.58 in a wood) because the painted figure itself is dark; the pool beside the figure is what the fix changes (1.0 before, 2.5 after in the lone-soldier test; 1.01 vs 2.68 on the shots), so that is what the test asserts (at least 1.8:1), plus the base at least 1.3:1. The pool contrast is lower on hills (1.14 to 1.99) and range (1.59 to 1.83), where the terrain is already light, which is why only the wood is asserted.
- **A bigger miniature moves everything hung on it:** the health bar and status tag sit lower, a two-unit stack fans wider (1.16 token widths) and `counterFootprint` (the clear patch improvement art leaves under counters) grew with it.
- **The grid line sinks into dark art.** A 0.14 alpha ink hairline is invisible over a wood; the fix is a pale casing under the ink core on edges that touch a wood, hills or range (open ground keeps the faint line), as roads do.
- **Fading terrain art under a lens is a cache concern:** `TerrainCache.setArtFade` marks chunks stale instead of rebuilding the cache, so the old chunks keep showing until each repaints in place (no flash). A fade that needed a new cache key would have rebuilt every chunk on a lens switch.
- **Dead variants cost shipped bytes:** the dev terrain directions (A, B, S1, S3) were 4.7 MB of PNGs in `app/public`, loaded by nobody once S2 was chosen. Retire a rejected art direction in the same change that adopts the winner.
- **A gated shots job can wait 40 minutes for a box slot** (see the 2026-10-11 slot entry), so batch the look variants into one run.

- **Pre-merge master before queueing a box verify of a results branch.** The starved swiss verify then failed at its first step, `merge`, on journal.md and ledger.jsonl conflicts (append-only files every lane writes). Run `git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only master <branch>` first; if the only conflicts are the journal or ledgers, union-merge them on the branch, then verify. Otherwise a 66-min slot wait buys a 10-s failure.

## 2026-10-11: The AIs saw the user coming; they lacked the means to stop him (playtest 8.8.1)
Replaying his Sparta game showed that every AI made him its top threat the turn after his first capture (T28, or T33 for Byzantium) and switched to a Contain war. Three problems undid them:
- **Bankrupt Germany.** Crown upkeep put Germany's 1-city, 0-Gold treasury into debt, and it lost 4 units mid-invasion.
- **A coalition nobody could afford.** Each Alliance offer was refused over the 60 Gold signing fee. The fee is waived only against a VP-based coalition target, and he had only 4 VP.
- **Peace that looked free.** War plans are charged for the city an enemy army will take, but "Peace with X" isn't, even when X declared the war and refuses peace. So Japan dropped Contain for Peace on the turn his army arrived, and lost Kamakura the next turn.

Separately, plain Hard Sparta never had a war plan at all, because `sparta_search_only` = 1 switched off every Sparta war gene outside Search. Turning it off took Sparta's First Blood from 0/64 to 64/64 and the human-seat benchmark seat-only result up 2.93 pp [+0.20, +5.66].
## 2026-10-11: Landing the Quick-speed removal onto the 8.8.1 text fixes
- **Both sides had "fixed" the same numbers in opposite directions.** 8.8.1 kept base figures and scaled them at the surface (`scaleCost`, `atStandard`); the branch made goal thresholds Standard in the generated rules (Sakoku 30, hoard 1,000) and left some surfaces scaling. Git merged all of it without a conflict and Sakoku read 60 turns in three places. After any speed or pace change, grep every `scaleCost(` and `factionGoals` use for a base-versus-Standard mismatch and check the screenshot, because the unit tests passed.
- **A price on an icon chip and the same price in prose must come from one scaled value.** `pieceStats` printed the unscaled cost while the prose carried the scaled one, and the strip-the-prose step then left a stray "160" beside a chip saying 80 (a bug since the A2 art pass, only visible next to a correct figure).
- **`CARGO_TARGET_DIR` breaks the Playwright fixtures:** `app/e2e/fixtures.ts` looks for `target/release/legatum-server` under the repo, so a verify run with the variable set fails every test in 0 ms with "Build first". Use it only for cargo-only steps, or fix the fixture to honour the variable.
- **On a box running ten jobs, a handful of Playwright tests and vitest timing tests fail for load, not for the change**: 10 s waits for the title ("Single Player", "Continue"), `ERR_NETWORK_CHANGED` on audio files, a CDP "Could not find node" in the contrast spec, 5 s vitest timeouts. Rerun just those specs (spec paths, one build) before debugging; each passed on rerun here.
- **AI fixture tests carry hidden pace assumptions:** the production fixture builds option costs at its own build turn (20, Standard ramp), so a test that sets `v.turn` later does not change costs, and a per-turn rate chosen for Quick costs changed which unit won the strength-per-turn score (Pikeman against Soldier). Compare scores, not the comment's "about a dozen turns".

## 2026-10-11: Cloud batch logs leak the AWS account ID
`tools/cloud/run-batch.sh` runs each spot server with `set -x`, so its `batch.log` echoes the results bucket `legatum-sims-<account id>`. Thirteen of these logs were committed under `docs/sim-results/hard-vs-human/runs/`, putting the account ID in the tree ahead of open-sourcing. They are now untracked and `docs/sim-results/**/*batch.log` is gitignored; keep the logs locally, and commit only reports and summaries. Earlier commits still hold them, which is one reason the public repo should use a fresh history.
## 2026-10-11: The WASM cache key is broader than the WASM build
`scripts/build-wasm.sh` keys its exact-input cache on every tracked file under `crates/`, but `crates/wasm` only compiles `legatum-core` and `legatum-ai`. A branch that touches only `crates/sim` (a benchmark example or harness) still misses the cache, and each miss on the box costs 3,000–7,300 CPU-seconds. Between 10:51 and 11:24, 7 of 12 box builds missed; build-wasm is 45% of tracked CPU over 24 h. `wasm-cache-key-narrow` (Luna) is queued to key on the WASM crate's dependency closure only. Also: `.gitignore` does not untrack files that were committed before the rule, so three `.progress/*RESUME.md` files stayed tracked until `git rm --cached` removed them.

## 2026-10-11: Era objectives are the HUD, and the race panel is zero-sum with the dock
The user played 8.8.1 and found the VP objectives only on the era-up card ("it was entirely unclear to me that there were VP goals in the first era"). The first era has none, so turn 1 showed nothing and later eras' three objectives lived on a takeover and a sheet. Now `eraObjectives.ts` (one model: `eraHud`, `howYouWin`, `closeObjectives`) feeds one `ObjectiveRows` list that the race panel, the takeover, the research header and the report all draw, so a rule is worded and counted once.
- **The right column cannot grow.** The race panel and the This Turn dock share the column height (the minimap takes the rest), so a block added to one is taken from the other's rows. The first draft (family words, a bar under each line, a sub line, a note) was 250 px and left the race panel with no rows at all, even at 1440x900. What worked: the family as an icon, one line per objective with the progress as the fill behind it, the era name in the title, and a two-line note only in a preview. That is about 135 px open and 170 px in a preview. At 1000x600 the "Nobody yet" row then steps aside (and the crowns strip too in a preview or after scoring); both are on the sheet. Measure the race rows (yours and the leader's whole) whenever this block changes.
- **The dock's own objective row was removed**, not kept beside the block: the same rule twice costs 34 px of dock, and the race panel already shows every objective with its progress.
- **A preview needs the real first-era data.** The core sends progress for the next era before you enter it (`objective_progress` covers entered eras plus one), so Ancient shows Classical with what you hold now. Fight objectives count from the day you enter the era, so their preview reads 0 with a tooltip saying so.
- **Fixtures that stage an era leave the research data behind** (`withV2({ era: 3 })` changes the players' era, not `view.research`): a staged screenshot can pair "Medieval scored" with "Ancient technologies" text. Read the real game's first-era shot for the truth.
- **Box queueing dominated the iteration time (up to 70 minutes waiting for a slot).** Make every box job carry everything: tsc, the affected vitest files, every spec you need, soft assertions that still write the screenshot and a geometry JSON, and the failure screenshots as artifacts. A spec that asserts before it shoots loses the picture exactly when you need it.

## 2026-10-11: e2e server path follows CARGO_TARGET_DIR
`app/e2e/fixtures.ts` and `staging.spec.ts` hardcoded `target/release/legatum-server`, so any verify that built with a scratch `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (the EPERM workaround) failed every Playwright test in 0 ms with "Build first". Both now use `serverBinary()`: `LEGATUM_SERVER_BIN`, else `<CARGO_TARGET_DIR or target>/release/legatum-server`.

## 2026-10-11: "Distinct" needs a measurement, and the first measurement was wrong twice
The user, looking at an Iron and a Coal medallion side by side on the 8.8.1 map: "Coal and iron look way too similar... All the icons, miniatures, etc need to be visually distinct." Both were dark grey rock on the same olive field in the same brass ring. `tools/art/distinctness.py` (with `render-sets.mjs`, guarded by `app/src/artDistinct.test.ts`; thresholds and the calibration in `tools/art/distinctness.json`) now draws every set at the size it is shown and scores each pair.
- **Mean Delta E over the content, in the worst of five ways of seeing** (normal, protan, deutan, tritan, greyscale) is the right score for painted art. SSIM and silhouette overlap are useless for paintings: fine texture drives SSIM to 0.2 for everything, and every sprite on a similar blob scores 0.8 on IoU. A colour histogram was useless too (every painting has the same tonal spread, so the greyscale histograms all overlapped at 0.95+). SSIM and IoU stay for single-colour icons, where shape is all there is.
- **Compare the content, not the frame.** A medallion's brass ring and a roundel's plate and owner disc are shared by every member and dilute the score; the resources set compares only the inside (12% cropped), the roundels only the ink silhouette.
- **No metric sees what a person sees as "the same kind of thing".** Wheat and Cattle score as alike as the old Coal and Iron at 14 px (both warm tan), yet read apart by subject; they are an accepted pair with a reason. The Hoplite and the Phalangite (the user could not tell them apart in play) score 0.38 on Delta E and only 0.67 on a silhouette gist, with about 80 of 528 figure pairs at or above that: upright figures with a vertical spear share a silhouette, so that pair needs a non-silhouette cue (era badge, pose, strength mark), not a stricter threshold. The check is a floor against near-copies, not a taste test; the UI reviewer and the user's eyes remain.
- **What the check found beyond Coal and Iron:** the far-zoom resource marks were identical for Iron and Coal (both the red dot; fixed: red square against a near-black disc ringed in red); the far-zoom unit roundels were 22 variants of one body with a different stick, so an Archer and a Spearman scored 0.95 at 16 px (fixed: the roundels are class symbols, a shield, a bow, a horse, a tank, a plane, a drone, a civilian figure; roundels of one class match on purpose, listed in `groups`); the old Settle lens tiers Great and Poor were 2 Delta E apart under protanopia (`settle-lens-absolute` landed a colour-blind-safe ramp first and `siteBands.test.ts` guards it; `map/lensPalette.test.ts` covers the other lens palettes, which pass); Cataphract and Horseman, Cuirassier and Legionary, Autonomous Armour and Tank and Composite Archer and Ranger icons were near-copies at 16 px; four of the five new era miniatures repeated their predecessor's pose and colours (Cuirassier/Horseman scored 0.75; left to the unit-distinct work); the 28 city miniatures read alike across eras at 48 px (report-only, needs a repaint pass).
- **A painted set sharing one base (the unlock dioramas' ground oval) starts near 0.5 for every pair**, so its bar is higher (0.66) than for loose figures; check a new set's median before trusting the default.
- **Repaint prompts that work:** brief the pair as a contrast in silhouette, hue and value at once (iron: low wide ingots, rust red, mid-light field; coal: tall round heap, blue-black, very dark field) and ask for a self-check at 32 px. The Luna result passed first time: Coal and Iron now score 0.30 to 0.33 (a 27 Delta E gap even in greyscale), down from 0.67 to 0.70.
## 2026-10-11: Era-distinct unit mocks
- **The lookalike score missed the pair the user actually confused.** `tools/art/distinctness.py` (units-miniature, 45 px, mean Delta E) gave Hoplite against Phalangite 0.38 and Spearman against Phalangite 0.41, well under its 0.60 flag. Yet in play the user could not tell them apart. At about 30 px of figure every upright foot soldier with a vertical spear shares one silhouette. `distinct-art` added a silhouette ("gist") term, but no threshold flags that pair without flagging most foot units. Judge in-play crops as well; a score alone does not clear a set.
- **Miniatures never get bigger than about 45 px.** `tokenHalf` caps at 18, so zooming in leaves the figure the same size. Telling units apart has to come from silhouette, pose or a badge, never painted detail.
- **The route preview already uses red-ringed Roman numerals for turns.** The first era badge (red numeral on a parchment pill) looked like a route turn marker, so it became a square tab filled with the era's metal.
- **Playwright wipes `test-results/` on every run, including other specs' output dirs under it.** Two runs in one box job (original art, then swapped art) lost the first set. Write shots outside it (`UNIT_ERA_SHOTS=shots/unit-era`) and pass that path to `--artifact`.

## 2026-10-11: Supervisor landings
- **A passed verify goes stale when master takes a cross-cutting change.** `blunder-audit` passed on the box at 11:54, then the Quick-speed removal landed and its test (`GameSpeed::Quick`) no longer compiled. The text merge was clean, so `git merge-tree` showed nothing. Before landing a branch verified earlier, re-merge and re-verify whenever master has changed `crates/` since the verify's merge base.
- **zsh reads `$T:c` as a history modifier.** `git show $T:crates/...` in zsh dropped the `:c` and failed with "ambiguous argument". Write `"${T}:path"` in Bash-tool commands.
- **Inside the sandbox, `kill -0` on a process from another tool call fails.** A background `while kill -0 PID` wait exited at once and reported nothing. Wait on a log line (Monitor with `tail -F | grep`, or `until grep -q`), not on a PID.

## 2026-10-11: Why the AIs couldn't stop a Sparta conqueror (the user's 8.8.1 game, replayed exactly)
All three AIs made the user their top threat within one turn of his first capture, so noticing wasn't the problem. They failed because:
1. Crown upkeep bankrupted one-city Germany, which lost 4 units (fixed: above-median holders only).
2. Every AI-to-AI alliance offer failed on the 60 Gold fee, so no coalition formed.
3. "Peace with X" is valued at 0 while war plans carry a retaliation cost, so a capable AI sues for peace the turn the enemy army arrives (Japan lost Kamakura that way).
4. Threat defence stops at one archer plus Walls.
Plain-Hard AI Sparta never attacks because `sparta_search_only` defaulted to 1, which forced every Sparta war gene off outside Search. Flipping it gives First Blood 0% → 100% and the seat benchmark +2.9 pp. The replay tool `crates/sim/examples/p881_trace.rs` reproduces a user save turn by turn with every AI plan candidate.

## 2026-10-11: The FIFO box queue starved every job
A Codex change made box slots FIFO (a4ef14cc5). It wrote each waiter's PID with `printf "$(_slot_queue_pid)"`. The command substitution runs in a subshell, so BASHPID was a PID that had already exited. `_slot_queue_skip_dead` then treated every waiter as dead and advanced `serving` past all of them, and each waiter compared `serving == ticket` forever ("waiting for a slot: 0th in queue"). The local test passed because macOS bash 3.2 has no BASHPID and fell back to the live `$$`. Fix: write `${BASHPID:-$$}` directly, and treat `serving >= ticket` as head. Stuck waiters keep the old code in memory, so after deploying they must be killed (`remote.sh --kill ID`, with stdin redirected from /dev/null inside a loop) and re-run.

## 2026-10-11: Too many agents on one box means hour-long queues
With about a dozen agents and three Codex lanes verifying at once, box jobs waited 20–70 minutes for one of its 5 slots. Several branches (era objectives among them) couldn't finish their last gate before the wrap-up. Full-suite Playwright on every verify multiplies this. Next time, cap the number of concurrent agents that verify (or batch landings through one landing agent), or raise the slot count, before adding agents.
## 2026-10-11: The war scenarios show Hard defends like the user but can't attack like him
On the war-scenario suite cut from the user's playtests, Hard meets his goal 95–98% of the time on the two defences but 0–3% on the attacks (Hard+Search does better only on Sparta's First Blood). Three failures: plain-Hard Sparta never declares with 5 Spearmen at T29 and marches home; Hard proposes Peace the turn after it is attacked, even while winning; Search turns down a war Hard half-wins. The full-game benchmark's 27.7% hid that the gap is offence and commitment. The user's 8.8.1 Sparta game says the same: the AIs didn't treat him as a threat once he started winning battles.
## 2026-10-11: Research screen: whole-card click, fixed-height queue strip (branch claude/research-click)
The cause of the "everything jumps down" complaint was the queue strip, not the cards: it was a header row when empty and grew a list of chips (two rows at 1000x600) on the first pick, pushing the whole card grid down while the pointer was over it. Fix: the strip always renders one slot per queueable technology (filled chip or dashed placeholder, in one CSS grid so the row count cannot change), the head row has a reserved height, the state tag is `position: absolute` over a reserved top edge, and animation uses only box-shadow and a fixed-position chip (a `transform: scale` on the card would itself change its bounding box). Gotchas: `Tip` triggers stop click propagation (a click on the cost pins its breakdown), so whole-card handlers must ignore clicks inside the `Tip`, buttons and links and only act on the card's own surface; `animation.cancel()` rejects `finished` (happy-dom does it eagerly), so attach a catch; a button that is replaced by a status when pressed drops focus to `<body>`, so move focus to the card. Pinned by `app/e2e/research-click.spec.ts` (bounding boxes of all cards and the strip before and after each pick) and the states in `app/src/ui/TechPanel.test.tsx`.

## 2026-10-11: Crown grace weakens the rubber band; the cavalry hunch didn't hold
- The crown grace (upkeep only after 30 turns held, above-median holders only) causes no AI regression (+0.5 pp seat-only over 2,048 games). Units lost to upkeep fall 4.5 → 1.9 per game and the leader's upkeep falls from 36% to 17% of city Gold. But lead changes drop 11.4 → 9.7 and one-team crown sweeps rise 3% → 8%. Watch whether late games feel decided too early; the levers are a shorter grace or more decadence.
- Forcing a mounted share into the AI seat's army cost 5–10 pp in every mix (horseman garrisons die to Spearmen), and the combat simulator's best-response mix lost 5.5 pp in full games. Arena results don't carry over. Hard makes only about two unit-on-unit melee strikes in the field per game, so getting armies to fight in the open comes before mounted tactics.

## 2026-10-11: Era-distinct units built (plaque, strength chips, lineage repaint)
- **Brief repaints across classes, not just within one.** I briefed the Infantry and the Musketman in separate tasks, each "advancing with a bayonet forward". Both passed their own neighbour checks but looked alike to each other (gist 0.78), and the Infantry had to be repainted as a grenade thrower. List same-era and adjacent-era units of every class as neighbours in each brief.
- **A new line in the attack forecast can overflow the heaviest card.** The era headline pushed the severe-aftermath forecast past 1000x600 (`aftermath-forecast.spec` caught it). Fold new facts into existing rows: here each side gets an era tab. Add a whole line only for the warning case, an outclassing unit.
- **"Outclasses" must test base strength as well as the era.** A newer unit that is ahead only because of a promotion or your wounds is not outclassing you (`outclasses` in `map/unitEra.ts`).
- **Canvas marks have no hover, so give them an in-game key.** The unit panel shows a one-line key while the chips are live, and the Guide explains them. In the 1440 bottom dock, an extra element in the unit panel's body becomes its own grid column and breaks the layout, so put new rows inside an existing column (`.up-stats`).
- **A full vitest run on a loaded box can fail `store.test.ts` and `offerPacing.test.ts` on timing.** Both passed when run alone on the same tree. Rerun them alone before treating them as real.
- **`agent-worktree.sh acquire <branch>` fails when that branch is still checked out in another pool slot.** It hard-resets a free slot first, so the failure costs nothing on a clean slot. Claim the slot that already has the branch by writing its `.busy` lock.
## 2026-10-11 landing pass: six branches, what bit us

- **A flex-wrap strip that can wrap makes the race panel's height depend on timing.** The crowns strip ("New: free 30 turns, then +4 Gold/turn") wrapped to a second line during the first layout, `reserveRace` measured that taller panel, and the turn dock lost 16 px at 1000x600 (the siege card was sliced). Strips in the race panel are `nowrap` with an ellipsis; the tooltip carries the full text. Debug by logging `--race-min`, `--dock-height` and `.vp` children heights on both trees (a patched `expectDockWhole`).
- **The era-objectives block shrinks until every race row is whole when the window allows it** (level 4 is the heading line alone); at 1000x600 it still stops at "your row and one more". The race panel reserves the dock at least 130 px so an opened Cities list keeps its heading and first row at 1440x900.
- **Crown grace changes tests that relied on crown upkeep to force disbands.** The property fuzz case `boundary_production_and_upkeep` now turns the free turns off with the `crown-upkeep-after=0` rules experiment.
- **The contrast audit is screenshot based: a control half under a pinned footer reads the footer's pixels.** It now skips any control whose top or bottom third is covered, not only its centre. A "#f2ead8 on #f2ead8" failure means that.
- **Stale box jobs from dead agents can jam the slot queue** (every waiter "0th in queue" with free slots). Stopping your own job with `tools/cloud/remote.sh --kill JOB` and resubmitting got it moving; do not kill other agents' jobs.
- **Gates run concurrently, then land in order:** each later branch re-merged master after an earlier landing (conflicts: VpTrack and vpModel.test for the HUD, premium.tsx and ItemArt.tsx for unlock art, data.ts for text artifacts). Take the later-landed side's structure and re-apply the branch's intent.

## 2026-10-12: Compact "How you win" and the research fold (claude/ui-892-fixes)
- **The compact block costs 70 px; the race panel has no spare room.** At 1000x600 the panel is about 288 px tall and the dock below cannot give any, so 6 whole rows (8.9) need the crowns strip and the "Nobody yet" row out of the panel (they are on the objectives sheet) once the block is there. `fit()` hides them only when the rows cannot all show. Specs about crowns and tokens set `legatum.dev.no-era-hud` (the 8-empire cases cannot show both).
- **A `fit()` ladder that accepts "two rows" as good enough keeps the decoration and starves the list.** Take the try with the most whole rows. The `clarity.spec` guard "all eight rows show at 1280x800" caught the version that took the first try with two.
- **The research screen's real problem was the head and the strip, not the strip being sticky** (it never was: it sits outside the scrolling body). A three-line Science note beside the era badge, a 115 px queue strip and a three-line objectives header left about 275 px of body for a 215 px card with its buttons. Fixes at max-height 700 px: the note spans the head, a one-row slot (24 px controls), a one-line first-era header.
- **`era-research.spec.ts` audits targets at 24 px:** a 20x18 queue control failed it. Use the audit's minimum when squeezing a strip.
- **Staged "mid-era" shots must keep the state the screen reads:** with Science flowing the "No Science yet" note is gone and the head is 28 px shorter; the fold spec stages `science_per_turn` for that reason.

## 2026-10-17: Swiss interest has no cap (claude/swiss-interest)
User: "is there a reason we have a cap at 5? I feel like we shouldn't have a cap." Interest is now floor(treasury / 100) Gold a turn, shown as "Switzerland: interest" in the Gold breakdown; the sheet, picker and Guide read "Each city earns +1 Gold for every 2 population. Every 100 Gold in your treasury earns +1 Gold a turn in interest." (both numbers from `RULES`). The `interest-cap=N` rules experiment restores a maximum, so before/after runs use one binary.
- **No runaway in the sims.** Hard + Search, Standard, 256 games per cohort, paired seeds and seats, cap 5 against uncapped. Switzerland's win share: 4 empires 26.4% to 23.3% (34 to 30 of 129 seats), 8 empires 12.5% to 14.5% (32 to 37 of 256); pooled 17.1% to 17.4%. Paired seat deltas, 4p -3.1 pp (95% -9.3 to +3.1), 8p +2.0 pp (-0.8 to +4.7): noise. Human-seat benchmark (8 maps, all seats, paired salts): Hard 256 salts per map 32.0% to 31.5% (-0.4 pp, -1.9 to +1.0), Hard + Search 64 salts 29.1% to 30.3% (+1.2 pp, -1.8 to +4.1). Seat-only and opponent-only arms don't exist for a rule change (it applies to every seat): the AI has no gene for interest, so only the all-seats arm was run.
- **The AI never hoards enough for the cap to matter.** Treasury at T150 (uncapped): median 293 Gold at 4 empires, 151 at 8 (cities median 6 and 4); 13% and 8% of Swiss seats hold 1,000 or more; maximum 1,199 anywhere, at any of T50 to T200. Interest at T150 (4p, 8p): median 2 and 1, mean 3.7 and 2.7, maximum 10 and 11 (the old cap was 5). Interest is 5.6% (4p) and 4.1% (8p) of a Swiss seat's gross Gold income at T150, mean 7.2% and 7.3%; at T100 4.5% and 4.2% (mean 7.4%). It reaches 17-20% only at the 90th percentile of hoarders. The AI spends past the Reserve (upkeep, purchases), so a human who sits on 3,000 Gold is the case these runs do not measure: +30 Gold a turn at that point, against a mid-game net income of about 16 to 24.
- **Interest is not Power, and (since the same day's decadence change) decadence does cut it.** `swiss_interest` is its own breakdown line, added after the cities' yields, and Power sums the cities' yields, so a big hoard does not raise Switzerland's lead over the median. This entry first said decadence never touched it; the user then ruled that everything that feels like income is taxed, so decadence now takes its rate from the interest too (see "Decadence is proportional to the lead" below). The tolls (+1 Gold per 2 population) are a city yield and are cut. The Gold surcharge is off by default (`surcharge_max` 0).
- **The Gold forecast prices interest on the treasury before this turn's purchases; resolution pays it on what is left.** With the cap at 5 the two agreed above 500 Gold; uncapped they differ by spend / 100 (the bicycle parity test expected 9,716 and got 9,712). The test now models it; the forecast is unchanged (it is a per-turn figure, off by a few Gold only in a turn with a big purchase).
- **Three fixed-order tape fixtures rehashed** (`city-strike-awareness`, `diplomacy-alliance`, `production-queue-buy`: each has a Swiss seat), none of the orders changed, pins.json did not move. Sim telemetry now records each Swiss seat's treasury, interest, city Gold and net Gold at T50, 100, 150 and 200 (`GameTelemetry.swiss_treasury`).
## 2026-10-17: Agents translate code into rule text
Player-facing text like "+1 Gold per complete pair of population" and "every 100 Gold held adds" came from agents paraphrasing the code (`floor(pop/2)`, treasury `/100`). No guide or check enforced plain language: the UI reviewer judged layout, and the text test only caught punctuation fragments. Fix (built): the "Game text: plain language" guide and glossary in legatum-ui, the copy lint (`app/src/test/copyRules.ts`, run by `ui/textArtifacts.test.tsx` over the rendered corpus in `test/playerText.tsx` and by `ui/copySource.test.ts` over app and core string literals), copy review in the UI reviewer, and a brief line in legatum-workflow. The audit is `docs/design/copy-audit-2026-10-17.md`.

## 2026-10-17: An agent overrode a stated user preference for a guideline
The Settle lens agent replaced the user's requested green with a blue-violet palette for colour-blind safety, and added unexplained band dots and formula-speak hover text. The user couldn't read the lens. A single-hue lightness ramp in the user's colour satisfies both. Agents must satisfy stated preferences and guidelines together, or ask, never silently pick one.

## 2026-10-17: Copy audit findings
- **A rendered corpus beats grepping source for copy.** Linting the strings the game actually prints (including a 60-turn mock game's report) found "moved 2 hexes to 2 tiles NE of Lugano", which no source grep for one phrase would flag. Source scans need literal extraction: scanning whole lines flagged Rust's `Vec::retain` and a `multiplier` variable.
- **Wrong facts hide in long text.** The Guide said Sakoku takes 60 turns: the 30-turn target was passed through `cost()`, which doubles prices. Only goal numbers that are prices may be scaled.
- **Prose parsed by regex blocks rewording.** `unlockSentence` stripped "Upgrade from X; Population N required;" out of building blurbs, so the blurbs couldn't change. Upgrade text is now composed from `predecessor` and `minPopulation`, which also fixed Byzantium's University saying "Upgrade from Library" (its Library is the Imperial Scriptorium).
- **Breakdown labels are copy too, and they must match across core, mock and every TS lookup** (`research.ts` and `eraResearch.ts` find rows by label). "Base cost"/"Standard costs" became "Listed cost"/"Game pace" in all of them at once.
- **"Not enough gold:" was a protocol prefix** (UI code branches on `startsWith`); capitalising it meant core, mock and four matchers in one change.

## 2026-10-17: Blue rectangles and a green block of sea were stand-in terrain chunks (playtest 8.9.2)
The user saw flat, hard-edged, light-blue rectangles over his cities (T114) and a 4-hex block of sea with a greenish land wash and blurred terrain south of Bern (T136). Cause, in `TerrainCache.draw` (`app/src/map/terrain.ts`): a chunk of the current level that is not drawn yet shows whatever lower layer sits under it, and three things made that ugly and long-lived.
- **The overview was the stand-in at any distance.** Level 0 is rendered at 0.3 device px per world px, and its borders, grid and coast lines are floored at CSS pixels (`px = 1 / zoom` of that level), so stretched 3 to 11x over a normal view they became fat blue bands and blurred land: chunk-shaped patches beside sharp chunks. On a Retina Mac (dpr 2) the overview is never the real view, only a stand-in.
- **The previous level was blitted even when stale and counted as "used".** A zoom-out kept all 79 of the old level's chunks "fresh" every frame, so the LRU (cap 110) evicted the new level's own ring chunks every frame (`4:1:5` and `4:2:5` were re-rendered 54 times in 9 s; 300 renders in 9 s at idle). The eviction loop also re-read `chunks.size` each pass and so dropped half of what it should. A big window pins more than 110 chunks, so visible chunks went missing and their holes showed the stand-ins for as long as the user kept the view.
- **Fill-in was one chunk per frame** (7 ms budget), so after a zoom or a Settle lens switch the holes lasted seconds (8 s for 60 chunks in headless Chromium at 20 fps).
Fix, round 1: `chunkCache.ts` holds the rules (tested without a canvas): the view's chunks and ring are pinned and the limit grows with the view, stand-ins never count as use and a stale previous-level chunk is never drawn, and while a visible chunk has never been drawn the frame may spend 20 ms on chunks. That alone made a big zoom-out worse (the user's lead saw it on `t117-1000x600-before-after.png`): with no stand-in more than two levels away, most of the view was a blank parchment hole for about 2 s, which reads as "the game broke".
Fix, round 2: a stand-in layer (`STANDIN` in `terrain.ts`) holds the whole map (6 chunks) at the overview's scale, is always cached and refreshed every turn, is never evicted, and is drawn under every level, so a view is never blank. It is drawn *as if seen at a zoom of 0.62* (`chunkZoom`), not at the 0.15 the old overview was drawn for, so its borders are about 1.5x the right width when stretched instead of 5-10x. A previous-level chunk is still used as a better stand-in when it is finer, or at most two levels coarser. `legatumMap.terrain` reports chunks, renders and visible chunks missing; `e2e/map-chunk-cache.spec.ts` asserts, on both saves, that the frame two animation frames after a zoom out (three levels) and a zoom in has no blank parchment where the settled frame has terrain (it failed with 130 blank cells on round 1), that every visible chunk fills, that the cache goes quiet, and that the map equals a fresh one after zooms and a turn (copies of the user's saves in `crates/wasm/tests/fixtures/saves/p892-*.legatum`).
Round 3 (8.9.3, the user: "weird zoom artifacts again"; `~/LegatumRuns/bug-saves/p893-zoom-artifact*`): while zooming, chunk-shaped patches of the soft stand-in layer (blurred coast, a land-colour blob in the sea) showed beside sharp chunks. The patches were stand-in, drawn *under* the level as intended, showing where the level had no chunk yet. A wheel or pinch zoom crosses a level (1.4x) every few ticks, each crossing needs a whole new set of chunks drawn one or two a frame, and the previous level was the only stand-in, itself half filled after a quick zoom. I could not run the Mac's WKWebView, so whether its chunks only fill slowly or some never fill is unknown; both are now handled. Fixes in `terrain.ts`: (1) the level in use is **held through a zoom gesture** (while its chunks scale by under 2x, until the zoom has rested 250 ms), so a zoom across a boundary needs no new chunks; past that it advances one level at a time, and only once the held level is complete; (2) **every cached level close in scale is a stand-in**, nearest on top, up to 3 levels coarser (2.8x keeps the coast and tiles in place; the soft layer stretched 5-14x does not) and any finer level, drawn only while a visible chunk is missing, and only the stand-in chunks that cover a gap count as used; (3) a chunk that **throws or comes out blank** (sampled every sixth chunk, then the level audited) is dropped and retried after 20 frames instead of staying marked drawn; (4) memory is given back at once (an evicted chunk's canvas is zeroed) and the soft layers' canvases are **reused** across rebuilds rather than reallocated each time a scout reveals tiles (Safari frees dropped canvases only at garbage collection, and refuses new canvases meanwhile). `legatumMap.terrain` adds `exposed` (visible chunks showing only the soft layer), `level`, `wanted` and `failures`. The spec adds a wheel-zoom test: a quick zoom across a level boundary holds the level (fails on round 2) and, with Chromium slowed 4x to stand for a loaded Mac, no chunk shows only the soft layer for more than 1.5 s (about 0.4 s real) after the camera stops. Not done: soft layer edges are not feathered; a fast zoom out still shows it for a moment where the view grows past anything cached.
Lessons: (1) a cache that draws a lower layer for a missing chunk must make sure that layer is honest at the scale it is shown (pixel-floored strokes are not scale-free); (2) anything that blits a layer must not touch its LRU stamp, or a fallback pins itself; (2b) a fix that removes a misleading stand-in must replace it with an honest one, not with nothing: look at the frame right after the change, not only the settled one; (3) an "is it settled?" probe (renders per second at idle, visible chunks missing) catches thrash that a screenshot taken at a good moment hides; (4) a 2.5 s wait before a screenshot is not "settled" in a software renderer, so poll the cache instead. Severity: cosmetic and transient (no information is lost, the rectangles are scenery drawn at the wrong detail), but with the thrash it burned CPU whenever the window was large; a hotfix build is optional.
How the redo satisfied both (`claude/settle-lens-green`): one emerald hue at rising coverage (26% to 84% of progressively deeper greens), because over terrain a pale-to-deep ramp of *different* colours fails: mid greens vanish on forest and hills for protan sight, and pale mint vanishes on tundra for deutan. Letting each band darken what is under it by more than the last keeps the order in lightness over every terrain (min ΔE00 between neighbours 4.4, 7.8, 11, 11.3 at the full wash). The text got the same treatment: a plain sentence on the hover, the points and weights behind a Details toggle.
## 2026-10-17: Faction pieces compare with what they replace (claude/piece-compare)
- **Compare against the standard item with only faction-wide rules, not the raw item.** The Ruhr Foundry costs 54 and the German Workshop would too (the 90% new-family rule), so the raw comparison called it 12 cheaper, and the Mongol Outrider's fourth move is every Mongol military unit's. `standardUnitStatsFor`/`standardBuildingStatsFor` in `data.ts` hold that split; a modifier that belongs to the named piece goes in `unitStatsFor`/`buildingStatsFor` only.
- **Two replacements are a rename with no difference:** the Spartan Hoplite (ordinary Spearman numbers; its "starts promoted" is every Spartan unit's) and the Byzantine Imperial Scriptorium (a plain Library). The cards say "Same numbers as ..." and `pieceCompare.test.ts` lists them as design flags. The Spartan piece with a real difference is the Agoge, which the sheet does not show.
- **The hovercard's "What it gives" line for Walls came from the standard blurb ("+10 city strength") even for the Alpine Redoubt (+15).** Any line built from `BUILDING_STATS[kind].blurb` is wrong for a faction replacement; read `buildingStatsFor` for numbers.
- **e2e fixtures on the mock engine (`?client=mock` and a dynamic import of `src/test/*Fixture.ts`) run locally with no WASM or server build,** while specs on `coreFixture` need both: a stale local `src/wasm-pkg` fails the WASM unit tests, so build screenshots and card checks on the mock and leave the WASM specs to the box gate.


## 2026-10-17: Two faction "unique pieces" were renames
Showing each unique piece against what it replaces exposed two duds:
- **The Spartan Hoplite is identical to the Spearman.** "Starts promoted" applies to all Spartan units. The Agoge is Sparta's real difference, but the sheet doesn't show it.
- **The Byzantine Imperial Scriptorium is identical to the Library.**

Design flags are with the user. Show comparisons by default, because they surface design gaps.


## 2026-10-17: Map overlays split from lenses
- **A panel that grew by 200 px crashed the page at 1000x600.** With Yields, Resources and Borders on, the lens panel left the unit panel no room, and `useScrollHint` flipped its hint on and off in a layout effect ("Maximum update depth exceeded" in a `Tip`, which was only where React gave up). A box squeezed to height 0 now keeps its last answer. Any change that makes a bottom-left panel taller needs a 1000x600 shot with a unit selected.
- **A pixel count of "ink-coloured" pixels is noisy, and covering is what matters.** Counting icon pixels per tile under a lens was thrown off by brown terrain that matched the gear colour and by sub-pixel camera shifts. The check compares each tile with itself on the same frame, ignores pixels where the map behind was already that colour, and fails only when a tile loses most of its pixels.
- **A lens chip centred on the tile sits on the top row of a 3 or 4 point yield stack,** which is why the chips move to the side when Yields is on. The geometry test (`overlayLegibility.test.ts`) found it before any screenshot did.
- **`R` is the ranged attack,** so Resources is `O` (and Borders `N`, Yields `Y`).
## 2026-10-17: Hoplite phalanx and the Scriptorium's catch-up discount (claude/unique-pieces)
- **Phalanx +5 was chosen by the Hoplite pair matching the next era's Phalangite, not by a mirror arena.** A Hoplite is 22 (20 and one promotion level) and a Phalangite 26, so +5 makes a pair 27 against 26, which is what a unique piece one era behind should be worth. The sweep (`combat_scenarios --phalanx`, strengths 0 to 8, `docs/sim-results/unique-pieces/phalanx-scan.txt`) agrees: three Hoplites against three Phalangites go 44% (+0), 51% (+3), 53% (+4 and +5), 55% (+6), 56% (+8) under the Hard policy. Pairs against two Spearmen did not move beyond noise (62% to 55%), and two Hoplites against one Composite Archer sat at 96 to 98% at every strength, so the "counter-class unit one era ahead still does well" check cannot be made in an arena: Spartan wound immunity and kill-healing already crush ranged units at equal Production.
- **Arena win shares for Sparta saturate.** The scripted Human policy won 100% for the Spartans in nearly every row even at +0, because focus fire kills a unit a turn and every kill heals 100 HP. Judge a Spartan combat rule by how the number moves between strengths in the one row that is not saturated (an era-ahead melee line, 3 against 3), never by the level.
- **The phalanx is judged at the fight's stage, from the snapshot** (`rules::battle::has_phalanx_neighbour`): a neighbour that dies in the same stage still counts. One neighbour is enough (a line of Hoplites does not stack), only the same empire counts, and the upgraded unit is a Phalangite so the bonus ends on upgrade. That makes the first upgrade nearly worthless to a paired Hoplite (27 against 26); the AI does not weigh it yet.
- **The forecast counts only neighbours the viewer can see** (`forecast.rs::phalanx_neighbour`), so a hidden defending Hoplite beside another one can beat its forecast by 5 strength.
- **The Scriptorium's discount shares the diffusion cap.** One Scriptorium anywhere in the empire adds 10 points on techs at least one rival knows, never past 50%, so it adds nothing once five rivals know a tech: it is a catch-up tool, not a permanent saving. A University over the Library keeps it (`data::has_building` treats the family as satisfied).
- **Putting a rule on a unique piece means editing three lists, not one.** The difference lives only in `pieceCompare.ts` `PIECES[...].rules` (the sheet, build card, hovercard, Guide and unlock card read it); the same sentence in `UNIQUE_PIECE.rules` or `CARD_TEXT` prints it twice. "Starts promoted" was a faction rule filed under the Hoplite, which is why it looked like a difference.
- **AI genes `phalanx_formation` and `scriptorium_value`** (default on) let the benchmark separate the rules from the AI's use of them: arms are `docs/sim-results/unique-pieces/arms.json`.
## 2026-10-17: Aircraft were invisible to the player (claude/air-understandable)
