- **A Fighter with no orders looked like nothing happened.** The user bought one at T204 and saw only "rebase". The fix is state, not a button: every aircraft card leads with what it is doing ("On guard: covers 4 tiles around Bern", "Ready: 3 targets within 5 tiles", "Striking this turn, so it will not intercept"), the dock lists Fighters on guard, the map rings what each kind covers, a finished aircraft gets a card stating its job, and an interception is a report line from both sides.
- **When both acting and not acting are fine, the not-acting state needs an explicit answer for the turn.** A Fighter or Bomber with an enemy in reach asks in the Return queue until you strike, "Stay on guard" or "Hold" (`store.airHeld`, per turn), or it would nag every turn. A Fighter with nothing in reach never asks.
- **Aircraft are in the unit-kind tables as ranged units now** (Fighter `ranged` 58 = its strength, `range` 3 = strike range; the 4-tile guard radius is `FIGHTER_RANGE`), so `validate_strike`, `forecast_air_strike`, the AI and the unlock hovercards read one table. A Fighter with a strike order is marked used before interceptions, so it cannot intercept that turn.
- **A test that checks a strike landed must remove enemy Fighters first:** an enemy Fighter within 4 tiles intercepts a Fighter's strike too (any air strike), which made the first version of the Rust test fail.
- **`import.meta.glob` (Vite only) breaks Playwright specs that import fixtures.** `celebrate/moments.ts` importing `unitArt.ts` pulled it into every spec that loads `siegeFixture` ("glob is not a function"). Keep Vite-only imports out of modules the mock engine and fixtures reach; resolve the image in the component (`Moment.unit`, `MomentLane`).
- **Playwright deletes `test-results/` at the start of a run:** shots written there by a spec vanish when you rerun one test. Write review shots under `.progress/art/<feature>/`.
- **Is a Fighter that only intercepts worth buying? Barely.** 640 all-AI 8-player Hard games (`tools/sim/air-usage.sh`, old AI, Fighters never strike): some empire knew Flight in 95.8% (median first turn 205), Bombers were built in 67.2% and struck in 48.1% (8,688 strike orders, but the median first strike is T250 in a median 256-turn game), Fighters were built in 39.1% but met a strike in only 7.3% of games (159 interceptions, 1.8% of strikes). With strikes allowed (default now), Fighters strike in 9.1% of games (139 orders) and Bomber use is unchanged; the human-seat benchmark (Hard and Hard + Search, seat-only and opponents-only, 8 maps x 64 salts) moved by at most +0.6 pp, inside noise, because those games end before aircraft matter. Options if aircraft should matter more: Modern arrives about T205 and aircraft barely fire before the game ends, so cheaper or earlier air power, or a wider interception radius (Fighters reduce Bomber damage over a wider area), would change more than the strike range does.
## 2026-10-17: Cities overview and "where to build this" needed no new core data
- **The view already held every figure.** Each own city's `CityDetail.options` carries `turns` for every item it can build, computed from that city's own Production with its banked progress counted, and `research.unlocks[].cities` carries the blocker and the from-zero turns for an item not yet researched. `whereToBuild.ts` only chooses between them; a per-item, per-city WASM query was not needed and the turn is no slower.
- **`UnlockCity.build_turns` ignores banked progress and `ProductionOption.turns` counts it.** Use the option for anything researched (a city with 120 of 600 banked toward the Great Library really is nearer) and the unlock quote only before the technology is known.
- **A Great Work is missing from both `options` and `unavailable` while another city holds it,** and the reason ("Great Work under construction in Bern; only one per empire") exists only in the unlock quote. Detect "already building elsewhere" from the queues (current orders included) rather than by parsing that text, so a choice made this turn counts before the core knows it.
- **A list that sorts by turns re-sorts under the pointer when a click changes a row's state.** A city that has just taken the one allowed Great Work keeps the turns the core quoted (state "elsewhere"), so the order holds. The Fastest line still ignores it.
- **Two things are now called Cities:** the top-bar overview (own cities, key C) and the older "Cities" list in This Turn (own and foreign, a keyboard path to every known city). Not merged here; the Guide names the second "the Cities list in This Turn".
## 2026-10-17: The first AI offensive that beat the user: what made it work, and what did not matter (branch claude/golden-horde)
- **What it was:** the 8.9.2 Mongol AI took Switzerland's Aarau on T147 after a 70-turn war (declared T78, a failed first strike, 58 turns without a battle and without peace, then six turns of concentrated fire from three or four shooters at range 2 while the melee waited, and a Phalangite walked into the city after the shooters killed the garrison). It was the generic Hard machinery working: the city-assault coordinator (`city_assault.rs`), the plan layer's forecast-priced Campaign (`plan.rs`) and an army that was never disbanded. The Swiss had no garrison in Aarau for about 40 turns and a 2,235-Gold hoard. (The brief called the user's empire Byzantium; the save is Switzerland/Switzerland.)
- **The genes everyone suspected did nothing.** `troop_runway`, `capture_economy_percent`, `price_bought_walls`, `staging_damage_percent` (50 and 200) and `war_commit_ratio` (250) give run-for-run identical outcomes in 128 runs of the scenario (an identical result across arms can be a dead parameter file, so a positive control ran: Mongols `aggression` 0 gives 25% and 100 gives 50%, so the seat's parameters do apply). `restless_far_war` is Sparta-only. Test the control before reading "no effect".
- **A scenario objective must not depend on the defender's expansion.** The first version ("the Swiss own at most 4 cities") scored a captured Aarau as a failure whenever the Swiss AI founded a sixth city in the meantime; "own Aarau" (`OwnCity`) is robust. Use `cities_captured` in the report to cross-check an objective.
- **A scenario state built from a recent save needs no migration:** `war-build` with `current_rules` keeps the real Power history instead of refilling it, and the replay on the current engine is hash-exact (the 8.9.2 crates are identical to master's). Edit `tools/sim/war-scenarios.sh` only when no run is in progress: bash reads a running script incrementally and the 128-run baseline's report step died with a syntax error.
- **The mechanism does not transfer by itself.** From the same T136 position no other AI as attacker (Japan, Germany, Celts, the Swiss AI, Sparta against four enemies) took a city in 14 turns. The Mongol success needed an enemy city six hexes from its own, nothing in it, and shooters a tier above the walls.
## 2026-10-17: The race panel's rows lost the budget race (claude/race-panel-late)
- **The user's T203 save showed one row and "7 more" that could not be reached, even at 1440x900.** The panel's chrome (head 29, coalition 24, a scored era's two-line block 70, footer 32) took what a siege card in the dock left, the rows box was cut to the one whole row that fit, and that row (yours) is `position: sticky; top: 0; bottom: 0`, so it covered the whole box: scrolling moved the other rows under it. A pinned row needs a box at least three rows tall (`data-narrow` unpins it), and the last page needs a spacer as tall as the pinned row.
- **`reserveRace` reserved three rows against a dock that kept its whole body (130 px).** The dock's chrome (heading, primary, "Ready anyway") is about 149 px at 1000x600 and cannot shrink, so the race got 221 px. Now the race reserves four whole rows (a floor, not a size: beyond them the dock keeps its natural height, or the 8.4 rule that the dock body ends on a whole row and the Cities list's first row break) and the dock's scrolling body gives up height down to 64 px, never the primary action (`raceFit.ts`, pure and tested). The siege card is the one thing it can cut: a compact one-line card at short windows is the open follow-up.
- **A fit that reads `clientHeight` can run against a stale ceiling.** The panel's max-height follows `--dock-height`, published by a `ResizeObserver` after the dock has yielded; the fit ran first, snapped the box to 3 rows and stayed there. Publish `--dock-height` from the fit itself once the dock has yielded, and re-fit when it changes. Also count margins (not in `offsetHeight`) of the panel's children: a missing 2 px dropped the fourth row.
- **An era already scored is the band alone.** The compact block's second line ("Modern: 1 VP scored, kept for good") said nothing to do this era. The band now carries the check and the era's name.
- **`agent-worktree.sh acquire` waits 20 minutes when every pool slot is busy or dirty (a pool with only an untracked `src/wasm-pkg` counts as dirty).** Kill it and make a worktree by hand (`git worktree add -b <branch> .claude/worktrees/<name> <sha>`, symlink `app/node_modules`, copy `src/wasm-pkg`) when the pool is full.

## 2026-10-17: faction win rates on master (8 Hard AIs, 512 games)
- **`legatum-sim --out X` doesn't put the report at X.** It always writes `docs/sim-results/<date>-<label>.{md,json}`, so `remote.sh --artifact X` comes back empty. Fetch those two paths instead. `--speed` no longer exists, and passing it fails before any game runs.
- **AI-only faction spread is 6.8x** (target 1.5x):
  - Byzantium 19.7%, Athens 18.6%, Sparta 17.6%;
  - Germany 11.5%, Switzerland 11.1%, Celts 10.9%;
  - Mongols 7.6%, Japan 2.9%.
  
  The 95% range is about ±3 pp. The user has said balance waits for a competent AI, so read this as a pointer, not a tuning target. Japan (Japan) at 2.9% is the clear outlier and is worth checking for an AI mis-play of its dense-city style before blaming the numbers. All 512 games ended by VP hold; 55% of holds were broken.

## 2026-10-17: the Codex queue sat empty for six days
- **The Luna queue (`.progress/astra-queue/pending/`) was empty from 10-11 to 10-17 while the lane runners idled.** No one noticed, because the supervisor that normally refills it was paused for usage. When the supervisor is paused, the lead checks `ls .progress/astra-queue/pending` at each wrap-up and queues mechanical work (art fixes from UI reviews, docs, cleanup) there.
## 2026-10-17: Coach review of the 8.9.2 Swiss win (what the AI missed, what the rubber bands did)
- **Decadence taxes the challenger almost as hard as the leader.** It is measured against the median empire, so in an 8-empire game with five small empires both leaders pay: the user lost 20.5% of his Production, Gold and Science over the game, Sparta 17.8%, everyone else 0–4%. It delayed his winning Great Work about 9 turns but didn't change who was likely to win; switching the bands off from his save moved his proxy seat −16 to +36 pp depending on the turn.
- **Swiss interest dodges decadence:** 1,668 Gold of interest replaced about 80% of the 2,084 Gold decadence took from him.
- **The threat model is blind to an economic leader until ~45% of the target.** The race term starts at 40% progress and everything else scores conquest, so a 4–2 VP leader with a 1,000+ Gold hoard and a 2x economy was nobody's top threat for 50 turns.
- **Hard is a poor stand-in for the human mid-game:** taking over his seat at T193 it abandoned the Grand Bazaar he was finishing and lost every run; proxy v4 reproduced his result best. Compare branches with the same turn and seat model's control, and use his real orders (tape seat) for late branch points.
- **Replay tooling:** `game_dump` plus `playtest_ai_trace` at the release tag on the box reproduce a 202-turn save with 0 hash mismatches in about 20 minutes; city yields in the trace are net of decadence, so gross = net × 100 / (100 − rate). Review: `docs/playtests/reviews/2026-10-17-8.9.2-switzerland.md`.


## 2026-10-17: An AI that sues for peace and attacks again is usually a candidate-list gap
The 8.9.2 Mongols proposed Peace to the VP leader on T168 and declared on T172, with the same Contain war scored +200 on every other turn. Cause: when a Campaign ends, the plan falls back to the best candidate that is not the ended objective, and Contain on the same enemy was missing that turn because the target list (`contain_candidates`) follows the threat model's top threat, which flickered 72 to 75 between two rivals. The "war in progress stays on offer" clause covered only a running Contain plan. Peace is priced 0 (`quiet_candidate`), so an empty list means Peace and `wants_peace` offers it. Check the candidate list on the turn a plan ends before blaming valuation noise. A Peace followed by a declaration within 10 turns happens about 5.5 times per game (`peace_flip_10` in `hard_vs_human`). Gene `peace_credibility` (on for Hard since 2026-10-17) and gene `contain_after_campaign` (still off) address it; details in the hard-vs-human journal.

## 2026-10-17: Coach review of the 8.9.2 Sparta rush at T87 (why the AI can't do what the user does)
- **AI Sparta's one-city target list is hard-coded by faction name:** `conquest.rs` (from acc83ca04) lets a one-city Sparta target only Japan cities, or an unwalled Byzantium city within 7 hexes; `military.rs` sorties and `production.rs` assault-wing triggers repeat it. On this map the user's first two victims (Byzantium at 8 hexes, the Swiss) were never candidates. Even with those wars forced by the what-if runner, the AI marched on Japan (Byzantine captures 4 in 30 runs). Still on master.
- **`threat.rs::plan_threat_defense` swaps Walls in progress for a shooter** whenever no shooter stands within one hex of an exposed unwalled city (a garrison stepping out triggers it). `keep_walls` only covers the emergency path in `production.rs`. Kyoto switched at 52 of 60 and fell.
- **AI defence passes against AI attackers and fails against the human:** the defenders that fell to him survived to T87 in 30/30 runs when an AI Sparta or AI Mongols was forced into the same wars. Measure defence changes against the proxy rusher or the user's tape, not only Hard attackers.
- **What-if runs that only salt AI seeds are one sample:** Hard is nearly deterministic and the dice come from the game seed, so 30 salts gave identical first 90 turns. Reseeding the dice per salt (as `war_suite` does) moved one control from 21/30 seat wins to 1/30.
- **Rush genes don't make a rush:** knobs at military/aggression 100 with production-military 200% left the AI's T20/T40 army unchanged (3 and 4.5 units); `production.rs::desired_army` sets the early quota.
- **Box congestion:** two box jobs waited 35 minutes in a 13-deep queue. Also, never `grep | xargs kill` a pattern your own command line contains: it killed the shell running it.
## 2026-10-17: forest edge tiles read as grass (playtest 8.9.2, user screenshot near Neuchâtel)
- **Cause:** the forest scatter kept only 45% of its lattice points at a wood's edge and dropped a ragged band of them, and a 1–3 hex grove drew one centred cluster per hex. Measured as pixel coverage (the share of a hex on an opaque forest-sprite pixel, `forestEdge.test.ts`): edge hexes of a wood 0.55 (one-hex arms down to 0.25), a three-hex grove 0.50–0.55, interiors 0.98.
- **Fix (`organicTerrain.ts`):** edge keep rate 0.8 and a narrower ragged band for forests (hills keep the thin edge); a floor of three clusters on every wood tile, placed on its inland side so the gap faces the open neighbour; groves get two small companion clusters per hex. Now: edge hexes 0.76–0.79 on average (min 0.56), arms 0.5+, groves 0.66–0.73, interiors unchanged at 0.98. The test fixes the floors (mean > 0.68, min > 0.5, grove > 0.6) and that the open half stays thinner than the inland half.
- **Gotchas:** sprites carry about 55% alpha inside their square, so a lone cluster per hex never fills a hex; measure coverage on the sprite PNG alpha, not the sprite count. In a worktree `app/src/wasm-pkg` is a symlink into the main checkout: Vite answers 403 for it, so replace it with a real copy before running Playwright.

## 2026-10-17: "research panel by faction" timed out at 1000x600 because the Technologies button was hidden, not because research was slow
- **Symptom:** the screen-tour test hit its 2-minute timeout with `browserContext.close: Test ended` on three gate runs after the cities overview landed. It passed at 1440x900 and at the parent of the cities-overview commits (reproduced on the box: master fails, `e155666ef` passes), and only for the Switzerland context.
- **Cause:** not where-to-build (nothing there runs until a card's Details or hovercard opens). The Cities button took room in the top bar, so the compaction ladder reached its `nav` step for the crowded Switzerland bar at 1000 px and set `display:none` on the Technologies button, which the research cell also opens (by design). `getByRole('button', { name: 'Technologies' })` ignores hidden elements, so `click()` waited out the whole test timeout; the trace's last action was that click and the failure screenshot shows the game screen with the research cell still there.
- **Fix:** the spec's `openResearch` clicks the Technologies button when it is visible, otherwise the research cell, and fails in 5 s naming the missing opener. Other specs that open research at 1000x600 with a crowded bar should use the same fallback if they start timing out.
- **Reading a timeout like this:** look at the last trace action and the final screenshot before suspecting render cost; a 2-minute timeout whose last action is a click is a locator that never matched.
## 2026-10-17: The end of a game needed a history the engine never kept (claude/postgame)
(1) **The only per-turn record was the archive's telemetry, and it rarely survives.** `legatum_wasm::history::Recording` kept VP, cities, population, Science, Gold and army per turn, but a save's archive is dropped on load whenever its `rules_fingerprint` differs, and that fingerprint hashes every core and wasm source file. Any game carried across a build (every playtest release) lost its whole history. The record now lives in the core state (`GameState.history`, `core::history::record` at the end of `resolve_turn`): per empire per turn VP, cities, population, Science made, treasury, army strength, techs and era, plus a chronicle of landmark events (founding, captures, razing, war, peace, eras, crowns, Great Works, wonders, era objectives, goals, eliminations, holds, the win). It is public only through `PlayerView.history`, and only once the game is finished. Saves from before it get their series and wars, peace and eliminations backfilled from the archive telemetry on load (when the archive is from another build). About 45 KB of JSON for 200 turns and 8 empires. The state hash now includes it, so the determinism fixtures and the ranged-garrison replay pins were repinned. (2) **Real end states make honest fixtures.** Both of the user's 10-17 saves replayed exactly on their own engine (28be168, every tape hash matched), so the report's fixtures are his Swiss win and his loss: the latter was unfinished at turn 221, so the AI played on with his seat idle until the Celts won at turn 249. The fixtures are trimmed to about 350 KB each (no archive or AI memory, and rivals' map memory cleared). (3) **Reward emblems (`emblem-*`) don't render through `Icon`:** it resolves only `assets/icons` and the faction glyphs. Use `Glyph` (exported from `ui/ringMedallion.tsx`), which also handles `treaty-*`. (4) **A reveal's numbers need the same skip as its CSS.** Screenshots with `animations: 'disabled'` fast-forward CSS but not a requestAnimationFrame count-up, so the standings showed 0/10 until the scene was settled with a click. The tour clicks the scene before its shot. (5) **A crown that flip-flops makes a busy VP line.** The real games' Strongest Army changed hands 26 and 56 times. The chart shows it as it was (step lines plus a crowns lane) rather than smoothing it. (6) **A screen-reader span escapes its scroll panel.** The timeline's `.sr-only` spans (absolute) had the dialog as their containing block, so the dialog's `scrollHeight` grew to 2,141 px although nothing visibly overflowed; `position: relative` on the scroll panel fixed it. (7) **Killing a box verify job leaves its gate-cache locks behind.** `remote.sh --kill` on a running `verify-remote` left `sim-build-master` locks under `/data/LegatumRuns/gate-cache/locks/` whose pid was dead, and the next verify of any branch with that master snapshot sat in a 2 s poll for up to the 4 h timeout. Remove locks whose `pid` file names a dead process (only your own) and the waiting job carries on.

## 2026-10-17: the release check timed out behind the box queue
- **The 8.9.3 release check (`tools/release/check.sh` via `remote.sh`) was killed by the client-side `timeout 7000`.** The box queue was 10–20 jobs deep, so queue wait plus the check ran past it, and nothing was reported. For release checks on a busy box, use `timeout 14000` or more, or wait for a quiet queue. A killed job can also leave stale locks in the box's shared step cache (see the post-game entry).

## 2026-10-18: a killed box verify can leave the next one hanging
- **After an agent killed its own verify job, the next verify of the same branch sat at "app-wasm (cached)" for over 1.5 hours at low box load.** The likely cause is a stale lock in the box's shared step cache, as in the post-game entry. Killing box jobs from inside an agent may be refused by the permission system. Run a fresh `verify-remote.sh <branch> --app --no-cache` instead, which bypasses the shared cache, and let the hung job time out (6 hours). **Confirmed and better:** the hung run ended after 15,302 s with `app-vitest-lock-timeout app-build-lock-timeout app-tsc-lock-timeout`, the stale locks from the killed job on the same code tree. The clean way around it is to rebase the branch onto current master. The step locks are keyed by code tree, so a new tree gets fresh locks, and nothing on the box needs clearing or killing.
## 2026-10-17: Decadence is proportional to the lead and taxes every income (claude/decadence-proportional)
The user's two rulings after the coach's 8.9.2 review: "all things that feel like income, production, etc should be taxed by decadence" and "it should be proportional to how far ahead each civ is." Rate = `L x c / 100` percent with `c` = 40 (`decadence-slope`), replacing `45 L / (L + 45)`, which stays behind `legacy-decadence` (`decadence-max`/`decadence-k` set it; `surcharge-max=100,decadence-slope=0` is v2 Strong). Full numbers in `docs/sim-results/vp-v2/decadence-proportional.md`.
- **Audit: Swiss treasury interest was the only recurring income outside the city-yield step.** Tolls, long-road commerce, building, wonder and faction bonuses, Capital, Citizens, the Japan network and the Germany's projects are all inside `yields_base`/`yields_for_item` before decadence, so they were already cut. Interest now shows as a gross line plus "Decadence −N% of interest: luxury", rounded like a city's yield, and is still not counted in Power. Exempt, with reasons: one-off transfers and rewards (tribute, Iron Price gold, plunder, capture bounties, ruin, camp and wonder-discovery rewards), spending, banked Production (a store) and the strategic-resource stockpile (a supply of deposits, not Gold, Production or Science).
- **c = 40 is the user's anchor, and the tail is why not steeper.** Four slopes over 512 games each (Hard + Search, 4p + 8p, paired seeds), against the old curve on the same binary family: T100 leader wins 49.4% old, 55.2 / 51.4 / 47.1 / 47.8% at c 25 / 40 / 50 / 60; lead changes 9.7 old, 8.3 / 9.0 / 9.6 / 10.2; top Power over median 34% old, 43 / 37 / 33 / 32%; game length 250 old, 231 / 240 / 247 / 254. c 50 reproduces the old curve's mean (top empire's mean rate 16.9% against 16.7%) but 7% of its games ever pass a 50% rate (c 40: 2%) and two pass 100%; c 60 stalled a game at 600 turns with a 294% rate (a dead economy, no way back). A typical 30% lead pays 12% at c 40 where the old curve took 18%, so the old curve's pressure at moderate leads is softer here; `decadence-slope=50` is one knob away.
- **The rate does not always settle: runaway conquerors push it past 50%.** The median game's peak rate is 22% (p90 39%) at c 40, but 11 of 512 games pass 50% and the highest is 87%: an Sparta empire with 25 to 30 cities against two or three small survivors keeps a lead of 100% or more over the median (the mean of the middle two empires). Power is measured after decadence, so the loop does shrink the lead, but not when the pack stays small. No cap (the user rejected one); raised to the lead as a question. `decadence_cut` clamps to the yield so a rate past 100% means zero, never negative.
- **The runner-up pays about half the leader's rate, not nine tenths.** Mean 6.8% against 14.1% at c 40 (old curve 9.5% against 16.7%). A leader twice as far ahead pays exactly twice (unit test).
- **Human-seat benchmark (AI-impact gate): not worse.** Hard 256 salts per map 30.1% to 33.4% (+3.4 pp, CIs 28.5 to 31.5 and 31.9 to 34.9), Hard + Search 64 salts 27.7% to 32.8% (+5.1 pp, CIs overlap). All-seats arm only: a rule change applies to every seat and the AI has no gene for it, as for the Swiss-interest change. The gains are on the two Sparta maps (8.6: 28% to 55%; 8.4: 85% to 93%). **Rerun after rebasing onto master's phase 1 AI genes** (Hard, 256 salts): control 31.0% (29.4 to 32.5), branch 32.1% (30.3 to 33.8), +1.1 pp, still not worse. The 8.6 Sparta gain holds (32% to 55%); the Switzerland 8.5 map fell both times (49% to 45%, then 55% to 39%): Switzerland in the user's seat is the one place the interest cut may bite, not investigated.
- **War scenarios: Golden Horde attack success fell about 5 points (control 44%, branch 39% over 256 runs, same seeds, paired 95% interval -8.6 to -2.0; 45% and 40% over 128; identical after the rebase).** Above the 30% floor but below the baseline band of 43 to 50%, about 1.2 standard errors: a rule change, not an AI change, so decadence is the only thing that moved (not investigated which seat pays it; the new rate is above the old curve's at leads over about 100%). Flagged, not fixed.
- **Sim telemetry gained two numbers:** the highest decadence rate any empire paid in a game and the runner-up's mean rate (`vp_race.decadence_max_seen`, `second_decadence_percent_sum`; report rows in `tools/sim/vp_v2_report.py`).
- **`tools/sim/hard-vs-human-report.py` crashes drawing its chart once the ledger holds a row without `overall_score`** (row 188, "current-master Sparta overreach desertion diagnosis; full games", a comparison row). The new row is appended before the crash, so the data is kept, but the exit code is 1 and a `&&` chain stops. Not fixed here.
- **Verification flakes on a loaded box.** `verify-remote.sh --app` showed 3 unrelated Playwright failures once (network-changed console errors, a pick-animation timing check, a research screen-tour timeout) and 28 on a rerun while the box ran many jobs (browser sessions closed, 90 s timeouts, in specs that passed on the first run). None touch decadence.
## 2026-10-17: The Keshig UI (claude/keshig-ui)
- **`unit.ranged > 0` no longer means "can't capture, can't reinforce".** A mounted archer has a ranged number and still charges and takes cities. Every UI check of that kind (capture and reinforcement warnings, siege strike mode, the attackable-enemy chips, the foreign-city attack button, the forecast's win label) now asks `attacksInMelee(kind)`, mirroring core. Grep for `.ranged > 0` when a unit gains a second way to fight.
- **A shot and a charge are two forecasts of one target, so the card shows both** (`ui/DualForecastCard.tsx`): each with its key (R then click; right-click), its class lines and its verdict, and the single card says why there is no shot (out of range, blocked) beside the charge. The spear line's +18 against mounted appears only on the charge line, which is the whole point of choosing.
- **Reach for Mongol units leaves enemy tiles out** (they slip past enemies, so `reachTiles` never ends on one): `attackableEnemies` treats an enemy beside a reachable tile as a target for a Mongol unit. This was already wrong for the Mongol Horseman's strength chips.
- **A comparison rule and a piece-card rule must not say the same sentence.** The vs line carries "Shoots or charges each turn, never both."; the piece card keeps the limit of a shot. Two short sentences fit a build card where the one long one did not.
- **Core's research-card effect lines are faction-agnostic** ("Horsemen can upgrade to Cataphracts"), so `factionEffectLabel` in `data.ts` rewrites it for the Mongols; the parsers tolerate the "and lose the bow" tail.
- **The mock engine reads base `UNIT_STATS` for shots (`mock/forecast.ts isRangedShot`)**, so it gives no shot forecast for a Keshig. Screenshots of the two-option card run on the real core (`coreFixture` and `/?client=wasm`).
- **The Mongol sheet had no room for a third strength/price row at 1000x600** until the lore went to one line and two rows to two lines each; `piece-compare.spec.ts` asserts every sheet fits.
## 2026-10-17: hill hexes read as plains (playtest 8.9.3, user screenshot), and the audit of every other feature
- **Cause:** the same as the forests, worse: the hill sprites are sparse mounds (only about 60% opaque even packed), the scatter kept 45% of its points at an edge, and a lone hill drew one sprite. Pixel coverage (`terrainCoverage.test.ts`): edge hexes of a hill range 0.28 (min 0.14), one-hex arms 0.25, a lone hill 0.33, interior 0.6.
- **Fix (`organicTerrain.ts`, one per-kind table `FEATURE`):** hills get the forest rule with their own numbers (spacing 1.15 R, scale 0.9, edge keep 0.85, floor of four clusters on the inland side, three companions for a lone hex or a grove). Now edge 0.71 (min 0.51), interior 0.84, arms 0.5+, lone hill and groves 0.75. A first pass at 0.9 interior made a range a busy blanket that hid resource markers; the final numbers leave the grid and markers readable.
- **Audit:** the game has only Forest, Hills and Mountains as painted organic features. Mountains already read (edge 0.70, arms 0.85, lone 0.69, grove 0.6). Desert (tan hex, wavy dune marks across it) and Tundra (pale hex, snow patches) are glyph-per-hex in `terrain.ts` and read at the edge in both saves. There is no marsh, jungle or snow terrain. The test now holds a floor for the three painted kinds; only the hills assertions fail on the old code.
- **Method:** `e2e/terrain-edges-shots.spec.ts` picks edge and exposed hexes of every feature in a real save (all six neighbours explored, nearest the player's first city), so before/after shots need no hand-picked coordinates.

## 2026-10-18: Luna outruns a single landing reviewer
- **Four Luna lanes finished about 12 branches overnight, while one serial Claude landing reviewer (full verify on a busy box, plus a benchmark for rule changes) landed far fewer.** Split the landing: rule changes go to a Sonnet lander, which runs the AI-impact gate; small UI and script fixes go to a Haiku lander. Check `git log master` for `astra/` merges at each check-in, so finished branches don't pile up unlanded.
- **AI formation rules go after distance, never before it.** The first `phalanx_formation` ranked a spot next to a Hoplite above terrain and distance; against the then-current master it cost the Sparta seat 116 to 64 wins on the 8.4 map. Used only as a tie-break after distance and exposure it returned to 117. The earlier run against the older master had shown no such drop, so re-run the seat-split arms after merging master (`docs/sim-results/unique-pieces/README.md`).
- **Two Hard + Search tests pinned numbers the phalanx moves.** `mongols_win_t14_search_stages_and_captures_before_walls` declared a turn early while the formation was first-priority, and `mongols_win_contain_never_starts_an_immediate_failed_siege` no longer holds its "15% forecast at T53" premise (the +5 lifts the capture odds). The latter now judges the war by its outcome only (declared and aborted for want of army fails).


## 2026-10-18: separately gated features still broke each other's layout
- **The 8.9.4 pre-release UI review found three blockers that every feature branch's own gate missed.** The new CITIES entry truncated the research prompt. The Fighter ready card's long title pushed its buttons off at 1000x600. The research queue head overprinted its strip. Each feature passed its own specs, but none re-checked the shared top bar or moment cards after the others landed. Run the full UI review on the combined master before every build, not only per branch, and add every feature screen to the screen tour in the same change.

## 2026-10-18: fixing the 8.9.4 review's blockers and majors (claude/ui-review-894-fixes)
- **The top bar's compaction ladder had the Technologies button last, though the code comment said first.** The research cell opens the same dialog, so `TOP_BAR_COMPACTION` now starts with `nav`; every e2e that clicked the Technologies button by name now clicks `.topbar .research-chip`, which is always there. Iron and Coal chips are painted art at 24 px (`StockpileIcon`: painted from 20 px up, the engraved mark below), because the engraved 16 px marks were two brown discs with the names hidden. `topbar.spec.ts` now also runs at 1000x600.
- **A moment card scrolled as a whole, so its buttons went with the text.** `.moment-card` is a flex column: kicker and footer pinned, `.moment-body` scrolls. The aircraft card carries the city in its kicker and the title stays "Fighter ready" (`readyNotice` returns the kicker too).
- **A one-per-empire building that is not a Great Work (the Yam Relay Station) was treated as an ordinary item by where-to-build,** so it read "Fastest: Khanbaliq ... already being built". `oncePerEmpire` in `whereToBuild.ts` covers both. The opened list's summary is sticky under the city head (`--cp-head-h`, published by `CityPanel`) and names the item.
- **Piece-compare cost gaps came from the static tables;** the build card now passes both quoted option costs (`pieceComparison(..., quoted)`), so the gap matches the two prices on screen under the opening production ramp. Surfaces with no quote (sheet, Guide, unlock hovercard) still use the tables.
- **After the game ends, three surfaces stayed live:** the unit panel (`Awaiting orders` and its actions), a goal label past its target (`cappedGoalLabel`: the core's "1,860 of 1,000 Gold held"; the label is text from the core, so the cap is applied where the UI prints it), and the coalition banner's seat label (`empireTitle` names factions). The race panel's hold strip and coalition line were already hidden by `astra/postgame-race-panel-after-end`.
- **Label overlays drawn on the canvas sit under the HUD panels:** the "Fighter guards 4 tiles" ring label was clamped to the canvas, which runs under the left dock. `clearSpot` in `airOverlay.ts` moves a label to the nearest spot clear of the panel rects (`lensAvoid`).
- **The Imperial Scriptorium had no line on the Byzantium sheet:** a strength with no paired price is allowed (`FactionPair.price` optional, a `solo` row); Byzantium's other prices already pay for its strengths.
## 2026-10-18: review-only agents must not leave edits in the main checkout
- **The UI reviewer may add missing screens to the tour spec, but it left that 3-line edit uncommitted in the main checkout.** That blocked `git merge --ff-only` for another agent's landing, which correctly refused to stash or touch it. Agents that edit must work in a worktree, or commit their one allowed change straight away. At each check-in, the lead runs `git status` on the main checkout; the only expected dirty file is `app/src-tauri/Cargo.lock`.

## 2026-10-18: the worktree pool handed one slot to two agents
- **`tools/ops/agent-worktree.sh` gave `pool-1` to the UI-fix agent while the plain-language agent was still using it.** A `git merge master` from one agent then landed on the other's branch (17395c7c8). It was harmless this time, but it could mix work. Until the pool's lock check is fixed, long-running agents should use their own `git worktree add` rather than the pool. Queued as a fix.

## 2026-10-18: Luna rule branches go stale fast on a busy master
- **The first Luna rule branch to land (territory-sight) took over 5 hours.** Master moved more than 150 commits during gating, which forced three repins. The branch also had two real bugs: the exploration pass bypassed the new sight rule, and two test oracles were stale. On a night with many landings, land rule branches in a quieter window, or rebase and gate them immediately after Luna finishes, before other big merges. Always re-read Luna's rule code against the brief; its tests can pass while missing a path.

## 2026-10-18: the 8.9.4 chunk-cache failure was sprite arrivals repainting the whole cache, only visible on a loaded box (claude/chunk-cache-quiet)
- **Symptom.** `e2e/map-chunk-cache.spec.ts` ("p892-sea-rect-t141 at 1440x900") failed in the release check and in a full verify on the box with "the cache keeps rendering chunks", 5 renders per 1.5 s, for the whole 40 s window. It passed 3 of 3 serial runs, 20 of 20 parallel runs and 2 of 2 whole-file runs on the box, on the tag and on master; c3856eca4 (the commit it "passed" at) and `playtest-8.9.4` render the same 107 chunks for the first zoom and 418 for the five zooms. **No commit between them regressed the cache**: the failure is load-dependent and the fixed 40 s window is the only thing that changed (the box ran 21 Playwright workers plus other jobs). It is not an eviction problem (`evictions` 0, `pinned` 88 of a limit of 110 at 1440x900).
- **Cause.** A city sprite that finishes decoding bumped `cityArtEpoch()`, and `TerrainCache.draw` answered every bump by marking **every cached chunk stale**. The sea save loads about 17 sprite files over a session; on a fast machine they arrive in the first seconds, but on a slow one (CPU throttle 3 on Chromium with sprite responses spaced 2.5 s apart reproduces it) they trickle in while the view fills, and each arrival repaints the whole view and its ring again (about 90 chunks at about 100 ms each in headless Chromium's software canvas, where `drawImage` of the terrain sprites is 40% of the time). With the same arrivals the original spec's zoom sequence rendered 933 chunks instead of 418 at full speed and **failed at throttle 3** ("visible chunks still missing"); with the fix it passes at throttle 3 with 422 to 438 renders.
- **Fix.** `cityArt.ts` records which sprite files a drawing found still decoding (`traceCityArt`) and which have arrived (`cityArtArrivals`); each chunk keeps the set it waits for (`awaits`) and repaints once, when the last of them is in. No other chunk repaints for an arrival (a failed load counts as settled, so nothing waits for ever). The spec keeps all its assertions, prints the cache's counters when a settle fails (`pinned`, `limit`, `stale`, `evictions`, `epochs`, `artRenders`, added to `legatumMap.terrain`), and gains "late city art repaints the chunks that hold a town once each" (sprite responses delayed 3 s plus 2.5 s each): 273 repaints before, 76 after, bound = the number of cached chunks.
- **A Mac player.** Probably not noticeable in the cache going busy for ever: nothing was spinning, the cache always went quiet once the sprites were in. What the Mac saw was extra repaint waves of the whole view (and ring) on each first-time sprite (a new era, a rival's city seen for the first time, the first view after loading), each costing a chunk render per frame: choppy frames for some seconds, less on a GPU canvas than on the box. The fix removes that.
- **Method.** The CDP throttle (`Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate`) plus `page.route` delays on `/assets/*-{hamlet,town,city,metropolis,walls}*.png` reproduces a loaded machine deterministically; a single 20-way parallel run of the same test did not. Time to quiet on the first zoom was 13 s unthrottled (7.6 s filled, the ring after), so the 40 s window had only 3x of headroom on its own.
## 2026-10-18: Scouting decides how fast the AI expands; the fixed-map benchmark cannot see it (claude/ai-value-scouting)
The task was value-scored scouting for the Sparta rush (the coach's 8.9.2 review said scouting was the binding limit). Half of that was wrong and the measurement method itself was the bigger finding.
- **What AI Sparta's scouting actually looks like (the review's `t1-hard` branch, home hex (17,13), dice reseeded per run, 192 runs):** its capital is 7 hexes from the Mongols and Japan, 8 from the Swiss and Byzantium, 10 from the Celts, 13 from Athens, 22 from Germany. The scout finds Japan on turn 9, Byzantium on 15, the Celts on 22 and the Mongols on 25, then stands beside the Mongol city (the Hard `sentry` gene) from turn 28 for 15 to 40 turns. The Swiss, Athens and Germany stay unfound until turns 52 to 60. Scouts die about once a game (median first death turn 54, killed by the Mongols, Japan, the Celts and barbarians), not early. Rival scouts see Sparta on turn 4 (Mongols), 5 (Byzantium), 12 (Athens), 20 (Celts), 24 (Japan and the Swiss) and 35 (Germany).
- **More scouting does not make AI Sparta take more cities.** Captures by turn 87 are 1.2 to 1.5 (the seed set alone moves it: 1.55 on seeds 1 to 128, 1.17 on seeds 1000 to 1383). Forcing the war on Byzantium first (the user's choice) halves it (0.72); forbidding Japan, 0.78; never making peace, 1.32; giving the seat the whole map explored from turn 1 cuts it to 0.58 (first war turn 27, not 18). Japan is the best first target for the AI, so finding Byzantium sooner would not help. The conversion limit is the storm and the chain after the first capture, not intel.
- **Six scouting genes built on this, one worked.** `scout_sentry` (sentry only beside a rival whose public army is 60% of ours, one scout kept exploring), `scout_spotter`, `declare_in_sight` (wait for a fresh sighting of an unwalled target), `scout_rescout`, `target_intel` (stale-sighting cost) and `border_evidence` (a rival's explored border counts as seeing it) each sat within 1 point of the control in 9,000-game leagues. They are kept on `claude/ai-value-scouting-experiments`. The one that works is `scout_find`: while a rival's first city is unfound (before turn 40) the scout's frontier score adds a weight per hex of reach that unexplored land lies inside our nearest city.
- **`scout_find` makes the AI expand about 8% faster, and wins.** League of fresh 8-player Hard lobbies (4 seats candidate, 4 seats built-in, `run-batch.sh --opponent-params`, 9,000 games per row, SE 0.5 points; the null candidate sits at 51.2%): weight 10 52.6%, 25 53.8%, 50 55.5%, 100 56.8%, 200 57.6 to 58.1%, 400 58.2%, 800 57.9%, 1600 58.8%. A window of turn 20, 40 or 80, with or without "only while a rival is unfound", and skipping far ruins all gave 56 to 58%. Candidate seats at weight 200 have 3.54 cities at turn 100 against 3.26 (2.33 against 2.21 at turn 50), the same techs, the same eliminations. Merged with master, weight 200: 58.1% (seeds 7100001+) and 57.7% (held-out 9100001+) at 8 players, 54.5% at 4 (null 50.4%), 51.2% at 2 (null 50.8%): the more rivals, the more finding them early is worth. No cost in simulation speed (9.0 against 9.2 ms a turn).
- **The fixed-map human-seat benchmark gave the opposite answer, and it was noise.** At 8 maps x 128 salts the Hard seat with `scout_find` scored -0.4, -10.7, -5.4 and -9.9 points at weights 10, 25, 50 and 100 (control 35.2%): not monotone, with single maps swinging 60 wins either way (8.5 Switzerland 66 to 33, 8.6 Sparta 79 to 30). Every salt of a map shares one set of combat, movement and barbarian dice, so a map's win rate is mostly whether that one dice sequence suits the new opening, and any change in behaviour flips some maps. The intervals the report prints (salt bootstrap) measure only AI RNG and say nothing about this. Treat the fixed-map benchmark as unreadable for any change to early exploration or opening order, whatever its interval; use a league on fresh seeds (above), or run the benchmark with `LEGATUM_HVH_RESEED_DICE=1`, which gives every salt its own dice (new this change).
- **Gotchas.** The what-if tool's single map is a poor judge too: `border_evidence` for the rivals cut Sparta's captures by 0.4 on seeds 1 to 128 and by 0.0 on seeds 1000 to 1383. `run-batch.sh` terminates its servers when the launching shell exits, so a batch started with `(... &)` dies with "exit 254"; run it with the harness's background option. The box queue was 10 to 14 deep for hours: submitting duplicate build and test jobs only lengthens it, and killing them is not allowed, so read the tree twice before each submission. In zsh `set -- $cfg` does not split; use a bash script file.
## 2026-10-18: moving master with update-ref leaves the main checkout stale
- **An agent landed by running `git update-ref refs/heads/master` from a temporary worktree.** Master is checked out in the main checkout, so its index and files stayed at the old tree, and 55 files showed as staged reverse changes. A careless commit there would have reverted the landing. The fix was `git reset -q`, then `git checkout HEAD -- <changed files>`, keeping the long-standing local `Cargo.lock` edit. Land with `git merge --ff-only` run in the main checkout (or `git -C <main checkout>`), never `update-ref` on a checked-out branch.

## 2026-10-18: the Military Engineer (siege rework built)
- **The rule:** Walls halve every attack on a city's HP unless a Military Engineer stands on the attacker's tile; the Engineer's siege tier (Mathematics Catapult, Machinery Trebuchet, Metallurgy Bombard) cancels Walls up to its tier, and against higher walls it cancels the lower tiers and half the halving. Catapult and Bombard retired (old saves load them as Engineers), the Mongols' half city damage is gone. Rules in `legatum-rules` §4 "Walls and the Military Engineer", design values in `legatum-design`.
- **Coverage and price came from the lab, not taste:** one Engineer per unit on its tile captured about as often as one per four units beside them (50% against 46%, Classical and Renaissance pooled, against 27% with none), so "the unit it's with" was not too weak; it costs 3 more Engineers per four units, which is why the price is 40, not 60 (970 Production per capture at 40, 2,450 with no Engineer). `docs/sim-results/siege-engineer/`.
- **A partial cancel needs a middle value:** against a Castle city with a tier-1 Engineer, 25% damage captured 20% (worse than no Engineer, 49%), 50% 57%, 75% 64%, 100% 80%. Anything at or below the uncovered 50% makes a lower tier pointless; 100% makes the tiers pointless. 75% it is.
- **Lab Engineers must walk the engine's own route.** The first lab policy put each Engineer on the ring tile nearest its unit; the engine's path chose another tile, so most "covered" fights were uncovered and the Engineer looked useless (typical cell 0%). Cover is read from where an Engineer stands when the fight is planned: a unit that steps and attacks in one turn needs its Engineer to step to the same tile in the same round, so every policy (lab, AI) computes the unit's fight tile from the path. A real AI therefore pairs each Engineer with a unit and orders it to that unit's end tile (`crates/ai/src/engineers.rs`).
- **The AI had no way to plan around walls, so it needed four changes, not one:** forecasts must see cover (real Engineers near, else virtual ones priced in Production: `forecast_siege`, `city_assault_succeeds`); Engineers must be built (value 5000 and a purchase/switch pass: production only reconsiders idle cities and banked progress makes switching free); `assessed_campaign_allowed` and `WallsBeforeArrival` must stop treating a wall as a ban once Engineers are possible; and the Engineers must follow the army. The war scenarios show the build-up takes about 15 turns for the AI (the first Engineer 8 turns after the war starts).
- **Human-seat benchmark (512 games per arm, Hard):** genes on for the seat only: 22.1% to 18.2% (-3.9 points, interval -7.2 to -0.6, a real loss); genes on for the opponents only: 22.1% to 25.0% (+2.9, interval -0.6 to +6.6, so the opponents did not get better at beating the seat). Engineers built per game barely moved (seat 1.55 to 1.82; the five AI empires 4.58 to 4.78). So `walls_as_cost`, `engineer_for_walls` and `sally_engineer` ship default 0; the AI already builds Engineers without them. Numbers in `docs/sim-results/siege-engineer/benchmark/results.md`. **Correction (2026-10-20, coach):** in the 8.9.6 release no AI built a Military Engineer in either playtest (233 and 244 turns) or in any control branch of their what-ifs, so "the AI already builds Engineers without them" does not hold for the shipped build; and a Hard seat can't show the cost of the genes being off (see the 2026-10-20 coach entry).
- **Coal is dead:** the Catapult and Bombard were Coal's only sinks. Coal deposits, the stockpile chip and the Guide still mention it. Decide whether to remove Coal or give it a use.
- **War suite after the rule change** (Hard, 64 runs): The Golden Horde comes 43% to 79%, its hold scenario 56% to 20% (the Mongol attacker is stronger without its half-damage rule); the three new scenarios: walls rise 1%, walled Keystone and Anvil with Engineers 0%, defender facing Engineers 0% (the AI loses a city every run; its sally gene never fires because the garrison is a single unit). Predictions were written first (35, 25, 30%).
## 2026-10-18: longer games by asking more of every era objective (VP pacing)
- **User direction:** air power arrives too late (Flight first known at a median of T205, the first bomber strike at T250 in a 256-turn game), so give each era fewer VP on average and let the game run longer. **Landed:** every era-objective count is 150% of what it was (`experiments::DEFAULT_OBJECTIVE_SCALE`, knob `objective-scale=N`). The VP target (10), the 10-turn hold, the crowns and 1 VP per era are unchanged. Report: `docs/sim-results/vp-pacing/README.md`.
- **Measured (8 Hard empires, 384 games, same seeds):** median game 244 to 308 turns (p10 205 to 251, p90 298 to 412), no-winner games 0 to 1.3%, turns left after the first Flight 42 to 104 (games with 60+: 27% to 83%), games with an aircraft strike 45% to 89%, aircraft strikes landed per game 12 to 72. Lead changes 9.5 to 11.5, the T100 leader wins slightly less (46 to 43%), the last lead change stays at 95% of the game. Hard + Search at 4 empires reproduces it (median 237 to 304).
- **Raising the VP target does not work as a dial.** 11 gives a median of 286 but a tail to 442 (p90) and 4% of games with no winner; 12 gives a median of 538 and 44% of 8-empire games unfinished at the 600-turn cap. Winners already hold nearly every permanent point (5.4 of 6 era objectives, 2.0 of 2 Great Work), so each extra VP has to come from a crown held at the same time as everything else, under crown upkeep and decadence.
- **Scaling the objectives barely moves the average score.** The share of entering empires that score an era falls only from 53% to 49% (still inside the 40 to 60% band), and the winner's VP mix is unchanged (5.5 era, 1.5 goal, 2.0 Great Work, 1.4 crowns). The game runs longer because the late-era objectives need bigger empires, so the last permanent points arrive later.
- **Flight's turn is research, not the end of the game.** The first-strike median stays near T244; what changes is how many games are still running after it. `era_entries` in the sim telemetry is keyed by the 1-based era entered minus one (key 5 is Modern, key 6 the Future), and Flight (Modern) is learned about 35 turns after the first Modern entry, so measure the air window from `research_turns` (Flight), not from an era entry.
- **Cost:** about +60 turns. If the 3 to 6 hour target meant 0.8 to 1.5 minutes a turn, the median is 4.1 to 7.7 hours and one 8-empire game in four passes 360 turns. `objective-scale=125` (median 277, 74 turns after Flight) is the cheaper step if that is too long. Real turn times at T250 to T400 were not measured.
- **The human-seat benchmark does not drop** (all seats, Hard 27.6% to 31.2%, Hard + Search 21.5% to 26.2%; the opening milestones are identical) and the war suite is unchanged (delta 0.0 on all ten scenarios). Master itself is off the older war baselines (`war-golden-horde` 0%, 743 "bugs" such as units standing in foreign cities), with and without this change.

## 2026-10-18: the 8.9.4 "water on land" patches were a real-WebKit canvas race (claude/chunk-cache-quiet)
- **Symptom (the user, T36, `bug-saves/p894-rects-t36*`).** Rectangular patches of sea, with coast lines, wave rings and the grass tufts of the land under them, drawn over inland Grassland, Forest and Plains near Karakorum, with straight chunk-aligned edges. The save has no water within ten hexes: the map data is right.
- **Reproduced only in the real engine.** Chromium and Playwright's Linux WebKit on the box (fresh load, zoom sequences, turns, simulated scout exploration, CPU throttling) never showed it. A small Swift harness (`tools/ui/webkit-mac/`: an invisible 1440x900 Retina `WKWebView` on this Mac, the map canvas read back with `toDataURL`, no screenshots) showed it in 3 of 5 and 4 of 8 fresh loads of the save, always after a zoom between two levels, always settled (not a transient).
- **Cause.** Chunks are drawn from shared scratch canvases (land, mask, coast lines, band, blur temp) that the next chunk overwrites. WKWebView defers canvas operations, so a chunk's `drawImage` of a scratch canvas could run after the next chunk had rewritten it: the chunk showed a rectangle of another chunk's coast over its land. Giving every chunk fresh scratch canvases removed it, and so did giving only the land canvas fresh, or only the mask, lines or band canvas (any one allocation per chunk synchronised enough); a 1 px readback after `putImageData` of the mask and lines did not; a **1 px `getImageData` of the finished chunk** did (0 of 11 fresh loads, against about half), and costs nothing measurable (zoom fills in 0.3 to 0.5 s either way, frame gaps unchanged). It is now the last line of `renderChunk`.
- **Why the tests missed it, and what catches it now.** Every Playwright engine on Linux and Chromium on the Mac draws these canvases in order. `tools/ui/webkit-mac/check.sh` runs the real thing N times and fails if any run differs from the others by a patch (32 px cells, 30% changed). It needs a built `app/dist` (build it on the box and pass `--dist`). Run it before a playtest release and after any change to how chunks or soft layers are drawn. A hotfix was warranted: the user saw it in 8.9.4.
- **Harness gotchas.** A near-transparent window still gets its animation frames throttled now and then, so the body waits for real frames before it reads the canvas (otherwise a run shows the previous zoom and looks like a huge diff); the app eases the camera for a moment after loading (Playwright's reduced-motion emulation hides that), so the camera is set until it holds; the port 8765 on this machine belongs to another service, so the script picks a free one.

The same day the user saw stand-in rectangles at T36 in 8.9.4.** A map-render test failure that repeats across two runs is real until proven otherwise; don't ship past it as a flake. Map rendering needs a test in the Mac's engine (Playwright WebKit on the box), not only Chromium.

## 2026-10-18: Decadence on the mean, 50-turn Power window (claude/decadence-mean)
The user answered the runaway question: "a 30 or 50 turn rolling average (so the penalties don't start accruing immediately after conquering) and relative to mean." The lead decadence is measured on is now Power over the **mean** of the living empires (`scoring::decadence_lead_percent`; `decadence-median` is the old measure), Power is a **50-turn** trailing average (`DEFAULT_OVERREACH_WINDOW`, `RULES.overreach.powerWindowTurns`, knob `overreach-window`), and the slope is **45** (was 40). Crown upkeep and the Gold surcharge still use the median (`ahead_of_median`, `lead_percent`): the user said to ask before moving the crown rule, so it did not move. `PlayerPublic.mean_lead_percent` is the new public lead; the UI says "above the average empire" for decadence and "median" for crowns. Numbers: `docs/sim-results/vp-v2/decadence-mean-window50.md`.
- **c 45 keeps a typical leader at today's rate:** top empire's mean rate 14.9% against 13.7%; race metrics unchanged (T100 leader wins 50.4% against 51.3%, lead changes 8.7 against 9.0, game 244 turns against 239). Games that ever pass a 50% rate: 2 of 512, against 6 for the current rule. The highest rate seen was 66% (one game), against 62%: the 87% of the previous study came from an older master, so "well under 87%" is true but is not an effect of this change.
- **A longer window costs about one deserted unit a game (1.7 to 2.7)** and shortens the tail (games past 50% at c 60: 27 with 30 turns, 16 with 50). The lag keeps the rate up after an empire's yields fall. Kept, per the user's instruction to keep 50 unless sims show a clear problem; worth watching.
- **Human-seat benchmark: not clearly worse.** Hard, 256 salts, control reproduced by flags on the same binary: 26.0% against 23.5% on salts 1 to 256 (-2.5 pp), 24.2% against 24.4% on salts 257 to 512, pooled -1.2 pp. Each change alone (window, measure) is within 0.6 pp of the control.
- **Master fails its own attack check.** `war-scenarios.sh --check` gives the Golden Horde attack 1% of 256 runs on master 446ccd4ce (the branch 0%), against 44% on 403a2c96f a day earlier. One of the 55 commits between them broke it; not this change (on the branch decadence does not even run in the scenario, whose stored Power history has 30 entries against a 50-turn window; on master it does).
- **A rebase that stops on a conflict ships a half-applied tree to the box.** A benchmark started in the same shell command as a conflicted `git rebase` built plain master (its result matched the control to the decimal). Never start a cloud job in the same command as a rebase; check `git status` first. Also: `target/cloud/linux-arm64` keeps old example binaries when a later build asks for a different example, so a `hard_vs_human` from an older build can sit next to a fresh `legatum-sim`; rebuild with `LEGATUM_CLOUD_EXAMPLES` naming every example you will run.

## 2026-10-18: Allies fight their wars together; Call to Arms and Hire an army are gone (claude/allies-fight-together)
The user's rule: "allies fight in wars together", nothing more ("Call to arms is too complicated"). An Alliance is one side in war: `share_wars` joins every member to every enemy of any member after anything that starts a war or an Alliance, signing in wartime joins both ways, a declaration drags both sides' allies in, and an Alliance that would pit allies against each other is refused. The rule is in `legatum-rules` section 5; the UI lists the extra wars from `DiplomaticAction.new_wars`.
- **Peace needs every team on both sides (the one real design choice).** Either-ally-signs would let an enemy peel the weakest or bribable ally, would let an AI ally end a human's war, and would let Byzantium's tribute end a whole coalition for the price of its cheapest member. So `PeaceProposal.consents` collects agreement from `side(from) + side(to)`; one refusal ends the offer; leaving an Alliance keeps the wars and shrinks the required set to the pair, so "to make peace alone, leave first" costs only the existing leaving rules. Tribute therefore buys peace from the payee's whole side (priced on all its armies) and is refused while Byzantium's own allies fight that side.
- **Two Peace offers between the same two sides in one boundary** (the enemy offering to each of two allies at once) both passed the planning-time check and both were created; the AI then answered both and failed validation ("One diplomatic order per other team per turn"). `era_research` caught it. `same_peace` now also guards creation at the boundary.
- **A war the AI can neither win nor reach is a pure cost.** The first Alliance valuation let an AI sign into a hopeless war on a leader because the +25 coalition and +40 leader terms outweighed its small penalty; `join_war_value` now applies -80 when the combined side is under 80% of the enemy's side or no army can reach it (unless the enemy is counting down to victory).
- **Old saves.** `.legatum` saves hold retired `CallToArms` entries in three lists (offers, cooldowns, replies: replies keep 128 AI explanations, so most mid-game saves have some) and recorded `CallToArms` orders in the archive tape. `diplomacy::skip_retired` drops the entries when the lists load and the archive parser forgets the orders (the archive is incompatible with the new rules anyway and is dropped), so no playtest save is refused. Scenario files embed a hashed state, so five were re-encoded with `rehash_scenarios`.
- **Measured (Hard, human-seat benchmark, 256 salts per map, `LEGATUM_HVH_RESEED_DICE=1`, all seats, same maps, control = master, one spot server each):** 31.5% control against 32.0% candidate on the merged code (an earlier pair read 32.7% and 32.4%): no change. `coalition-against-sparta`: Hard holds 50 of 50. Wars between teams of one Alliance cannot happen, so in games with few Alliances the AI plays as before.
- **War suite moved for two understood reasons, neither a bug in the attacker (64 runs, Hard; candidate: Nicaea 0% before the fix, Golden Horde 10%, hold 85%, thin 95%).** (1) *Nicaea:* the scenario says Byzantium cannot pay the tribute, but that was only true because `plan_hire` set its Gold aside for a hire. With hires retired Byzantium paid 15 of its 17 Gold on turn 19 and the war ended. Fixed in the scenario (start treasury 5 Gold, re-encoded): 8 of 8 won again. (2) *Golden Horde and its hold:* the Swiss now value an Alliance higher (allies join their war) and propose it before Peace, so their Peace offer lands on turn 140 instead of 136, exactly when the Mongol plan has flipped to Peace (`CampaignEnded` on turn 141, mid-siege) and the Mongols accept. That plan flip exists on master too; the EU4-wars work (war follow-through) is the fix, not this branch. Traced with a scratch example that prints each turn's diplomatic orders and the plan of one seat.
## 2026-10-18: the 8.9.4 Mongol attack on Lugano (T120) was a 30% bet on a 72-turn-old picture of the city
Replay of `p894-mongol-attack-t126-1540.legatum` at tag `playtest-8.9.4` (the tape; probes patched into a scratch worktree, nothing committed). Mongols are `Mongols` (P2), the user's Swiss P0, Lugano is city 14.
- **The declaration:** T120 plan Economy (value 0) to `Campaign { against: P0, target: Lugano }`, value 2,546 (terms: capture at 30% +2,623, failed siege -14, era deficit -40, aggressor risk -23). Economy was the only other candidate. The forecast force was 3 Composite Archers and 2 Phalangites, 5 turns, no march, losses 23. No Keshig exists in their army (Phalangite, Composite Archer, Marksman, Trooper only).
- **The input that was wrong:** their Lugano was last seen at T48 (pop 3, no Walls, strength 15). At T120 it was pop 7 with Walls and an Embassy (strength 39, the city fired for 59 to 107 a shot), and empty of garrison. The Mongol Scout had sat four hexes away since T112. Patching the true numbers into the forecast at T120 offers no campaign on Lugano at all; at T125, on what they now saw, Thun and Biel price 0% and 3% (values -369 and -89). So a bet at 30% was really about 0 to 5%. The same 30% bet on the same city was made at T45 and dropped in two turns.
- **How it died:** the five arrived one by one (two Archers at range 2 on T121, Phalangites a turn later). The city struck first (76 on an Archer), then killed it (100), a second Archer (107) and with his Marksman (44, 55) a Phalangite; his Horseman took a 72-point chunk off a third. The Mongols dealt 15 damage to a Horseman and none to the city. The plan went to Peace on T122 (the first sight of Walls) but the units stayed inside the city's range and kept dying. They lost 3 of 11 military units (7 left on T126), not the whole army. He bought nothing; his 3 units were 3 to 5 hexes away until T121.
- **Not the cause:** he was at 0 VP, no Contain, no coalition, so `runaway_read`, `coalition_front` and `campaign_commit` were not involved (`campaign_commit` only charges leaving a war on an open, unwalled target). Sparta had been at war with him since T104 and allied with the Mongols on T122, but its army was on Byzantium.
- **A model bug found on the way (idea #24d):** under VP v2 `WinFacts::of` fills population and territory only from the crowns in play, so both are 0 for all eight teams. `price_campaign` adds the captured population and territory to the capturer only, so its share of both becomes 100% and the win chance goes from 11.75% to 99.2%. Cities and economy alone give 2,350 (term +352 at 30%), not 9,920 (+2,623). Every Campaign has been priced as a near-win since v2; the genes that depend on capture value were tuned under that. Needs a measured fix, not a quiet one.
- **Scenario Lab cut for the Mongol side** (not run; the box queue was deep): `tools/scenarios/extract-state.sh ~/LegatumRuns/bug-saves/p894-mongol-attack-t126-1540.legatum --out ~/LegatumRuns/p894-states --turns 120`, then `tools/scenarios/cut.sh --state ~/LegatumRuns/p894-states/T120.json --seat Mongols --id mongol-lugano-t120 --title "Lugano" --question "Can you take a Swiss town the AI could not?" --blurb "Turn 120. You have eleven units and three cities. Lugano looks soft from a long way off." --briefing "Turn 120. You are the Mongols with three cities and eleven units. Switzerland's Lugano lies south of your border. You last saw it long ago. Scout it first, then take it before turn 140 if you can." --objective take:Lugano --turns 20`. The question it answers: whether a person who scouts first finds a way in (the AI's own forecast says no), so whether a fresh sighting is the whole fix.

## 2026-10-18: box fixes: dead-holder locks, forward-only script sync, 12 slots, disk budget, nextest lanes, idle filler (runbook: docs/ops/cloud-box.md)
(1) **Every `mkdir` lock wait must check that the holder is alive.** `tools/astra/lockdir.sh` stores `pid + start time` (a recycled pid is not the holder) and takes a dead holder's lock over by renaming it aside, so only one waiter wins; `verify-branch.sh`'s branch lock and gate-cache step locks use it. (2) **`box_sync` is forward only:** `/data/bin/VERSION` is the last commit that touched `tools/cloud/box`; a caller syncs only if its box scripts are clean, on master and a descendant of the box's version (`LEGATUM_BOX_SYNC_FORCE=1` to test unmerged ones). (3) **Debug-profile builds were the disk:** 57 GB of `target/debug` in one slot (18 GB incremental, 26 GB debuginfo). Jobs now set `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` and `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=line-tables-only`, each slot is trimmed to 34 GB after its job, and a job waits for 40 GB free instead of dying with ENOSPC. (4) **Slots follow the disk and the cores:** 600 GB and 12 slots; cargo jobs and test threads are scaled to the cores per held slot. Old-code waiters only try s1..s5 and strict FIFO then blocks everyone behind them, so new-style waiters may take s6+ while an old head blocks. (5) **Lane `cargo test` ran at 130% CPU for an hour** (one test binary after another, mostly single-threaded sims); the box's cargo shim rewrites simple `cargo test` to `cargo nextest run` plus doctests. (6) **The box role cannot delete from S3** (IAM allows get/put only), so the filler queue marks work finished with `results/<id>/DONE` and never removes objects; a first version that deleted the claimed spec re-ran the same batch in a loop. (8) **Twelve slots without a per-job core budget ran the box at load 365 on 64 cores:** every job sized itself for the whole machine (verify lanes, 21 Playwright workers, 32 WebKit workers, wasm-opt and rayon on every core). `budget.sh` now gives each job about 1.25 x cores / jobs (6 at full occupancy) and the job-side scripts apply it themselves, so queued jobs from older box-job versions are covered; only jobs already running at deploy time keep the old sizes. (7) **Low-priority filler work must not look like load:** `verify-branch.sh` sizes Playwright workers from the load average, so it subtracts the filler's cores (`/run/legatum-filler-cores`).

## 2026-10-18: the box queue jams on slots and disk, not CPU
- **For hours, the box ran at load 4 to 7 of 64 cores while 28 jobs queued.** Two of its five slots sat in `verify-branch.sh` lock-wait loops on stale `vrbranch` and gate-cache locks left by killed jobs; that wait never checks whether the holder is alive. The other three held long single-threaded debug test runs. `/data` was at 97%, so `gc` could only free idle slots (to 90%). Each slot holds about 70 GB of cargo targets, so disk, not CPU, caps the slot count. Fixes: locks cleared; dead-holder lock checks, forward-only `box_sync` and disk sweeps (agent `box-ops`); the volume going to 600 GB and 10 slots (user approved). Check `box.sh status` load against queue length when gates stall: low load with a long queue means locks or slots, not compute.

## 2026-10-18: the budget runs out mid-month once the box is busy
- **By the 18th, October's AWS spend was most of the way to the monthly cap.** The cloud box alone is a steady daily cost once agents keep it busy, before any optimization fleets. At that rate the cap runs out about a week before the month does, and the 100% launch ban would stop the box and spot runs late in the month. The user raised the cap (`infra/cloud-sims` `monthly_budget_usd`; `run-batch.sh`'s printed cap was stale and is fixed). Check month-to-date spend before launching any multi-dollar run, and raise the cap with the user before it bites.

(1) **Celts's war paths are all hard-off.** `plan.rs::campaign_candidates` returns nothing for Celts, and `contain_war`, `military.rs::open_front` and the staging path return early for it, yet the plan layer still values a Contain war on the leader ("leader's race", "joint strike").
