## 2026-10-18: agents fall back to local CPU when the box queue is long
- **While the box queue was jammed, agents started running heavy work on the Mac: a release `combat_lab` sweep at about 1100% CPU, debug `legatum_sim` tests in a pool worktree, and a local Docker build the lead had OKed.** The user's Mac sat at 100%. Briefs now state the user's offload rule (anything holding the CPU high for more than about a minute goes to AWS). The real fix is the box's 12 slots and lock repair, which keep the queue short enough that nobody is tempted. When the Mac is busy, run `ps -Ao pid,%cpu,etime,comm -r | head` and `lsof -a -p PID -d cwd` to find the worktree and agent, then message that agent; don't kill its processes.

## 2026-10-18: the first milestone climb, and why a held-out gain can still lose games
- **A short-horizon milestone objective buys cities with Settlers, and full games punish it.** CMA-ES on his Sparta 4-player map (T40) and Swiss map (T100) gained +0.046 and +0.035 of milestone score on held-out salts and +0.11 on a held-out Sparta map. All of it came from Settlers (pipeline 3, more sites): Sparta still eliminated nobody by T40 against his three. The same Sparta genes in his seat on the eight benchmark maps lost 4.9 pp of wins [−9.2, −0.4], and in the rivals' seats made them weaker (+4.5 pp). Put a full-game term (the benchmark maps cut at T100/T150) into the fitness, and never ship on milestone score alone.
- **Decision genes are global, so a faction climb leaks into every faction.** The gate applied the Sparta-tuned decisions to Switzerland, Japan, the Celts, the Mongols and Germany. Per-faction decision overrides in `AiParams` are a precondition for per-faction tuning.
- **Both climbs plateaued within five generations**, and then the step size grew instead of shrinking. Most of the 34 genes are inert for these targets. More generations won't help; new knobs will (`opening_troops`, priced target costs, a treasury buffer).
- **Simulation cost is not the bottleneck.** About 2,700 games (Sparta T40 and Swiss T100) take ~15 s on one 64-core spot server, and the server's launch minute costs more than the games. The account's spot quota (448 vCPUs, shared) blocked a third of the generations. The driver retries every 2 minutes, and `run-batch.sh` can die with exit 254 on a transient AWS CLI error, so the driver retries that too. Both climbs, the baseline and the checks came to 50 batches and about $0.43 of server time.
- **Gotchas:** `run-batch.sh` prints the hourly price before the cost ("about $0.658/h each"), so parse "server-hours used: … (about $X". `build-linux.sh` uses its own checkout's root, so run the worktree's copy. Never wrap a box job in a short `timeout`: two of mine were killed while still waiting in the slot queue. The suite's maps come from his seeds on today's code, so terrain changes since his release can move a site. That makes a milestone "his number on a near-identical map", not a replay.
## 2026-10-18: scenario files written by agents skipped the copy lint
- **The 8.9.5 candidate failed `app-vitest` on one test: two coach-cut Scenario Lab files said "victory points", "armed unit" and "at war".** The copy lint (`textArtifacts.test.tsx`) checks every scenario card and briefing, but the coach committed the JSON without running it. Fixed on master (931d4271d), and the coach definition now runs the lint before committing scenario text. Use perl, not BSD sed, for `\b` word-boundary replacements on the Mac: sed silently matched nothing.

## 2026-10-18: aircraft reused ground rules they shouldn't
- **In 8.9.5 a Bomber's strike failed with "No line of sight: the Forest... blocks the shot".** The air strike path reused the ground ranged-attack validation, including line of sight. A bought Bomber also never entered the Return queue, because the air UI queued aircraft only when a target was already in reach at war. And strikes on the declaration turn were refused, though ground attacks resolve that turn. When adding a unit class, list which ground rules it should NOT inherit (line of sight, terrain, zone of control), and give it its own idle state in the turn queue. Fixed: `los::ranged_clear` is true for class Air (one change covers resolution, the forecast and the AI), air orders validate against the same surprise-war projection as ranged orders (`rules::validate_orders`), and every aircraft with no order, hold or sleep is an `air` decision in the Return queue and dock count, target or not (Strike, Hold or Stay on guard, Rebase, Sleep; a team at Peace shows as "declare War and strike"). Two traps on the way: the Fighter's old rule that it "needs no orders" had to go from the Fighter cards, Guide and build text, or the queue contradicted them; and `warQueuedNow` moved from `commands.ts` to `diplomacy.ts` so `airWing.ts` could use it without importing the store.
- **In 8.9.5 a Bomber's strike failed with "No line of sight: the Forest... blocks the shot".** The air strike path reused the ground ranged-attack validation, including line of sight. A bought Bomber also never entered the Return queue, because the air UI queued aircraft only when a target was already in reach at war. And strikes on the declaration turn were refused, though ground attacks resolve that turn. When adding a unit class, list which ground rules it should NOT inherit (line of sight, terrain, zone of control), and give it its own idle state in the turn queue. Fixes are in progress (agent `bomber-fixes`).

## 2026-10-18: a sub-agent waiting on its own background verify looks "completed"
- **The Military Engineer agent ended its turn with "Verify round 7 is running. Waiting for the result."** Its task showed as completed, but the harness resumes it when its own background job reports ("waiting on its own background work"). Don't resend the task or start a duplicate. Check the box job is still running (`box.sh status`), then wait for the agent's handback.

## 2026-10-18: eight long-running agents burn extra credits fast while doing nothing
- **Extra usage nearly doubled in about 50 minutes** with 8 Claude agents alive (one Opus, seven Sonnet, contexts of 200–300k tokens each), most of them only waiting on box verifies and landing. Every tool round re-reads the whole context, so a long-lived agent that polls or waits in many short rounds costs money while doing nothing. On extra credits, count the agents' context sizes, not just the number of agents. Stop at a clean point (WIP committed, handoff note in `docs/status/next-tasks.md`) well before the cap rather than letting agents die mid-task.

## 2026-10-18: `git commit -a` in the main checkout swept another agent's work onto master
- **A one-line learnings commit by the lead (18845fff4) used `commit -am`** while an agent's unfinished AI edits (stale intel, idea 24) sat uncommitted in the main checkout. Master then failed fmt and two `plan_tests`, and every other branch's verify failed on it. Restored in 2d5cff754; the work lives on `claude/ai-stale-intel`, which must `git revert 2d5cff754` after merging master.
- **Rules:** in the main checkout, commit with explicit paths (`git commit -m … -- path`), never `-a` or `add -A`. Agents never edit files in the main checkout; they use their own worktree. Run `git status --short` before any commit there.

## 2026-10-18: two branches that both edit a shared rule helper need landing in a known order
- **The bomber fixes made `los::ranged_clear(kind, …)` return true for aircraft, while the Military Engineer branch (landed first) deleted `ranged_clear` and the `kind` argument everywhere**, because retiring siege units left no indirect-fire class. The bomber branch's full verify passed against the old master and was worthless after the Engineer landed: a clean textual merge would have silently brought back "No line of sight" for aircraft. When two open branches touch the same helper, land the smaller one first, or tell the second one what the first changes.

## 2026-10-19: a quick type check in a worktree without node_modules
- **A box verify's local log folder can hold the previous run's logs** (`.progress/verify-remote/<branch>/steps/` still showed yesterday's PASS files while the summary said `app-tsc FAIL`), so read the summary, not the folder. To find a TypeScript error in seconds: symlink the main checkout's `app/node_modules` into the worktree's `app/`, run `./node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit -p .` with Node 22, ignore the `wasm-pkg` "Cannot find module" lines (the WASM package isn't built there), then remove the symlink. Here it was an unused import left by a merge.

## 2026-10-19: the release check's Chromium step flakes under box load
- **8.9.6's release check failed `playwright` on six celebration and art-map tests** (45 s timeouts, a null bounding box, a truncated trace zip) while the scenario re-recording and the WebKit container ran beside it on a busy box. The same code had passed every Playwright test in a branch verify an hour earlier, and a rerun of just those two specs passed 23 of 23 in 89 s. Rerun the failed specs alone before treating a release-check Playwright failure as real. The Mac WebKit map check prints `stats().renders` exceptions from its probe but compares images correctly (0 differing over 3 runs).

## 2026-10-20: the race panel footer must not wrap (allies branch, `race-panel-late` at 1000x600)
- **A footer that fits in one state and wraps in another moves the rows.** In the turn-203 save the allies branch shows a pending Peace offer, so the footer read "Diplomacy inbox (1)". "4 more" fit beside it and "All objectives" on one line, but "Back to top" is about 25 px wider, so the footer wrapped to two lines (29 px to 62 px) and the rows box shrank from 169 to 137 px: the last row (Byzantium) sat half under the panel's edge only after paging. Nothing in the race panel's code had changed; the fixture's game state had. Fix: the footer is one line in every state (`nowrap`, 6 px gap, no extra link margin in `vpHoldings.css`). Lesson: when a layout test starts failing after a rules change, dump the heights of the neighbours of the thing that moved (`vp-foot` here), not the thing itself, and check which pending-decision counts the new rules put in labels.
## 2026-10-20: "founding a city evicted the enemy and moved my unit" was three explicit orders, not a rule (8.9.6, T155)
- **Replaying T155 of `p896-mongol-found-evict-t155-2027.legatum` at `playtest-8.9.6` moved exactly four units next to Zürich, and each had a recorded `MoveUnit` order:** the user's escort 80 (`MoveUnit` to the adjacent hex (5,25), a single-hex click, so no path), and three Athenian units 249, 176 and 253 that Athens's own AI ordered away (a Phalangite stepping out of the ring, a Marksman marching on, a 55 HP Phalangite retreating to heal). `found_city` (phase 5) runs after movement and touches only tile ownership; nothing in core displaces a unit when a city appears, at War or Peace. The enemy "left" because movement resolves before founding, so the player first sees them gone and the city standing. `rules/cities_tests.rs::founding_a_city_at_war_moves_no_unit` pins it.
- **Where the user's own move came from is not in the save:** every `MoveUnit` comes from a click through `commands.ts::moveSelected`; after Found City the panel advances the selection to the next unit that needs orders, which is the escort on the same tile, so a click on the map right afterwards moves it. Not changed without a repro.

## 2026-10-20: the combined city-attack forecast is its own call, not a field on `BattleForecast`
- **`mock/forecast.ts` mirrors core's `BattleForecast` field for field, and `cityStrike.wasm.test.ts`, `uniquePieces.wasm.test.ts` and others assert `mock.forecast(...)` equals `core.forecast(...)`.** A new field on the struct would have meant mirroring the stage-pooled city/garrison maths in the mock to keep parity. The whole-fight numbers (`CityAssaultForecast`, other queued attacks pooled) are therefore a separate call (`forecast_city_assault`, `client.assault`), optional on `GameClient`: the mock returns nothing and the card falls back to the single-attack lines. The old TS overflow estimate in `forecastModel` (pooled hits at a 100% roll) was deleted: rule logic belongs in core.
- **The forecast's capture chance includes the city's own shot:** the Walls fire before movement, wound a melee attacker and weaken its charge, so the assault loops over the 41 shot rolls too (a ranged strike is planned from the same snapshot, so its strength is untouched). Checked against resolution by averaging 300 seeds (`forecast::tests::the_assault_pools_every_queued_attack_and_matches_resolution`).
## 2026-10-20: a scenario cut on master during a branch's verify needs a re-encode when the branch lands
- **The two lab scenarios added to master after the allies branch's last verify (`japan-big-empire`, `swiss-walls-athens`) failed `scenarios.wasm.test.ts` with a state-hash mismatch** once the branch landed: the branch changes the serialized state (retired Call to Arms lists, new war fields), and scenario files embed the state hash. A merge of master that only adds docs and scenario files can still break after landing; before a fast-forward, list files under `scenarios/` that arrived with the last master merge and run `scenarios.wasm.test.ts` on the merge. Fix: `cargo run --release -p legatum-sim --example rehash_scenarios -- <files>` on the box and commit the two files (baselines were not re-recorded).

## 2026-10-20: Athens ruins are additive (20 Science on top of the normal reward)
- **Rule change (`astra/athens-ruins-bonus`):** Athens no longer replaces every ruin reward with 30 Science; it takes the normal roll (Gold, Science, +1 population or a free Scout) and adds 20 Science on every ruin. Core (`units.rs::explore_ruin`), the TS mock, the celebration card, Guide, faction sheet and `game-rules.md` agree.
- **Human-seat benchmark** (Hard, all seats, `LEGATUM_HVH_RESEED_DICE=1`): candidate 43.8% (95% 39.8 to 47.9, 64 salts per map) against control 39.0% (37.3 to 41.0, 256 salts per map, master at the same commit minus this change): no regression. Only an Athens seat ever reads a ruin differently, so the benchmark mostly samples noise; the war suite starts at war and does not reach it.
- **Type gotcha:** wrapping a `switch` in an IIFE widened the literal fields (`tier: string`, `counter: string`), and the fix is annotating the constant with the function's own return type.
- **A full-game test that passed by seed luck.** `legatum-ai::full_game scripted_orders_all_validate_and_succeed_in_a_real_game` listed no failures on the allies branch, but once an unrelated rule (Athens ruins) changed the game's trajectory it reported a dozen "Proposal withdrawn or rejected at this boundary" and "Proposal already closed" failures: with Peace needing every team on both sides, an ally's acceptance finds the offer already ended by another member's refusal in the same boundary. It is simultaneous contention, so the test now filters both messages like its other contention reasons.

## 2026-10-20: the slot-queue test failed only on the box
- **`tools/cloud/box/tests/test-slot-queue.sh` exited 1 on the 64-core box (and passed on the Mac) because it waited for two ticket files.** The second waiter deletes a killed waiter's ticket as soon as it runs, which on a fast machine happens before the 50 ms poll sees two files, so the count never reached 2. The test now waits for `next-ticket` to reach 3. The same slow-versus-fast timing difference makes a count-based wait unreliable whenever the code under test cleans up after itself; poll a monotonic counter instead.
## 2026-10-20: a siege prop behind a miniature disappears, and a stacked pair fills its whole hex
- **The Engineer's cover prop spilled half a hex because the gear was placed beside the covered unit, and the unit is already pushed to the hex's right by the stack spread** (`render.ts`: a stack of two spreads +-1.16 token half-widths; the hex is only about 4.4 half-widths wide). Drawing the prop behind the figure fixed the spill but made it vanish: every miniature stands in a soft vellum pool (`MINI.halo`) that washes out anything behind it. It now stands in front of the pair's feet, right-aligned inside the hex (`siegeProps.ts::siegePropRect`, a unit test over seven zoom levels).
- **The same release had a cause that looked like a rule:** see the 8.9.6 T155 entry above. A second card-fitting lesson: at 1000x600 the whole-fight forecast card needed four rounds of shedding lines before the walled, garrisoned, two-attacker case fit; the test that matters is the worst case, not the screen-tour's single-attack one.
- **A "Coming soon" slot outlived its feature twice:** the Scenario Lab's "Waiting for the Military Engineer" card stayed after the Engineer and then the real scenario (`swiss-walls-athens`, "The Walls of Athens") shipped. The slot is deleted; `SOON_SLOTS` is empty and the Lab e2e asserts the real card.
## 2026-10-20: allied cities are transit-only (`astra/allied-city-transit`, landed)
- **Rule:** a unit may pass through an allied city but cannot end its move there (core `movement.rs`/`units.rs`, the TS mock, path preview and reach map, every AI proxy, `game-rules.md` section 1 and 5). Measured on current master plus allied wars: human-seat benchmark (Hard, all seats, 256 salts per map, `LEGATUM_HVH_RESEED_DICE=1`) 42.1% (95% 40.5 to 43.8) against a control of 39.0% (37.3 to 41.0) taken before the allies branch landed, so the difference mixes both rules and shows no loss; war suite check `war-golden-horde-thin` 95% of 64 (floor 30%).

## 2026-10-20: strategic supply replaces the stockpile (increment 1, `claude/strategic-supply`)
- **The AI barely builds the buildings supply gates.** `crates/sim/examples/supply_census.rs` (64 eight-player Standard Hard games): Hard empires have a median 4-5 cities and 0.3 Forges at T150 (0.7 at T250, Factories 0.3, Pikemen about 1). So a deposit's economic value to the AI is small in this increment, and resource wars will matter more once Hard grows wider or the Copper/Silver lines land.
- **Deposits already change hands in AI wars.** 7.3 strategic deposits per game passed with a captured city before the AI valued them, and 8.4 with the valuation (62 of 64 games had at least one; same seeds, though the second run also carried a master merge). Shortages (an idle user) hit 53 empires in 36 of 64 games.
- **The map is already scarce enough:** 11 Iron and 10 Coal deposits per 48x32 map, 1.4 Iron and 1.25 Coal nearest each start. Empires of 8-12 cities hold a median 4-6 Iron supply, so 2-3 of 66-80 such empires could put a Forge in every city. Placement needed no change.
- **Old archives keep the stockpile in their provenance hash.** Tests that check an archived state against its recorded FNV hash (`threat_response`, `attack_opportunism`, `ranged_garrison`) failed once the stockpile fields were dropped. The fix is to splice the old field back into the hashed JSON (`with_stockpile`), not to change the recorded hash, so the fixture still proves it is the user's game. Scenario states are different: they carry their own hash, and `rehash_scenarios` re-encodes them (19 of 26 changed).
- **Builds in progress have to hold supply.** Without that, two cities could each start a Forge with one spare Iron, and one would finish idle. Validation runs city orders in list order, so the second start is refused with its reason. The AI's `supply::within_budget` mirrors this so its orders never fail.
- **Byzantium's levy cap (2) blocks a third Pikeman.** Supply tests that need several Pikemen use another faction.
## 2026-10-20: the era curve lands on the Engineer and then accelerates (user 2026-10-19)
- **Merge with the Military Engineer (landed first, c86feb376).** The Engineer retired the siege units and added `city_defense_covered` and `city_defense_vs`; the era branch had `era` in every city line. The merge keeps both: the cover argument and the `era` argument, and `siege_city_bonus` has the Bomber only (20 on the curve). The stale-intel gene had been taken off master (`2d5cff754`), so the branch's copy of it was dropped, not merged. Fixtures a branch regenerates (`city-strike-awareness.json` is handwritten) must be taken from master before `repin.sh`: the branch's copy still had the retired `CallToArms` order and `rehash_fixtures` died on it.
- **Human-seat benchmark** (Hard, all seats, 256 salts per map, `LEGATUM_HVH_RESEED_DICE=1`; a rule change cannot be split into seat-only and opponents-only arms, `docs/sim-results/hard-vs-human/README.md`): master before the merge 39.0% against the merge 35.4% (-3.6 pp, 95% CI -6.2 to -1.0); master after the merge, allies and genes 33.9% against the accelerating curve 31.3% (-2.6 pp, CI -4.8 to -0.4). The Mongols seat read 21.9%, 1.2% and then 0.0% on the three master states, so its collapse came with the Engineer and allies work before the curve changed anything.
- **The curve now accelerates** (`data::ERA_STEP_PERCENT` 25, 30, 35, 40, 45, 50): melee 20, 25, 32, 44, 61, 89, 134. The exact products are 32.5 and 43.9 for the third and fourth: the table rounds a .5 down, because the user's list says 32. Era duels: one era ahead the melee line wins in three attacks with 51 to 56 HP left until the Renaissance and in two with 81 to 90 from then on; two eras ahead is one blow from the Modern era. War suite check (`war-golden-horde-thin`, 128 runs): 67% against 100% on the 30% curve (floor 30%), so the early Mongol attack is worse on a curve whose first steps are smaller. Combat lab `check` was already red on master for the Engineer (Walls halve damage to city HP: Sparta's walled melee cells fell from 100% to 8%); the fixed baseline was rewritten on the Engineer rules and this curve, and the Ancient cells did not move because of the curve.
- **A latent AI bug the new curve exposed.** `legatum-ai::era_research` failed at turn 115: the scripted AI sent a unit to end its move in an allied city, which the engine refuses ("Allied units can pass through Thessalonica but can't stop there"). Only the V7 proxy knew the rule. `ScriptedBrain::orders` now drops a `MoveUnit` that ends in an ally's city.
- **Display bug the 30% curve hid.** The Keshig's bow is a design number (18) scaled by its Classical era: 17 on the new curve, 18 on the old one (26/26). Text built from `RULES.keshig.ranged` showed 18; the faction sheet, the Guide and the tests now read the scaled value from `unitStatsFor`.
- **Box habits that cost time.** `cargo test` on the box is nextest in the debug profile, and `runner::tests::opening_league_twenty_standard_games...` takes 18+ minutes there: use `cargo nextest run --workspace --cargo-profile release-checked` (4 minutes). Two AWS spot batches launched in the same minute made the second fail with exit 254; launch one at a time. `verify-remote.sh` run from a worktree writes its logs into that worktree's `.progress/`, and a branch whose `chart.png` or `ledger.jsonl` diverges from master fails the verify merge: take master's chart.

## 2026-10-20: the Mongols raise Walls and train Engineers from the Medieval era (user decision 2026-10-19)
- **How it is built.** Walls are an Ancient technology (Masonry), so the gate is the player's era, not a technology: `data::available_to_player(faction, engineers_absorbed, era, item)` and `faction_excluded_building_at(.., era)` (era 2, `MONGOL_WALLS_ERA`). `available_to_faction` now says yes to the Mongols for the whole line and the Engineer, so a research card lists Walls for them only once their era allows it (the Masonry card, researched in the Ancient era, no longer lists it for them, but `tech_has_benefit` is true for every faction and technology now: Masonry stopped being a stepping stone). The TS mock mirrors it with an `era` argument that defaults to the Medieval era, so a caller that forgets it sees the late-game rule.
- **AI.** The capture bonus for a Workshop city (`mongol_engineer_capture_bonus`, +500) now applies only before the Medieval era, because after it the Engineers can be trained anyway. The Mongol build lists need no code change: the AI builds from the city's option list, which core now fills with the Walls line in the Medieval era.
## 2026-10-20: the Scenario Lab has no "win the game" objective
- **To let the user test "can a human Japan win from T100", the closest objective is `score` over a long window** (`--objective score --turns 150`); `core::scenario::Condition` only has holding cities, city and unit counts, owning a city and a rival's city cap. A normal VP victory still ends the game, so a win shows, but the result card scores VP, not the win. A `WinGame` condition would be the clean fix if more "can you win from here" scenarios come up. The state for an 8.9.6 save played on today's engine came from `extract-state.sh` (replayed on the save's own engine) and `cut.sh --state`, so the scenario runs under the new rules.

## 2026-10-20: armour rules open ground, Tanks get 5 moves (user decision 2026-10-19)
- **Rule as built.** From the Modern era (`combat::ARMOUR_ERA_TIER` 5) the melee line's +18 against mounted applies only while the melee unit stands in a city or on Forest or Hills, attacking or defending; its own tile counts. The era is the melee unit's (Infantry, Humanoid Robot), not the mounted unit's: a Rifleman still has the bonus everywhere. The decision named Jungle, which the game does not have. A meeting engagement has no cover. Where the bonus is lost the breakdown carries a line of 0 ("Open ground: no bonus against armour", added to `Breakdown.parts` directly because `add` drops zeros), so the forecast does not hide it. The unit era chip on the map counts the class bonus but not terrain, so it does not see the rule.
- **Era duels in the open (mean rolls):** Tank against Infantry in the same era now wins in 3 attacks with 51 HP left, and an Infantry attacking a Tank dies after dealing back 29 HP; before, the Tank died. Tank has 5 moves (`data::TANK_MOVES`); the Autonomous Armour keeps 4, which is a step down from its predecessor and worth the user's eye.
- **Benchmark.** Human-seat benchmark (Hard, all seats, 256 salts, reseeded dice): 31.3% on the accelerating curve against 32.0% with this rule (+0.7 pp, CI -0.8 to +2.1): no change.
- **Cover is read, not stored.** `PlayerView::has_cover` (forecast, AI) and `rules::battle::unit_has_cover` (resolution) both read terrain and cities; the AI's `fight_bonuses` takes the view and both positions.

## 2026-10-20: air range, Bomber 8 and Fighter 5 (user decision 2026-10-19)
- `data::BOMBER_RANGE` 8 (was 5) and `FIGHTER_STRIKE_RANGE` 5 (was 3); the Fighter's guard radius stays 4, so it now strikes farther than it guards. Card text, ring labels, the Air wing headline and the Guide read the rules export, so only tests held the old numbers. The AI's "are Bombers likely near this city" reach is `BOMBER_RANGE + FIGHTER_RANGE`, now 12 hexes, so a Flight-era rival 11 away now keeps a Fighter at home on guard.
- A Bomber now sees every enemy city within 8 tiles as a target: tests that "removed the enemy" to test the no-target state must remove cities too (`AirWing.test.tsx::quiet`).
## 2026-10-20: the open-ground rule is gated by the armour, not the melee unit (coordinator correction)
- The first build judged the rule by the melee unit's era, so a Rifleman kept the +18 against a Tank in the open, the case the user complained about. It is now the mounted unit's era (`unit_era_tier(other) >= ARMOUR_ERA_TIER`): against the Tank and the Autonomous Armour every melee unit needs a city, Forest or Hills; against the Cavalry and older it never does. The Autonomous Armour has 6 moves (the Tank 5), not the Tank's predecessor-beating 4 the first build left it with.
## 2026-10-20: era VP is scored by accident today
- **The AI's pursuit of era objectives has no measurable effect** (2 × 256 Hard games, 8 empires, same seeds; `docs/design/research/era-objectives-tradeoffs-2026-10-20.md`): with objective-seeking turned down as far as the knobs go, empires scored 5,670 objectives against 5,673, and each objective's met-share was within a few points whether it was drawn, not drawn or not pursued. 26 of 50 pool objectives are met by more than 15% of empires that never aimed at them; Fight objectives are 72% of all era scores ("destroy N units" alone 28%); 33% of era scores land on a turn when the same empire scores another era; seven objectives are dead (4% or less). This matches the user's complaint that he could "get all the points by accident". There is no knob that fully turns objective-seeking off (`objective_bonus_percent=0` still leaks through `weight.max(1)` in `objective_capture_value`); a real `objective_seek` gene is part of the redesign.

## 2026-10-20: five idle orders became two (Sleep and Skip)

- `HoldUnit`, `HealUnit` and `WakeUnit` are gone from core, AI, mock and app; `SleepUnit` is the one standing order for every unit and `SkipUnit` the one-turn order. Resting (heal, +3 fortify a turn) never needed an order: `heal_and_fortify` already rested every unit that had not acted, so Hold and Heal only differed in what they did to `standing_order` and `needs_orders`. That is why the AI could switch every Hold to Skip with identical play (it re-issues orders every turn and ignores `standing_order`), and why a Sleep issued by an AI unit would only have cost time: `sleeping_threats` builds a visibility map per team with sleepers every movement round.
- Old data still loads through serde aliases: orders `HoldUnit` and `HealUnit` read as `SleepUnit`, `WakeUnit` as `SkipUnit`, and the saved standing orders `Fortify` and `Heal` as `Sleep`. `StandingOrder` kept its place in the view as a one-variant enum. A tape that holds an old Hold now replays as Sleep, so its states hash differently and wake events appear (run `tools/sim/repin.sh`).
- Behaviour that changed for players: a Sleeping garrison wakes when a new enemy comes into sight within its movement range (old Fortify never did), and a sleeper stays asleep at full HP (old Heal woke it with a "healed" notice; `UnitHealed` is removed).
- Core never auto-fires a unit: ranged units shoot only on an explicit `RangedAttack`, so "Sleep holds fire" costs nothing. Only the AI converts its stay-put order into a shot (`fire_from_holds`, now over `SkipUnit`).
## 2026-10-21: the unit triangle ends at the Modern era (user decision 2026-10-20)
- **Replaces the open-ground rule** (cities, Forest, Hills) of 2026-10-19: `combat::triangle_applies` is true only when both units are from before the Modern era (`TRIANGLE_END_ERA_TIER` 5), and it gates `class_bonus`, `fight_class_bonus` and `add_fight_class_bonus` (core), `triangleApplies` (TS mock) and the unit card matchup line. The cover plumbing (`own_cover`, `PlayerView::has_cover`, `unit_has_cover`, `OPEN_GROUND_LABEL`, the AI's position arguments) is gone. Tank 5 and Autonomous Armour 6 moves stay.
- **What it does to the numbers.** An Infantry or a Tank gets no +18 from any class, and nobody gets one against them; a Cavalry (Industrial) still gets it against a pre-Modern ranged unit and a Rifleman against a Cavalry. With the era steps already +28 and +45 at the end, the Modern and Future fights are decided by strength, cost and moves alone. The duel table changes only where a Modern or later unit meets a counter-class unit.
- **The era chip on a unit** used the class bonus and now follows the gate through `addFightClassBonus`.

## 2026-10-21: `remote.sh --timeout HOURS` is now `--budget SECONDS`
- **The Codex task `review-verify-remote-timeout-budget` replaced `--timeout HOURS` with `--budget SECONDS`** (one budget covering queueing and running; expiry leaves the job attachable). An old call fails at once with "cloud: unknown option --timeout" and exit 0 from the wrapper's tail, so a backgrounded release check looks finished. The 8.9.7 release check is `remote.sh <sha> --npm --budget 14400 --label release-N -- bash -lc 'tools/release/check.sh ...'`.

## 2026-10-21: the main checkout's node_modules goes stale after a lockfile landing
- **The 8.9.7 Mac build failed on `Cannot find module '@testing-library/user-event'`**: the release recipe symlinks the main checkout's `app/node_modules`, which predates the vitest/vite bump and error-toast branches that changed `package-lock.json`. Fix used: delete the symlink and `npm ci` inside the release worktree (Node 22). After any lockfile landing, either `npm ci` in the main checkout when no agent is running app tests, or let release worktrees install their own.

## 2026-10-21: a spot restart of the cloud box kills every running job
- **The box (c8g.16xlarge, spot) relaunched at 04:42 UTC and every job died with exit 143** ("connection dropped, reconnecting", then "Terminated"), including the 8.9.7 release check 17 minutes in. `box.sh status` shows the relaunch time on its first line. After one, rerun every job that was running and tell the agents not to read those failures as real. Start long jobs (release checks) with `--keep-on-interrupt` so a dropped client doesn't kill them.

## 2026-10-21: 8.9.7 UI review fixes, and what broke around them
- **A tech card listed Walls for the Mongols with a price they could not pay.** Core now sends `TechUnlock.locked_note` ("Walls for the Mongols from the Medieval era") for any listed unlock the faction cannot build yet; the research card, hovercard, unlock moment and tech detail show it in place of the build line and prices. The mock mirrors it (`mock/view.ts` `lockedNote`).
- **The new `a [AEIOU]` copy rule caught a real bug at once**: "Becomes a Irrigated Farm" in the improvement description. Run new copy rules against the rendered corpus before assuming the list is clean.
- **Found City now advances past whatever stands on the new city's tile** instead of clearing the selection for good: the first fix (selection stays empty) broke the touch flow, and selecting a stacked escort made the next click on the city a move.
- **An open Cities list in the This Turn panel needs a minimum height**: at 44px its sticky header covered every city, so a tap on the first row was intercepted (the touch spec).
- **A spec that changes the viewport must restore it**: the Scenario Lab "N more below" check left 1000x600 on, and the Technology-discovered card then covered "See result".
- **A spec that selects a unit right after Continue races the game's own first selection**; wait for the first selection, then choose.
- **Two primaries since the idle-orders merge were Sleep (unit card) plus the dock's call to action**, not End Turn. `ui/turnLead.ts` now decides who leads: a selected unit's card holds the primary and the dock steps back (`.cta.stepped`), unless the call to action is urgent (siege, offer, treasury, broken order) or nothing is pending, when the card's choice steps back. Also fixed: the Sparta tour tests Sleep's tooltip (Heal is gone), the turn-one tour drops the tile-price step (tile buying is gone), and a hovered danger button on the unit card is deeper red for AA contrast.

## 2026-10-21: landing the Codex removal batch (tile buying, Aid Station and Power Plant, Embassy and Academy)
- **Benchmark deltas against the master control (human-seat, Hard, 64 salts per map, reseeded dice, all-seat; rule removals cannot be split seat-only and opponents-only because every seat gets the rule):** tile buying 37.3 to 36.5 (-0.8 pp), Aid Station and Power Plant 39.8 to 39.1 (-0.7 pp), Embassy and Academy 39.1 to 37.9 (-1.2 pp), crown upkeep 39.1 to 36.7 (-2.4 pp). All inside the 95% interval (about +/-3.5 pp). Single maps move up to 14 points on 64 salts, so read only the overall.
- **Luna's removal branches were never run through the app gates.** `remove-dead-rule-switches` left stray `);` lines that broke four test files, `remove-aid-station-power-plant` left an unused import and a Playwright test that used the Factory (which needs a Forge first), and `remove-embassy-academy` left a spec asserting "Needs an Academy first". The fast profile does not run the new specs they add, so run the affected e2e specs by name on the box (`remote.sh . --npm -- bash -c 'scripts/build-wasm.sh; (cd app && npm run build); cargo build --release -p legatum-server; cd app && npx playwright test --project=game <specs>'`).
- **Two retired-building migrations now coexist.** `core::save_migration::drop_retired_buildings` (Aid Station, Power Plant, Embassy, Academy) and the older `wasm::json::migrate_removed_buildings` with its load notice (Embassy, Academy). Fold the second into the first when the next building is retired.
- **`remove-dead-rule-switches` is blocked by the TypeScript mock.** `app/src/mock/setup.ts` sets `legacy_vp: true` so mock and WASM agree on the five legacy titles; with the switch gone the WASM emits VP v2 `TitleChanged` events the mock never does, so about 60 mock/WASM parity tests in 10 files fail. It lands after the mock is retired (or after the parity helpers ignore VP v2 events).
- **`remove-crown-upkeep` stalled a Hard duel through a Celts AI bug that crown upkeep had been hiding (fixed on the branch).** `hold_metrics` seed 6 (Athens against Celts) hit the 1200-turn cap with 5 and 11 VP and 98 broken holds. No crown moved after turn 126: the Celts held all three crowns and the target (10) only with its live goal (Raise the Warbands, 2 VP), and lost the goal every one or two turns, so every hold broke at turn 1. Cause: the Celts AI keeps one camp per city and clears the doubles, but it counted camps by the nearest city while the goal counts the city whose territory holds the camp (`goals::wild_camp_cities`); where they differ it cleared a city's only camp, lost the goal, called the Wild again (11 camps) and repeated (123 completions, 122 losses, 1,475 own camps cleared). Crown upkeep neither caused nor cured it: master with upkeep zeroed (`overreach-gold-x10=0`) has 0 stalls in the same 192 duels, because the removal also drops the AI's crown valuation and the games diverge early; seed 6 is the game where the Celts ends on 9 permanent VP plus the goal against a 10 target. `Planner::territory_city` now reads `CityDetail.territory`; seed 6 ends by a hold win at turn 418, and 192 Hard duels (seeds 1 to 192) have 0 stalls (before the fix on this branch 1; master with upkeep 0). A game stalls whenever one team's target depends on a live goal and the owner's own orders break it: look at who breaks the hold before suspecting a rule. Benchmark (human-seat, Hard, 64 salts per map, reseeded dice, all seats, 2026-10-21): master 37.9% [34.4, 41.4] against this branch with the removal and the fix 36.5% [33.0, 40.0] (-1.4 pp, inside the interval; the removal alone read -2.4 pp earlier on another master); the Celts map moved 6.2% to 9.4%.
- **Spot restarts kill every box job at once (exit 143, "box is stopping").** A verify that died then proves nothing; rerun it.

## 2026-10-22: the AI values supply (`claude/ai-supply-value`)
- **The Forge was not unvalued, it was outbid.** In 64 census games a Forge-ready city offered a Forge scored about 290 and lost to the due-turn ladder (Granary 1,100, Laboratory 1,400, College 800, Market 900) and to Settlers and troops (2,000 and more). A flat `industry_priority` of 300 per Production (a Forge 900, a Factory 1,500) doubles Forges (0.73 to 1.26 at T250) and Factories (0.30 to 0.55); 600 gives 1.55 and 0.64. After that the binding limit is supply: median Iron supplied is 3 and used 1 to 2 with 3 to 4 Forge-ready cities, so the next lever is deposits (Mines, expansion, capture), not valuation.
- **The priority must yield to a stalled-growth city** (`faction_tests::industry_payback_does_not_delay_stalled_growth_at_either_speed`, the Hydroponics test): the same food test gates the boost.
- **A Forge started is often abandoned: 46% of Forge builds at priority 600, 42% at 300 (34% at baseline).** The city's build is replaced by military production (Military Engineers, Drones, Artillery, Soldiers). Progress is banked, so it is not all lost, but the Forge's timing is at the mercy of war production. Not changed.
- **The public economy share can't make a deposit city a target.** `price_campaign` feeds a deposit's Production into `TeamFacts.economy`, worth 1 to 3 points of win chance against capture terms of 1,000 to 5,000. Raising `supply_value_percent` from 0 to 300 left captures with a deposit unchanged (8.4 / 8.2 / 8.0 a game). A dedicated `supply` term (`supply_term` 400, tripled when short) is in, but its effect on deposit captures is also under noise at any weight up to 2,000: about half of all cities hold a deposit, so the base rate is 40 to 50%. Deposit transfers rise (7.4 to 8.3 a game) only through the demand the Forges create (`unmet`). A real test needs the choice among reachable targets, not the capture count.
- **Pikeman against Forge for the same Iron:** a Pikeman's strength premium over a Phalangite (23% for 55 Production) is worth about 0.4 Production per turn over 30 turns; a Forge gives 3. So at peace a contested Iron goes to the Forge and Pikemen fall (0.64 to 0.45 per empire at T150); at war the premium counts 8 times and the Pikeman wins. `supply_war_percent` 0 restores the old behaviour.
- **Gate (1,024 games per arm, reseeded dice):** seat-only -1.9 pp [-4.7, +0.9], opponents-only -0.5 pp [-3.5, +2.5]. The seat leans worse: a Forge is 160 Production for +3, so a human-quality seat does better with the ladder; it is inside noise but not clearly harmless. `industry_priority` 150 is the fallback (Forges 0.34 / 1.06 at T150 / T250 in the 64-game census).
- **Idle buildings no longer count toward "N Forges" objectives** (`Objective::progress`); the AI's `lacking` count is unchanged, so it can still think a city with an idle Forge is done. The unsupplied 25% now applies in `crates/ai/src/{tactics,analysis,dual,threat,opponents}.rs` (`supply::attack_total`, `ranged_total`, `defense_total`, `power`); the frozen proxy brains and the app's `aftermath.ts`, `siege.ts`, `unitEra.ts` don't.
- **A stale background script clobbers edits.** A regex rewrite of three AI files that backtracked catastrophically finished minutes later and overwrote an edit made in between; check `git diff` after a long-running codemod, and parse call arguments instead of using a dotall regex.
- **The box was saturated (12 slots, 11 queued)** during the gate: 256 games took 42 minutes there, 1,024 took about an hour on this 16-core Mac at nice 15, then two hours at load 30. The local `target` in the scratchpad (`CARGO_TARGET_DIR`) built `hard_vs_human` for local arms files.
## 2026-10-21: the game-speed layer is gone, the tables hold the real prices (increment 1)
- **What went.** `GameSpeed`, `GameSetup.speed`, `PlayerView.speed`, `speed.scale_cost(x)` (always `x * 2`), `expected_length`, `science_history_turns`, `RuleExperiments.production_half_speed`/`scale_production`, `GoalSpec.speed_scaled`, the "Game pace" breakdown line, the sim's `RunOpts`/`RunMeta` speed, the app's `scaleCost`/`GameSpeed`/`RULES.speeds` and the TS mock's pace maths. Every old "base tuning" number was doubled in place (units, buildings, techs, improvements, START_GOLD 60, growth 30 + 16/pop, capture resistance 6, XP [16,46,90,150,226], barbarian turns and Gold, Alliance 60/120, wonder rewards, the idle cap 60). New constants: `data::EXPECTED_GAME_TURNS` 300, `FIRST_THIRD_TURNS` 100, `rules::SPARTA_DESERTION_INTERVAL` 5, `economy::ramp_price(turn, listed)`. Old saves, tapes and lobby configs that carry `"speed"` (even `"Quick"`) still load: serde ignores the key, and the archive `Metadata` keeps a write-never `speed` field so `deny_unknown_fields` does not reject old archives.
- **How "the default game did not change" was proved.** The state hash is FNV over the whole JSON, so dropping the `setup.speed` key moves every hash; "pins unchanged" cannot be literal. Instead: master with a hash that strips `"speed":"Standard",` (baseline) against the branch (plus a verification-only patch that restores two roundings, below) over 570 games (8-player Hard, Hard v2 with tuned openings, primitives, Rush, Normal, Easy, mixed brains, 1v1; seeds 92-99xxxxx, full length): every per-turn hash of every game identical. Replaying baseline tapes through the new engine (`--tape-in`) isolates engine from AI: that is how the unscaled consumers below showed up. The harness lives in the session notes of this change; rebuild it with `legatum-sim --tape-out` on both trees and compare `games[].hashes`.
- **Two roundings do move, by design.** Tech diffusion is now `floor(real * (100 - pct) / 100)` (it was floor on the half price, then doubled: up to 1 Science apart) and the Sparta premium is exactly 3/2 of the real price (the Settler was `(3f + 1) / 2` on an odd half price: 113 became 112.5 -> 225 instead of 226). So the repinned tapes differ from master's by the removed key AND these two; the verification patch that re-adds the old rounding gives identical games, which is the proof that nothing else moved.
- **The traps (all caught by the harness, none by the tests).** Code that read an UNSCALED table value and now sees double: tribute prices (`tribute::PERCENT` 10 -> 5 so the price is the same), the AI's keep values / loss ratios / military budgets / engineer cover cost (all in half Production points: `legatum_ai::tuned(cost) = cost / 2`; a future AI pass may rescale them), the strategy classifier's Rush threshold (`early_military_cost >= 60` -> 120), and `o.cost += SETTLER_COST_STEP` in an AI test that added the half-unit step to an already-real option cost (kept as +20). Genes and tuned genomes that went through `scale_cost` were doubled with their bounds (`settle_patience`, `reserve_deficit_turns`, `surplus_upkeep_turns`, `objective_horizon`, `campaign_march_patience`, `troop_runway`, the opening genes `settler_start`/`settler_interval`/`gold_save`/`war_deadline`/`rush_timeout`/`recovery_turns`, the primitive genes `escort_civilians_wait`/`scout_upkeep_until`/`scout_upkeep_intel_age`/`market_first_economy_horizon`; `tools/league/tuned-five-openings.json`, `tuned_openings.rs`, `tools/evolve/genome.py`). Old ledger genomes (`docs/sim-results/ai-quality/...best.json`) are in the old unit: double those genes before reusing them.
- **Ops.** `tools/ops/agent-worktree.sh acquire` fails with "master is already used" when a pool directory it wants does not exist yet (it runs `git worktree add <pool> master`): create the pool with `git worktree add --detach .claude/worktrees/pool-N <branch>` first. `cargo test` on the box is nextest: it takes no `-q`. A `timeout` around `remote.sh` kills the box job.
## 2026-10-21: the Mongol seat's 3% on the 8.5 map is a Sparta rush the default Mongol opening does not answer
- **Not the Engineer.** Reseeded, the seat reads 0 to 5% on every master state since 10-10 (0.4% just before the Engineer landed, 1.2% after). The 16 to 22% ledger rows (`athens-ruins-1020`, `transit-1020`) are shared-dice readings (18% and 21% shared, 3% to 4% reseeded on the same commits), so a single-map shared-dice number is noise of about 20 points.
- **What happens:** Sparta's capital is 2 to 3 hexes from the Mongol capital and it declares on turn 18 to 20 in 128 of 128 games; the seat has 1.5 units at T30 and loses its first city on turn 22 to 26. The same seat played by Germany, Japan, Athens or Sparta wins 28 to 36%; the Mongols on the `Rush` opening win 30% (19 of 64), on Default, GreedyExpand, Snowball or Economic 0 to 5%. Walls and Engineers from the start are worth about 5 points (not significant). The recommendation (AI opening choice, no rule change) and all numbers are in `docs/sim-results/mongols-1021/README.md`.

## 2026-10-22: the opening ramp is gone; the early prices were retuned instead (increment 2)
- **What changed.** `economy::ramp_price` (half the listed price on turn 0, the full price from turn 100) and the "Early-game discount" breakdown line are deleted, with the no-op flags `opening-ramp`/`capital-production` and the `early-military-discount` experiment (its only quote line was the ramp line, so deleting that line broke the `buy_cost_for` debug assertion for the experiment; delete a lever together with its breakdown line). Settler 70, +50 per trained Settler; Granary and Brood Hall 50, Workshop and Market 60, Library 80, Walls 60 (Swiss 80, Athens Library 70), Farm/Pasture/Quarry 30, Mine 40; units, later buildings and techs are untouched. Numbers and method: `docs/sim-results/2026-10-22-opening-ramp-removal.md` (400 games: first Settler T18 and second city T25 before and after; games ~8% shorter; first Soldier T30 to T32).
- **Plain prices were enough, units were not retuned.** Cheaper units (50-70% of listed) did not help the opening and would have made every later war cheaper, because the ramp's discount was temporary and a table price is forever. The price of the ramp's removal is that a Spearman is 80 from turn 1 (it was 40) and the early buildings stay cheap all game (the ramp made them full price from turn 100).
- **AI valuations that read table prices moved with the retune.** `city_keep_value` read the Settler's price as a city's worth; it now has its own constant (`CITY_KEEP_BASE` 75) so a 70-Production Settler does not make cities cheap to lose. Four AI-decision tests picked a Settler where they expected a Soldier because a Settler (70) is now cheaper than a Soldier (80), where the ramp kept it at about twice (78 against 41 on turns 4 and 8); their scenarios now set the Settler's price at the empire's third (170). `macro_economy`'s upgrade test saw the AI buy a 40-Production Mine (80 Gold) instead of upgrading a garrison.
- **A recorded-orders fixture is price-sensitive.** `threat_response.rs` replays a user's orders from a turn-35 save; orders address units by global id, so units that complete 3-4 turns later (full price instead of the ramped one) shift every later id and the recorded conquest never finishes. Both tests are `#[ignore]`d until the orders are re-recorded (`docs/status/next-tasks.md`). A topped-up stored Production does not help.
- **The mock found a latent crash.** The new trajectory put barbarian units (owner 255) in view around turn 31 and `diplomacyAi.team(255)` dereferenced an undefined player; `team()` now returns -1 for them like core's `TeamId::BARBARIAN`.
