A full box is a reason to use AWS spot (`tools/cloud/run-batch.sh`, `hard-vs-human.sh --cloud`), not the Mac. Briefs now repeat: "box full → spot, never local".

## 2026-10-22: a Celtic hunter showed "Seen" because an ally saw it
- **The user's Celtic Archer read "Seen" with no enemy nearby.** Its `revealed_to` held Sparta, his Alliance partner: allies share vision and always see hunters (by design), and the badge counted any team. Being seen by an ally is not exposure; the badge must count only rivals (Codex task `hunter-seen-badge-allies`).
## 2026-10-22: Japan's decadence is not mostly Food, and the vow is what stops Japan closing (Japan's Century review)
- **Food in Power, measured.** In his run Food was 63–65% of Japan's Power, against about 41% for the others, so the hypothesis from the 8.9.6 review (decadence taxes Japan for its Food network) looked strong. Measured as the rule measures it (net yields, lead over the mean), taking Food out drops his static lead from about 110% to about 45%; with the feedback (higher net output raises Power) the rate settles nearer 30–35% than 50%. Throwaway switch `power-no-food` (Power = Production + Gold + Science): Hard Japan from the scenario's T100 wins 41 of 100 instead of 11 (score median 1220 against 1010; decadence at T150 29% against 42%), but in 2,048 balanced 8-empire games Japan goes only from 3.1% to 4.2% while Sparta goes from 10.4% to 17.6%. With no decadence at all balanced Japan wins 9.1%: decadence is about two thirds of Japan's gap to a fair 12.5%, and its Food part is a small slice of that. The VP-scaled variant (`decadence-vp`: half rate unless you lead or tie on VP) makes Japan worse (2 of 100), because a VP-leading Japan pays full while its rivals pay half. Both switches live only on the throwaway worktree `~/LegatumRuns/coach-tools/coach-japan/wt`; the balanced control reproduced the published 8.9.7 run exactly (63 Japanese wins), so they are inert when off.
- **The vow as a closer.** From his T250 (8 VP, the 30 closed turns done, six intruders, four empires at War) Hard in his seat reaches 10 VP the next turn in 100 of 100 runs and loses the hold within 1–2 turns in 100 of 100; Japan wins 14 of 100 eventually, 30 with the bands off. Hard Japan from T100 closes in 88 of 100 runs and holds the vow at T250 in 22. One enemy unit of any empire at War ends the vow, so its counter is a tag, not an occupation.
- **Scenario runs replay from the archive's own state.** A Scenario Lab run's tape starts mid-game: replay `archive.initial_state` with `resolve_turn` (0 hash mismatches here); `Tape::initial_state` builds a new game. `play-time-report.py` can't read a scenario run (`turn_times` is empty); the per-turn `ms` in `turns` has the times. Engine events serialize with a `type` tag, and serde_json maps sort their keys, so the first key of an event object is not its name.


## 2026-10-22: free Alliances landed; what the merge needed, and the benchmark
- **Free Alliances (user decision 2026-10-21) cost the human-seat benchmark 1.2 points, inside the noise.** All seats, Hard, 128 salts, reseeded dice, master 00ab72845 as control: 39.8% [37.5, 42.0] without the change, 38.6% [35.9, 41.1] with it (-1.2 pp). Rows are in the ledger.
- **A branch that deletes a rule meets every later change to the same functions.** 129 master commits (the speed removal, crown upkeep removal) touched the diplomacy fee code, so the merge had 29 conflicts; most resolved as "the branch's removal, on master's speed-free signatures". The merge also silently kept `solvent()` calls whose definition the branch had deleted and dropped the `CAPTURE_BOUNTY_GOLD_PER_POINT` constant that sat inside a conflict hunk: compile and vitest caught both, so run `cargo check --all-targets` and vitest right after resolving.
- **More Alliances exposed an AI bug: a Skip or Sleep order for a unit standing in an ally's city is refused ("must move out this turn")**, which the opening-brain validation test (`mixed_opening_orders_validate_in_real_fogged_games`, seed 8140002) caught once Alliances stopped costing Gold. Every brain now drops those stay orders (`drop_stays_in_allied_cities`); the unit is left without an order. That moved the `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape` pin.
- **Archived-state hashes move with Alliance Gold.** The turn-32/33 replay in `ranged_garrison` signs Alliances, so its two current hashes were re-pinned; `settler_escorts` now ignores the new Alliance offers when it counts non-research orders.
- **Celtic hunters lose the -1 move (user 2026-10-22) and keep the +20 Production surcharge.** The rules reference said "+10 cost/-1 move" while the code and the faction sheet had said +20 since the V9 balance (the brief repeated the stale +10, and the first Codex branch "fixed" the code to +10); the landing restored +20 so the change is the move alone, and the rules reference now says +20. Human-seat benchmark, all seats, Hard, master 49274c80f as control: same dice, the Celts' map reads 48.4% (31/64) before and 28.1% (18/64) after, but that is dice chaos (one shared dice sequence per map); with reseeded dice and 128 salts the Celts' map is 10.9% (14/128) both before and after, the overall score 39.3% both, and no other map moves. Rows are in the ledger.

## 2026-10-23: the scheduled ops check had been a no-op since 10-18
- **Every `legatum-ops-check` run lasted about 14 seconds:** the scheduled session spawned the supervisor agent in the background ("I'll pass on its report when it finishes") and then ended, so the supervisor never ran. Nobody noticed the box idling at 36% over 6 hours with the filler daemon stopped. The prompt now spawns it with `run_in_background: false`, makes it keep the filler fed with named batches (logged in docs/ops/filler-log.md) and append to `.progress/supervisor/journal.md` every run. Check `list_task_runs` durations after changing a scheduled task: a run that ends in seconds did nothing.

## 2026-10-22: Siege replaced the Military Engineer; the price does not move the war suite
- **What changed.** Siege is a permanent Gold upgrade (160 Gold, era-scaled like units; Mathematics, own land, one order; attacks on a city ignore Walls, Machinery adds Castles, Metallurgy Star Forts) that uses 1 Iron per unit (`data::SIEGE_USES_IRON`, one switch). The Engineer unit, its build card, upkeep, stacked cover, capture rule, AI planning and three genes are deleted; old saves migrate at JSON level (the covered military unit gets Siege, a lone Engineer gives nothing).
- **Iron is a separate supply user.** A Siege Pikeman needs 2 Iron (the Pikeman's own plus Siege's), and an AI fixture without an Iron entry read as no spare supply until the helper treated a missing entry as unlimited. Idle Siege units keep Siege and fight 25% weaker.
- **The attack check: 54% with Siege against 65% on master (64 runs each).** Before the Alliance pricing fix landed, `war-golden-horde-thin` read 0% on master and on the branch alike, at 160, 80, 40 and 20 Gold and with Iron on or off (the drop was free Alliances, see "the war-suite attack check fell to 0% because of free Alliances"), so price could not be read there. On the merged tree (`claude/siege-upgrade` with the fix) it reads 54%, above the 30% floor: the 24 Gold treasury buys a Siege unit only late, and the rest of the force attacks at half strength against the Walls. On the pre-fix tree a trace showed the AI on a Peace plan with no Siege bought. Allowing partial cover in the forecast (committed, run at 160 Gold) still read 0% there, so it was reverted and the AI keeps the benchmarked all-or-nothing rule.
- **A war-suite build ships only the committed tree.** Variant experiments (prices, Iron off) must be committed in their own worktrees, or every arm reads identically.
- **Benchmark.** Human-seat benchmark, Hard, 8 maps x 128 salts, all seats: 42.8% master against 45.1% with Siege; seat-off and opponents-off arms 46.1% and 43.8%. Neutral.
## 2026-10-22: why the AI Celts win 2.4%, and a what-if trap (coach review of the 8.9.7 Celts game)
- **The Celts' weakness is an AI gate, not their rules.** Every city-attack path in `crates/ai` returns early for Celts (city assaults, campaigns, open-city strikes, rush sieges, Contain marches, second fronts), so the AI Celts sacked 10 cities in 2,048 balanced seats. On a throwaway branch (`COACH_CELTS` env flags in core and AI), balanced 8-empire Celts: control 2.4% (reproduces the published run exactly), all gates lifted 5.2%, every Celtic rule removed 2.0%, each rule alone 1.7–2.2% (no concealment, no sack, no −2 melee, no land damage, no hunter stat changes). In the user's seat from his T50/T100/T150: 4/2/2 of 40 → 20/18/18 with the gates lifted. A faction's strengths and drawbacks are both invisible when the AI never does the thing they modify; check that the AI exercises a faction's mechanic before tuning the mechanic.
- **Reseeding a what-if branch redraws the era objectives.** `branch_whatif`-style runners reseed by overwriting `setup.seed`, and `objectives.rs` derives every era's objectives from `setup.seed` (`Rng::derive(seed, [era, OBJECTIVES, slot])`), so a reseeded branch from T50 plays with different objectives than the real game (e.g. "Capture 2 enemy cities" instead of "Market ×9"). Paired control-versus-variant comparisons on the same salt stay fair; absolute levels are not the user's game. Reseed a separate dice stream instead.
- **`legatum-sim --out-dir` writes the 2,048-game result as plain JSON of about 800 MB** (not `.json.gz`); profile it on the box and delete it before artifacts come back.
- **Five factions have only six city names:** the Celts, Germany, the Mongols, Sparta and Athens (`crates/core/src/rules/cities.rs`, mock `app/src/mock/setup.ts::CITY_NAMES`); Switzerland, Japan and Byzantium have 20. The user's seventh Celtic city was "Celts Colony 7". Content gap, reported to the lead.

## 2026-10-23: climb targets from his saves: what a save can and can't tell you (`tools/ai-quality/climb-targets.py`)
- **One game is many saves; the map seed is the key.** 56 save files are 26 games; `metadata.seed` groups them (the p84/p85/p86 bug saves share the seed of the matching `playtest-8.x` game, and `p892-*` has six saves of one 8-player game). Two of those games are only repro snippets (T6, T19) and are dropped.
- **Only 8.9.4 and later saves carry the chronicle** (`game.history.chronicle`: Captured, Razed, Eliminated, War, Peace); `players[].fight.captures` exists from 8.8.1 and counts razed cities; 8.3 to 8.6 have neither. For those, captures and losses come from telemetry city deltas with his `FoundCity` orders netted out (tape turn `t` lines up with telemetry frame `t`); on six chronicle games it matched the exact capture and loss counts except razes (a raze never raises his own city count), so it is a lower bound.
- **Two games look unfinished but ended in losses:** `p892-ai-beat-user-final` (his words: "the AI legitimately beat me") and `playtest-8.6-germany-2-vs-mongols` (he said he was about to lose). Their saves have no `winner`, so `RESULT_OVERRIDES` in the script carries the result and the source. A new playtest that ends early needs the same entry.
- **Seat `difficulty` for the human is always `Normal`** (a default, not a handicap); the AI seats are `Hard` in every save.
- **Medians at a checkpoint mix versions:** the 4-player 8.5 Switzerland game had 13 cities at T100 (8.9.x Swiss games: 4 to 6), so the faction table also carries 8.9.x-only and all-games medians; the climber should prefer those for T100+ on the current rules.
- **The Swiss hoard at 5,000 Gold (user 2026-10-22) makes it a rare goal, not a certain one.** Master de34c553a against the branch, Hard, balanced 8-empire seats (2,048 games, seed 9210001): Switzerland's win share 16.2% (331) -> 11.3% (231); its hoard was held at some point in 85.8% of seats at a median of turn 143 before, and in 24.2% of seats at a median of turn 227 after (so never reached in 75.8%, against 14.2%; the P90 moves from turn 216 to 397). Nobody else moves by more than 2 pp except Japan (1.6 -> 2.1%, run to run noise). Human-seat benchmark, all seats, reseeded dice, 128 salts: 39.3% [36.6, 41.9] -> 39.1% [36.7, 41.6], the two Swiss maps 5 -> 3 and 90 -> 91 wins of 128. The tour spec that stages the hoard (`victory-race.spec.ts`) still staged 1,000 Gold; the extended Playwright profile skips the screen tour, so the stale numbers showed only when the tour ran.
- **Sakoku without the intruder rule (user 2026-10-22): a closed Japan holds its 2 VP after the 30 closed turns whatever stands in its land.** Measured on master de34c553a against the branch, Hard, balanced 8-empire seats (2,048 games, seed 9210001): Japan's share 1.6% (33 wins) -> 3.9% (80), the vow reached in 9.3% of seats -> 12.4% (median turn 241 -> 229); the other seven move by at most 1.1 pp (Athens 18.9 -> 17.8, Byzantium 29.1 -> 28.5). Human-seat benchmark, all seats, reseeded dice, 128 salts: overall 39.3% [36.6, 41.9] -> 36.8% [34.1, 39.5] (-2.5 pp, the intervals overlap); the Japanese map 92 -> 81 wins of 128 and the two Spartan maps 48 -> 42 and 88 -> 80 (a stronger Japan costs the neighbours of the seat), the other five maps unchanged. A Playwright fixture (`sakokuRivalState`) only set the held-goal flag when no unit was parked, so the rewritten "unit in Japan does not break the vow" spec failed until it set the flag in both cases.

## 2026-10-23: the war-suite attack check fell to 0% because of free Alliances, not stale scenarios
- **Bisect of `war-golden-horde-thin` (64 Hard runs per commit):** 89% through 10-22 01:21, 67% after move-through, era objectives step 1, supply AI and the ramp removal, then 0% from the free-Alliances merge (`72fc33073` alone on its old base: 89% → 54%). An earlier version of this entry blamed scenarios cut under old rules; that was wrong for this drop.
- **Mechanism (plan traces, unconfirmed by the bloc census still running):** with Alliances free, an allied bloc forms around the Swiss; the Mongol campaign on Aarau then prices negative and the plan flips to "Contain" a bloc member, so the Mongols never attack. Likely a design consequence of free Alliances plus "allies fight together", not a bug; the balanced-game bloc census decides it.
- **Bloc census (512 eight-player Hard games, old tree 0ac97a0f8 vs free Alliances 72fc33073):** alliances formed per game 6.3 → 19.8; largest bloc at T100 1.3 → 5.3 teams of 8 (up to 7); wars opened per game 36 → 18; captures 23.2 → 20.9; the share of wars with a capture unchanged (32% → 35%). Free Alliances plus "allies fight together" made a five-to-six-empire bloc from about T100: fewer wars, not more stalled ones. The AI was joining Alliances without pricing the wars it inherits (fix in progress, `claude/alliance-inherited-wars`).
- **Dead end (alliance fix v1):** pricing only the cost of the wars an Alliance makes us join (`inherited_front_cost` in `join_war_value`) left the thin check at 0%: the "allies who'd join our wars" gain (about 10–30 points per ally, up to ~150 for a five-ally bloc) outweighs it. Both sides of the Alliance value have to move.
- **The AI pricing fix recovers the thin check but not the blocs** (512 games, fix `dae4aa835`): largest bloc at T100 5.3 → 5.0 of 8, wars per game 17.9 → 22.5 (old tree 36.1), captures 20.9 → 19.0, peaces signed 40.6 → 50.1. When allies already share an enemy (a coalition against the leader), an Alliance inherits no new war and the shared-enemy and coalition terms make every Alliance positive; the signing fee and upkeep were the only restraint. Testing an AI bloc-size cost; if that fails, the rule needs a cost (the user's call: one Alliance at a time is the lead's pick).

## Mercenary Companies and the Byzantine Heir (2026-10-23)
- **Bought peace was not Byzantium's gimmick; the Heir goal was.** 2,048 balanced 8-empire Hard games, same binary, bought peace switched off by a sim-only override: Byzantium 30.1% to 27.0%. With the Heir unavailable (bought peace on) it fell to 13.0%, a fair share. Byzantium finished the Heir in 86% of games (median T138) and won 35% of those against 0.4% of the rest. Lesson: when one faction wins far above fair, size each suspected lever with an override batch (3 minutes, cents) before building the replacement. Results: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/mercenary-companies/results.md`.
- **Companies plus a public Heir: Byzantium 31.0% to 22.9%, best over worst 8.4x to 4.6x.** The Companies carry the drop (22.7% before the Heir became public); the public Heir moved it 0.2 pp because rival AIs already priced a city that shelters him and rarely meet him in the field. Byzantium's Heir completion fell 86.7% to 80.0%, eliminations rose 4.5% to 9.2%. Still 1.8x a fair share: the next lever is the goal's price, not the Companies.
- **The AI hires about 23 Companies a game, 93% Soldiers, from every seller evenly.** Hiring is gated on a war with a purpose and the runway after the 2 Gold upkeep; 33 Companies in 2,048 games went unpaid and turned barbarian. Scoring strength per Gold over the whole contract (price plus 60 Gold of upkeep) did not move the mix: the sellers' rosters (and Iron supply for Troopers) decide it.
- **Human-seat benchmark** (8 maps x 256 salts, reseeded dice, master vs branch): 39.2% vs 40.1%, +0.9 pp [-0.9, +2.6]. No drop.
- **The attack regression check read 0% on a master from before `98e01c48c` (free Alliances had broken it) and reads 68% on this branch merged with the fix.** Merge the latest master before reading a war check.
- **Deleting a file can delete a helper another agent just started using.** `crates/ai/src/tribute.rs` also held `gold_as_army`, which master's Celtic sack valuation began calling the same day; the merge compiled nowhere until it moved to `plan.rs`. Grep a deleted module's `pub(crate)` functions on master before landing.
- **Scenario state hashes embed the diplomacy state:** removing `DiplomacyState::tributes` re-hashed five lab scenarios (`rehash_scenarios`), and the runner tape pin moved because Byzantine AI play changed (`tools/sim/repin.sh`, intentional).
- **Companies are exempt from the strategic supply by design:** the shop only lists kinds the seller can field with spare supply, and a hired unit uses none of Byzantium's. Faction variants (Tagmatic +4, the Keshig, Celtic hunters) do not travel: a hired Trooper is a Tagmatic Soldier, and hunters and the Keshig are not for hire.
- **Correction (same day): `run_in_background: false` didn't help.** The scheduled session still spawned the supervisor asynchronously, said "it's running", went idle, and nothing ran (runs still 15 s; the journal last moved 10-11). Scheduled tasks now do the checklist in their own session with no sub-agent. Rule: a scheduled task must do its work inline.
- **Second correction (same day): the inline run then stalled on its first Bash call** (`box.sh util`, which takes under 2 s by hand). It was still "running" 10 minutes later. A scheduled session runs in the default permission mode, and no allow rules cover `tools/cloud/*`, so every command waits on an approval nobody is there to give. It also ran on Opus at high effort, not the Sonnet the task was meant for. Either the task gets a permission mode or allow rules from the user (a security setting, so the user's call), or it can't run unattended. Check a run's transcript (`list_events`), not only its status.

## 2026-10-23: the legacy-name rename and the Codex queue
- **Appending to a pending Codex task file is a race.** I appended the Heir-name instructions to `rename-legacy-factions.md` with `cat >>` after the runner had already moved it to `running/`. That created a new pending file holding only the appended text, with no header. The runner ran it as a nameless review task on **gpt-6-sol** (the default model), which breaks the Luna-only policy, and it did nothing. To change a queued task, write a new task file with a full header. Never append to one.
- **Don't union-merge a file the other side has mass-renamed.** Merging master into the rename branch with `git merge-file --union` on `hard-vs-human/ledger.jsonl` kept both spellings of every row (543 rows, 271 duplicates). For a mechanical rename, take the other side's file and re-run the idempotent script (`tools/dev/rename-legacy-factions.py`) instead. Every other conflict was resolved the same way.
- **Old names survive only as load aliases:** serde aliases in `crates/core/src/types.rs` (`Faction`), `crates/ai/src/params.rs` (gene names) and `crates/server/src/staging.rs`, plus the alias test. Saves, tapes and climb parameter files written with the old names still load. Branches cut before the rename run the script before merging master.

## 2026-10-23: the climb daemon waited 12 hours on a finished spot run
- **`os.kill(pid, 0)` finds a zombie.** The climb daemon starts each spot batch with `Popen`. To tell whether a run is still going, it checked `os.kill(pid, 0)`, but it never reaped the child, so a finished run stayed a zombie (`<defunct>`) and counted as alive. The Celts' gen-14 batch finished on its server at 13:00, and the daemon then waited until 01:15 the next day. That was the last climb running, so round 1 sat stalled while its status line kept saying "in flight on spot (586 min)". Restarting had always fixed it, because a new daemon isn't the zombie's parent. Fix: `climb_program.py::pid_alive` reaps with `os.waitpid(pid, WNOHANG)` first. Rule: a supervisor that polls its own children must reap them. When an "in flight" age is far past the batch's `max-hours`, treat it as stuck.

## 2026-10-23 — Perf baseline: a third of every turn is the strategic-supply check recomputed per production option
- At 8 Hard empires the WASM End Turn already averages 54 ms late game (p95 63, max 87); the cost is spread across every seat's decision view. Native `perf` (frame pointers on, `tools/perf/native-perf.sh`) puts `resources::deposits` (a full 1,536-tile scan allocating a `Vec<Deposit>` per call) at 21% and `eras::current_era` at 6%, both reached through `shortage_after` for each production option of each city in `view::city_detail`. Numbers and tools: `docs/sim-results/perf/turn-latency.md`.
- Fat LTO plus one codegen unit for the WASM build is 18-23% faster per turn and 4.5% smaller (the sim profile's win, but never applied to `scripts/build-wasm.sh`).
- Wall-clock WASM numbers for the first 10 turns are inflated by V8's tier-up (24 ms against 6 ms native); read the 30+ buckets.
## 2026-10-23: launch media (README loops and teaser)
- **Animated WebP beats GIF for a painted map.** The same 8 s late-game loop was 4.2 MB as a 720 px GIF (256 colours, banded) and 1.2 MB as a 720 px animated WebP; at 1100 px wide it is 1.8 MB. `docs/media/src/webp.sh` (ffmpeg frames, then `img2webp -lossy`) makes them. GIF stays as the fallback (`gif.sh`) if GitHub ever fails to animate one: check the first public render.
- **A Chrome DevTools screencast (`Page.startScreencast`, jpeg 92) records real compositor frames with timestamps,** which an ffmpeg concat list turns into a clean 30 fps clip. Playwright's own `recordVideo` is too low a bitrate for UI text. The recorder is `app/e2e/media/recorder.ts`; the clips are `capture.media.ts` (not `.spec.ts`, so the e2e gate ignores them), run with `playwright.media.config.ts` on the box.
- **Fixtures show their seams on camera.** A fixture with two human seats shows "Pass to Rival" when the turn ends (set the second seat's `isHuman` false); fixture empires named after their peoples read as "Japan · Japan"; `tsc -b` type-checks everything under `e2e/`, so an unused import in a capture file fails the box build; `localStorage` setup inside `addInitScript` cannot see module constants.
- **`<video>` in a README is stripped; `<picture>` is supported.** The README links a poster to the MP4 in the repo; drag the MP4 into github.com's editor once to get an inline player (`docs/media/PLAN.md`). The default branch is `master`, so every absolute link says `master`.

## 2026-10-24: finished Codex work waits for the lead
- **Codex finishing a task doesn't wake the lead session.** Overnight, eleven Codex branches finished between 23:37 and 02:01: five perf fixes, robustness, the soundtrack, the score ledger, two UI-fix batches and the TS-mock removal. None was landed until the morning. The lead only wakes on its own background jobs and agent hand-backs. Fixes: the ops check now flags finished `astra/*` branches unlanded for over 3 hours and sends one notification, and a Claude "lander" agent batch-integrates them on one branch with one verify.

## 2026-10-24: payoff moments on the map (moments-fx)
- **Towns and borders are baked into terrain chunks, so an animation has to hold them back, not paint over them.** `TerrainCache.setHold` takes what a moment is still animating (towns and walls the overlay draws, a town's old population or era, a tile's owner before its claim) with a signature; only chunks near a tile whose hold changed repaint, at the few instants it changes, never per frame. Painting a "before" state over a chunk that already shows the "after" cannot work (a new town or a new border line shows through). Live layers (roads, improvements) instead draw twice: the view from before outside the revealed area, the current view inside it (`drawRevealed`).
- **A one-off engraving of a painted sprite reads as pen and ink** (Sobel on the luminance over vellum, the silhouette outlined, hatching only in the deepest shadow, sepia at about 85% alpha) when it sits on a pale vellum ground; without the ground and with hatching in the midtones it read as a dark blob. Capped at 256 px so the first use costs no visible hitch.
- **Several moments land on one capital in a busy turn** (a wonder, five era objectives, a crown, a war and a peace on 8.9.6 T151): VP seals merge per empire per turn, and seals take slots beside a rising building or another seal instead of stacking on it.
- **Frame-stepped captures freeze `performance.now()`**, so the perf counters read 0 in them: measure frame cost on the real clock (`perf63` in `moments-fx.media.ts`).
- **A media test with no action timeout hangs for its whole hour** when an alarm (a war declared at turn start) covers the turn dock: the harness closes alarms, takeovers and unlock cards before ending a turn, and sets a 45 s default timeout.
- **`.moment-lane.reduced *` (specificity 0,2,0) loses to a card rule like `.moment-card.unlock .unlock-seal` (0,3,0)**: a new card animation needs its own reduced-motion override, and `::after` is not covered by the lane's rule at all.
## 2026-10-24: landing the overnight Codex batch
- **Combined perf win, measured once: a late turn went from 40.0 to 21.7 ms native** (End Turn plus the follow-up view, 8 Hard empires, 201+). The per-branch A/Bs each had a different baseline and box load, so they don't add up; the combined A/B (master against the landed branch, concurrent box jobs, `tools/perf/native-turns.sh`) is in `docs/sim-results/perf/turn-latency.md`. The biggest single levers were reusing counts across a view's production options (supply) and caching the post-turn view; removing allocations alone barely moved anything.
- **Save validation (`legatum_core::validation`) rejects hand-built fixtures that were silently inconsistent.** Fixtures that flip `players[i].isHuman` or `team` without the matching `setup.players[i]`, give an aircraft an id above `next_unit_id`, delete a city but leave `tileCity` pointing at it, or give a hand-built `GameSetup` a map below 8x8 now fail to load ("Invalid saved game structure"). Fix the fixture (set both sides, bump the counter, clear the tiles), never the check. `air-wing.spec.ts` hit two of these (aircraft ids, removed cities).
- **A branch's own gate can miss specs it doesn't touch.** `ui-fixes-899-1` changed the diplomacy copy and moved Alliance costs from the button chip to a list beneath it, and passed its tour and layout specs; `diplomacy.spec.ts` (not in that subset) still asserted the old copy and the chip. When a change edits shared UI copy, grep `app/e2e` for the old strings.
- **`astra/score-ledger-build` was not landed: five Playwright specs still assert the old card sheet.** `vp-holdings.spec.ts` (three tests: the "All objectives" own-goal line, the hold strips in the sheet, the VP-chip card sheet), `vp-v2.spec.ts` (the era-objectives sheet and the lead list) and `premium2.spec.ts` (head pinned, medallion cards) use `.vh-cards`, `.vh-era`, `.vh-slip`, `.vh-hold-row`, `.vh-board-head`, which the ledger removed (its DOM is `.sl-table`, `.sl-row[data-row]`, `.sl-col`, `.sl-hold`). The branch ran only the tour and layout specs. The ledger's own `e2e/tour/score-ledger.spec.ts` covers the new states, so port or retire those assertions, then land the branch (it merged cleanly with everything else; the merge is on `claude/land-1024`).
- **`oss-retire-ts-mock` was not landed: it fails seven Playwright specs on its own** (`aftermath-forecast` turn 29, `city-assault-forecast` walled city, `era-takeover` city art, `overlays` Yields over Settle, `wonder-visibility` city wonder at both sizes; master passes all of them). Its fixture builders now take their maps from the WASM mapgen instead of the TS mock's, so every hard-coded tile, wonder and pixel crop in those specs is a different scene. The branch ran only the specs that import the removed code, never the whole suite. Also note `FixtureClient extends WasmClient`, so the 33 `*.wasm.test.ts` files that were mock-versus-core parity tests now compare WASM with WASM (about 190 assertions that can't fail); they should be rewritten as literal expectations or deleted. After the validation change, its `reportText` fixture also needs `setup.players[i].is_human` set beside `isHuman`. The merge plus those fixes is on `claude/land-1024`.
## 2026-10-24: the Astra runner reads its model mapping at start
- `tools/astra/runner.sh` defines `model_slug` when each lane loop starts, so changing the mapping (here `astra` → gpt-6-astra) only affects lanes started afterwards. Running lanes keep mapping `astra` to Sol until they're restarted. For an urgent one-off on a new model, call `codex exec -m <model>` directly with the review sandbox (`-s read-only`), as the 2026-10-24 AI audit did. Don't restart lanes that have tasks in flight.

## 2026-10-24: landing batches of Codex branches
- **Split refactors go stale fast.** `astra/review-split-lobby` passed its own checks, but merged with master it no longer compiled: master's LobbyScreen had changed underneath it (missing `playerIds`, `playerColor`, unexported `SeatsTable`). Release file-split refactors only when no feature work touches those files, and land them soon after they finish.
- **A pipe hides a failed fast-forward.** `git merge --ff-only ... | tail -2 || fallback` never runs the fallback, because the pipe's exit status is `tail`'s. Don't pipe git merges in a landing script, or set `pipefail`.
- **Rivals' Great Works can't be landmarks yet:** a city's buildings are only in `CityView.detail`, which the view carries for your own cities, so `landmarks.ts` shows only yours. Showing a rival's needs a public "great works" field on the city view from the core (the Great Work milestone itself is public).
- **The WebKit screen tour fails 14 specs on master as of 2026-10-24 (company, factions, heir, post-game, research-unlocks, turn-one; box job 20261024T170446Z-684d83).** A branch's WebKit failures are only its own if they are not in a same-day master run: compare before chasing them.
- **Construction animation took two rejected rounds (2026-10-24), and both failed for the same reason: they were effects laid over finished art, not the building being built.** Scaffolds that don't follow the structure, and a clip reveal of the finished painting, both read as cheap. Real construction needs art made for it (staged paintings of the same building) and a researched design approved first; until then it is switched off behind one flag, with the plan, holds, landmark placement and staged-art loader left in place.

## 2026-10-24: real games rarely have big battles from the player's seat
- **The designer's own empires field only 1 to 6 units,** so his saves can't show a complex battle from his seat. To film or stress-test combat, copy a late-game save, move the human seat to the empire with the biggest army in contact (strip the turn archive so the copy is consistent), and never write the original. The busiest real turn found across four games had 13 movers and 5 clashes, playing in 3.8 s. Busier turns need a synthetic fixture (30 shots and 20 clashes is about 6 s), and that's what the pacing test covers.

## 2026-10-24: the AI gate for seven Codex branches (`docs/sim-results/ai-quality/gate-1024/`)
- **Gate method that worked: one binary, gene groups as arms, effect = on minus off.** Where a branch ships its genes on, set the benchmark binary's defaults to 0 for a bench build (so "baseline" rivals play the old way) and give arms the on values; or keep defaults on and use arms that turn one group off (effect is minus the delta, and the opponents arm flips the same way). One cloud batch of 9 to 15 arms, 4 servers, took about 45 minutes. At 1,024 games per arm the interval is +-3 pp, so the -1 pp / +1 pp landing rule cannot separate a real 1 pp effect: re-run a borderline arm on fresh salts (`--seed 1001`) and pool before deciding. The settler-capture gene read opponents +0.9 then +2.6 pp and was left off.
- **A branch that deletes a gene's code leaves an inert arm.** The revive branch removed the `siege_exempt` and `plan_persist_skip` checks, so its "restore the old exemption" arm was identical to the control in every game (identical games across arms mean the arm does nothing, as before). Check that an arm's gene is still read (`grep` the code) before trusting a flat result. The dedicated arm after rewiring showed the removal cost Sparta's 8.4 seat 64 to 48 wins of 128, as the old code comment said, so Sparta keeps its exemption.
- **Merging two supply fixes made one double-charge.** master and the ruins branch had both fixed Siege-upgrade Iron budgeting in `supply.rs::within_budget`; git merged them cleanly into a first loop that reserved Siege and a second that reserved it again. A clean merge of two fixes to one function needs a read of the result.
- **Changing a gene's default breaks tests that assumed it.** Four AI tests failed once `peace_settler_capture` defaulted to 0 and `vp_peace` priced Peace in the pre-hold test; tests of a gene's behaviour should set the gene.
- **VP race effect on holds:** in 2,000 all-AI eight-player games the five `vp_*` genes left holds broken at 49.4% to 49.8% and first-hold wins at 65.5% to 67.9%; the branch probe's 49% to 60% (32 seeds) was noise. The measured gain is in the human-seat benchmark, not in all-AI hold statistics.
## 2026-10-24: landing on a red verify "because the box was overloaded" left master red
- combat-fx and moments-fx both landed after verifies that failed, judging the failures to be load flakes because they varied between runs and a master-identical branch also failed. A clean master verify on a quieter box then failed 36 Playwright cases and 2 vitest files. Many plausibly came from the animations changing timing and state (era-takeover reduced motion, celebrate ordering, improvement states), not load. **Rule:** never land on a red verify. If the box is overloaded, wait, or re-run the failing specs alone with `--repeat-each=3` on master and the branch, and land only when master passes and the branch fails no spec that master passes. The fix is queued as `master-green-1024`.

## 2026-10-24: the TS mock engine is gone, and parity tests can turn vacuous
- `app/src/mock` (about 19k lines) was deleted. The app and its tests now use only the Rust engine via WASM (one-engine rule).
- **Trap found on the way:** once the test fixture client extended `WasmClient`, 33 `*.wasm.test.ts` mock/WASM parity files compared WASM with WASM, about 190 assertions that could never fail, while all still passed. Converting them to literal expected values fixed it. When one side of a parity test is removed, the test must change shape; a green parity suite proves nothing by itself.
- **Fixture trap:** fixtures built from the WASM mapgen moved hard-coded scenes (7 specs). Place the terrain, units and cities a test needs explicitly.

## 2026-10-24: `codex exec` in a background shell waits forever on stdin
- Run directly from a backgrounded Bash call, `codex exec ... "<prompt>"` printed "Reading additional input from stdin..." and waited 1 h 42 min with no output. Always add `< /dev/null` when calling `codex exec` outside the runner. The runner redirects its own input.

## 2026-10-25: a Codex task can't land on master
- The overnight Astra "night lander" landed nothing. The runner appends "Do not merge into master; the lead reviews your branch" to every write task, and that suffix overrides the task prompt. Only the lead (Claude) can land. Overnight Codex work therefore ends as verified branches plus a report, and the morning landing is the lead's job. The overnight Codex burn was also low (about 2%), because most tasks were gates waiting on cloud CPU, or work blocked on prerequisites (`construction-site` waits for wonder tiles to land). To use Codex quota overnight, queue independent work that doesn't depend on unlanded branches.

- **2026-10-25: Don't bundle a pure rename with gated rules work.** The Lithium → Silicon rename (7068140cb) shared a branch with the era-objective and cavalry changes; when that branch failed the AI gate (−5.0 pp), the rename was stuck too, and the user saw "Lithium" in 8.9.10. Land cosmetic and rename commits on their own branch so a gate failure elsewhere can't hold them.

- **2026-10-25: Codex reports link screenshots by lane-worktree path, and those paths go stale.** A lane worktree checks out its next task's branch as soon as one finishes, so `.claude/worktrees/lane-N/docs/...` links in `.reviews/astra-*.md` usually 404 by the time the lead reads them. Pull the files from the task branch instead: `git show astra/<name>:<path> > out.png`.

- **2026-10-25: Many small UI branches off the same master conflict pairwise when landed one by one.** Wonder tiles, land-5, resource chips, the unit panel, unworked claims and Copper all touched UnitPanel, ScoreLedger, TileCard and SupplyChips, and each lead-side merge hit a new test conflict, costing Claude turns per branch. Hand Codex one integration task that merges the whole batch in order, repins and runs the full verify, and have the lead land only that branch.

- **2026-10-25: A faction-wide "at war with X" flag can silently pin an AI's best units at home.** In the Athens playtest, Switzerland's lingering war with the Mongols made `military.rs::plan_military` run `garrison_cities` before any siege planning, with no Mongol unit in sight. Its only modern unit sat four hexes from the target city all game while the Ancient units it sent couldn't survive the city strike. Garrison duty should follow visible threat, not war status (`astra/analyze-athens-game-1025`, gene off until gated). Also: Germany's capture valuation counted a fictitious loss of the leader's science crown and ignored losing its own Engine goal. Audit VP-delta code for both sides' titles and goals.

- **2026-10-25: The main checkout's app/node_modules goes stale when a landed branch adds a devDependency.** The 8.9.11 Tauri build failed in `tsc -b` on a missing `@testing-library/react` after master gained it. The box verify passed because it installs fresh. Run `npm ci` (Node 22: `~/.nvm/versions/node/v22.14.0/bin`) before every local release build.

- **2026-10-25: The regular verify skips the screen tour, phone and tablet projects, so layout redesigns can pass verify and still fail the release check.** The 8.9.11 sidebar and unit-panel redesign passed every verify (724/724) but broke 8 specs that only `tools/release/check.sh` runs (Yam Relay tour selectors, phone report sheet, tablet slots). For any branch that moves or removes a control, run the tour and touch projects on the box before landing, not just at release time.

- **2026-10-26: The 1,024-game AI gate is too noisy for −1.5 pp point-estimate decisions.** One night's gates measured the same master control at 342, 381 and 399 wins out of 1,024 (different frozen commits, settings and runs). Paired 95% intervals are about ±3 pp. Rules passed alone and failed in bundles (claims + Japan +0.2, all five −5.7) in ways the singles don't explain. User-decided rules were dropped on noise. Fix under way (`gate-noise-audit`): one frozen control per day, a known run-to-run spread, more games or sequential testing, and a rule that designer-decided rules ship unless they clearly hurt the AI.

- **2026-10-26, overnight AI findings (details in `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/`, on each task's branch):**
  - **Strategy lab r1:** of six committed whole-game strategies played as the human seat against Hard (7,168 games), only air dominance beat the baseline seat (+3.6 pp [+0.4, +6.8]). r2's confirmation run didn't hold up (+1.4 pp [−2.0, +4.8]), so nothing dominates yet. Opponents buy Fighters only in proportion to their own Bombers, a counterplay gap.
  - **Air war (Japan game):** every AI reached Flight and none built a Fighter. Germany kept interrupting its aircraft builds.
  - **Gold:** both income and spending style. The ordinary purchase pass only considers what it can afford this turn, so the AI never saves toward a dearer, higher-value buy. In one game an AI earned 7,007 and spent 7,001.
  - **Blunder review (12 Hard games):** 14 solo attacks into city fire, 91 parked-army turns, 129 turns at war with over 300 Gold unspent, 30 rejected orders.
  - **Local opportunism genes:** neutral on the benchmark but 0/64 on the war check with defensive reservations on. Those reservations are too conservative.
  - **Climb r3/r4:** all 12 candidates failed the arena gate. Celts, Japan, Mongols and Sparta have plateaued, which points to structural limits rather than tuning (see the architecture proposal, `docs/design/research/ai-architecture-2026-10-26.md`).

- **2026-10-26 morning findings:**
  - **Gate noise:** at identical settings master reproduces exactly (399/1,024 twice, every hash matching). The 342–399 spread came from engine revisions and dice reseeding; different salt blocks still vary by about 4 pp. The new gate (`astra/gate-noise-audit`) freezes one control, stratifies maps, corrects for repeated looks, and accepts designer rules unless the harm is clear. Under it the truce (−1.2), Strongest Army (−0.5) and walls over terrain (−0.9) are allowed; Modern auto-breach (−3.8) and aircraft Oil (−16.7) are held for AI adaptation.
  - **Strategy lab r3/r4:** Fighter corridor defence +3.0 pp; Japan air-and-capture bundle +10.7 pp [+6.2, +15.3], the designer's own winning plan. No confirmed counter yet (pillar 10 watch).
  - **Air genes:** defensive Fighters triple Fighter counts and quadruple interceptions but cost −13 pp, because they overbuild. Aircraft rebasing alone is +4.0 pp and is on.

- **2026-10-26: A saturated box makes the release check's browser tests flaky, and a Codex task told to "pass the release check" can loop for hours.** With the climb filler holding the box, `integrate-8912` passed the full verify (747/747) at 10:15, then spent seven-plus hours re-running `tools/release/check.sh`. Every attempt failed Playwright on timing, and its fixes after 10:15 only touched e2e timeouts. Give release-check tasks a stop rule (say, two attempts, then report), run release checks when the filler is paused or at higher priority, and land the verified commit when the later diffs are test-only.
- **2026-10-26: Search (the AI's searched policy) never submits air strikes.** Its order wrappers drop aircraft actions, so Search opponents built planes and never used them (`astra/ai-air-defense-r2`, `astra/fix-aircraft-oil-gate`). Benchmarks that mix plain Hard and Search seats measure different air behaviour per seat. Task `search-air-execution` checks which AI the app's Hard actually uses and fixes it. Also, from strategy lab r5: the designer's Japan air-and-capture plan replicated (+7.3 and +6.0 pp), and layered Fighters suppress it (−3.2 pp), so a counter exists.
- **2026-10-26: Tag the release before building the app, or set `LEGATUM_BUILD_LABEL`.** The title screen's label comes from `git describe --match 'playtest-*'` at Vite build time (`app/vite.config.ts`, `src/buildLabel.ts`). Building 8.9.12 before tagging it made the title read 8.9.11 (as "Dev · after 8.9.11"), and the designer thought the install had failed.

- **2026-10-26 evening findings:**
  - **The app's Hard uses plain `ScriptedBrain`, not Search** (`astra/search-air-execution`), so Search dropping air orders didn't affect the designer's games. In his Chur game the stalled AI (Japan) ran at **Normal**, which never runs the Hard-only siege pass. Check which difficulty the lobby gives each AI before reading playtests as "Hard is weak".
  - **Army modernization:** valuing units by combat value against the enemies they'll meet and reconsidering production passed at +2.2 pp over 4,096 paired games, and is on. Example: Hiroshima chose an Archer over better units on a flat ranged bonus (1,666 vs 1,585) and kept that build for twenty turns.
  - **Staging ring:** the re-decision fix makes Chur's ring leave, but it drops the war check to 0/64 because it also abandons advancing sieges. Forecast arrivals must match executable orders.
- **2026-10-26: Check the save's recorded difficulty before analysing a playtest.** Every save records the setup (`game.setup.players[].difficulty`, omitted when Normal) and `archive.metadata.seats[].difficulty`. The designer's 8.9.12 game ran every AI at Normal although he always picks Hard, while his three previous saves are Hard. That is a lobby or settings-migration bug (task `fix-lobby-difficulty`). The lead and Astra both read the Chur game as Hard failures before looking.

- **2026-10-27 overnight findings:**
  - **Lobby difficulty:** a new lobby reset every AI to Normal. The choice lived only in memory, and Normal is omitted from setup JSON. Fixed (`astra/fix-lobby-difficulty`): Hard is the default, the choice persists under a version-independent key, and each AI's level is shown in diplomacy and score panels.
  - **Strategy lab r6:** with saving actually working, the designer's save-then-buy pattern loses for the AI: buying VP buildings −7.7 pp, buying an army −9.5 pp. 35 of 381 army packages executed only partly, because supply accounting ignored retained production reservations (a real defect). His edge comes from timing and target choice, not from saving as such. The air-and-capture plan still wins (+6.6 pp).
  - **Staging ring r2:** using executable orders keeps the war check at 38/64 and makes the Chur ring leave within six turns, but the quality gate is inconclusive (seat −1.4, opponents +1.4). Gene stays off.
