- **2026-10-27: Check Codex's quota before queueing, not after.** On 10-27 Codex's weekly window was used up and running on paid credits (`rate_limits.primary.used_percent` in the latest `~/.codex/sessions/**.jsonl`). A Sol task queued minutes before the user said "Luna only" had already started, and the auto-mode classifier blocks the lead from killing a running Codex process. Read the quota line before every Sol or Astra queue, and while Codex is exhausted, queue only Luna.

- **2026-10-27: What the research and our own results say about making the AI good (`docs/design/research/ai-strategy-options-2026-10-27.md`).** Three independent lines point at the same bottleneck: the oracle best-of-eight policy study (66% against 27.7% in his seat), the search levers (omniscience +0.1, more samples and horizon zero or negative) and the strategy lab (his own air-and-capture plan is +7 to +11 pp once the AI executes it) all say the gap is operational war execution, not evaluation, information or tuning. The outside literature says the same: shipped 4X AIs never built an operational layer (Soren Johnson says rushing the human was avoided on purpose), full-game RL and LLM agents fail on Civ-like games (CivRealm), while portfolio/puppet search over scripts plus small learned evaluators is what works at our scale. Our $0.88 ES pilot (rush defence 54% → 85%) is the local proof that learning fits a small, local decision. Proposed order: operational planner, engagement search, learned evaluator, bundles; a learned-controller league only after transfer is shown. Compute for the whole program is a few hundred dollars; the binding budget is Codex and Claude tokens and the noise of the gates (±3 pp per 1,024 games).
- **2026-10-27: Estimate agent work in wall-clock time, with the bottleneck named.** The AI research doc gave "3–4 months" and "10–17 Codex lane-weeks" (human-team pacing); the real limits are build-test-fix rounds (about half a day each, a 2,048-game gate is about 45 minutes on 4 spot servers), merges in shared AI files, token budgets and the designer's playtests. Re-planned as about 2–3 weeks to the checkpoint (`plans/ai-war.md`). Also: check `get_usage` before quoting a Claude budget (the weekly window had reset; the extra-credit cap didn't bind), and check the AWS month total before promising compute (October hit the monthly cap the same day).
- **2026-10-27: A noninferior gate can hide a large single-map drop.** 8.9.13 (army modernization merged) passed the frozen-control gate overall (+1.0 pp on 4,096 games, interval −1.2 to +3.3), but the Germany map fell from 360 to 281 wins of 512 (about −15 pp). Read the per-map rows in `gate-decision.json`, not only the overall decision; this one is queued for W0.4 of `plans/ai-war.md`.

- **2026-10-27: War fixtures can now carry each AI's saved memory, and the Athens stall at T220 was the garrison reservation, not memory.** `ScenarioFile.ai_memory` (optional) holds every AI seat's `OpponentMemory`, which is all `ScriptedBrain` persists; `tools/scenarios/war_memory.rs` replays a save with the recording engine's brains (Athens 1,561/1,561 AI order lists, Chur 593/593, final memory equal to the save's). Old scenarios give identical per-run results. With the memory carried and `local_mongol_guard` back at its 8.9.10 value 0, `war-swiss-tepid-t220` reads 0/64 (the designer's stall); with today's default (gene on since `a3c046bd7`) 23%, so 8.9.13 already repairs part of it, and a clean-memory start does better (51%). `war-swiss-tepid-t210` takes Athens in 64/64 either way (the Hard Athenian AI is not the designer), so it is a control, not a reproduction. T180 and the Chur ring stay at 0% with or without memory. Also: an attacker scenario with "hold every starting city" failed 62 of 64 Chur runs because other empires took a population-1 Japanese city; scope keep conditions to the front (`docs/sim-results/hard-vs-human/war-scenarios/README.md`, 2026-10-27 sections).
- **2026-10-27: Hard bombs but never captures, so an "AI plays his seat" scenario cannot test the air stage; the war suite can now replay his recorded orders.** The designer's Japan bomber game (T214 and T219 fixtures, `war-german-air-t214`, `war-german-air-t219`) had Japan's Hard AI given his four Bombers and two Autonomous Armour: it hit Cologne with the same first two strikes (102 and 87) and never walked a unit into the stripped city, so Germany kept every city in 64 of 64 runs (predicted 70% and 90%; both goals met 100%). Replaying his recorded orders for the window (`scenarios/war/scripts`, `crates/sim/examples/war_script`, recorded from the tape by `extract-war-states.sh`) reproduces his result to the damage roll (the same 102, 87, 124, 89 on Cologne): Germany's AI loses three cities by T218 in 64 of 64 runs and is eliminated on T221, 0% on both goals. In those 128 runs it ordered no Fighter and bought none; its `air_rebase` default saves its Bomber and a second Bomber is built, but it strikes ground targets. Orders name hexes, so replay survives a changed position; use it only where the AI cannot play the attacker's side (untested past 8 turns, where the two worlds may diverge). Also: (1) the Japan T142 fixture's "hold all ten" read 0% because Kyoto (20 HP, 1 citizen, no Walls) falls on turn 3 in every run and the failed `keep` ends the run, so a goal that must let the window finish uses `win` only; (2) with Sparta played by an AI the exchange is about even (Japan loses 7.5 combat units a run, Sparta 7.9 and Athens 1.3), nothing like his 30 for none, and Sparta and Japan sign Peace in 45 of 64 runs where he refused every Japanese offer for 150 turns; (3) all three first predictions missed by 30 to 70 points, because the AI as attacker is much weaker than the designer and a weak city decides a hold-all goal. Details in `docs/sim-results/hard-vs-human/war-scenarios/README.md`.
- **2026-10-27: A replayed-orders script fits for about 12 turns, not 17.** `war-german-air-early` from the Japan bomber game: the same recorded orders from T205 gave Germany 100% on "keep 4 of 6" (predicted 5%) because from T207 his strikes and Armour routes name hexes where the real game's units stood (`WAR_TRACE_FAILED=1 war_trace` shows each skipped order); from T207, T208 and T209 also 100%, from T210 4%, as in his game. Check a script's fidelity by starting it at several turns and keeping the earliest start at which it still reproduces the recorded result.
- **2026-10-27: Raising the AWS cap is two steps, and the lead can't do either.** A higher `monthly_budget_usd` doesn't lift the launch ban already tripped: the `legatum-sims-deny-launch` policy stays attached to the launcher until it is detached (`infra/cloud-sims/README.md`). The permission system denies the lead `terraform apply` and `aws iam detach-user-policy` even after the user picks an amount, so prepare the edit and the preview (`terraform plan`, one in-place change), then hand the user both commands.
- **2026-10-27: An oh-my-zsh update prompt can eat the first character of a pasted command.** The user's paste of `cd ~/code/legatum/infra/cloud-sims && terraform apply` ran as `d ...`, so `terraform apply` ran in the repo root ("No configuration files") and nothing changed. When handing the user commands, give the `cd` as its own block, and check the outcome (`read_terminal`, or `terraform state show` for the budget) rather than assuming it ran.

- **2026-10-27: A stranded unit used a foreign city as its stepping stone out of closed land (war-suite flag "unit 66 (Ranger) stands in the foreign city Hiroshima", `war-japan-chur-ring` run 41, T155).** Real rules bug, not a detector false positive: Peace made on T154 left a Swiss Ranger in Japanese land, and the Swiss AI (its trapped-unit mover, `ai/src/diplomacy.rs`, single adjacent MoveUnit) ordered it onto the adjacent Japanese city because `path::exit_distances` counted the city centre as passable and the shortest egress ran through it; the engine accepted the step (the Mongols-only `neutral_city` block in `rules/movement.rs` never applied) and it ended the turn inside the empty city. Fix: `path::exit_distances_around` treats city centres on closed land as walls; `rules/movement.rs::exits_from` (resolution), `PlayerView::exit_distances` (validation, settlers, AI) and `app/src/diplomacy.ts:borderSteps` use it. Regression tests in `rules/movement_contention_tests.rs` (they fail without the fix). Goal results on the ring scenario are unchanged (0%, 64 runs, same losses). Not touched: the frozen AI proxies (`proxy_v4`..`v8`) keep the old egress; a Courier Hall holder's cities are open at Peace by `passage_allowed`, so a non-Mongols unit might still walk into one (unchecked).
- **2026-10-27: The W1 operation record (gene `operation_planner`, off) turns the bombed-but-never-entered Swiss T220 into a capture every time, and breaks a melee-only storm.** One record per front owns roster, aircraft, rendezvous and home guard, takes its units' orders before every other army pass, and every two turns rolls commit / gather / withdraw forward from its own view (`crates/ai/src/operation.rs`, `search::evaluate_operation`). War suite, 64 runs, paired with the W0.2 baseline (gene off reproduces it run for run, 1,344 of 1,344): `war-swiss-tepid-t220` 23% to 100% (bombers and the walk-in now belong to one order set; T220-fresh 51 to 100), Golden Horde 100 to 93 (60/64), thin 90 to 89, `war-walls-rise` 81 to 92, German air T210/T214 4 to 31 and 0 to 60 (the German operation retakes the city the replayed Japanese script captured; the script never defends it, so read this as an upper bound), Swiss T180 and the Chur ring still 0, German stand 93 to 89 and Japan T142 78 to 75 (intervals cross zero), and **Sparta's First Blood 100 to 4**: its five Spearmen have no shooter, and the commit executor only sends melee in once the city is at 0 HP or a volley drops it, so a melee storm never starts; the existing assault coordinator (which stormed on its forecast) no longer sees those units. Withdraw-on-tie plus a per-city cooldown also bounced the objective between Anvil and First Foundry. Suite mean 43 to 49, parity 8 to 10 of 21. Also: (1) `LEGATUM_ROLLOUT_THREADS=1` makes every war_suite thread queue on one shared rollout thread (the suite ran at 120% CPU and took 440 s instead of about 110 s even with the gene off, because some opponents are Hard + Search); use 4 for suite runs and 1 only to time one decision. (2) Native cost per decision on one thread: about 10 ms at T30 with 4 empires, 40 to 70 ms in the 8-empire Golden Horde, 75 to 110 ms at T143 to T187, and about 390 ms for a seat's first decision of a game (generating its sampled map, as Hard + Search also pays); a rollout turn with three seats at war costs about 9 ms, so the 12-turn cap is the budget lever. (3) Over 4 to 6 turns the win-chance score separates alternatives only when a capture or a game end happens inside the horizon (T220: 3,853 against 1,619); elsewhere it differs by tenths of a percent, so a tie rule decides.
- **2026-10-27: W1 round 1: the operation's commit must start with the existing assault coordinator, ties must not withdraw, and a withdrawal must close the whole front.** First Blood (five Spearmen, no shooter) went 100% to 4% in the first version because commit only sent melee in after a volley; running `coordinate_city_assaults_at` on the roster first and letting the melee wing storm a city without Walls brings it back to 100% (run 0 takes Anvil on T32 and First Foundry on T36, as plain Hard). Hysteresis (a new operation stands on commit and changes only for +100 of 10,000 win chance) and a front-wide cooldown after a withdrawal stopped the objective bouncing between the two German cities. Round 1, 64 runs paired with W0.2: suite mean +10.0 points (43 to 53%), T220 23 to 95%, T220-fresh 51 to 95%, walls-rise 81 to 100%, German air T210/T214 4 to 18% / 0 to 76%, German stand 93 to 92% and Japan T142 78 to 76% (both inside noise), Golden Horde 100 to 89% (57/64; -10.9, interval -18.8 to -4.7), T180 and Chur still 0. The four "stands in the foreign city" flags in Japan T142 are other seats' units (e.g. P3's Assault Trooper, unit 79, run 60, T155) stranded in Sparta's land after a Peace and stepping into the city as egress, the pattern `claude/fix-stranded-egress` fixes; `WAR_TRACE_FLAGS=1 war_trace` prints each flag with the unit's owner and move.
- **2026-10-27: W1 rounds 2 to 4: the operation's own losses came from home defence, Peace vetoes and short horizons, not from committing; and the Swiss T180 and Chur cases do not move with a better score or a purchase (stop rule).** (1) *Golden Horde 57/64 in round 1 was a lost home city, not an early commit:* the roster emptied a Mongol city a neighbour at Peace stood beside, and it declared and took it (run 10, T141). Keeping a city's garrison while any foreign army is within 6 hexes brought it to 61/64, then 64/64. A static allocation mirroring `defend_cities` (nearest units until the threat is covered) crushed it to 26%; reverted. (2) *Japan T142 (the defender) fell to 70% because the operation vetoed every Peace:* the Hard defender survives by signing Peace (45 of 64 runs at baseline). Vetoing only while the last decision found fighting on better than withdrawing by the margin restored it to 79 to 82%. (3) *A plan that drops the war mid-assault* (T210, a Phalangite lost, plan to Contain another team) used to end the operation and let Peace through; now a committed operation decides at once instead. (4) *Adding a third alternative shrank the horizon from 6 to 4 under the 12-turn cap,* and T220-fresh fell from 89% to 34%: the hold victory at the end of T223 dropped out of the rollouts. The cap is now 18 turns with the enemy seats re-planning every other turn; that saved little, because a rollout turn is about 9 ms of whole-world resolution in an 8-empire late game, not planning. (5) *Zero-sum material term and buy-then-attack, two rounds, no change on T180 or Chur:* in both the defender converts its Gold (3,152 and 6,481) into garrisons the turn the army shows up, the attacker's army is one to two eras older, and the attacker is broke (219 and 43 Gold), so a reinforce choice is offered rarely (chosen in 2 of 48 traced runs, both T180; 2 purchases ordered, 0 dropped by the supply budget). Final round (r4), 64 runs paired with W0.2: mean +7.9 points, T220 23 to 92%, Golden Horde 100%, thin 90 to 98%, First Blood 100%, German stand 93 to 92%, Japan T142 78 to 82%, T210 100 to 92% (-7.8, single-sample rollout noise flips commit and withdraw by about 120 points), T180 and Chur 0%. Gene off still reproduces W0.2 run for run.
- **2026-10-27: W1 round 5: a new operation must stand on the ordinary passes, not on commit, and near-tie resampling alone does not remove noise; the planner still bleeds about 1.5 more units a run where attacking cannot win.** (1) Rolling the alternatives again on a second fresh sample when the best two were within 300 points (gene `operation_tie`) left T210 at 89% and T220-fresh at 56%, and roughly doubled the cost of those decisions (Chur up to 280 ms). (2) The bleeding came from hysteresis standing on commit: a new operation committed unless the rollouts clearly showed otherwise, so in Swiss T180 it lost 12.7 units a run (plain Hard 8.1). A new operation now stands on withdraw (plain Hard) and takes the army only when committing beats it by the margin; T210 is back to 98% and Golden Horde, First Blood and both defences hold or improve (German stand 95%, Japan T142 84%). (3) A reinforce chosen on noise on the last turn of T220-fresh pulled the walk-in away from a city already at 0 HP; reinforcing is now offered, like gathering, only while the army is short of its force (T220-fresh 32% to 62%). (4) Hopeless cases, off vs on (round 7, 64 runs, combat units lost per run, ours / theirs): T180 8.14 / 2.98 vs 9.54 / 2.95; Chur 8.90 / 5.78 vs 10.54 / 5.50, cities lost 0.98 vs 1.00; T180 against the designer's recorded orders (new `war-swiss-tepid-t180-replay`) 6.70 / 2.00 vs 8.48 / 2.29, 0% either way, predicted 15% and 45%. The likely cause is the information limit, not the executor: determinized rival treasuries scale ours by the public economy title, so a poor Swiss seat models Athens as poor and its rollouts do not see the Infantry Athens buys. (5) Rebuilding `war_extract` on an old engine to record a seat's orders: copy the current `tools/scenarios/war_extract.rs` into the engine tree's `crates/wasm/examples/` and build there; the binaries left from W0.2 predate the `seat:first-last` argument and silently write no `orders-<seat>.json`.
- **2026-10-27: The T180 replay is the key finding of W1: even against his real, nearly undefended Athens, Spearmen and Archers can't open a walled 200-HP city, so the Swiss stall was mostly army quality, not operations.** Against the designer's recorded orders (`war-swiss-tepid-t180-replay`: one unit walks in on T185, nothing bought for Athens) the Swiss AI takes nothing in 64 of 64 runs with the gene off or on. The operation planner fixes coordination (Swiss T220: bombers plus a walk-in), not an army one to two eras behind its target.
- **2026-10-27: W1 last round: bleeding is fixed by a stricter start and a loss stop; a treasury floor from visible enemy troops changed nothing; seat stagger and short horizons cost T220.** (1) A start margin (`operation_start_margin` 300: committing must beat the ordinary passes by 300 of 10,000 before the operation takes the army; Chur run 27 committed on a +120 edge and lost seven units in two turns) plus a loss stop (withdraw when roster deaths exceed the decision's rollout forecast, pro rata, by 2 units; then 10 turns before a new operation) bring the hopeless cases to plain Hard's losses: T180 8.20 vs 8.14, T180 replay 6.71 vs 6.70, Chur 9.73 vs 8.90 (still +0.8). (2) Flooring the enemy's sampled treasury at the purchase price of the troops seen appearing at its cities since we watched gave identical results with or without it (64-run subsets), so it was removed: without event history the view cannot tell a bought unit from a trained one, and the rollouts' Hard defenders buy anyway. (3) Swiss T220 fell from 85% (r7) to 51 to 65%: staggering later decisions by seat moved the Swiss decision off the turn it mattered (65% without, 51% with; the gene is now off), and the start margin skips some near-even commits (run 1: +242). Two samples per decision (cap 24) did not restore it (57%), so it is not plain sampling noise. (4) The German air gains of r1 to r7 (up to 57% and 76%) disappear once the operation stands on the ordinary passes: they came from committing counter-attacks into cities the replayed Japanese script never defends, an artifact of the script.

- **2026-10-27: W0.4 ranked the AI candidates on one frozen-control gate (`docs/sim-results/ai-war/baseline.md`); three things in it are not obvious.**
  - **The human-seat gate's rivals never bomb.** Its opponents are Hard+Search, and Search drops air strikes unless `search_air_orders=1`. Turning that on costs the seat 22 pp: those rivals now bomb as the app's plain Hard already does. Until the gate's control has it on, every air gene is measured against rivals that never strike. That's why air defense r2 reads −4.7 pp in the seat (Fighters bought against nobody), and why its opponents-only −24 pp mostly comes from `air_capture_bundle` turning on the same Search air pass. Re-freeze the control with `search_air_orders=1` before judging air work.
  - **8.9.13's Germany-map drop is the valuation gene.** With `modernization_value=0` on every seat, Germany 8.6 goes from 144 back to 186 of 256 (+16 pp; 8.9.13 lost about 15), while Switzerland 8.3 falls from 87 to 52. A noninferior overall result can hide one gene's map-specific cost. None of the seven candidate arms recovered it.
  - **Gene arms that can't act cost a full arm of games.** `search_air_orders` only acts in Search and opening wrappers, and the benchmark seat plays plain `ScriptedBrain`, so its seat-only arm would replay the control. A combined arm whose extra gene is inert in one role likewise replays the single arm there. Check where a gene is read (`grep`) before paying for an arm.
  - **Comparing two binaries' games:** a bench that adds telemetry fields (the strategy-lab `hard_vs_human`) changes every game-record hash even when the games are identical. Compare on the fields both binaries record (`air`, for one, grew new subfields). The bench reproduced master on 128 of 128 games that way.
  - **Box tests run under nextest:** grepping `cargo test` output for `test result` on the box shows only the doc-test lines. The real summary is nextest's `Summary [...] N tests run`.

- **2026-10-28: 8.9.14 UI review fixes: four layout traps worth knowing.** (1) The unlock card scrolled as a whole (`overflow-y: auto` on the card), so its primary button scrolled out of view at 1000x600; the body (`.unlock-body`) scrolls and the footer is `flex: none`, and the tour now measures `getBoundingClientRect().bottom <= innerHeight` for the longest cards. (2) A `float: right` count inside a `<summary>` wraps across the divider and the next row in the 196 px rail; stack it as a block there. (3) Elements inside a closed `<details>` still return client rects, so "is it on screen" checks use `checkVisibility()` (the "+N more empires" counter counted hidden rows until it did). (4) The rail's own `.sidebar-scroll { height: 88px }` rule in `sidebar.css` is dead: `:root .game .right-sidebar .seatbar > .sidebar-scroll` outranks it, so the rail scrolls the whole column. Left as is, since the rail looks right with it.
- **2026-10-27: Checking the build label needs a count, not the first match.** The bundled JS also holds older "Playtest 8.9.x" strings (release notes), so `grep … | sort -u | head` showed 8.9.10 and 8.9.11 for the 8.9.14 build. Use `grep -rao "Playtest 8\.9\.[0-9]*" app/dist | sort | uniq -c` and look for the new label (it appears once).

- **2026-10-28: the human-seat benchmark's rivals are plain Hard now; the old control was 36%, the real one is 16%.** (1) The eight benchmark maps carry `search: true` on every AI seat (the lobby's experimental Hard+Search level), and Search skips air strikes, so the gate's rivals never bombed while the app's default Hard does. `hard_vs_human` now plays rivals as plain `ScriptedBrain`; `--rival-search` or an arm's `"rival_search": true` restores Search rivals. Same binary, 2,048 games, 8.9.14: control 331 (16.2%) against 745 (36.4%) with Search rivals (+20.2 pp [+17.9, +22.5]); Germany 35 against 121 of 256. Ledger rows and W0.4 numbers were all measured against non-bombing rivals; only compare new numbers with new numbers (`docs/sim-results/ai-war/baseline.md`, W2.0). (2) Against bombing rivals the verdicts flip or sharpen: `air_defense=100` seat-only +3.2 pp [+1.6, +4.8] (it read -4.7), modernization off costs the seat 3.7 pp [-5.5, -1.8] (8.9.14's gain holds), and modernization off for the rivals helps the seat 3.4 pp. Re-test any air gene measured before today. (3) The `zero_offset_reproduces_the_archived_game_output` test in `hard_vs_human.rs` had been failing on master: `cargo nextest` doesn't build example tests, so nobody saw it. Run `cargo test -p legatum-sim --example hard_vs_human` when touching that file (on the box: `tools/cloud/offload.sh`). (4) An arms file naming a gene the binary doesn't have panics every server in seconds (`unknown field`), costing a launch and a few cents; check the gene exists on the branch you build before launching. (5) The 5-arm, 2,048-game batch took 11 minutes and 0.74 server-hours (about $0.48); the Search rivals were the expensive part of the old gate (14 arms, 7.78 server-hours).

- **2026-10-27: war fixtures from the 8.9.13 Athens and Sparta games (`docs/sim-results/hard-vs-human/war-scenarios/README.md`, "Fixtures from the designer's two 10-27 games"); five things that are not obvious.**
  - **A recorded-orders replay reproduces his game in run 0 and only partly in other seeds.** Run 0 keeps the game's own seed, and on the 8.9.13 build it reproduces every capture on the same turn (6 of 6 cases; the AI memory carried by `extract-war-states.sh` with `memory,0:101-177` makes the brains replay the real game). Seeds 1 and up change the dice and the brains' tie-breaks, and his orders (exact paths and hexes) stop fitting as soon as a unit dies or stands elsewhere. The Sparta-game defences therefore read 95 to 100% for the AI (Germany's Berlin hangs on one roll: a Hoplite left at 6 HP in run 0 is killed in run 1), against his total loss. Scan start turns before trusting a verbatim script; where nothing reproduces (Germany), ship it as a regression guard and say so.
  - **A goal that does not name its victim or its cities measures something else.** An any-city capture goal read 14% on `war-sparta-next-door-t101` (the Spartan army took a Mongol city in 9 of 64 runs; Athenian cities: 0). A `MinCities` defence goal read 71% on `war-japan-hoplites-t43` (28 of 64 runs lost all three cities and founded a new one: 28% is the real figure). `Condition::CapturedFrom` and `Condition::MinStartingCities` exist for this; use them for any capture or hold goal.
  - **The start state can break the script.** At T42 (the declaration turn) one Hoplite stood in Japanese land at Peace, which today's rules forbid; `modernize` moves it a tile and his T42 orders for it no longer fit, so even run 0 diverged. Start the war fixtures on the first turn at war (T43).
  - **The 8.9.13 to 8.9.14 difference on these seven is exactly the two modernization genes.** A tree with the AI and core files of 8.9.14 reverted to `a58e80856` equals the current build with `modernization_upgrade` and `modernization_return` at 0 (`--ai-params modern0.json --params-seats all`), run for run on all 448 runs; the stranded-unit egress fix changes nothing here. The genes moved the goal result in 0 to 4 runs of 64 a case.
  - **`cities_lost` in a run report is capped by the keep condition**: a run stops at the first failure, so a mean of 1.46 starting cities lost does not say how many he would have taken.


- **2026-10-28: Fighters (`air_defense=100`) on the fixed gate, and the planner re-ranked.** Plain Hard rivals bomb, 2,048 games: Fighters seat-only +3.2 pp [+1.6, +4.8], rivals-only -1.3 [-2.9, +0.3], all seats +1.0 [-0.6, +2.6]; the war suite is identical with the gene on (64 runs by 21 scenarios, 49% both). The campaign planner reads +1.2 [-0.5, +2.9] in the seat and +1.0 [-1.0, +3.0] as rivals: neutral in full games, as before. The control reproduced master game for game (2,048 of 2,048) in all three batches, including on a binary with the planner merged in (genes off equals master). `air_capture_bundle` and `search_air_orders` are not on master, only on `astra/search-air-execution`, so arms using them need that branch first. Merging a feature branch just for a benchmark binary: take only the AI crate and keep master's war tooling when the war files conflict (`git checkout --ours` on the sim files), because the benchmark builds only `hard_vs_human`. Details: `docs/sim-results/ai-war/baseline.md` W2.0.
- **2026-10-28: Sub-agents' AWS spot launches can be refused by the permission system as real-money actions,** even within an approved budget. The gate-fix agent's launches went through, but the door-defense agent's were refused hours later. When a brief needs spot servers, say so and plan a local fallback: a quarter-size local gate (512 games per arm) gives about ±4 pp. If an agent is refused, the user approves the launch; the lead doesn't relaunch it for the agent.
- **2026-10-28: Door defense is neutral in full games and still worth shipping; a four-arm 2,048-game gate on plain Hard rivals now costs about $0.10 and 3 minutes.** Seat -1.1 pp, rivals-only -0.3, all -0.8, every interval across zero, while the war suite gains 6.5 points and an adaptive Sparta's wins halve. The full-game benchmark is dominated by economy and expansion, so a war-only gain shows up there only once wars decide games; judge war genes on the war suite plus "no full-game loss", not on a full-game gain. The old gate's cost (7.78 server-hours) was the Search rivals: with plain Hard rivals a gate runs in 0.15 server-hours.
- **2026-10-28: Search's rollouts model every rival as default Hard, so turning a defence gene on by default changes archived Search reviews, and zeroing it in the test's seat brain does nothing.** `SearchRollout` builds rival brains with `ScriptedBrain::with_difficulty` (built-in genes), so `door_defense` on made the T51 Mongols-partial review prefer Economy over the Campaign (4,704 against 4,530). Seat-side genes (e.g. `modernization_value`) can be zeroed in the archived brain; rival-side behaviour can't, so pin only what the review must still compare.

- **2026-10-29: In a worktree, copy `app/src/wasm-pkg` (or build it with `LEGATUM_OFFLOAD=1 scripts/build-wasm.sh`); never symlink it, and never trust the main checkout's copy.** Vite resolves a symlink to its real path, so a spec that injects a fixture (`await import('/src/test/…')`) gets a second, uninitialised copy of the module and fails with `__wbindgen_free` of undefined. The main checkout's package was also built before Siege became built in, so `browserFixtures.test.ts` saw a Siege offer on a Rifleman; the stale package fails tests that have nothing wrong with them. A `node_modules` symlink is fine.
- **2026-10-29: the siege gear prop is cosmetic and capped by the unit's own era; the combat tier is not.** `siegePropFor` reads the technologies for the tier but the unit's era for the art (an Ancient Archer with Metallurgy draws a catapult, not a bombard). `shotKindFor` is passed the drawn prop, so the animation's missile at a city follows the art (catapult and trebuchet throw stones, a bombard shot) and the two cannot disagree.
- **2026-10-29: a tile-chosen item's tooltip printed its card copy twice.** `renderOption` builds `detail` from the card copy plus `effect`, and a Wonder site's `effect` appended `itemCardText` again after "Replaces ... on this tile ·". Every Wonder site (the Yam Relay Station and Great Works) showed the doubled text. A site's effect now adds only the yield given up; the Yam spec asserts the copy appears once.
- **2026-10-29: tile placement state lives in the store (`place`), and the city panel applies the pick through a ref set after its `if (!d)` early return.** A `useEffect` declared after that return broke every foreign-city render ("Rendered fewer hooks than expected"); the effect now sits above the return and calls `applyPlacePick.current`. Map clicks on a lit tile only set `place.pick`, so Build, Queue and Buy keep using the panel's own `buildNow`/`append`/`toggleBuy`. Also: `TitleScreen.test.tsx`'s autosave test fails about one full-suite run in three under load and passes alone; treat a lone failure there as flaky.
- **2026-10-29: map placement draws its lit tiles under the counters and banners, and only the hovered tile above them.** Drawn last, the lit rings struck through city names and unit tokens. A placed Wonder site gets its own `sites` list on the improve overlay and a gilt double ring, because a thin seat-colour ring vanished into the territory border. Local `npx playwright test e2e/tour` serves whatever `app/dist` was last built (a stale one showed per-tile Yam cards), so run tours on the box.

- **2026-10-30: Focus fire and stragglers (genes `focus_fire`, `gather_hold`, off; `claude/focus-fire`; `docs/sim-results/ai-war/w24-focus/`): small real gains, and the AI's losses are mostly positional, not spread fire.** (1) *Where units die:* a temporary pass trace (each order tagged with the `plan_military` pass that gave it) found that on the turn of death units are mostly already retreating (`recover_city_fire` 29%, `recover_wounded` 9%) or defending (`defend_cities` 18%); the pass that put them there the turn before was `defend_cities`, `siege_war`, `press_strike` or `campaign`. A retreat order can't save a unit inside a walled city's range, since the strike fires before movement. Judge a straggler fix by the pass of the turn before. (2) *Held-out seeds (runs 64-191) with both genes:* exchange 1.14 to 1.20, losses -4.5%, units lost per capture 8.77 to 8.18, Mongols Chur T137 17% to 32%; mean goal +0.4. Focus alone moves kills more than losses (in-sample exchange 1.16), the straggler hold moves losses (1.19); together 1.21. (3) *The 53:3 fixture (Japan T142) does not move* (73% to 71%, exchange 1.28 to 1.29): its losses come from defence passes and the door's own strikes, which run before focus fire. (4) *Doubling the kill weight (`focus_fire` 200) is worse than 100* (in-sample losses 5,567 to 5,644, fewer kills; Mongol bombers T150 losses 422 to 488), so the kill prize at cost plus threat is about right. (5) *Exempting moves within two tiles of an own city from the hold* (instead of one) gave back most of Berlin T65's gain (losses 110 to 172 of 173): defenders walking to the front one at a time die like attackers. (6) War-suite runs report engagement shape now (`attack_groups`, `lone_attack_groups`, `units_hit`, `units_hit_killed`, `isolated_losses`). (7) On this master, `war-golden-horde-thin` (the `--check` attack case, floor 30%) reads 0% locally with the genes off, and `war-walls-rise` 0%: the attack check is already failing before this branch.

- 2026-10-30: W2.3's hollow read changes how AIs value a rich, under-defended leader but doesn't turn into attacks. In the designer's 8.9.17 Mongols game at T96 (10 cities, 5 units, 1,432 Gold), Switzerland (at war since T56) and Byzantium (since T86) had taken nothing, while the AIs captured or razed three Athenian cities. This matches `mongols-rich-and-empty` staying 0/50. The gap is between wanting the war and executing it; diagnosis is on claude hollow-attack.

- **2026-10-31: the hand win estimate (`winchance::WEIGHTS`) is worse than a uniform guess on current master, and a refit on 276 box games fixes it** (`docs/sim-results/value-net/`). On 76 held-out Hard self-play games (12,211 seat-positions), the estimate Hard's plan reads has Brier skill −33% against uniform (−80% at T1–60, +3% after T200), and the fitted Search-leaf weights −41%. A linear refit on the same inputs gets +14%, and the 1,217-parameter value network +15%. It is overconfident, most of all early. The weights were fitted 10-04 at +25% skill, before VP v2 zeroed population and territory and moved the crowns. Ranking moved far less (the seat with the top estimate is the eventual winner 42.4% of the time against 43.3%), so the plan's *choices* may change less than its numbers. But several genes are fixed charges in chance units (`peace_credibility`, `campaign_commit`, 300 each), and a calibrated estimate's smaller differences let them weigh more. Gate a recalibrated estimate on behaviour (wars, captures), not just wins. Refitting `WEIGHTS` alone is a cheap arm worth running beside `learned_value`.
- **2026-10-31: the build box can't run PyTorch, and can't read run-batch results.** It has no `python3-venv`, no pip and no `ensurepip`, and its role may list only the sims bucket's `box/` prefix (`s3:ListBucket` denied on `batches/`). Training on the value network's rows runs on the Mac (niced, 4 threads) until the lead decides to install `python3.12-venv` there. A full Hard 8-player game at 48x32 costs 6.6 core-seconds on Graviton (4-player 3.7), so 50,000 training games is about 1.3 spot server-hours.
- **2026-10-31: a spot server AWS reclaims leaves no trace in S3, and `run-batch.sh` gzips every `*.json` in a server's output.** The first 50,000-game value-data batch "ended without finishing" with no `batch.log`. `aws ec2 describe-instances` on the instance ids (still listed for about an hour) showed `StateReason Server.SpotInstanceTermination` on both, after 6 and 10 minutes in us-east-1a, where the c8g price had just dropped to $0.66. The user-data's EXIT trap never got its uploads out. Check the StateReason before suspecting the binary. For long data runs, use more and shorter slices, and re-run the lost seed ranges (`tools/sim/value-generate.sh` does both). Readers of `--bin` outputs must also accept `name.json.gz`: the finish hook compresses the schema file too.
- **2026-10-31: a better-calibrated win estimate doesn't change what plain Hard's plan choice wins** (`docs/sim-results/value-net/README.md`, "Decision regret"). On 3,069 fresh positions, the learned network (Brier skill +18% against the hand formula's −34%) picks a different top plan 18% of the time, mostly Peace where the hand formula picks Contain. Forcing each pick for 20 turns from the true state and playing out 24 reseeded games shows no difference: +0.04 pp of win rate per position [−0.05, +0.14]. The two picks' outcomes differ by 3 pp on average, and in 55% of disagreements the seat loses with either. A calibrated estimate is worth having for search leaves and reports, but a step that only re-ranks today's candidates can't buy wins. The candidates and their execution have to change, the plan's architecture A, before evaluation can matter. The regret harness (`value_regret`) is reusable for any estimate or candidate change: about $0.25 for 768 games.
- 2026-10-30: **Why the designer out-economies Hard as well as out-fighting it.** Source: five of his recent wins (Japan 10-26, Athens 10-27, Sparta 10-27, Swiss 10-28, Mongols 10-30), using `history.empires` series and tape build orders; scripts in the lead's scratchpad (`econ/series.py`, `econ/builds.py`).
  - **He builds almost no army early.** In his first 60 turns he queued Walls, Granary or Farm, Settler, Market and Laboratory, with 0 to 2 military units. The AIs' production orders in that window name 6 to 15 unit items each.
    - **Correction (2026-10-31, coach):** production orders overcount units, because an AI that switches back to a Soldier repeatedly counts each time. In the Mongols game the AIs actually trained 2 to 4 military units by T60, against his 2. The robust measures are army strength (his T50 army was a third to half of the AI median in the Japan, Athens and Swiss games, and equal in the Mongols game) and upkeep paid: 76 Gold by T100 for him, against 225 to 405 for the AIs.
  - **His army strength stays far below theirs** until he needs it. At T75 it was 31 to 73 against an AI median of 92 to 128.
  - **He banks Gold.** His treasury at T50 was 160 to 421, against an AI median of 21 to 64.
  - **He converts Gold to army in a few turns.** His army jumps at T100 to T150 to 492 to 670, about the AI's best.
  - **Cities and techs stay level to T50** (tech diffusion), then he pulls ahead. At T100 he had 5 to 14 cities against an AI median of 0 to 6. Population, and science from about T75, follow the cities.
  - **Correction (2026-10-31, army-intent census, 512 games):** at T50 the AI's army is near its defensive minimum (3.05 armed against a minimum of 2.41). Idle upkeep by T50 is about 6 Gold, so his T50 Gold lead does *not* come from the AI's army. The idle army grows later: 54% of all AI upkeep is paid with nothing marching, and by T100 the share idle reaches 76 of 167 Gold. The source of the early Gold gap is still open. Candidates are his Markets against the AI's purchases (tile improvements and buys) and spending style; measure income against spending before claiming it.
  - **The AI's army is pure cost** (mid-game onward). It pays production, plus 1 Gold a turn per unit beyond 2 (3 for the Swiss), against about 13 Gold of income. It never uses that army to punish an empty empire, so he wins the economy *because* the AI pays for an army it doesn't use.
  - **Design read:** the rock-paper-scissors loop is broken on the AI side. Greedy expansion should lose to a rush, and nobody rushes him.
  - **Levers:** two candidates.
    - The AI's value for standing army against economy. The step 3 evaluator should learn this from outcomes instead of hand weights.
    - The AI actually punishing an undefended neighbour.
  - **Also seen:** AI expansion stalls after about T75, at a median 4 to 6 cities.
- 2026-10-31: **`hollow_march` 3 full gate is neutral and stays off** (batch `w25-march-1030-221329`, 2,048 per arm, control 348): all seats +0.20 pp [-1.61, +2.00]; rivals-only +0.63 [-1.02, +2.29]. Rivals-only reads, if anything, softer for the designer's seat. The census lost 4% of captures, and it leaves the designer's own positions at 0%. Its code stays on `claude/hollow-attack` 5069dcfbf as input to the army-follows-intent work, which reworks the same garrison and army-size decisions.
- **2026-10-30: Focus fire and straggler hold are on by default (`focus_fire` 100, `gather_hold` 50; `claude/focus-on`): the 2,048-game gate was neutral, so they ship for the war tests.** Batch `w24-focus-1030-215647`, control 348/2,048 wins (17.0%). Seat win-rate change in pp with 95% interval: all rivals and seat together (`focus_all`) +0.20 [-1.67, +2.06]; rivals only (`focus_opp`) -0.54 [-2.39, +1.32]; our seat only (`focus_seat`) -0.34 [-2.13, +1.44]. All inside noise, and the war suite and engagement measures improved (see the W2.4 entry above). Only one determinism pin moved (`scripted_hard_city_fire_tape` in `crates/sim/tests/pins.json`): the Hard scripted brain now fires jointly in that tape; the mixed tapes and fixtures are unchanged. `sparta_142_raid_counts_arrivals_before_sending_the_first_phalangite` compares the whole recorded plan, so it pins both genes to 0.
- 2026-10-31: **Hard in the app has no *whole-game* strategy layer** (`docs/design/research/hard-strategy-layer-2026-10-31.md`).
  - **Correction (lead, same day):** "no strategy layer" overstated it. Hard does run the goal-driven plan (`plan.rs`, on since 10-04, `scripted.rs:40` `plan_layer`). It picks Economy, Campaign, Contain, Peace or Snipe by chance to win, with persistence and aborts. The five `Strategy` labels were superseded by that plan on purpose. What's missing is a whole-game strategy: a build, research, army and Gold path toward a power spike.
  - **Committed whole-game strategies were tried twice and lost.** On 10-09, opt-in bundles scored 43.2% against 52.3% for the tuned blend. On 10-26, in the strategy lab, only air dominance beat baseline, and that didn't replicate. So the plan deferred them to step 3.
  - **Likely reason, now visible:** the execution gaps below. A committed strategy running on broken execution loses to a blend that hedges.
  - **The labels are dead code.** The `Strategy` enum (Rush, Wide, Science, Turtle, Economic) is never set for app Hard: `ScriptedBrain::with_difficulty` (`session.rs:42-67`) leaves it None. `Posture` only runs under Search with postures, which is off. The phased OpeningBrain runs only in Hard v2, also off.
  - **Per-game intent is one war objective** from `plan.rs`, on a 10-turn horizon. Everything else is a faction personality (5 knobs plus banked genes).
  - **Build, research, army size and Gold are multipliers and quotas,** with no goal, timing, phases or abort. Nothing plans a power spike such as reaching the Modern era first for Flight.
  - **Why intent never becomes action:** the plan owns no units or Gold, and earlier passes spend both first. Contain needs 2 or more units within 4 hexes of the target, and nothing moves them out of the cities. The Peace override needs `searched`, which plain Hard never has.
  - **Next:** one saved, per-game strategy that owns its army and budget. The skateboard is Conquer vs Economy-then-buy.
- 2026-10-31: **Two more full gates (2,048 per arm), both neutral.**
  - **`hoard_at_war` 2** (batch `w26-1030-231320`, control 345): all seats −0.24 pp [−1.57, +1.08]; rivals-only −1.03 [−2.22, +0.17]. The designer's seat wins less against Swiss rivals that spend their hoard at war, which is the right direction. Shipping it at 2.
  - **`learned_value` 1** (batch `w27-1030-232243`, control 348): seat-only −1.22 [−2.90, +0.46]; rivals-only −0.44 [−2.44, +1.56]; all seats +0.39 [−1.60, +2.38].
  - **Better calibration isn't better decisions.** The network predicts game outcomes far better than the hand formula (held-out Brier +18% against −34%), but swapping it into `winchance` doesn't help Hard choose. Its plan options are still limited by what the execution layer can carry out. Kept off; the next step is the regret harness and using it as the committed-strategy selector.
- 2026-10-31: **`dead_war_peace` 12 full gate is neutral; shipping it for the case the designer cares about.** Batch `w28-1030-233855`, 2,048 games per arm, control 352: all seats +0.34 pp [−1.58, +2.27], rivals-only −0.34 [−2.28, +1.59], seat-only +0.24 [−1.35, +1.83]. On his 8.9.17 Mongols game from T96, the gene makes the Celts declare on him in 32 of 32 replays (12 before), and Hard in his seat wins 19/32 (24 before). This continues the pattern that the 2,048-game benchmark can't see fixes for his real failure cases. Judge by fixtures cut from his games ([workflow skill]).
- 2026-10-31: **The decision audit ranks Hard's mistakes against the designer's own decisions** (`docs/sim-results/ai-war/decision-audit.md`, `tools/decision-audit/run.sh`). Five of his wins were audited (42,496 branch games, about $0.60). Every game replayed with 0 hash mismatches.
  - **A one-turn swap measures nothing** (+0.06 cities): Hard takes over the next turn and makes the same choices again. Swap a 10-turn policy instead.
  - **His policy beats Hard's in his seat in every game:** +0.51 ±0.03 cities @20 and +0.25 VP @50.
  - **Army is the biggest cost: +0.21, positive in all 5.** Hard pulls back or skips units where he attacks.
  - **Production and buys: +0.13.** Hard makes 80 to 396 purchases a game against his 0 to 7, so in his seat Hard overspends.
  - **Garrison: +0.12.**
  - **Raze: +0.11.** Hard razes captured cities in 4 of 5 games.
  - **Settling, research and diplomacy:** about 0.
  - **`winchance` overrates army size,** so the audit ranks by cities and VP. Any decision source plugs in (`--alt hard`, `params:FILE`, `orders:FILE.jsonl`), so an LLM's orders can be scored the same way.
- 2026-10-31: **Better evaluation of today's plan options doesn't change outcomes** (step 3 regret harness, `crates/sim/examples/value_regret.rs`). Setup: 768 fresh games, 3,069 positions, each disagreeing pick forced for 20 turns and then played out 24 times on the same dice.
  - **Results:** network vs hand formula +0.04 pp regret saved [−0.05, +0.14]; refit 0.00 pp [−0.05, +0.05].
  - **The options barely differ:** where picks disagree, the two picks' win rates differ by 3.1 pp, and in 55% the seat loses either way.
  - **Conclusion:** the plan options (Economy, Contain, Peace, Campaign) don't carry enough difference in what actually happens. Improving the chooser can't help until the options themselves do something different: committed strategies that own units and Gold, and execution that marches. A well-calibrated value estimate is still useful for Search leaves and as a selector between real strategies.
- 2026-10-31: **A committed Conquer strategy that targets the weakest neighbour first makes Hard easier for the designer** (rivals-only arm, 512 games: his seat 83 → 122 wins). The AIs snowball off each other while he grows untouched. In an 8-empire field with a strong human, target choice must weigh the VP race and the leader ("stop the leader", 10-24), not only capture value. Also: musters slow enough to let Walls rise left 93% of assaults hitting a full-HP city. Muster speed and wall-aware target choice matter as much as committing. See `docs/sim-results/ai-war/committed-strategy/README.md` on the skateboard branch.
- 2026-10-31: **The W0.4 gate read opponent arms backwards, and its "ship-monitor" label meant "not shown harmful".** From the external review (`.reviews/astra-full-review-1031.md` 1.2).
  - **Sign:** the recorded outcome is always the designer's seat win. For `opponent-only` arms a lower seat win rate means stronger rivals, but `quality_gate.compare` treated higher as better. `w04_gate.py` now keeps the raw seat delta (`delta`, `simultaneous_interval`) and decides on a role-normalised strength effect: `seat-only` +delta, `opponent-only` -delta, `all-seats` has none (its seat delta is seat gain minus rival gain, so the label is `role-mixed`; read the seat and opponent arms). Synthetic controls for both signs: `tools/sim/test_quality_gate.py`, `tools/ai-quality/test_w04_gate.py`.
  - **Enforcement:** a repeated `ctl` that does not reproduce game for game now fails the analysis; commit and binary come from the batch's own `BATCH_INFO` and slice logs (all slices and batches must agree) and zero or missing identifiers are rejected (`quality_gate.check_provenance`). Batches record only the 8-hex commit and the 16-hex binary prefix, so those are what is verified.
  - **Label:** AI-development gates now end at `inconclusive`, not `ship-monitor`; only a protocol with `policy: designer-rule-ship-unless-clear-harm` (a rule the designer decided on other grounds) keeps `ship-monitor`.
  - **Re-read of w24 to w28 (all had been `ship-monitor`):** only `hw2_opp` (`w26`) is `noninferior` (rivals stronger by +1.03 pp, adjusted [-0.77, +2.82]); `march_opp` (w25) is -0.63 strength, and every other arm is `inconclusive` or `role-mixed`. Noninferior is not improved: the descriptive interval of `hw2_opp` still spans zero narrowly.
  - **Benchmark chance:** the 12.5% "one seat of eight" reference was wrong. The benchmark is one 2-player and seven 4-player maps, so equal seats give (0.5 + 7 x 0.25) / 8 = 28.1%, a reference rather than a prediction.
  - **Diagnostics:** `ScriptedBrain::production_diagnosis` finished orders through `finish` only, not the live path (`finish_logged`, goal actions, air, budget, allied-city filters). It now runs the live path on a copy, and `order_diagnosis` returns an `OrderTrace` (proposed, then per stage added and rejected orders with reasons, then executed). `crates/ai/tests/order_trace.rs` checks a traced run emits the same orders and leaves the same memory as an untraced one. Only the Easy/Normal `finish` stage drops orders in those fixtures; the budget, goal and allied stages are covered by a unit test of the diff, not by a live case.
- 2026-10-31: **The AI gate's decision label is backwards for opponents arms** (Astra review §1.2). `w04_gate.py` passes human-seat wins to `quality_gate.compare`, which treats higher as better. For a `mode: opponents` arm, a higher seat-win rate means *weaker* opponents. Read the raw deltas, where negative means tougher rivals, until the gate-repair fix lands. Also: the benchmark is 8 maps, one 2-player and seven 4-player, so the equal-seat reference is 28.1%, not "12.5%, one seat of eight". The 16–17% control is below even-seat odds.
- 2026-10-31: **First clear benchmark gain in weeks: keep captured cities and stop routine overbuying** (batch `w29-1031-013833`, 2,048 games per arm, control 358; strength in pp, positive means a stronger AI).
  - **Both genes** (`raze_value` 1 + `buy_hold` 3): seat-only +5.03 [+2.98, +7.08]; opponents-only +3.47 [+1.48, +5.46], so the designer's seat wins 3.5 points less.
  - **Each alone,** opponents-only: `raze_value` +2.10 [+0.44, +3.76]; `buy_hold` +2.54 [+0.53, +4.55].
  - **Where the fixes came from:** the decision audit against his own play, not more behaviour. Hard razed 40% of its captures because it valued a city at 75 + 20 × pop and read any nearby army as certain loss. It also bought routine items at 200% about 4 turns before they would finish.
  - **Lesson:** economy-side mistakes the audit ranks can move the full-game benchmark where war-behaviour changes did not.
- 2026-10-31: **Top-down offense keeps failing in full games; audit-led narrow fixes work.**
  - **Three rounds of top-down offense lost on the rivals-only arm** (designer's seat wins out of 512, control 83). Committed strategies r4 122, r5 109; the funded operation r6 172. The operation did turn `war-hollow-swiss-t76` from 0% to 80%.
  - **Its failure chain:** it released garrisons without a reserved answer (the seat's cities lost rose from 2.96 to 3.56). It took units from working wars (Chur 48% to 0%). Shooters died to the city strike once Walls rose.
  - **Meanwhile the decision-audit fixes moved the gate clearly.** `raze_value` + `buy_hold`: rivals-only strength +3.47 pp, seat-only +5.03.
  - **Method from here:**
    1. Rank Hard's mistakes with the audit against his decisions.
    2. Fix one narrow mistake behind a gene.
    3. Confirm with the audit, then the role-correct gate.
  - **Stop building new offense layers** until the audit names an offense mistake narrowly. The operation code stays on `worktree-agent-a8e22f87f1bf20048` (`7f7fa6258`, gene `funded_operation` off) for reference.
- 2026-10-31: **Density fixes are measured by rectangles, and the card or panel has to give up its decoration at 1000x600** (astra review §3, branch `ui-density-1031`; specs `app/e2e/unlock-density.spec.ts`, `city-density.spec.ts`).
  - **Unlock card:** `UnlockMoment` gave a card with one item plus a resource reveal (Education: University and Niter) the big hero art, which pushed the Niter row below the card body at 1000x600. A DOM check said "visible" because the row existed. The fix is by count: one benefit gets the hero, two or more use the compact ledger rows. Three or more (Iron Working has four) on a window shorter than 700 px also drop the seal and quote and shrink the art to 48 px, so every row and both buttons sit inside the viewport (the specs compare each `.unlock-tile` rectangle with the viewport and the card body).
  - **City panel:** the sticky `.cp-head` was 150 px for a damaged walled city (name, numbers, a healing bar and a fire line) and 185 px at 1440x900. Healing, fire and bounty now sit under it and scroll away, the numbers share one line at every height, and a queue row is one line instead of two. Complete production choices at 1000x600 with the picker under the head: 2 → 3 (an idle city at open, 2 → 3 too; a busy city with a 5-item queue needs one scroll to reach the picker, its first card is still below the fold at open). The idle auto-scroll (`revealIdleProductionPicker`) is unchanged.
  - **Not done:** a busy city still shows no choices without scrolling, because Now producing, the queue, growth and tiles come first. Folding the queue's first row into Now producing is the next lever.
- 2026-10-31: **`capture_hold` (+ `strike_kill`) confirmed on the full gate; the quarter gate's seat effect didn't replicate.** Batch `w30-1031-032745`, 2,048 games per arm, control 407 = 19.9%, up from 358 now that raze/hold are on in this binary. Opponents-only strength: `capture_hold` +2.64 [+0.90, +4.37]; both genes +3.32 [+1.51, +5.13]. Seat-only: +0.05 and +1.17, both inside noise. The 512-game quarter gate had read seat-only +3.91 [+0.98, +6.84]. Lesson: a quarter-gate seat effect concentrated on one map (8.4 Sparta, 24 → 43 of 64) isn't evidence. Confirm on 2,048 games before claiming a seat gain.
- 2026-10-31: **Checking the build label: grep `app/dist`, not the installed `.app`.** Tauri embeds the web assets compressed inside the binary, so `grep -r "Playtest 8.9.18" /Applications/Legatum.app` finds nothing even on a correct build. Check `app/dist/assets/index-*.js` in the build worktree instead. The release-notes list there includes the new label.
- 2026-10-31: **The unlanded-branch check missed India because it only looked at recent Codex finishes.** India (decided 10-25) was built on `astra/india-regional-courts` on 10-26 and passed its checks, but was never merged; the held `japan-tokugawa` task waited on it. The ops check's "unlanded over 3 hours" rule reads finish times from recent `astra.log` lines, so branches older than its window drop out, and there are 148 unmerged `astra/*` branches, which hides one important one among many abandoned ones. A finished write task needs a same-day outcome (merge, fix, or a recorded reason to park), and the check should compare against agreed features, not a time window (audit: `docs/status/agreed-vs-built.md`).
- 2026-10-31: **Where the Mac's disk went: `.claude/worktrees` was 459 GB.** About 270 GB was four persistent Codex lanes (`lane-1`..`lane-4`), each with a 60–75 GB `target/`: incremental build caches grow without bound. Another ~190 GB was gate, release and finished-agent worktrees going back to 09-28. Most of those were "dirty" only from a rebuilt `Cargo.lock`, regenerated baselines or untracked run outputs, so a plain `git worktree remove` refuses them, and harness agent worktrees are locked (`git worktree list --porcelain` shows `locked claude agent`). A 23-day-old `legatum-server` from a release e2e run was still listening. A worktree is cleaned up when its work lands, not later. The ops supervisor now owns this.
- 2026-10-31: **A failed release check uploads no Playwright report.** The 8.9.19 check failed on 2 of 792 Chromium specs (`layout.spec.ts` "fits the window at 1100x700", and `wonder-visibility.spec.ts` "hovering the wonder explains it at 1000x600"). The box's artifacts held only `.box-timings`, and the full log (`/tmp/rc-*.log`) stayed on the box, so the error text wasn't available locally. The quickest triage was to rerun just those specs on master with `--repeat-each=3 --workers=2` through `tools/cloud/remote.sh`: 66 of 66 passed, so it was load flake on a box running six Codex lanes' verifies at once. The release check should upload `app/test-results` and the Playwright report when it fails.

## 2026-10-31: a verify log's sha is the master snapshot, and a train makes open branches conflict
The `sha` printed at the top of a `verify-remote.sh` log ("box: job ... commit <sha>", "[box] ... sha <sha>") is the master snapshot the branch is merged onto, not the branch tip. To confirm a verify covered a branch's latest commit, compare the job's start time with the tip's commit time (or read "Master snapshot:" against the branch ref). Also: landing a large integration train (train-1031, 17 branches) made every other open branch conflict on `game-rules.md` and `crates/sim/tests/pins.json`; plan a remerge-and-repin pass for open branches right after any train lands.

## 2026-10-31: release builds from a Claude session need sccache off for the WASM step too
The sandbox refuses sccache ("Operation not permitted"), so `scripts/build-wasm.sh` fails at the first crate unless `RUSTC_WRAPPER=` is exported for the whole build script, not only the Tauri step. The sandbox also blocks reading `~/.Trash` (moving into it works), and the installed binary does not contain the build label as plain text (Tauri compresses the frontend), so confirm the label with `grep -l "Playtest N" app/dist/assets/index-*.js` in the build worktree instead.

## 2026-10-31: the Mongol capture-pin exemption cost the designer a Settler snipe
In Playtest 8.9.20 (turn 52) he declared war to take an unescorted Mongol Settler from an adjacent tile; the Settler moved away and escaped. Cause: a civilian is normally pinned and captured when a hostile military unit steps onto it, but `rules/movement.rs` skips the pin for a Mongol civilian that has its own move this turn (part of slip-past). Nothing in the attack preview warned him, so the rule read as a bug (pillar 5). The designer had already decided to remove slip-past that morning; the removal was split out of the held faction-trims task as `mongol-sparta-trims`.

## 2026-10-31: `hard_vs_human` example tests pin single games outside `pins.json`
`crates/sim/examples/hard_vs_human.rs` has tests (`rival_search_is_the_old_benchmark`, `plain_hard_rivals_are_the_default_benchmark`) that assert one benchmark game's outcome as JSON. The repin step does not touch them, so any AI behaviour change (here, Settler raids now including Mongol Settlers) shows up as a plain test failure while the verify still reports "no pins diff". Update the expected JSON in the same change and say why in the commit.

## 2026-10-31: a lone civilian's tile is never contested, and side-wide Peace surprises
Two findings from the designer's 8.9.21 Aztec game. (1) `rules/movement.rs` plans same-tile meetings only for tiles absent from the occupancy snapshot, so a tile holding only a hostile civilian never produces a battle between two hostile claimants; both bounce and nobody captures. His turn 22 loss was not this: his Scout was 2 hexes away and a barbarian Soldier adjacent, so the barbarian captured in the first movement step. (2) Peace ends the war between whole sides (`peace_parties`), so accepting Athens's offer also made Peace with its new ally the Mongols, who had joined by allying with Athens mid-war (`alliance_wars`). The rules worked as designed; the cards never said which wars a Peace ends or why the Mongols joined. Both fixes queued as `capture-and-peace-clarity`. How to trace a playtest turn: copy the autosave, run `cargo run --release -p legatum-sim --example tape_unit_trace -- <copy> <first> <last> 0`, with `DUMP_AT=<turn> DUMP_TO=<file>` for the state before that turn, and read `archive.tape.turns[*].orders` and `archive.telemetry` from the save's `game` JSON string.

## 2026-10-31: an empty 0-HP city went uncaptured because the chosen captor was blocked
Designer's 8.9.21 game, turn 44: Shangdu at 0 HP, its 28-HP Mongol garrison walking out, three of his Soldiers ordered in. The Mongol parting-attack escape dropped the attacks; the first claimant was blocked ("tile occupied", the leaving garrison still held the tile in the snapshot) and `team_captors` had already chosen that unit, so the other two got "another teammate is capturing the city". Two lessons: a hostile unit leaving the target tile should be a dependency, not a block; and a captor choice must fall back when the captor fails. Turn 43 was the rules working: four attacks broke the city's last 12 HP and the overflow left the fortified garrison at 28 of 100, and a city falls only when its garrison does. `tape_unit_trace` now takes `EVENTS=1` to print every event in the traced turns, which is how this was found.

## 2026-10-31: the Swiss retake worked even though its campaign kept aborting
In the designer's 8.9.21 game the Swiss retook city 27 on T89, but their saved campaign log flapped: started T85, ended "insufficient_army" T86, restarted T87, ended T88, restarted T89 (`ai_opponent_memory["6"].campaigns.events`). The tactical passes (`strike_kill`, the opportunity strikes) kept attacking through the aborted turns, which is what won. Two lessons: the tactical layer now carries counterattacks on its own; and the campaign's commit/abort has no hysteresis against a target it just lost, so a stronger or less wounded attacker would have seen the Swiss dither. Candidate AI change: a lost city keeps a retake campaign alive for a few turns unless the force ratio collapses.

## 2026-10-31: merging `no-grandfathering` onto the cleanup and trims (`claude/train-1101`): deleting the migrations moved every old-format fixture

- **The known clash was bigger than one migration.** `rules-cleanup` kept old saves loadable with a load-time filter on crown keys; `no-grandfathering` deletes every migration and refuses other rules versions. The designer's call (no grandfathering, no migrations) means the crown-key filter goes too, and every state file that still names the deleted `MostTerritory` and `MostPopulous` crowns stops loading: 94 files (scenario states, AI and search archives, tapes, the `.current` copy saves, postgame fixtures), found by decoding every `.json`, `.gz`, `.zst` and the gzip-base64 scenario blobs. One script dropped the two keys (dict keys, list entries, `{"title": ...}` items) and wrote `save_version` 7 into the files that had 6; the old-version saves stay as refusal evidence. One `RULES_VERSION` bump (6 to 7) covers the whole train.
- **Which fixtures must stay unparseable.** `original-three-save.json` is the unmodified parent-engine save and its test asserts it cannot load; stripping it broke that test. Restore it, and check a fixture's test before running a bulk strip over it.
- **A bump needs the version inside every embedded state.** `rehash_scenarios` decodes each scenario through the wasm loader, which now refuses a state whose `save_version` is not current, and it stops at the first failure with only the last 15 lines in a `| tail`, so 16 war scenarios looked fine until the rest failed. Run it unfiltered.
- **A `.current` copy made through a migration can hide a lost rule.** The T203 late-race save kept its four Great Works as a legacy milestone plus a landmark-site migration. The cleanup deleted the milestone VP, no-grandfathering deleted the migration, and the combined engine scored those four teams 2 VP lower, so the Coalition alarm the spec reads vanished (Sparta 6/10 against 60% needed with a 15-point gap, 8/10 with the sites). The fix was to re-convert the original save through the engine that still had the migration (a scratch test in a worktree at the cleanup merge, writing `state_to_json`) and write that back as the `.current` copy.
- **Simultaneous contention grew a new case.** `full_game::scripted_orders_all_validate_and_succeed_in_a_real_game` failed on a Mongol ranged shot at a city Byzantium burned with its Raze order the same turn (Raze resolves before ranged fire). The test now treats a shot at a city razed that turn like a barbarian's moved target.
- **Other stale expectations:** `settler_routes` pins the state hash of three recorded starts (stripping the crown keys changed them), the plain-Hard first-map snapshot in `hard_vs_human.rs` (a T269 VP-hold win in 8.3 became no win), and the wasm history test of the removed legacy war rules was deleted.
- **Pins moved against master:** `default_ai_tapes` 16936341241511439516 / 6136715174920339980 / 1270325365555326785 became 3949770044240811493 / 14515071654567630563 / 443221552828584113, `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape` 18276880455648802876 became 1102315824648781614 (the Mongol movement and Sparta reward changes, decadence off and the India AI all change decisions or hashes); `garrison-current-hashes.json`, all war scenario hashes and four of the determinism fixtures moved with the state shape.
- **Combined human-seat benchmark (seed 310001, 256 salts x 8 maps = 2,048 games per binary, `LEGATUM_HVH_RESEED_DICE=1`, plain Hard rivals, paired bootstrap over salts).** Master (train-1031 included) 524/2,048 (25.6%), train 496 (24.2%): **-1.37 pp [-3.61, +0.93]**. The overall number hides large map swings that follow the rules, not noise: Switzerland 8.3 58 to 139 (+31.6 [+24.2, +39.1], probably decadence off), Sparta 8.4 181 to 71 (-43.0 [-50.4, -35.5], probably the seat's lost kill and capture Gold), India map 8.4 4 to 44 (+15.6 [+10.9, +20.7], probably the India AI), Germany 8.6 -8.2 [-15.2, -0.8], Sparta 8.6 -9.4 [-17.6, -1.2], the rest within noise. Two Celts 8.4 games (salts 145 and 189) ran to the 1,200-turn cap with no winner (master had none): one ended 9 VP against the 10 target. Not diagnosed; decadence off removes the brake on a runaway, so watch stalls.
